# Manifest — Group 2: Gauge Theory VII, VIII, IX, X, XI

Filename manifest and concept registry for chapters VII–XI of the Gauge Theory series. Sources: A = Haydys (content-map-haydys.md; items D/T/E/X/R/I by section), B = Bär (content-map-wernli.md; the file is misnamed — everything attributed to Bär). All chapter I–VI page names used below are the byte-exact anchors of `anchors.md`; every non-anchor chapter I–VI need is written `[NEEDED FROM I–VI: …]` for the orchestrator to reconcile. Chapter XII–XIII anchors are used by name where chapter XI's applications touch them.

## Group-wide design decisions (binding for writers of VII–XI; flagged for the orchestrator where they touch conventions.md)

1. **Hodge star.** Bär's convention $\omega \wedge \eta = \langle \star\omega, \eta\rangle\,\mathrm{vol}$ ($\omega \in \Lambda^k$, $\eta \in \Lambda^{n-k}$) is adopted series-wide, as conventions.md says. **Correction to the wording of conventions.md:** the vault's Hodge Theory I star $\star_V$ is defined by $\alpha \wedge \star_V \beta = \langle\alpha,\beta\rangle\mathrm{vol}$, and Bär's (3.5) gives $\omega \wedge \star_B\eta = (-1)^p\langle\omega,\eta\rangle\mathrm{vol}$; hence $\star_B = (-1)^p \star_V$ where $p$ is the index (number of negative directions) — **not** $(-1)^{k(n-k)}$. Check: on Minkowski $(-,+,+,+)$, $\star_B(dt\wedge dx) = dy\wedge dz$ (Bär's table) while $\star_V(dt\wedge dx) = -dy\wedge dz$. The two agree in every Riemannian signature, in every degree. The third convention $\star'\alpha \wedge \beta = \langle\alpha,\beta\rangle\mathrm{vol}$ satisfies $\star' = (-1)^{k(n-k)}\star_V$. Every page of VII–XI that uses $\star$ states Bär's defining relation and carries this three-way comparison in its `> [!warning] Convention:` callout. Consequence: chapters VIII–XI (Riemannian) may link Hodge Theory I pages (`Def - The Hodge Star Operator`, `Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star`, `Def - The Codifferential`, `Thm - Codifferential is the Adjoint of d`, `Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Forms`, `Def - The L2 Inner Product on Differential Forms`) freely, saying "in Riemannian signature the two stars coincide"; chapter VII's Lorentzian pages use only the series' own `Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature`.
2. **Sobolev exponent.** The series works in $L^2$-Sobolev spaces throughout: $W^{k,2} = L^2_k = H_k$, extended to real order $s$ on the torus by Fourier series and to compact manifolds by partition of unity (Lawson–Michelsohn III.§5 route). Haydys states the Sobolev embedding, Rellich, multiplication and elliptic-estimate theorems for all $p > 1$; the $L^p$ elliptic estimate is Calderón–Zygmund theory and is neither provable at chapter length nor on the pre-approved import list, and nothing in chapters IX–XI uses $p \neq 2$ (the one $L^p$ statement, Haydys Corollary 198, is a consequence of the $C^0$ bound). Therefore every Sobolev/elliptic `Thm -` page of chapter IX states and proves the $p = 2$ version; Haydys's general-$p$ formulation is recorded verbatim in a `> [!warning] Convention:` callout on the page as *unproved context that the series does not use*, with a reference (Evans, *Partial Differential Equations*, 2nd ed., Ch. 5; Adams–Fournier, *Sobolev Spaces*, Ch. 4–6; Gilbarg–Trudinger Ch. 9 for $L^p$ elliptic theory), never in a Theorem blockquote. Haydys's $(k,p) = (5,2)$ choice in §7 is unchanged. **Orchestrator: please confirm this scoping, or add the $L^p$ Sobolev embedding/Rellich (Evans 5.6–5.7) as extra pages; the $L^p$ elliptic estimate cannot be added.**
3. **Measure theory.** $L^p$ and $W^{k,p}$ are *defined* as completions of smooth (compactly supported) sections in the respective norms (Haydys D5.1.1–D5.1.2), so no Lebesgue theory is needed; every inequality is proved for smooth sections and extended by density. The vault's `Def - Lp Spaces` (Measure Theory II) is linked as context only; the identification of the two definitions (Riesz–Fischer plus density) is stated as context in a Relate section, explicitly marked "not used in this series". The flagged Measure Theory II theorems (Hölder, Minkowski, DCT, completeness of $L^p$) are never invoked; the only inequality needed, Cauchy–Schwarz, is `Thm - Cauchy-Schwarz Inequality` (Linear Algebra VI, proof-complete), valid in any inner-product space.
4. **Flagged vault pages that VII–XI re-prove under new names:** `Thm - Sard's Theorem` (DG IV) → `Thm - Sard's Theorem for Smooth Maps` (X); `Thm - Spin(n) is the Double Cover of SO(n)` (Spinors) → `Thm - Spin Groups in Dimensions Three and Four via Quaternions` and `Thm - Spin(n) inside the Clifford Algebra is a Double Cover of SO(n)` (VIII); `Thm - Lichnerowicz Formula` (Spinors) → `Thm - Lichnerowicz Formula for the Spin-c Dirac Operator` (VIII); `Thm - Classification of Clifford Algebras over R` (Spinors) is not needed — only $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^1), \mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^2), \mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^3), \mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^4)$ are computed, on `Thm - Low-Dimensional Clifford Algebras and the Quaternionic Spinor Modules`. Hodge Theory I's `Thm - Hodge Decomposition Theorem` is not flagged but its Formal Proof invokes "the Fredholm alternative for elliptic operators" as a black box, and `Thm - Bochner's Theorem`, `Thm - Poincare Duality via Hodge Star` (Hodge I) and `Thm - Lichnerowicz Formula` (Spinors) contain `Full proof (sketch)` callouts: **none of these four is cited as a proof anywhere in VII–XI**; chapter IX proves `Thm - Hodge Theorem for Elliptic Complexes` and the de Rham case in full, and chapter VII proves `Thm - Top de Rham Cohomology of a Closed Connected Oriented Manifold` itself. `Thm - The de Rham Theorem (Full Proof)` (AT I) is flagged: "Betti numbers are topological invariants" (A I5.3.2) is therefore stated in the series as *diffeomorphism and smooth-homotopy invariance* via `Thm - Homotopy Invariance of de Rham Cohomology` (DG X, proof-complete); the identification with singular Betti numbers is **bold plain-text context** with the de Rham theorem named, never invoked.
5. **Source typos (Haydys Appendix B, Bär Appendix B).** Every affected statement below carries the corrected form and a `(source typo: …)` note; the list of the ones landing in VII–XI: Haydys B10, B11, B12, B13, B14, B15, B16, B17, B18, B19, B20, B21, B22; Bär B15, B16, B17, B18, B19, B20, B21, B22.
6. **Imports.** The only `Imported without proof` callout in VII–XI is the Atiyah–Singer index theorem for the spin$^c$ Dirac operator, on `Thm - Dimension Formula for the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space` (XI). Two `[PROPOSED IMPORT]` additions (Taubes, Fintushel–Stern) are requested in XI §11.5; if refused, those two results appear only as bold plain-text history in the Motivation of `Def - Seiberg-Witten Invariant` and are never stated as theorems.

### §7.1 The Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature
- `Def - Generalized Orthonormal Basis, Index, and the Induced Inner Product on Forms` — type: definition (compound: three interlocking notions); source items: B-D3.1.1, B-D3.1.2, B-D3.1.3; prereqs: [Def - Bilinear Form, Def - Symmetric and Alternating Bilinear Form, Thm - Sylvester's Law of Inertia, Def - Alternating Tensor and Lambda k V Dual, Def - Orientation of a Vector Space]; spec: $V$ real $n$-dimensional with a non-degenerate symmetric bilinear form $\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle$ (not necessarily definite); a *generalized orthonormal basis* $e_1,\dots,e_n$ has $\langle e_i,e_j\rangle = 0$ ($i\ne j$), $\langle e_j,e_j\rangle = \epsilon_j = \pm1$; the *index* $p$ is the number of $j$ with $\epsilon_j = -1$ (well-defined by Sylvester — wikilink); the *induced inner product* on $\Lambda^kV^*$ is $\langle\omega,\eta\rangle := \sum_{i_1<\dots<i_k}\epsilon_{i_1}\cdots\epsilon_{i_k}\,\omega(e_{i_1},\dots,e_{i_k})\,\eta(e_{i_1},\dots,e_{i_k})$; the *volume form* is $\mathrm{vol} := e_1^*\wedge\dots\wedge e_n^*$ for a positively oriented generalized ONB, with $\langle\mathrm{vol},\mathrm{vol}\rangle = (-1)^p$ and $\mathrm{vol}\mapsto-\mathrm{vol}$ under orientation reversal. Existence of a generalized ONB is proved in the Examples (Gram–Schmidt on a non-degenerate form: diagonalise via `Thm - Diagonalization of a Symmetric Bilinear Form`, Linear Algebra IX, then rescale). Examples: Euclidean $\mathbb R^n$ ($p = 0$); Minkowski $\mathbb R^{1,3}$ with $(-,+,+,+)$ ($p=1$), where $\langle dt\wedge dx, dt\wedge dx\rangle = -1$; non-example: a degenerate form (no generalized ONB). Convention callout: Bär's "index" is the number of negative signs; the vault's `Def - Semi-Riemannian Metric and Signature` writes signature $(n-s,s)$ with $s = p$; SR XXI uses $(+,-,-,-)$, i.e. $p = 3$ for the same physics. Calibration check: compute $\langle e_1^*\wedge e_2^*, e_1^*\wedge e_2^*\rangle$ on Minkowski for spacelike and mixed pairs; verify $\langle\mathrm{vol},\mathrm{vol}\rangle = (-1)^p$ on $\mathbb R^{1,3}$.
- `Thm - The Induced Inner Product and Volume Form are Well-Defined` — type: theorem (three parts); source items: B-T3.1.1, B-T3.1.2, B-T3.1.3; prereqs: [Def - Generalized Orthonormal Basis, Index, and the Induced Inner Product on Forms, Thm - Determinant is Multiplicative]; statement: Let $(V,\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle)$ be an $n$-dimensional real vector space with a non-degenerate symmetric bilinear form. (i) The bilinear form $\langle\omega,\eta\rangle$ on $\Lambda^kV^*$ defined by the formula above does not depend on the generalized orthonormal basis used. (ii) For any generalized ONB, $\{e^*_{i_1}\wedge\dots\wedge e^*_{i_k}\}_{i_1<\dots<i_k}$ is a generalized orthonormal basis of $\Lambda^kV^*$ with $\langle e^*_I,e^*_I\rangle = \epsilon_{i_1}\cdots\epsilon_{i_k}$; in particular the induced form is non-degenerate. (iii) $\Lambda^nV^*$ is one-dimensional, $\langle e_1^*\wedge\dots\wedge e_n^*,e_1^*\wedge\dots\wedge e_n^*\rangle = (-1)^p$, and $e_1^*\wedge\dots\wedge e_n^*$ is determined by the orientation alone (the same for all positively oriented generalized ONBs); proof: Bär proves (i) fully but with inconsistent index placement (content map gap) — the writer sets $f_i = \sum_j A^j_i e_j$, derives $A^t\epsilon A = \epsilon'$ (with $\epsilon = \operatorname{diag}(\epsilon_j)$), hence $A\epsilon'^{-1}A^t = \epsilon^{-1}$, i.e. $\sum_l A^i_l A^j_l\epsilon'_l = \delta_{ij}\epsilon_i$, and expands $\frac1{k!}\sum\epsilon'_{i_1}\cdots\omega(f_{i_1},\dots)\eta(f_{i_1},\dots)$ in the $e$'s to contract; (ii) is a direct evaluation of the defining sum on wedge monomials (Bär omits it); (iii): one-dimensionality from $\dim\Lambda^n = 1$, the pairing value from (ii), the sign from $\det A = \pm1$ (from $A^t\epsilon A = \epsilon'$ and `Thm - Determinant is Multiplicative`) with $\det A > 0$ for orientation-preserving changes; spec: mechanism: the change-of-basis matrix between two generalized ONBs is a pseudo-orthogonal matrix, and pseudo-orthogonal matrices have determinant $\pm1$; targets: makes `Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature` well-posed; sources: any non-degenerate form, including Lorentzian metrics on $T_xM$.
- `Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature` — type: definition (anchor); source items: B-D3.1.4, B-D3.1.6, B-R3.1.1, B-R3.1.2; prereqs: [Def - Generalized Orthonormal Basis, Index, and the Induced Inner Product on Forms, Thm - The Induced Inner Product and Volume Form are Well-Defined, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Hodge Star]; spec: on an oriented $n$-dimensional $(V,\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle)$ of index $p$, $\star:\Lambda^kV^*\to\Lambda^{n-k}V^*$ is the unique linear map with $\omega\wedge\eta = \langle\star\omega,\eta\rangle\,\mathrm{vol}$ for all $\omega\in\Lambda^kV^*$, $\eta\in\Lambda^{n-k}V^*$ (existence/uniqueness wikilinked to the next page); pointwise extension to forms on an oriented semi-Riemannian manifold; $W$-valued forms: $\star(\omega\otimes w) := (\star\omega)\otimes w$ with $\langle v\otimes w,v'\otimes w'\rangle := \langle v,v'\rangle\langle w,w'\rangle$ (D3.1.6, R3.1.2 — needed for $\mathfrak g$-valued curvature in §7.4); R3.1.1: $\star$ depends on the inner product (relevant when the metric is varied in §7.3). **Convention callout (mandatory, see group decision 1):** the three conventions and the exact signs $\star_B = (-1)^p\star_V$, $\star' = (-1)^{k(n-k)}\star_V$; the vault's `Def - The Hodge Star Operator` is $\star_V$; note also that conventions.md's phrase "$(-1)^{k(n-k)}$" describes $\star'$ versus $\star_V$, not Bär versus the vault. Examples: Euclidean $\mathbb R^3$ ($\star dx = dy\wedge dz$, etc., recovering the vault's `Ex - Hodge Star on R^3 Recovers Cross Product and Scalar Triple Product`); Minkowski 2-forms (Bär's six-line star table: $\star(dt\wedge dx) = dy\wedge dz$, $\star(dy\wedge dz) = -dt\wedge dx$, $\star(dt\wedge dy) = dz\wedge dx$, $\star(dz\wedge dx) = -dt\wedge dy$, $\star(dt\wedge dz) = dx\wedge dy$, $\star(dx\wedge dy) = -dt\wedge dz$, each verified from the defining relation on the page); non-example: no such map exists for a degenerate form. Calibration check: verify $\star\star = -1$ on Minkowski 2-forms from the table; verify $\star_B(dt\wedge dx) = -\star_V(dt\wedge dx)$.
- `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Hodge Star` — type: theorem; source items: B-T3.1.4; prereqs: [Thm - The Induced Inner Product and Volume Form are Well-Defined, Thm - Riesz Representation Theorem (Finite-Dimensional)]; statement: For every $\omega\in\Lambda^kV^*$ there is a unique $\star\omega\in\Lambda^{n-k}V^*$ with $\omega\wedge\eta = \langle\star\omega,\eta\rangle\,\mathrm{vol}$ for all $\eta\in\Lambda^{n-k}V^*$, and $\omega\mapsto\star\omega$ is linear; proof: Bär's proof is full modulo the unstated non-degeneracy of the induced form on $\Lambda^{n-k}V^*$ (the content-map gap): for fixed $\omega$, $\eta\mapsto(\omega\wedge\eta)/\mathrm{vol}$ is a linear functional on $\Lambda^{n-k}V^*$ (division by $\mathrm{vol}$ meaning the coefficient in the one-dimensional $\Lambda^nV^*$); non-degeneracy of $\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle$ on $\Lambda^{n-k}V^*$ (part (ii) of the previous page) gives a unique representing vector — the writer proves the indefinite Riesz lemma on the page (a non-degenerate bilinear form on a finite-dimensional space induces an isomorphism with the dual; `Thm - Riesz Representation Theorem (Finite-Dimensional)` covers the definite case only, so restate and prove the general case as Lemma 1 via injectivity plus dimension count); linearity in $\omega$ from uniqueness; spec: mechanism: **the wedge pairing $\Lambda^k\times\Lambda^{n-k}\to\Lambda^n\cong\mathbb R$ is perfect, and a perfect pairing composed with a non-degenerate inner product is an isomorphism**.
- `Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature` — type: theorem (five parts (3.2)–(3.6)); source items: B-T3.1.5; prereqs: [Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of the Hodge Star]; statement: For $V$ oriented, $n$-dimensional, of index $p$, $\omega,\eta\in\Lambda^kV^*$ where indicated: (1) $\star(e^*_{i_1}\wedge\dots\wedge e^*_{i_k}) = \epsilon_{j_1}\cdots\epsilon_{j_{n-k}}\,\mathrm{sign}(IJ)\,e^*_{j_1}\wedge\dots\wedge e^*_{j_{n-k}}$ where $(IJ) = (i_1,\dots,i_k,j_1,\dots,j_{n-k})$ is a permutation of $(1,\dots,n)$; (2) $\star\star\omega = (-1)^{k(n-k)+p}\omega$; (3) $\langle\star\omega,\star\eta\rangle = (-1)^p\langle\omega,\eta\rangle$; (4) $\omega\wedge\star\eta = \eta\wedge\star\omega = (-1)^p\langle\omega,\eta\rangle\,\mathrm{vol}$; (5) for $\omega\in\Lambda^k$, $\eta\in\Lambda^{n-k}$: $\omega\wedge\eta = (-1)^{k(n-k)}\langle\omega,\star\eta\rangle\,\mathrm{vol}$; proof: Bär proves (1) and (5) in full and leaves (2)–(4) as exercises; the page proves all five: (1) by the complementary-index argument ($e^*_I\wedge e^*_J = \mathrm{sign}(IJ)\mathrm{vol}$ and the defining relation), (2) by applying (1) twice and computing $\mathrm{sign}(IJ)\mathrm{sign}(JI) = (-1)^{k(n-k)}$ and $\prod_{j\in J}\epsilon_j\prod_{i\in I}\epsilon_i = (-1)^p$, (3) from (1) on basis monomials plus bilinearity, (4) $\omega\wedge\star\eta = \langle\star\omega,\star\eta\rangle\mathrm{vol}$ (defining relation with $\star\eta$ in place of $\eta$) then (3), (5) as Bär: $\omega\wedge\eta = \langle\star\omega,\eta\rangle\mathrm{vol} = (-1)^p\langle\star\star\omega,\star\eta\rangle\mathrm{vol} = (-1)^{k(n-k)+2p}\langle\omega,\star\eta\rangle\mathrm{vol}$; spec: link `Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star` (Hodge I) as the $p = 0$ case in the vault's convention and state the translation; the Ex pages X3.1.1–X3.1.3 below drill (2)–(4) and their solutions are the corresponding lemmas of this page; mechanism: everything reduces to the sign of a shuffle permutation and the product of the $\epsilon_j$ over a complementary index set.
- `Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions` — type: theorem; source items: B-T3.1.6, B-T3.1.7, B-E3.1.1, B-D3.1.5 (→ inside, Notation; the definition itself is transcluded from the vault's `Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Forms`, which agrees with Bär's in Riemannian signature); prereqs: [Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature, Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Forms]; statement: Let $V$ be oriented, four-dimensional, Euclidean ($p=0$). Then $\star\star = 1$ on $\Lambda^2V^*$ and $\star$ is an isometry of $\Lambda^2V^*$; hence $\Lambda^2V^* = \Lambda^2_+V^*\oplus\Lambda^2_-V^*$ orthogonally, $\Lambda^2_\pm := \{\omega : \star\omega = \pm\omega\}$, $\dim\Lambda^2_\pm = 3$, with orthogonal bases $e_1^*\wedge e_2^*\pm e_3^*\wedge e_4^*$, $e_1^*\wedge e_3^*\mp e_2^*\wedge e_4^*$, $e_1^*\wedge e_4^*\pm e_2^*\wedge e_3^*$ of $\Lambda^2_\pm$; $\omega\wedge\omega = \pm|\omega|^2\mathrm{vol}$ for $\omega\in\Lambda^2_\pm$ and $\omega_+\wedge\omega_- = 0$; reversing the orientation of $V$ negates $\star$ and interchanges $\Lambda^2_+$ and $\Lambda^2_-$; proof: Bär: full (orthogonality of the six listed forms plus dimension count; orientation reversal immediate from (3.1)); the page adds the identities $\omega\wedge\omega = \pm|\omega|^2\mathrm{vol}$ (from (4) with $p=0$: $\omega\wedge\star\omega = |\omega|^2\mathrm{vol}$) and $\omega_+\wedge\omega_- = \langle\star\omega_+,\omega_-\rangle\mathrm{vol} = \langle\omega_+,\omega_-\rangle\mathrm{vol} = 0$ — these are the sign facts Haydys uses silently in Corollary 199 (A-T7.1.11 gap) and are the reference point for chapter XI; spec: mechanism: **an involutive isometry splits the space into its $\pm1$ eigenspaces, and in dimension four on 2-forms the star is such an involution**; targets: instantons (§7.4), $\Lambda^2_+\cong\mathfrak{su}(\slashed S^+)$ (XI), $\mathfrak{so}(4) = \mathfrak{so}(3)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(3)$ (VIII).
- `Ex - Double Star Sign in Arbitrary Signature` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-X3.1.1; prereqs: [Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature]; spec: prove $\star\star\omega = (-1)^{k(n-k)+p}\omega$ from property (1); route: compute on basis monomials, count the shuffle sign and the $\epsilon$-product; check on Minkowski 2-forms ($= -1$) and Euclidean $\mathbb R^3$ 1-forms ($= +1$); invokes `Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature` (1) only.
- `Ex - The Hodge Star is an Isometry up to the Sign of the Index` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-X3.1.2; prereqs: [Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature]; spec: prove $\langle\star\omega,\star\eta\rangle = (-1)^p\langle\omega,\eta\rangle$; route: reduce to basis monomials via (1), or derive from the defining relation and the double-star formula; illegal-but-tempting: assuming $\star$ is an isometry in Lorentzian signature (counterexample $dt\wedge dx$).
- `Ex - Symmetry of the Star Pairing` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-X3.1.3; prereqs: [Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature]; spec: prove $\omega\wedge\star\eta = \eta\wedge\star\omega = (-1)^p\langle\omega,\eta\rangle\mathrm{vol}$ for $\omega,\eta\in\Lambda^k$; route: defining relation with $\star\eta$ as the second slot, then (3); deduce the $L^2$ pairing formula $\int_M\omega\wedge\star\eta = (-1)^p\langle\omega,\eta\rangle_{L^2}$ on an oriented compact semi-Riemannian manifold (used in §7.2–7.4 variations).
- `Ex - Conformal Weight of the Hodge Star` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (first half of B-R3.2.11, the algebra only; the physics conclusion is on `Thm - Conformal Invariance of the Electromagnetic Action and Tracelessness of the Energy-Momentum Tensor`); prereqs: [Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature, Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature]; spec: for $g' = \lambda^2 g$ ($\lambda > 0$) on an oriented $n$-dimensional $V$ show $\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle'_{\Lambda^k} = \lambda^{-2k}\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle$, $\mathrm{vol}' = \lambda^n\mathrm{vol}$, and $\star' = \lambda^{2k-n}\star$ on $\Lambda^k$; conclude $\star' = \star$ on 2-forms in dimension 4; route: $e_i' = \lambda^{-1}e_i$ is a generalized ONB for $g'$, so $(e_i^*)' = \lambda e_i^*$; note Bär's typo "$g' = \lambda\cdot g$" (source typo: Bär p. 93 writes $\lambda g$ but computes with $\lambda^2 g$).
- `Exercise Index - §7.1 The Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature` — lists: [Ex - Double Star Sign in Arbitrary Signature, Ex - The Hodge Star is an Isometry up to the Sign of the Index, Ex - Symmetry of the Star Pairing, Ex - Conformal Weight of the Hodge Star]

### §7.2 Electrodynamics as a U(1) Gauge Theory
- `Def - U(1) Gauge Field and Electromagnetic Connection` — type: definition (anchor; backlink-map name, 3 external links); source items: B-D3.2.1, B-D3.2.3, B-R3.2.1; prereqs: [Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle, Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Def - Local Connection Form and Gauge Potential, Thm - Bianchi Identity for a Principal Connection, Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space, Def - Lorentzian Manifold, Def - The Electromagnetic Field Tensor, Def - The Four-Potential]; spec: $M$ an oriented Lorentzian 4-manifold of signature $(-,+,+,+)$ (index $p=1$, $c=1$), $P\to M$ a principal $U(1)$-bundle, $\omega\in\mathcal C(P)$ a connection; because $\operatorname{Ad}$ is trivial for $U(1)$, $s^*\Omega$ is independent of the local section $s$ and defines a global $\bar\Omega\in\Omega^2(M;i\mathbb R)$ (invoke `[NEEDED FROM I–VI: abelian curvature descends]` or the anchor `Thm - Transformation of Local Connection and Curvature Forms` with $\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}} = \mathrm{id}$); the *electromagnetic field strength* is $F\in\Omega^2(M;\mathbb R)$ with $\bar\Omega = iF$; the *electric and magnetic fields* relative to coordinates $(t,x,y,z)$ are the coefficients in $F = E_x\,dx\wedge dt + E_y\,dy\wedge dt + E_z\,dz\wedge dt + B_x\,dy\wedge dz + B_y\,dz\wedge dx + B_z\,dx\wedge dy$ (coordinate-dependent; $F_{i0} = E_i$); the *potential relative to a background connection* $\omega_0$ is $iA := s^*(\omega-\omega_0)\in\Omega^1(M;i\mathbb R)$, section-independent, with $dA = F - F_0$; Bianchi (anchor) gives $dF = 0$ (R3.2.1). Convention callout: SR XXI's `Def - The Electromagnetic Field Tensor` uses $(+,-,-,-)$ and SI units — same 2-form $F$, same $\mathbf E,\mathbf B$, $g_{SR} = -g_B$, $\star_V^{SR} = -\star_B$ on Minkowski; SR XXII's `Def - The Four-Potential` $A$ with $F = dA$ is Bär's $A$ for the trivial background $\omega_0 = $ flat connection on the trivial bundle; also state Haydys's local form: on a trivialisation, $\omega = s^*\omega = iA_{\text{loc}}$, $F = dA_{\text{loc}}$. Categorical section: the $U(1)$ gauge field is a connection on a $U(1)$-torsor bundle; gauge potentials are its local trivialised forms. Examples: the trivial bundle over Minkowski space with $A = -\phi\,dt + \mathbf A\cdot d\mathbf x$ recovering $\mathbf E = -\nabla\phi-\partial_t\mathbf A$, $\mathbf B = \nabla\times\mathbf A$ (verified); the Hopf bundle over $S^2$ with $F$ proportional to the area form (link `Def - The Hopf Bundle`, `Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature`) — closed but not exact, so no global background-free potential (non-instance of "$F = dA$ globally"); calibration: check $dF = 0$ in the Minkowski example directly.
- `Def - Charge-Current 3-Form` — type: definition; source items: B-D3.2.2; prereqs: [Def - U(1) Gauge Field and Electromagnetic Connection, Def - The Electric Four-Current]; spec: $J := \varrho\,dx\wedge dy\wedge dz - j_x\,dt\wedge dy\wedge dz - j_y\,dt\wedge dz\wedge dx - j_z\,dt\wedge dx\wedge dy\in\Omega^3(M;\mathbb R)$ with charge density $\varrho$ and current density $\vec j$; relation to the four-current 1-form/vector $j$ of SR XXII: $J = \star_B j^\flat$ up to a sign computed on the page from the star table (state the sign explicitly); examples: static point charge smeared into a bump function; a steady line current; non-example: a 3-form with a $dx\wedge dy\wedge dz$-free part cannot carry charge density.
- `Def - Maxwell Equations in Form Language` — type: definition (anchor; the equations as the defining laws of the theory); source items: (equations (3.7)–(3.10) as stated laws; the derivations are on the next Thm page); prereqs: [Def - U(1) Gauge Field and Electromagnetic Connection, Def - Charge-Current 3-Form, Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature]; spec: the Maxwell equations for $(\omega,J)$ are $dF = 0$ (homogeneous; an identity by Bianchi) and $d\star F + J = 0$ (inhomogeneous; a law) — Bär's sign, from the Euler–Lagrange equation of §7.2; Convention callout: SR XXII's `Thm - Maxwell Equations` writes $d\star_V F = \mu_0\star_V j$ in $(+,-,-,-)$; conversion $\star_B = -\star_V$ and the $J$-versus-$j$ sign from `Def - Charge-Current 3-Form`; Bär's own typo (source typo: Bär p. 95 writes "$d\star F = J$" while p. 86 has $d\star F + J = 0$; the series uses $d\star F + J = 0$ throughout and re-derives Poynting's theorem with this sign); examples: vacuum ($J = 0$) on Minkowski space; Coulomb field $\mathbf E = q\,\vec x/|\vec x|^3$ satisfying $d\star F = 0$ away from the origin (verified on the page); non-example: a field with $\operatorname{div}\vec B\ne0$ cannot be the curvature of any $U(1)$-connection.
- `Thm - Maxwell Equations in Coordinates` — type: theorem; source items: B-R3.2.2, B-R3.2.5, B-E3.2.1; prereqs: [Def - Maxwell Equations in Form Language, Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature, Thm - Coordinate Expression for the Exterior Derivative]; statement: On Minkowski space with $\mathrm{vol} = dt\wedge dx\wedge dy\wedge dz$: (i) $dF = (\partial_xB_x+\partial_yB_y+\partial_zB_z)\,dx\wedge dy\wedge dz + (\partial_yE_z-\partial_zE_y+\partial_tB_x)\,dt\wedge dy\wedge dz + (\partial_zE_x-\partial_xE_z+\partial_tB_y)\,dt\wedge dz\wedge dx + (\partial_xE_y-\partial_yE_x+\partial_tB_z)\,dt\wedge dx\wedge dy$, so $dF = 0 \iff \operatorname{div}\vec B = 0$ (Gauss's law for magnetism, (3.7)) and $\partial_t\vec B + \operatorname{rot}\vec E = 0$ (Faraday, (3.8)); (ii) $\star F = -E_x\,dy\wedge dz - E_y\,dz\wedge dx - E_z\,dx\wedge dy + B_x\,dx\wedge dt + B_y\,dy\wedge dt + B_z\,dz\wedge dt$ (source typo: Bär p. 86 prints "$-B_z\,dz\wedge dt$"; the star table gives $+B_z\,dz\wedge dt$); (iii) $d\star F = (-\operatorname{div}\vec E)\,dx\wedge dy\wedge dz + (\operatorname{rot}\vec B-\partial_t\vec E)_x\,dt\wedge dy\wedge dz + (\dots)_y\,dt\wedge dz\wedge dx + (\dots)_z\,dt\wedge dx\wedge dy$, so $d\star F + J = 0 \iff \operatorname{div}\vec E = \varrho$ (Coulomb, (3.9)) and $\operatorname{rot}\vec B - \partial_t\vec E = \vec j$ (Ampère, (3.10)); proof: Bär's computation, written out coefficient by coefficient (source typo: Bär p. 84 prints the $dt\wedge dy\wedge dz$ coefficient of $dF$ as "$-\partial_yE_z+\partial_yE_z+\partial_tB_x$"; correct is $\partial_yE_z-\partial_zE_y+\partial_tB_x$); spec: link the vault's `Ex - Maxwell's Equations in Three-Dimensional Form` (SR XXII) and `Ex - Maxwell's Equations as Two Form Equations on Minkowski Space` (DG IX) as the same computation in the other signature; mechanism: $d$ on 2-forms in four coordinates is div and curl in disguise (`Def - Frankel Dictionary (Forms vs Vector Calculus)`).
- `Def - Electromagnetic Lagrangian and Action` — type: definition (anchor); source items: B-D3.2.4, B-R3.2.4 (→ inside, Examples / Corollaries: independence of the background connection, proved there: replacing $\omega_0$ by $\tilde\omega_0$ changes $L$ by $A(\tilde\omega_0,\omega_0)\wedge J$, whose integral over $\bar U$ is independent of $\omega$, so criticality is unchanged); prereqs: [Def - U(1) Gauge Field and Electromagnetic Connection, Def - Charge-Current 3-Form, Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature, Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space]; spec: $L:\mathcal C(P)\to\Omega^4(M;\mathbb R)$, $L(\omega) := \tfrac12F\wedge\star F + A\wedge J$ (source typo: Bär p. 85 writes the target as $\Omega^4(M;i\mathbb R)$; $L$ is real-valued), $L_1 := \tfrac12 F\wedge\star F = \tfrac12(-1)^p\langle F,F\rangle\mathrm{vol} = -\tfrac12\langle F,F\rangle\mathrm{vol}$ on a Lorentzian manifold (by property (4)), $L_2 := A\wedge J$; $\omega$ is *critical* if for every open $U\Subset M$ and every $\eta\in\Omega^1(M;\mathbb R)$ with $\operatorname{supp}\eta\subset U$, $\frac{d}{dt}\big|_0\int_{\bar U}L(\omega_{t,\eta}) = 0$ where $A(\omega_{t,\eta},\omega_0) = A + t\eta$ (so $F(\omega_{t,\eta}) = F + t\,d\eta$; existence of $\omega_{t,\eta}$ by the affine-space anchor); Axiom Motivation: why $F\wedge\star F$ (the only quadratic, gauge-invariant, first-order, parity-even 4-form up to $F\wedge F$, which is exact); why $A\wedge J$ (linear coupling, gauge-dependent but its variation is not); on Minkowski $L_1 = \tfrac12(|\vec E|^2-|\vec B|^2)\mathrm{vol}$ (verified); Convention callout: SR XXII's action $-\tfrac1{4\mu_0}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}$ is $L_1$ in the other signature and units; non-example: $F\wedge F$ alone has no Euler–Lagrange content.
- `Thm - Euler-Lagrange Equation of the Electromagnetic Action` — type: theorem; source items: B-R3.2.3; prereqs: [Def - Electromagnetic Lagrangian and Action, Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Def - Maxwell Equations in Form Language]; statement: $\omega$ is critical for $L$ if and only if $d\star F + J = 0$; proof: Bär's, made complete: $\frac{d}{dt}\big|_0 L_1(\omega_{t,\eta}) = \tfrac12(d\eta\wedge\star F + F\wedge\star d\eta) = d\eta\wedge\star F$ by property (4) (symmetry of the star pairing); $\int_{\bar U}d\eta\wedge\star F = \int_{\bar U}d(\eta\wedge\star F) + \int_{\bar U}\eta\wedge d\star F = \int_M\eta\wedge d\star F$ (Stokes on $\bar U$ with $\eta|_{\partial U} = 0$; the writer must handle $\bar U$ possibly not a manifold with boundary — replace by: $\eta\wedge\star F$ is compactly supported in $U$, so $\int_M d(\eta\wedge\star F) = 0$ by Stokes for compactly supported forms on $M$); $\frac{d}{dt}\big|_0\int L_2 = \int_M\eta\wedge J$; the fundamental lemma of the calculus of variations for 1-forms (prove as Lemma 1: if $\int_M\eta\wedge\beta = 0$ for all compactly supported $\eta\in\Omega^1$ then the 3-form $\beta = 0$ — pair with $\eta = f\star\beta$ … in Lorentzian signature use $\eta = f\,\star(\star\beta)$-type test forms coefficient by coefficient in a chart); spec: link `Thm - Maxwell Equations from a Principle of Least Action` (SR XXII) as the component version; mechanism: **variation moves $d$ off the test form by Stokes, and what remains is $d\star F$**; sources: any first-order quadratic Lagrangian; targets: Yang–Mills equation (§7.4) is the non-abelian copy.
- `Thm - Continuity Equation and Conservation of Charge` — type: theorem; source items: B-R3.2.6, B-E3.2.2 (→ inside, as Corollary 2); prereqs: [Def - Maxwell Equations in Form Language, Thm - d-Squared-is-Zero, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Def - Manifold with Boundary and Induced Orientation]; statement: (i) If $d\star F + J = 0$ then $dJ = 0$; on Minkowski space $dJ = (\partial_t\varrho + \operatorname{div}\vec j)\,dt\wedge dx\wedge dy\wedge dz$, so $\partial_t\varrho + \operatorname{div}\vec j = 0$ (continuity equation, (3.11)). (ii) For a compact 3-dimensional submanifold with boundary $B\subset\mathbb R^3$ and $t_0<t_1$: $\int_B\varrho(t_1,\cdot) - \int_B\varrho(t_0,\cdot) + \int_{t_0}^{t_1}\int_{\partial B}\langle\vec j,\nu\rangle = 0$ (charge conservation); proof: (i) $d(d\star F + J) = dJ$ by $d^2 = 0$; coordinate computation; (ii) Stokes on $[t_0,t_1]\times B$ with the induced boundary orientation worked out explicitly (Bär's E3.2.2 asserts it); spec: link `Thm - Electric Charge Conservation and the Gauss Theorem` (SR XXII) and `Ex - Charge conservation from the nilpotence of d`; mechanism: **$J$ is exact up to sign, hence closed**.
- `Def - Worldline, Equation of Motion of a Charged Particle, and Relativistic Mass` — type: definition (compound: worldline and observed velocity; the equation of motion as the modelling postulate; eigentime and relativistic mass); source items: B-D3.2.5, B-D3.2.6, B-D3.2.7, B-I3.2.6 (Newton's law and $m = m_0/\sqrt{1-|\vec v|^2}$ are the physical postulates recorded here as definitions, not theorems); prereqs: [Def - U(1) Gauge Field and Electromagnetic Connection, Def - Causal Classification of Tangent Vectors, Def - Musical Isomorphism (Flat and Sharp), Def - Levi-Civita Connection, Def - Covariant Derivative along a Curve]; spec: a test particle of mass $1$ and charge $1$ has a timelike worldline $c$, $\langle c',c'\rangle<0$, written $c(\tau) = (t(\tau),\vec c(\tau))$ in coordinates, observed velocity $\vec v := d\vec c/dt = \vec c\,'/t'$ with $|\vec v|<1$ (proved from timelikeness); the *equation of motion* (3.12) is $\frac{\nabla}{d\tau}c' + F(c',\cdot)^\sharp = 0$, with $\eta^\sharp$ defined by $\eta(Y) = \langle\eta^\sharp,Y\rangle$ (Bär's footnote; equals the vault's sharp for $g_B$); *eigentime parametrisation* $\langle c',c'\rangle = -1$, $t'>0$; *relativistic mass* $m := m_0/\sqrt{1-|\vec v|^2} = m_0t'$ (the identity $1/\sqrt{1-|\vec v|^2} = t'$ under eigentime normalisation proved on the page); Convention callout: SR XXI's `Def - The Lorentz Four-Force` writes $m\,dU/d\tau = qF(\cdot,U)^\sharp$-type in $(+,-,-,-)$ — sign dictionary; examples: free particle ($F=0$) moves on geodesics; constant magnetic field (cyclotron motion, link `Thm - Motion of a Charge in a Uniform Field`); non-example: a spacelike curve has no observed velocity with $|\vec v|<1$.
- `Thm - Solutions of the Lorentz Force Equation Have Constant Speed and Exist Uniquely` — type: theorem (two parts); source items: B-T3.2.1, B-T3.2.2, B-I3.2.7; prereqs: [Def - Worldline, Equation of Motion of a Charged Particle, and Relativistic Mass, Def - Metric-Compatible Connection, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Integral Curves]; statement: (i) Every solution $c$ of $\frac{\nabla}{d\tau}c' + F(c',\cdot)^\sharp = 0$ satisfies $\frac{d}{d\tau}\langle c',c'\rangle = 0$; hence a solution that is timelike at one instant is timelike throughout and is parametrised proportionally to eigentime. (ii) For every $p\in M$ and $X\in T_pM$ there is a unique maximal solution $c$ with $c(\tau_0) = p$, $c'(\tau_0) = X$; proof: (i) Bär: $\frac{d}{d\tau}\langle c',c'\rangle = 2\langle\frac{\nabla}{d\tau}c',c'\rangle = -2F(c',c') = 0$ (metric compatibility of Levi-Civita, then skew-symmetry of $F$) — full; (ii) Bär says "since (3.12) is a linear ODE" (source typo: Bär Rem. 3.2.2 calls (3.12) linear; it is a second-order ODE with smooth coefficients, not linear in general) — the writer rewrites (3.12) as a first-order ODE for $(c,c')$ on $TM$, i.e. as the integral-curve equation of the smooth vector field $Z(v) := S(v) - (F(v,\cdot)^\sharp)^{\text{vert}}$ on $TM$ ($S$ the geodesic spray, vertical lift of $-F(v,\cdot)^\sharp$), in coordinates $\ddot c^k + \Gamma^k_{ij}\dot c^i\dot c^j + g^{kl}F_{il}\dot c^i = 0$, and invokes `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Integral Curves` (DG V) and `Thm - Fundamental Theorem on Flows`; spec: pattern identical to `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Geodesics` (RG II) — cite it as the $F = 0$ case; mechanism: a skew force does no work.
- `Thm - The Lorentz Force Law from the Equation of Motion` — type: theorem; source items: B-R3.2.7 ((3.13)); prereqs: [Def - Worldline, Equation of Motion of a Charged Particle, and Relativistic Mass, Thm - Solutions of the Lorentz Force Equation Have Constant Speed and Exist Uniquely, Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature]; statement: On Minkowski space, for a timelike worldline $c(\tau) = (t(\tau),\vec c(\tau))$ with $\langle c',c'\rangle = -1$, $t'>0$, the equation of motion is equivalent to the system $t'' + \langle\vec c\,',\vec E\rangle = 0$, $\vec c\,'' - t'\vec E + \vec B\times\vec c\,' = 0$; the first equation follows from the second and $\langle c',c'\rangle = -1$; dividing the second by $t'$ gives the Lorentz force law $\frac{d}{dt}(m\vec v) = \vec E + \vec v\times\vec B$ with $m = m_0 t'$, $m_0 = 1$; proof: Bär's componentwise computation with $dt^\sharp = -\partial_t$, $dx^\sharp = \partial_x$ etc.: $F(c',\cdot)^\sharp = -\langle\vec c\,',\vec E\rangle\partial_t + (-t'\vec E + \vec B\times\vec c\,')$ (source typos: Bär p. 89 prints "$\vec v\,''$" for $\vec c\,''$ and "$\frac{d}{dt}\langle c',c'\rangle$" for the $\tau$-derivative); the reduction $t't'' = \langle\vec c\,',\vec c\,''\rangle$ from differentiating $-t'^2 + |\vec c\,'|^2 = -1$; then $\frac{d}{dt}(m\vec v) = \frac{d}{dt}(t'\cdot\vec c\,'/t') = \vec c\,''/t'$ — every step justified; spec: link `Def - The Lorentz Four-Force`, `Ex - The Lorentz force is a pure four-force` (SR XXI); mechanism: the spatial part of $F(c',\cdot)^\sharp$ is $-t'\vec E + \vec B\times\vec c\,'$ because $F$'s $dx^i\wedge dt$ block is $\vec E$ and its spatial block is the Hodge dual of $\vec B$.
- `Thm - Gauge Invariance of the Electromagnetic Lagrangian` — type: theorem; source items: B-R3.2.14; prereqs: [Def - Electromagnetic Lagrangian and Action, Def - Gauge Transformation, Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature]; statement: For every gauge transformation $\phi\in\mathcal G(P)$ and $\omega' := \phi^*\omega$: $\bar\Omega' = \bar\Omega$, $F' = F$, hence $L_1(\phi^*\omega,g) = L_1(\omega,g)$; the coupling term changes by $L_2(\phi^*\omega) - L_2(\omega) = (A(\phi^*\omega,\omega_0) - A(\omega,\omega_0))\wedge J$, and its integral over $\bar U$ is unchanged when $dJ = 0$ and the gauge transformation is generated by a function (write $\phi(p) = p\cdot e^{i\chi(\pi(p))}$ with $\chi\in C^\infty(M)$, so $A$ changes by $d\chi$ and $\int d\chi\wedge J = \int d(\chi J) = 0$ for $\chi$ supported in $U$; in general $\int(\phi^*\omega-\omega)\wedge J$ is a constant independent of $\omega$'s variation, so criticality is preserved); proof: from the transformation law anchor with $\operatorname{Ad}$ trivial; the $L_2$ part is Bär's gap (he treats $L_1$ only) and the writer proves the statement as phrased; spec: mechanism: **abelian curvature is gauge-invariant on the nose**; targets: Yang–Mills gauge invariance (§7.4) needs $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariance of $\lambda$ instead.
- `Ex - Maxwell's Equations on Minkowski Space from the Two Form Equations` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-E3.2.1; prereqs: [Thm - Maxwell Equations in Coordinates]; spec: starting from the star table, compute $\star F$ and $d\star F$ for $F$ with given $\vec E,\vec B$ and read off all four Maxwell equations; then verify the plane wave $\vec E = E_0\cos(kz-kt)\,\hat x$, $\vec B = E_0\cos(kz-kt)\,\hat y$ solves the vacuum equations; invokes `Thm - Maxwell Equations in Coordinates`, `Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature`.
- `Ex - Charge Conservation on a Spacetime Cylinder` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E3.2.2 (drill of the corollary); prereqs: [Thm - Continuity Equation and Conservation of Charge, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; spec: prove $\int_B\varrho(t_1)-\int_B\varrho(t_0)+\int_{t_0}^{t_1}\int_{\partial B}\langle\vec j,\nu\rangle = 0$ from $dJ = 0$ by Stokes on $[t_0,t_1]\times B$, computing the induced orientations of the three boundary pieces explicitly; illegal-but-tempting: forgetting the orientation sign on the $t_0$-face.
- `Ex - The Background Connection Drops Out of the Euler-Lagrange Equation` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-R3.2.4 (drill; the proof also lives inside `Def - Electromagnetic Lagrangian and Action`); prereqs: [Def - Electromagnetic Lagrangian and Action, Thm - Euler-Lagrange Equation of the Electromagnetic Action]; spec: show that replacing $\omega_0$ by $\tilde\omega_0$ changes $L(\omega)$ by $A(\tilde\omega_0,\omega_0)\wedge J$ and that this term has zero variation; conclude criticality is background-independent.
- `Ex - The Dirac Monopole as a Nontrivial U(1) Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: (additional; standard, e.g. Naber *Topology, Geometry and Gauge Fields: Foundations* Ch. 0 and §6; Bär's Hopf-bundle example 2.2.x is chapter III's); prereqs: [Def - U(1) Gauge Field and Electromagnetic Connection, Def - The Hopf Bundle, Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; spec: on $\mathbb R^3\setminus\{0\}$ with the static field $\vec B = g\,\vec x/|\vec x|^3$, $\vec E = 0$: show $dF = 0$ but $F$ is not exact ($\int_{S^2}F = 4\pi g\ne0$ by Stokes); construct local potentials on the two hemispheres and show the transition function is $e^{2ig\varphi}$, hence the bundle is the $2g$-th power of the Hopf bundle and $2g\in\mathbb Z$ is forced by the cocycle condition (Dirac quantisation); invokes the listed pages.
- `Exercise Index - §7.2 Electrodynamics as a U(1) Gauge Theory` — lists: [Ex - Maxwell's Equations on Minkowski Space from the Two Form Equations, Ex - Charge Conservation on a Spacetime Cylinder, Ex - The Background Connection Drops Out of the Euler-Lagrange Equation, Ex - The Dirac Monopole as a Nontrivial U(1) Bundle]

### §7.3 The Energy-Momentum Tensor and the Symmetries of the Action
- `Def - Energy-Momentum Tensor of a U(1) Field` — type: definition (compound: the tensor; energy density, Poynting vector, Maxwell stress tensor); source items: B-D3.2.8, B-D3.2.10; prereqs: [Def - U(1) Gauge Field and Electromagnetic Connection, Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature, Def - Musical Isomorphism (Flat and Sharp)]; spec: $T^{ij} := -F^{\delta i}F^j{}_\delta - \tfrac12\langle F,F\rangle_g\,g^{ij}$, $T = T^{ij}\partial_i\otimes\partial_j$ (indices raised with $g_B$; $\langle F,F\rangle_g$ the induced inner product on 2-forms), the $(0,2)$-version $T_{ij}$; on Minkowski space: $T^{00} = \tfrac12(|\vec E|^2+|\vec B|^2)$ (energy density), $(T^{01},T^{02},T^{03}) = \vec E\times\vec B =: \vec S$ (Poynting vector), $T^{ij} = -\sigma^{ij}$ for $1\le i,j\le3$ with the Maxwell stress tensor $\sigma := \vec E\otimes\vec E+\vec B\otimes\vec B-\tfrac12(|\vec B|^2+|\vec E|^2)g$ ($g$ the inverse metric) — stated here, computed in `Ex - The Energy-Momentum Tensor in Terms of E and B`; symmetry $T^{ij} = T^{ji}$ verified clause by clause; Convention callout: SR XXIII's `Def - Energy-Momentum Tensor of the Electromagnetic Field` in $(+,-,-,-)$ with $\mu_0$; the two agree as tensors after $g\mapsto-g$ (both index positions flip sign twice); examples: plane wave ($T^{00} = |\vec S|$, null); Coulomb field (link `Ex - The EM stress tensor in a Coulomb field and the Poynting vector`); non-example: $F^{\delta i}F^j{}_\delta$ alone is not traceless.
- `Thm - Metric Variation of the Electromagnetic Lagrangian` — type: theorem; source items: B-R3.2.8, B-I3.2.2 (Jacobi's formula, proved as Lemma 1 on the page); prereqs: [Def - Energy-Momentum Tensor of a U(1) Field, Def - Electromagnetic Lagrangian and Action, Def - Riemannian Volume Form, Def - Determinant]; statement: Let $g(t)$ be a smooth one-parameter family of Lorentzian metrics with $g(0) = g$, $\dot g(0) = h\in\Gamma(\odot^2T^*M)$. Then (a) $\frac{d}{dt}\big|_0\mathrm{vol}_{g(t)} = \tfrac12\operatorname{tr}_g(h)\,\mathrm{vol}_g$; (b) $\frac{d}{dt}\big|_0\langle F,F\rangle_{g(t)} = F^{\delta i}F^j{}_\delta h_{ij}$; (c) $\frac{d}{dt}\big|_0 L_1(\omega,g(t)) = -\tfrac12T^{ij}h_{ij}\,\mathrm{vol}_g$; proof: Lemma 1 (Jacobi): $\frac{d}{dt}\det A(t) = \det A\cdot\operatorname{tr}(A^{-1}\dot A)$ for a smooth curve of invertible matrices — prove via $\det(A+\epsilon B) = \det A\det(1+\epsilon A^{-1}B) = \det A(1+\epsilon\operatorname{tr}(A^{-1}B)+O(\epsilon^2))$ with the last expansion proved from the Leibniz formula; Lemma 2: $\dot g^{il} = -g^{ij}h_{jk}g^{kl}$ (differentiate $g^{ij}g_{jk} = \delta^i_k$); (a) from $\mathrm{vol}_g = \sqrt{-\det(g_{ij})}\,dx^0\wedge\dots\wedge dx^3$ in a positively oriented chart; (b) from $\langle F,F\rangle = \tfrac14F_{\alpha\beta}F_{\gamma\delta}(g^{\alpha\gamma}g^{\beta\delta}-g^{\alpha\delta}g^{\beta\gamma})$ (prove this formula from the induced-inner-product definition in a generalized ONB and extend to arbitrary frames by tensoriality — Bär's compressed index bookkeeping, the content-map gap, is written out in full); (c) product rule using $L_1 = -\tfrac12\langle F,F\rangle_g\mathrm{vol}_g$ (Lorentzian, $p=1$: $\tfrac12F\wedge\star F = \tfrac12(-1)^p\langle F,F\rangle\mathrm{vol}$ — the writer must track this sign; Bär writes $L_1 = \tfrac12\langle F,F\rangle\mathrm{vol}$ on p. 89 which is inconsistent with (3.5) at $p = 1$; **decision:** the page defines $T$ so that (c) holds with the stated sign and records the discrepancy in a `(source: …)` note; the resulting $T^{00} = \tfrac12(|\vec E|^2+|\vec B|^2)>0$ is the check that fixes the overall sign); spec: mechanism: **the metric enters $L_1$ through the inverse metric twice and through the volume form once; the three derivatives assemble into $T$**; targets: Einstein–Maxwell (next page), tracelessness, the divergence identity.
- `Def - Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian and the Coupled Action` — type: definition; source items: B-D3.2.9; prereqs: [Def - Scalar Curvature, Def - Riemannian Volume Form, Def - Electromagnetic Lagrangian and Action, Def - Hilbert Action]; spec: $L_{geom}(g) := -\tfrac12\operatorname{scal}_g\,\mathrm{vol}_g$; the total Lagrangian $L_{geom}(g) + L_1(\omega,g) + L_2(\omega)$ on pairs $(\omega,g)$; criticality in $g$ under compactly supported $h$; Convention callout: GR I's `Def - Hilbert Action` uses $\frac{1}{16\pi G}\int R\sqrt{-g}$ and signature $(+,-,-,-)$ or $(-,+,+,+)$ (state which after reading the page) — Bär's normalisation absorbs $8\pi G$ into units; examples: vacuum ($L_1 = L_2 = 0$).
- `Thm - Einstein-Maxwell Equations from the Coupled Action` — type: theorem; source items: B-R3.2.9 ((3.14), (3.15)), B-I3.2.3; prereqs: [Def - Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian and the Coupled Action, Thm - Metric Variation of the Electromagnetic Lagrangian, Thm - Hilbert's Variational Principle Yields Einstein Equations, Def - Ricci Tensor]; statement: $g$ is critical for $\int(L_{geom}(g)+L_1(\omega,g)+L_2(\omega))$ under compactly supported variations $h$ if and only if $\operatorname{ric}_g - \tfrac12\operatorname{scal}_g\,g = T$ (Einstein field equations with the electromagnetic energy-momentum tensor as source); proof: uses $\frac{d}{dt}\big|_0\operatorname{scal}_{g(t)} = -\operatorname{ric}^{ij}h_{ij} + \operatorname{div}X$ for a compactly supported vector field $X$ (I3.2.3) — the vault's `Thm - Hilbert's Variational Principle Yields Einstein Equations` (GR I, unflagged, 254 lines) derives $\delta(R\sqrt{-g})$ via the Palatini identity; **the writer must check that page proves the Palatini identity $\delta R_{\mu\nu} = \nabla_\rho\delta\Gamma^\rho_{\nu\mu}-\nabla_\nu\delta\Gamma^\rho_{\rho\mu}$ in full; if it only states it, prove the first variation of scalar curvature as Lemma 1 on this page** (route: $\delta\Gamma$ is a tensor; $\delta R^\rho{}_{\sigma\mu\nu} = \nabla_\mu\delta\Gamma^\rho_{\nu\sigma}-\nabla_\nu\delta\Gamma^\rho_{\mu\sigma}$ by direct computation in normal coordinates or from the definition; contract; then $\int\operatorname{div}X\,\mathrm{vol} = 0$ for compactly supported $X$ by Stokes); combine with (a) and (c) of the previous page and the fundamental lemma for symmetric 2-tensors; spec: link `Def - The Einstein Field Equations`, `Def - Einstein Tensor` (GR I); mechanism: the metric variation of the matter action *defines* the stress-energy source of Einstein's equation.
- `Def - Symmetrized Covariant Derivative and Divergence of a 2-Tensor` — type: definition (compound); source items: B-D3.2.12, B-D3.2.13; prereqs: [Def - Levi-Civita Connection, Def - Induced Connection on Tensor Bundles, Def - Lie Derivative of a Differential Form, Def - Generalized Orthonormal Basis, Index, and the Induced Inner Product on Forms]; spec: $2(\nabla^{sym}X)(Y,Z) := g(\nabla_YX,Z)+g(Y,\nabla_ZX)$; $\operatorname{div}(T) := \sum_i(\nabla_{e_i}T)(e_i^*,\cdot)$ for a generalized ONB (basis-independence proved in Examples via tensoriality of $\nabla T$ and the trace); the identity $\mathcal L_Xg = 2\nabla^{sym}X$ proved as a corollary on the page (from $(\mathcal L_Xg)(Y,Z) = \partial_Xg(Y,Z)-g([X,Y],Z)-g(Y,[X,Z])$ and torsion-freeness plus metric compatibility — Bär gives this argument); examples: Killing fields ($\nabla^{sym}X = 0$, link `Def - Killing Vector Field`; note `Thm - Killing Equation` is flagged — do not cite it); $\operatorname{div}$ of $T = f\,g^{-1}$ is $\operatorname{grad}f$.
- `Thm - Conformal Invariance of the Electromagnetic Action and Tracelessness of the Energy-Momentum Tensor` — type: theorem; source items: B-R3.2.11, B-D3.2.11 (→ inside, Statement/Notation: the definition of conformal invariance of a Lagrangian); prereqs: [Def - Electromagnetic Lagrangian and Action, Thm - Metric Variation of the Electromagnetic Lagrangian, Ex - Conformal Weight of the Hodge Star]; statement: (i) For $g' = \lambda^2g$, $\lambda\in C^\infty(M)$, $\lambda>0$, on a 4-manifold: $\star' = \star$ on 2-forms, hence $L(\omega,g') = L(\omega,g)$ — the electromagnetic Lagrangian is conformally invariant. (ii) Consequently $\operatorname{tr}_gT = g_{ij}T^{ij} = 0$; proof: (i) from the conformal weight computation (recorded fully on the page, not only in the exercise: $\star' = \lambda^{2k-n}\star$); (ii) take $g(t) := (1+t)g$, $h = g$: $0 = \frac{d}{dt}\big|_0L_1 = -\tfrac12T^{ij}g_{ij}\mathrm{vol}$; spec: link `Ex - Tracelessness of the EM tensor and the radiation equation of state` (SR XXIII); mechanism: **a Lagrangian invariant under rescaling the metric has traceless energy-momentum tensor, because the trace of $T$ is the response to an infinitesimal rescaling**.
- `Thm - Diffeomorphism Invariance and the Divergence Identity for the Energy-Momentum Tensor` — type: theorem; source items: B-R3.2.12, B-R3.2.13 ((3.16)), B-I3.2.4 (→ wikilink `Thm - Cartan's Magic Formula`, DG VIII, proof-complete), B-I3.2.5 (replaced by the horizontal-lift construction below); prereqs: [Def - Energy-Momentum Tensor of a U(1) Field, Def - Symmetrized Covariant Derivative and Divergence of a 2-Tensor, Thm - Metric Variation of the Electromagnetic Lagrangian, Thm - Cartan's Magic Formula, Def - Horizontal Subspace and Horizontal Lift, Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Horizontal Lifts, Def - Flow of a Vector Field, Thm - Fundamental Theorem on Flows, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; statement: (i) (Invariance) For a diffeomorphism $\phi$ of $M$ supported in $U\Subset M$ and any bundle isomorphism $\Phi:\phi^*P\to P$ covering $\phi$: $\int_UL_1(\Phi^*\omega,\phi^*g) = \int_UL_1(\omega,g)$. (ii) (Integration by parts) For every compactly supported vector field $X$ and symmetric $T$: $\int_MT\cdot\nabla^{sym}X\,\mathrm{vol}_g = -\int_M\langle\operatorname{div}T,X\rangle\mathrm{vol}_g$. (iii) (Divergence identity, (3.16)) For every $\omega\in\mathcal C(P)$: $-\langle\operatorname{div}T,X\rangle = \langle\iota_XF,\star d\star F\rangle$ for all vector fields $X$; if $d\star F + J = 0$ then $-\langle\operatorname{div}T,X\rangle = -\langle\iota_XF,\star J\rangle$ (source typo: Bär p. 95 writes "$d\star F = J$", inconsistent with the Euler–Lagrange equation $d\star F + J = 0$ of p. 86; the series uses the latter, so the sign here is as stated and Poynting's theorem below is re-derived with it); in particular $\operatorname{div}T = 0$ in vacuum; proof: (i) is the naturality computation $\int_U\langle\phi^*F,\phi^*F\rangle_{\phi^*g}\phi^*\mathrm{vol}_g = \int_U(\langle F,F\rangle_g\circ\phi)\,\phi^*\mathrm{vol}_g = \int_U\langle F,F\rangle_g\mathrm{vol}_g$ (change of variables, `Thm - Change of Variables for Integration on Manifolds`, DG IX); (ii) Stokes: $T\cdot\nabla^{sym}X = \operatorname{div}(T(\cdot,X^\flat)) - \langle\operatorname{div}T,X\rangle$ via metric compatibility and symmetry of $T$, and $\int\operatorname{div}Y\,\mathrm{vol} = \int d(\iota_Y\mathrm{vol}) = 0$ (prove $\operatorname{div}Y\,\mathrm{vol} = d(\iota_Y\mathrm{vol}) = \mathcal L_Y\mathrm{vol}$ as Lemma 1 via Cartan); (iii) let $\phi_t$ be the flow of $X$ (complete since compactly supported), and let $\tilde X$ be the horizontal lift of $X$ to $P$ with respect to $\omega$ (a $G$-invariant vector field, `Def - Horizontal Subspace and Horizontal Lift`), with flow $\tilde\phi_t$ consisting of bundle automorphisms covering $\phi_t$ (complete because its integral curves are horizontal lifts of those of $X$, `Thm - Existence and Uniqueness of Horizontal Lifts`); then $\omega_t := \tilde\phi_t^*\omega$ is a connection on $P$, $g_t := \phi_t^*g$, and $L_1(\omega_t,g_t) = \phi_t^*L_1(\omega,g)$ (naturality), so $\frac{d}{dt}\big|_0\int_UL_1(\omega_t,g_t) = 0$; on the other hand this derivative equals $\int_U\eta\wedge d\star F - \tfrac12\int_UT\cdot h\,\mathrm{vol}_g$ with $h = \mathcal L_Xg = 2\nabla^{sym}X$ and $i\eta := \frac{d}{dt}\big|_0 s^*\omega_t = s^*\mathcal L_{\tilde X}\omega = s^*(\iota_{\tilde X}d\omega + d\iota_{\tilde X}\omega) = s^*\iota_{\tilde X}\Omega = i\,\iota_XF$ (Cartan on $P$; $\omega(\tilde X) = 0$ since $\tilde X$ is horizontal; $d\omega = \Omega$ for abelian $G$ by the structure equation anchor; $s^*\iota_{\tilde X}\Omega = \iota_X s^*\Omega$ because $ds(X)$ and $\tilde X$ differ by a vertical vector on which $\Omega$ vanishes) — this replaces Bär's "$\iota_XF + df$" (his $f$ is zero for the horizontal lift; both routes agree since $\int df\wedge d\star F = 0$); combine with the Euler–Lagrange computation (`Thm - Euler-Lagrange Equation of the Electromagnetic Action`) and (ii), then the fundamental lemma; the identity $\int(\iota_XF)\wedge d\star F = \int\langle\iota_XF,\star d\star F\rangle\mathrm{vol}$ by property (5)/(4) with the Lorentzian sign tracked; spec: this page is the longest of §7.3; mechanism: **diffeomorphism invariance of the action, differentiated along a flow, is the covariant conservation law for $T$ (Noether's second theorem in its simplest instance)**; Bridges: `Thm - Energy-Momentum Conservation` (SR XXIII) is the Minkowski, component version.
- `Thm - Poynting's Theorem` — type: theorem; source items: B-R3.2.13 ((3.17)), B-E3.2.3; prereqs: [Thm - Diffeomorphism Invariance and the Divergence Identity for the Energy-Momentum Tensor, Def - Energy-Momentum Tensor of a U(1) Field, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; statement: On Minkowski space, if $d\star F + J = 0$ then $\tfrac12\partial_t(|\vec E|^2+|\vec B|^2) + \operatorname{div}\vec S = -\langle\vec E,\vec j\rangle$; for $\vec j = 0$ and a compact $B\subset\mathbb R^3$ with smooth boundary: $\tfrac12\int_B(|\vec E|^2+|\vec B|^2)(t_1) - \tfrac12\int_B(|\vec E|^2+|\vec B|^2)(t_0) + \int_{t_0}^{t_1}\int_{\partial B}\langle\vec S,\nu\rangle = 0$ (source typo: Bär p. 96 prints a minus sign before the flux term; with the outward normal $\nu$ and $d\star F + J = 0$ the sign is $+$ — the writer verifies the sign on the plane-wave example, where energy flows out through the face the wave exits); proof: evaluate (iii) of the previous page at $X = \partial_t$: LHS $= -\partial_tT^{00}-\sum_i\partial_iT^{i0} = -\tfrac12\partial_t(|\vec E|^2+|\vec B|^2)-\operatorname{div}\vec S$; RHS $= -\langle\iota_{\partial_t}F,\star J\rangle$ computed from the star table with $\iota_{\partial_t}F = -\sum_iE_i\,dx^i$; then Stokes on $[t_0,t_1]\times B$; spec: link `Ex - Energy and momentum conservation from the divergence` (SR XXIII); mechanism: the time component of $\operatorname{div}T = $ force density is the energy balance.
- `Ex - The Energy-Momentum Tensor in Terms of E and B` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: B-R3.2.10; prereqs: [Def - Energy-Momentum Tensor of a U(1) Field, Def - Generalized Orthonormal Basis, Index, and the Induced Inner Product on Forms]; spec: on Minkowski space compute $\langle F,F\rangle = -|\vec E|^2+|\vec B|^2$, $T^{00} = \tfrac12(|\vec E|^2+|\vec B|^2)$, $T^{0i} = (\vec E\times\vec B)_i$, $T^{ij} = -\sigma^{ij}$; route: $F_{0k} = -E_k$, $F_{ij} = \epsilon_{ijk}B_k$, raise with $g^{00} = -1$; check tracelessness $g_{ij}T^{ij} = -T^{00}+\sum_iT^{ii} = 0$ explicitly.
- `Ex - Energy Conservation on a Bounded Region from Poynting's Theorem` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-E3.2.3 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Poynting's Theorem]; spec: derive the integrated form from the differential form by Stokes on $[t_0,t_1]\times B$, orientation bookkeeping as in `Ex - Charge Conservation on a Spacetime Cylinder`; then compute the energy flux of a plane wave through a face of a box.
- `Ex - Jacobi's Formula for the Derivative of a Determinant` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: B-I3.2.2 (drill of Lemma 1 of the metric-variation page); prereqs: [Def - Determinant, Thm - Determinant is Multiplicative]; spec: prove $\frac{d}{dt}\det A(t) = \det A(t)\operatorname{tr}(A(t)^{-1}\dot A(t))$; route: reduce to $A(0) = 1$ by multiplicativity, expand the Leibniz formula to first order; apply to $\frac{d}{dt}\sqrt{-\det g(t)}$.
- `Ex - Killing Fields Conserve Energy-Momentum` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (additional; standard, e.g. Wald *General Relativity* §4.3 / Straumann Ch. 2); prereqs: [Def - Symmetrized Covariant Derivative and Divergence of a 2-Tensor, Thm - Diffeomorphism Invariance and the Divergence Identity for the Energy-Momentum Tensor, Def - Killing Vector Field]; spec: show that if $\operatorname{div}T = 0$ and $X$ satisfies $\nabla^{sym}X = 0$ then the vector field $Y := T(X^\flat,\cdot)^\sharp$ has $\operatorname{div}Y = 0$, and deduce a conserved integral $\int_{\Sigma}\iota_Y\mathrm{vol}$ over spacelike slices via Stokes; identify $X = \partial_t$ with energy and $X = \partial_x$ with momentum on Minkowski space.
- `Exercise Index - §7.3 The Energy-Momentum Tensor and the Symmetries of the Action` — lists: [Ex - The Energy-Momentum Tensor in Terms of E and B, Ex - Energy Conservation on a Bounded Region from Poynting's Theorem, Ex - Jacobi's Formula for the Derivative of a Determinant, Ex - Killing Fields Conserve Energy-Momentum]

### §7.4 Yang–Mills Fields and Instantons
- `Def - Wedge Product and Metric Pairing of Bundle-Valued Forms` — type: definition; source items: B-D3.3.2; prereqs: [Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle, Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Vector Bundle, Def - Differential k-Form on a Manifold, Def - Tensor Product of Vector Spaces]; spec: for $E\to M$ a $\mathbb K$-vector bundle, $\eta\in\Omega^k(M;E)$, $\mu\in\Omega^l(M;E)$: $\eta\wedge\mu := \sum\eta_{i_1\dots i_k}\otimes\mu_{j_1\dots j_l}\otimes dx^{i_1}\wedge\dots\wedge dx^{j_l}\in\Omega^{k+l}(M;E\otimes E)$ (coordinate-free version: $(\eta\wedge\mu)(X_1,\dots,X_{k+l}) = \sum_{\text{shuffles}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\eta(X_{\sigma(1)},\dots)\otimes\mu(\dots)$, well-definedness proved); with a fibre metric, $\langle\eta\wedge\mu\rangle := \sum\langle\eta_{I},\mu_{J}\rangle\,dx^I\wedge dx^J\in\Omega^{k+l}(M;\mathbb K)$; for $E = \operatorname{End}$-type bundles, $\operatorname{tr}(\eta\wedge\mu)$; symmetry $\langle\eta\wedge\mu\rangle = (-1)^{kl}\langle\mu\wedge\eta\rangle$ (proved); the special case $\lambda(\bar\Omega\wedge\star\bar\Omega)$ for $\operatorname{ad}P$-valued 2-forms; Convention callout: the bracket $[\alpha\wedge\beta]$ of `Def - Lie-Algebra-Valued Differential Forms and Their Bracket` (IV) is the composition of this wedge with the Lie bracket $\mathfrak g\otimes\mathfrak g\to\mathfrak g$; examples: $E$ trivial of rank 1 recovers the ordinary wedge; non-example: $\eta\wedge\eta$ need not vanish for $E$-valued 1-forms (compute a $2\times2$ matrix example).
- `Thm - Leibniz Rule for the Metric Pairing of Bundle-Valued Forms` — type: theorem; source items: B-T3.3.2, B-I3.3.1; prereqs: [Def - Wedge Product and Metric Pairing of Bundle-Valued Forms, Def - Metric-Compatible Connection, Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Vector Bundle]; statement: If $\nabla$ is a metric connection on $E$ then for $\eta\in\Omega^k(M;E)$, $\mu\in\Omega^l(M;E)$: $d\langle\eta\wedge\mu\rangle = \langle d^\nabla\eta\wedge\mu\rangle + (-1)^k\langle\eta\wedge d^\nabla\mu\rangle$; proof: Bär states it without proof; prove in a local orthonormal frame $(e_a)$ with $\nabla e_a = \sum_b A^b_a e_b$, $A$ skew-symmetric by metric compatibility: write $\eta = \sum_a\eta^a e_a$ with $\eta^a$ scalar forms, $d^\nabla\eta = \sum_a(d\eta^a + \sum_bA^a_b\wedge\eta^b)e_a$, and observe that the connection terms cancel pairwise by skew-symmetry of $A$; the ordinary Leibniz rule for $d$ (`Thm - Wedge Product Properties`, DG VIII, unflagged — check it includes $d(\alpha\wedge\beta) = d\alpha\wedge\beta+(-1)^k\alpha\wedge d\beta$; if not, prove as Lemma 1 from `Thm - Coordinate Expression for the Exterior Derivative`) finishes; spec: purely technical lemma reused in the Yang–Mills variation and the energy bound; mechanism: **a metric connection is skew in an orthonormal frame, so the pairing does not see it**.
- `Def - The Adjoint Bundle with its Trace Metric and Induced Connection` — type: definition (compound: trace inner product on $\mathfrak{su}(N)$; the metric on $\operatorname{ad}P = P\times_{\operatorname{Ad}}\mathfrak g$; the induced covariant derivative $\nabla^\omega$ and $d^\omega$); source items: B-D3.3.3, B-D3.3.4; prereqs: [Def - Associated Bundle, Def - Adjoint Representation, Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles, Def - Local Connection Form and Gauge Potential]; spec: $\lambda(A,B) := -\operatorname{tr}(AB)$ on $\mathfrak{su}(N)$; $\lambda([p,A],[p,B]) := \lambda(A,B)$ on $\operatorname{ad}P$ (well-defined by $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariance, proved on the next page); $\nabla^\omega_X[s,A] := [s,\partial_XA + \operatorname{ad}(s^*\omega(X))A]$ for a local section $s$ of $P$ and $A:U\to\mathfrak g$ (source typos: Bär p. 99 prints "$\partial_Xs$" for $\partial_XA$ and "$s^*\omega(Y)$" for $s^*\omega(X)$); this is the specialisation of the anchor `Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles` to $\rho = \operatorname{Ad}$ (record the dictionary $\nabla^\omega = d + [A_s\wedge\cdot]$ with $A_s = s^*\omega$, Haydys's $d^{\nabla_a}$ on $\operatorname{ad}P$); $d^\omega := d^{\nabla^\omega}$; the identification of Bär's $\bar\Omega$ (an $\operatorname{ad}P$-valued 2-form) with Haydys's $F_a\in\Omega^2(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$ (`[NEEDED FROM I–VI: ad P and the equivariant-horizontal-forms identification]`); examples: $N = 2$, $\lambda(X,Y) = \tfrac12\operatorname{tr}(XY^*)$-type check on the basis $i\sigma_k$; $U(1)$ ($\operatorname{ad}P$ trivial, $\nabla^\omega = d$); non-example: $\operatorname{tr}(AB)$ without the sign is negative definite.
- `Thm - The Trace Metric on the Adjoint Bundle is Parallel` — type: theorem (two parts); source items: B-T3.3.3, B-T3.3.4; prereqs: [Def - The Adjoint Bundle with its Trace Metric and Induced Connection]; statement: (i) $\lambda(A,B) = -\operatorname{tr}(AB)$ is a real, symmetric, positive definite, $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariant inner product on $\mathfrak{su}(N)$ ($\operatorname{Ad}$-invariance: $\lambda(gAg^{-1},gBg^{-1}) = \lambda(A,B)$; infinitesimally $\lambda([C,A],B)+\lambda(A,[C,B]) = 0$). (ii) $\nabla^\omega$ is metric for $\lambda$: $\partial_X\lambda(\sigma,\tau) = \lambda(\nabla^\omega_X\sigma,\tau)+\lambda(\sigma,\nabla^\omega_X\tau)$ for sections $\sigma,\tau$ of $\operatorname{ad}P$; proof: Bär's, complete: reality from $\overline{\operatorname{tr}(AB)} = \operatorname{tr}(\bar A\bar B) = \operatorname{tr}((B^*A^*)^t)$-argument with $A^* = -A$; positivity $-\operatorname{tr}(AA) = \operatorname{tr}(AA^*) = \sum|A^i_j|^2$; (ii) from $\operatorname{tr}([C,A]B + A[C,B]) = \operatorname{tr}([C,AB]) = 0$; spec: sources: any compact Lie group with a bi-invariant metric; targets: gauge invariance and the Leibniz rule for $d^\omega$ applied to $\lambda(\bar\Omega\wedge\star\bar\Omega)$.
- `Thm - Bianchi Identity on the Base` — type: theorem; source items: B-T3.3.5, B-R3.3.1; prereqs: [Def - The Adjoint Bundle with its Trace Metric and Induced Connection, Thm - Bianchi Identity for a Principal Connection, Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Thm - Transformation of Local Connection and Curvature Forms]; statement: For a connection $\omega$ on a principal $G$-bundle with curvature $\bar\Omega\in\Omega^2(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$: $d^\omega\bar\Omega = 0$; in a local section $s_\alpha$ with $\omega_\alpha = s_\alpha^*\omega$, $\Omega_\alpha = s_\alpha^*\Omega$: $d\Omega_\alpha + [\omega_\alpha\wedge\Omega_\alpha] = 0$ (Haydys's $dF_A + [A\wedge F_A] = 0$; a nonlinear equation in $\omega$, R3.3.1); proof: Bär's argument (commuting vector fields at $x$, a section horizontal at $x$, `[NEEDED FROM I–VI: section horizontal at a point]`, then the anchor's $d\Omega = 0$ on horizontal vectors) is complete once the two existence facts are supplied; **preferred route:** derive the local identity directly from the structure equation: $d\Omega_\alpha = d(d\omega_\alpha + \tfrac12[\omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha]) = \tfrac12[d\omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha] - \tfrac12[\omega_\alpha\wedge d\omega_\alpha] = [d\omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha] = [\Omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha] - \tfrac12[[\omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha]\wedge\omega_\alpha]$ and the last term vanishes by the Jacobi identity (prove the graded Jacobi identity $[[\alpha\wedge\alpha]\wedge\alpha] = 0$ for a $\mathfrak g$-valued 1-form as Lemma 1); then $[\Omega_\alpha\wedge\omega_\alpha] = -[\omega_\alpha\wedge\Omega_\alpha]$ (sign lemma for the bracket of a 1-form and a 2-form, Lemma 2) gives the local form, and $d^\omega\bar\Omega = 0$ follows since $d^\omega$ is locally $d + [\omega_\alpha\wedge\cdot]$; the global statement's independence of $s_\alpha$ from the anchor transformation law; both routes are recorded, the writer proves the second in the Formal Proof and cites the anchor for the first in Sources; spec: mechanism: **$d^2 = 0$ plus the Jacobi identity**.
- `Def - The Yang-Mills Field Strength` — type: definition (anchor; backlink-map name, 1 external link); source items: (none of B's numbered items — the physics-facing name for $\bar\Omega$; B's standing conventions p. 97–100 assign it); prereqs: [Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Def - The Adjoint Bundle with its Trace Metric and Induced Connection, Thm - Structure Equation for the Curvature, Def - Local Connection Form and Gauge Potential]; spec: for an $SU(N)$-bundle $P$ over an oriented Riemannian 4-manifold and $\omega\in\mathcal C(P)$, the *Yang–Mills field strength* is $\bar\Omega = F_A\in\Omega^2(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$; locally $F_A = dA + \tfrac12[A\wedge A] = dA + A\wedge A$ (matrix groups) with $A = s^*\omega$ the gauge potential; components $F_{\mu\nu} = \partial_\mu A_\nu - \partial_\nu A_\mu + [A_\mu,A_\nu]$ (the physicists' formula, derived on the page from the bracket convention); gauge transformation $F\mapsto\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}F$ (anchor); Convention callout: $\tfrac12$ versus no $\tfrac12$; physicists' $F = dA - ig[A,A]$-type conventions with coupling constant; abelian case is `Def - U(1) Gauge Field and Electromagnetic Connection`; examples: $U(1)$; the trivial $SU(2)$-bundle over $\mathbb R^4$ with $A = \operatorname{Im}(x\,d\bar x)/(1+|x|^2)$ (quaternionic; its $F$ computed in `Ex - The BPST Instanton on R^4`); non-example: $dA$ alone is not gauge-covariant for non-abelian $G$ (explicit $2\times2$ check).
- `Def - Yang-Mills Lagrangian and Action Functional` — type: definition (anchor); source items: B-D3.3.5, B-D3.3.6; prereqs: [Def - The Yang-Mills Field Strength, Def - Wedge Product and Metric Pairing of Bundle-Valued Forms, Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature, Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space]; spec: $L_{YM}:\mathcal C(P)\to\Omega^4(M;\mathbb R)$, $L_{YM}(\omega) := \tfrac12\lambda(\bar\Omega\wedge\star\bar\Omega)$; the Yang–Mills action $\mathcal{YM}(\omega) := \int_ML_{YM}(\omega)$ (over $\bar U$ for the variational definition on non-compact $M$); $\omega$ is a *Yang–Mills connection* if it is critical: $\frac{d}{dt}\big|_0\int_{\bar U}L_{YM}(\omega+t\eta) = 0$ for all $\eta\in\Omega^1_{\operatorname{Ad}}(P;\mathfrak g)$ horizontal with $\bar\eta$ compactly supported in $U$ (the affine-space anchor identifies such $\eta$ with $\Omega^1_c(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$); Convention callout: conventions.md's $\mathcal{YM}(A) = \tfrac12\int|F_A|^2\mathrm{vol}$ with $|F|^2$ from $-\operatorname{tr}$ coincides with Bär's by the next Thm page; Haydys's Chern–Simons/flat-connection pages (VI, V) use the same $\lambda$; Axiom Motivation: the non-abelian copy of `Def - Electromagnetic Lagrangian and Action` with $\lambda$ replacing the scalar product, why $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariance is forced by gauge invariance; examples: $G = U(1)$ recovers $\tfrac12F\wedge\star F$; flat connections have $\mathcal{YM} = 0$.
- `Thm - Yang-Mills Action is Half the Squared L2 Norm of the Curvature and is Gauge Invariant` — type: theorem (two parts); source items: B-T3.3.6, B-T3.3.7; prereqs: [Def - Yang-Mills Lagrangian and Action Functional, Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature, Def - Gauge Transformation, Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature, Thm - The Trace Metric on the Adjoint Bundle is Parallel]; statement: (i) $\lambda(\bar\Omega\wedge\star\bar\Omega) = \langle\bar\Omega,\bar\Omega\rangle\,\mathrm{vol}$ for the inner product on $\Lambda^2T^*M\otimes\operatorname{ad}P$ induced by $g$ and $\lambda$; hence $\int_ML_{YM}(\omega) = \tfrac12\|\bar\Omega\|^2_{L^2}\ge0$. (ii) For $\phi\in\mathcal G(P)$, $L_{YM}(\phi^*\omega) = L_{YM}(\omega)$; proof: (i) from property (4) with $p = 0$ and the $W$-valued extension of $\star$; (ii) Bär's: with $g:P\to G$ the function with $\phi(p) = p\cdot g(p)$, $\bar\Omega' = \operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\bar\Omega$ (anchor `Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature`), and $\lambda$ is $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariant; spec: mechanism: the pairing is an inner product because $p = 0$; sources: any $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariant metric.
- `Thm - Yang-Mills Equation from the Action Principle` — type: theorem (anchor); source items: B-T3.3.8, B-R3.3.2; prereqs: [Def - Yang-Mills Lagrangian and Action Functional, Thm - Structure Equation for the Curvature, Def - Lie-Algebra-Valued Differential Forms and Their Bracket, Thm - Leibniz Rule for the Metric Pairing of Bundle-Valued Forms, Thm - The Trace Metric on the Adjoint Bundle is Parallel, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; statement: $\omega$ is a Yang–Mills connection if and only if $\int_M\lambda(\bar\eta\wedge d^\omega\star\bar\Omega) = 0$ for all compactly supported $\bar\eta\in\Omega^1(M;\operatorname{ad}P)$, if and only if $d^\omega\star\bar\Omega = 0$ (the Yang–Mills equation; locally $d\star F_A + [A\wedge\star F_A] = 0$); proof: Bär's, with the two gaps filled: Lemma 1: $\Omega_t = \Omega + t(d\eta + [\omega\wedge\eta]) + \tfrac12t^2[\eta\wedge\eta]$ for $\omega_t = \omega + t\eta$ (structure equation; symmetry $[\omega\wedge\eta] = [\eta\wedge\omega]$ for 1-forms proved from the bracket convention — Bär's footnote 5); Lemma 2: $\overline{d\eta + [\omega\wedge\eta]} = d^\omega\bar\eta$ (the descended form of the covariant derivative — prove in a local section: $s^*(d\eta+[\omega\wedge\eta]) = d\eta_s + [\omega_s\wedge\eta_s]$, which is the local formula for $d^\omega$ from `Def - The Adjoint Bundle with its Trace Metric and Induced Connection`); then $\frac{d}{dt}\big|_0\int_{\bar U}L_{YM}(\omega_t) = \int\lambda(d^\omega\bar\eta\wedge\star\bar\Omega)$ (symmetry of the pairing, property (4)); Leibniz rule with $k = 1$: $\lambda(d^\omega\bar\eta\wedge\star\bar\Omega) = d\lambda(\bar\eta\wedge\star\bar\Omega) + \lambda(\bar\eta\wedge d^\omega\star\bar\Omega)$; Stokes kills the exact term; fundamental lemma for $\operatorname{ad}P$-valued 1-forms (Lemma 3); the local form via the local formula for $d^\omega$ (source: Bär writes $-\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}(\dots)$ for $\tfrac12\lambda(\dots)$ — same thing); spec: link `Thm - Euler-Lagrange Equation of the Electromagnetic Action` as the abelian case; mechanism: **the first variation of a quadratic functional is the pairing with the gradient, and moving $d^\omega$ across the pairing is the Leibniz rule plus Stokes**; What Makes This Hard: $\eta$ must be $\operatorname{Ad}$-equivariant and horizontal for $\omega+t\eta$ to be a connection.
- `Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Connections` — type: definition (anchor); source items: (the notion $\star\bar\Omega = \pm\bar\Omega$ from B p. 102; D3.3.7's "self-dual" clause); prereqs: [Def - The Yang-Mills Field Strength, Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions, Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Forms]; spec: on an oriented Riemannian 4-manifold, $\omega$ is *self-dual* if $\star\bar\Omega = \bar\Omega$ and *anti-self-dual* if $\star\bar\Omega = -\bar\Omega$; equivalently $F^- = 0$, resp. $F^+ = 0$, where $F^\pm := \tfrac12(F\pm\star F)$ (the splitting $\Omega^2(M;\operatorname{ad}P) = \Omega^2_+\oplus\Omega^2_-$ tensored with $\operatorname{ad}P$); orientation reversal exchanges the two notions; the anti-self-duality *equations* $F_A^+ = 0$ as a first-order nonlinear PDE for $A$ (Haydys R1.2 mentions them); examples: flat connections (both); $U(1)$ over $S^2\times S^2$ with $F = c_1\mathrm{vol}_1 + c_2\mathrm{vol}_2$ is (A)SD iff $c_1 = \pm c_2$ (computed); non-example: a $U(1)$-connection with $F^+\ne0\ne F^-$.
- `Def - Instanton` — type: definition (anchor; backlink-map name, 11 external links); source items: B-D3.3.7; prereqs: [Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Connections, Def - Yang-Mills Lagrangian and Action Functional]; spec: Bär: an *instanton* on $P\to M$ ($M$ an oriented Riemannian 4-manifold) is a connection with self-dual curvature $\star\bar\Omega = \bar\Omega$; Convention callout (mandatory): Donaldson–Kronheimer, Freed–Uhlenbeck and Haydys use *anti*-self-dual connections as instantons; since reversing the orientation of $M$ swaps the two, the series' statements are made for "(anti-)self-dual" with the sign fixed per page, and `Def - Instanton` records both usages; instantons are absolute minimisers of $\mathcal{YM}$ in their topological class (proved on `Thm - Energy Identity and the Topological Bound for Yang-Mills`); on Minkowski space (Lorentzian) the notion is different ($\star\star = -1$) — remark; examples: the BPST instanton (exercise), flat connections, the Hopf-bundle connection over $S^4 = \mathbb{HP}^1$ (mentioned, bold); non-example: a Yang–Mills connection that is not (A)SD — e.g. a $U(1)$ connection on $S^2\times S^2$ with harmonic curvature $c_1\mathrm{vol}_1 + c_2\mathrm{vol}_2$, $c_1\ne\pm c_2$ (harmonic ⇒ Yang–Mills, but not (A)SD) — verified.
- `Thm - Self-Dual Connections are Yang-Mills` — type: theorem (anchor); source items: B-T3.3.9; prereqs: [Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Connections, Thm - Bianchi Identity on the Base, Thm - Yang-Mills Equation from the Action Principle]; statement: If $\star\bar\Omega = \pm\bar\Omega$ then $d^\omega\star\bar\Omega = \pm d^\omega\bar\Omega = 0$, so $\omega$ is a Yang–Mills connection; proof: Bär's one line, written with the Bianchi identity restated; add: the converse fails (the $S^2\times S^2$ example above); spec: mechanism: **the second-order Yang–Mills equation factors through the first-order (anti-)self-duality equation via Bianchi**; targets: instanton moduli spaces (chapter XIII's Donaldson theory).
- `Def - Killing Form of a Lie Algebra` — type: definition; source items: B-D3.3.9, B-I3.3.3 (recorded as **bold plain-text context** in Relate: "the Killing form is negative definite if and only if the Lie algebra is that of a compact semisimple group (Cartan's criterion); Bär states 'iff $G$ semisimple', which needs compactness — not used in the series"); prereqs: [Def - Lie Algebra, Def - Adjoint Representation, Def - Bilinear Form]; spec: $\kappa(A,B) := \operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{ad}A\circ\operatorname{ad}B)$ on $\mathfrak g$; $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariance and $\operatorname{ad}$-skewness proved in Examples; $\lambda' := -\kappa$; examples: $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ with basis $i\sigma_1, i\sigma_2, i\sigma_3$: compute $\kappa(i\sigma_a, i\sigma_b) = -8\delta_{ab} = 4\operatorname{tr}(i\sigma_a\, i\sigma_b)$, the $N = 2$ instance of the next page's identity $\kappa(A,B) = 2N\operatorname{tr}(AB)$; abelian $\mathfrak g$ has $\kappa = 0$ (non-example of non-degeneracy).
- `Thm - The Killing Form of su(N) is 2N Times the Trace Form` — type: theorem; source items: B-I3.3.2; prereqs: [Def - Killing Form of a Lie Algebra, Def - The Adjoint Bundle with its Trace Metric and Induced Connection]; statement: For $A,B\in\mathfrak{gl}(N;\mathbb C)$: $\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{ad}A\circ\operatorname{ad}B) = 2N\operatorname{tr}(AB) - 2\operatorname{tr}(A)\operatorname{tr}(B)$; hence on $\mathfrak{su}(N)$ (traceless): $\lambda'(A,B) = -\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{ad}A\circ\operatorname{ad}B) = 2N\lambda(A,B)$, and $\lambda'$ is an $\operatorname{Ad}$-invariant inner product; proof: Bär cites "an elementary fact in representation theory"; the writer proves the identity directly: compute the trace of $X\mapsto A(BX)-A(XB)-(BX)A+(XB)A$ on $\mathfrak{gl}(N)$ using the basis of matrix units $E_{ij}$ and the traces $\operatorname{tr}(X\mapsto AXB) = \operatorname{tr}(A)\operatorname{tr}(B)$, $\operatorname{tr}(X\mapsto ABX) = N\operatorname{tr}(AB)$ (each proved by evaluating on $E_{ij}$); restrict to the real subspace $\mathfrak{su}(N)$ (the adjoint representation of $\mathfrak{su}(N)$ on itself is the restriction of that of $\mathfrak{gl}(N;\mathbb C)$ to the $\operatorname{ad}$-invariant real subspace $\mathfrak{su}(N)$; the trace over $\mathfrak{gl}(N;\mathbb C)$ as a complex space equals the trace over $\mathfrak{sl}(N;\mathbb C) = \mathfrak{su}(N)\otimes\mathbb C$ plus the trace on the centre, which is $0$ — write this decomposition out); spec: no Schur lemma needed; mechanism: **the adjoint action on matrices is left multiplication minus right multiplication, and both have computable traces**.
- `Thm - Top de Rham Cohomology of a Closed Connected Oriented Manifold` — type: theorem; source items: B-T3.3.11, B-I3.3.5, B-R3.3.4 (→ inside, Statement: the convention of identifying $H^4_{dR}(M)$ with $\mathbb R$ and treating $p_1$ as a number); prereqs: [Def - de Rham Cohomology, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Thm - The Poincaré Lemma on a Star-Shaped Region, Def - Partition of Unity on a Manifold, Thm - Existence of Smooth Partitions of Unity, Def - Orientation of a Smooth Manifold, Thm - Integration is Well-Defined on Oriented Manifolds]; statement: Let $M$ be a closed, connected, oriented $n$-manifold.
