Then $\int_M:H^n_{dR}(M)\to\mathbb R$, $[\alpha]\mapsto\int_M\alpha$, is a well-defined linear isomorphism; in particular a top form is exact if and only if its integral vanishes; proof: Bär states it; follow Lee, *Introduction to Smooth Manifolds*, 2nd ed., Lemma 17.27 (a compactly supported $n$-form on $\mathbb R^n$ with zero integral is $d$ of a compactly supported $(n-1)$-form — induction on $n$ via fibre integration), Thm 17.30–17.31 (the connected-manifold argument: cover by charts, connect any two by a finite chain, use a partition of unity to write $\alpha$ as a sum of chart-supported forms and move mass between adjacent charts); every lemma spelled out: Lemma 1 (Poincaré lemma with compact supports on $\mathbb R^n$), Lemma 2 (two chart-supported forms with equal integrals in overlapping charts differ by an exact compactly supported form), Lemma 3 (chain of charts by connectedness), Lemma 4 (well-definedness of $\int$ on classes by Stokes); spec: the only use in VII is the identification of $p_1$ with a number, but the theorem is reused in XI (`Thm - Finiteness of the Seiberg-Witten Invariant`, lattice argument via $H^2$) and XIII; Bridges: `Thm - Poincare Duality via Hodge Star` (Hodge I) states the general duality with a sketch — this page proves the top-degree case fully; the Hodge-theoretic proof (harmonic $n$-forms are constant multiples of $\mathrm{vol}$) is a Cross-Field Exercise once chapter IX is available.
- `Thm - Energy Identity and the Topological Bound for Yang-Mills` — type: theorem (anchor); source items: B-T3.3.12, B-R3.3.3, B-I3.3.4 (→ inside, Lemma 1), (3.18); prereqs: [Def - Yang-Mills Lagrangian and Action Functional, Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Connections, Def - Instanton, Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions, Thm - The Killing Form of su(N) is 2N Times the Trace Form, Def - Pontryagin Classes, Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem, Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles, Thm - Top de Rham Cohomology of a Closed Connected Oriented Manifold, Thm - The Trace Metric on the Adjoint Bundle is Parallel]; statement: Let $P\to M$ be an $SU(N)$-bundle ($N\ge2$) over a closed oriented Riemannian 4-manifold, $p_1 := p_1(\operatorname{ad}P) = \int_M\frac{1}{8\pi^2}(\operatorname{tr}\bar\Phi\wedge\operatorname{tr}\bar\Phi - \operatorname{tr}(\bar\Phi\wedge\bar\Phi))\in\mathbb R$ (with $\bar\Phi$ the curvature of any connection on the frame bundle of $\operatorname{ad}P$; independent of the choice by Chern–Weil). (i) (Energy identity) For every $\omega\in\mathcal C(P)$: $\|\bar\Omega\|^2_{L^2} = \|\bar\Omega^+\|^2 + \|\bar\Omega^-\|^2$ and $\frac{N}{4\pi^2}\big(\|\bar\Omega^+\|^2 - \|\bar\Omega^-\|^2\big) = p_1$, i.e. $p_1 = \frac{N}{4\pi^2}\int_M\lambda(\bar\Omega\wedge\bar\Omega) = \frac{N}{4\pi^2}\langle\bar\Omega,\star\bar\Omega\rangle_{L^2}$. (ii) (Bound (3.18)) $\int_ML_{YM}(\omega)\ge\frac{2\pi^2}{N}|p_1|$, with equality if and only if $\bar\Omega = \pm\star\bar\Omega$ (sign $= \operatorname{sign}p_1$). (iii) If $p_1<0$ there is no self-dual connection on $P$, $\int L_{YM}\ge-\frac{2\pi^2}{N}p_1$ with equality iff $\omega$ is anti-self-dual; if $p_1>0$ there is no anti-self-dual connection, $\int L_{YM}\ge\frac{2\pi^2}{N}p_1$ with equality iff $\omega$ is self-dual; if $p_1 = 0$, $\omega$ is (anti-)self-dual iff $\bar\Omega\equiv0$; proof: Bär's six steps, gaps filled: Lemma 1 ($\bar\Phi = \operatorname{ad}\circ\bar\Omega$ — from the associated-bundle curvature anchor with $\rho = \operatorname{Ad}$, $\rho_* = \operatorname{ad}$; if the anchor lacks the curvature statement, prove it here from the local formula $\nabla^\omega = d + \operatorname{ad}(A)$ and $F = dA + \tfrac12[A\wedge A]$ with $\operatorname{ad}$ a Lie algebra homomorphism); Lemma 2 ($\operatorname{ad}X$ is $\lambda$-skew, hence traceless, so $\operatorname{tr}\bar\Phi = 0$); Lemma 3 (the Killing-form page); Lemma 4 ($\int\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{ad}\bar\Omega\wedge\operatorname{ad}\bar\Omega) = -2N\int\lambda(\bar\Omega\wedge\bar\Omega)$ using the metric pairing page); Lemma 5 ($\lambda(\bar\Omega\wedge\bar\Omega) = \langle\bar\Omega,\star\bar\Omega\rangle\mathrm{vol}$ by property (4) with $p = 0$ and $\star\star = 1$; then $\langle\bar\Omega,\star\bar\Omega\rangle = |\bar\Omega^+|^2-|\bar\Omega^-|^2$ from the self-dual decomposition page); then $0\le\|\bar\Omega\mp\star\bar\Omega\|^2 = 2(\|\bar\Omega\|^2\mp\langle\bar\Omega,\star\bar\Omega\rangle) = 2(2\int L_{YM}\mp\frac{4\pi^2}{N}p_1)$; cases as Bär; spec: this is where Haydys's silent identities of Corollary 199 (A-T7.1.11) live in the series — the $U(1)$ version with $c_1$ is `Ex - Energy Identity for U(1) Connections on a Four-Manifold`; mechanism: **the Yang–Mills energy splits into self-dual and anti-self-dual halves whose difference is a characteristic number; minimising the sum with fixed difference forces one half to vanish**; Bridges: Donaldson theory (XIII), Seiberg–Witten curvature bounds (XI).
- `Ex - The Yang-Mills Equation in Local Coordinates` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (additional; standard — Bär's local Bianchi form R3.3.1 as the pattern); prereqs: [Thm - Yang-Mills Equation from the Action Principle, Def - The Adjoint Bundle with its Trace Metric and Induced Connection]; spec: for a trivialised bundle over an open set of $\mathbb R^4$ with the flat metric, write $d^\omega\star F = 0$ as $\sum_\mu(\partial_\mu F_{\mu\nu} + [A_\mu,F_{\mu\nu}]) = 0$; check that for $U(1)$ this is $\partial_\mu F_{\mu\nu} = 0$ (Maxwell in vacuum, Euclidean signature); invokes the local formula for $d^\omega$ and the star on 2-forms.
- `Ex - The BPST Instanton on R^4` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: (additional; Belavin–Polyakov–Schwarz–Tyupkin 1975; route as in Naber *Topology, Geometry and Gauge Fields: Foundations* §6.2 / Atiyah *Geometry of Yang–Mills Fields* §2); prereqs: [Def - Instanton, Def - The Yang-Mills Field Strength, Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions]; spec: identify $\mathbb R^4 = \mathbb H$, $\mathfrak{su}(2) = \operatorname{Im}\mathbb H$; take the connection matrix $A := \operatorname{Im}\Big(\frac{\bar x\,dx}{1+|x|^2}\Big)\in\Omega^1(\mathbb R^4;\operatorname{Im}\mathbb H)$ (the writer verifies $\operatorname{Im}(\bar x\,dx)$ is imaginary-quaternion valued, so $A$ is $\mathfrak{su}(2)$-valued); compute $F = dA + A\wedge A = \frac{d\bar x\wedge dx}{(1+|x|^2)^2}$ and show it is anti-self-dual (or self-dual, depending on orientation — the writer fixes the orientation $dx_0\wedge dx_1\wedge dx_2\wedge dx_3$ and determines the sign by evaluating on the basis of `Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions`); compute $\|F\|^2_{L^2} = 8\pi^2$; invokes the listed pages and the quaternion calculus of `Def - Quaternions` (Spinors, vault).
- `Ex - Abelian Yang-Mills Theory is Maxwell Theory` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (additional); prereqs: [Def - Yang-Mills Lagrangian and Action Functional, Def - Electromagnetic Lagrangian and Action]; spec: for $G = U(1)$ with $\lambda(ia,ib) = ab$ show $L_{YM} = \tfrac12F\wedge\star F = L_1$ and $d^\omega\star\bar\Omega = 0$ is $d\star F = 0$; explain why the coupling $A\wedge J$ has no gauge-invariant non-abelian analogue without matter fields.
- `Ex - Energy Identity for U(1) Connections on a Four-Manifold` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (additional; the abelian case of T3.3.12 — the exact statement Haydys uses in Corollary 199, A-T7.1.11 and Appendix B item 17); prereqs: [Thm - Energy Identity and the Topological Bound for Yang-Mills, Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature, Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions]; spec: for a unitary connection $A$ on a Hermitian line bundle $L$ over a closed oriented Riemannian 4-manifold, with $F_A\in\Omega^2(M;i\mathbb R)$ and $c_1(L) = [\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F_A]$: prove the energy identity with the signs tracked for imaginary-valued forms: writing $F_A = iG$ with $G$ real, $F_A\wedge F_A = -G\wedge G$ while $|F_A|^2 = |G|^2$, so $c_1(L)^2 := \int_M\big(\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F_A\big)\wedge\big(\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F_A\big) = -\frac{1}{4\pi^2}\int F_A\wedge F_A = \frac{1}{4\pi^2}\int G\wedge G = \frac{1}{4\pi^2}(\|G^+\|^2-\|G^-\|^2) = \frac{1}{4\pi^2}(\|F_A^+\|^2-\|F_A^-\|^2)$, so $\|F_A^-\|^2 = \|F_A^+\|^2 - 4\pi^2c_1(L)^2$; this fixes the sign convention flagged in A-T7.1.11 (source typo: Haydys p. 65 writes the identity with a sign requiring $\int F^+\wedge F^+ = -\|F^+\|^2$; with $F$ imaginary-valued and the norm taken of the real form this is consistent — the exercise settles it explicitly and chapter XI cites this page); ⭐⭐.
- `Ex - The Yang-Mills Bound for SU(2) via the Second Chern Number` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: (additional; standard, Donaldson–Kronheimer §2.1.4 / Freed–Uhlenbeck Ch. 2); prereqs: [Thm - Energy Identity and the Topological Bound for Yang-Mills, Def - Chern Classes, Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem, Def - Pontryagin Classes]; spec: for an $SU(2)$-bundle with associated rank-2 bundle $E$, show $c_2(E)[M] = \frac{1}{8\pi^2}\int\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)$ from the Chern–Weil normalisation and deduce $p_1(\operatorname{ad}P) = -4c_2(E)[M]$ (route: compute $\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{ad}F\wedge\operatorname{ad}F) = 4\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)$ on $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ from the Killing-form page); rewrite the bound as $\|F\|^2_{L^2}\ge8\pi^2|c_2(E)[M]|$ with equality for instantons; check on the BPST instanton ($c_2 = 1$ after one-point compactification, bold plain text).
- `Exercise Index - §7.4 Yang–Mills Fields and Instantons` — lists: [Ex - The Yang-Mills Equation in Local Coordinates, Ex - The BPST Instanton on R^4, Ex - Abelian Yang-Mills Theory is Maxwell Theory, Ex - Energy Identity for U(1) Connections on a Four-Manifold, Ex - The Yang-Mills Bound for SU(2) via the Second Chern Number]

Design notes: (1) $Spin(n)$ is *defined concretely* inside $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^n)$ (Haydys R4.1.1 / I4.1.2) and proved to be a connected compact Lie group double covering $SO(n)$ (Lawson–Michelsohn I.2), so that no fundamental-group input is needed; Haydys's characterisation as the universal cover (D4.1.1, I4.1.1) is recorded as context with the chapter XII forward reference. (2) The spinor bundle is defined for general $n$ *given* a complex $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^n)$-module; the existence and uniqueness of the irreducible module (I4.3.1) is proved in full for $n = 3, 4$ (the only dimensions the series uses) and recorded as context with reference Lawson–Michelsohn Thm I.5.7–5.8 for general $n$. (3) The Clifford sign is $v\cdot v = -|v|^2$ (conventions.md); every page notes the vault's opposite sign and the recipe ($\mathrm{Cl}$ of the negative form).

### §8.1 Clifford Algebras and Spin Groups
- `Def - Clifford Algebra and Clifford Multiplication` — type: definition (anchor; compound: the algebra, its $\mathbb Z/2$-grading, Clifford modules); source items: A-D4.1.2, A-D4.1.3, A-E4.1.2 (→ inside, Examples: the exterior algebra $\Lambda U^*$ with $u\cdot\phi = \iota_u\phi - \langle u,\cdot\rangle\wedge\phi$, verified: $(\iota_u - u^\flat\wedge)^2 = -|u|^2$), A-E4.1.3 (→ inside, Examples: a quaternionic vector space as a $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^3)$-module, $h\cdot(h\cdot v) = -h\bar hv = -|h|^2v$), A-E4.1.4 (→ inside, Examples: $V\oplus V$ as a $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^4)$-module via $h\otimes(v_1,v_2)\mapsto(hv_2,-\bar hv_1)$, verified); prereqs: [Def - Clifford Algebra, Thm - Clifford Algebra Universal Property, Def - Quaternions, Def - Inner Product Space, Def - Tensor Product of Vector Spaces]; spec: for a Euclidean vector space $U$, $\mathrm{Cl}(U) := TU/(u\otimes u + |u|^2\cdot1)$; equivalently generated by $U$ with $u\cdot u = -|u|^2$, polarised $uv + vu = -2\langle u,v\rangle$; $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^n)$ generated by $e_1,\dots,e_n$ with $e_i^2 = -1$, $e_ie_j = -e_je_i$; the even/odd grading $\mathrm{Cl} = \mathrm{Cl}^0\oplus\mathrm{Cl}^1$ (from the automorphism $u\mapsto-u$ extended by the universal property); basis $\{e_I\}$, $\dim\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^n) = 2^n$ (proved: spanning by the relations; independence via the exterior-algebra module E4.1.2, which is faithful — this is the standard trick and avoids the classification theorem); a *Clifford module* is $V$ with $U\otimes V\to V$, $u\cdot(u\cdot v) = -|u|^2v$, equivalently an algebra homomorphism $\mathrm{Cl}(U)\to\operatorname{End}(V)$ (universal property, wikilink to the vault's `Thm - Clifford Algebra Universal Property`, which is stated for $v^2 = Q(v)$ — apply with $Q = -|\cdot|^2$); complex Clifford modules and $\mathbb C\otimes\mathrm{Cl}(U)$; Convention callout: the vault's `Def - Clifford Algebra` and Lawson–Michelsohn use $v^2 = -q(v)$ with $q$ positive (same as here) while the vault's page writes $v^2 = Q(v)$ with $Q$ possibly indefinite — dictionary $\mathrm{Cl}(U) = \mathrm{Cl}(U, -|\cdot|^2)$; Axiom Motivation: square roots of $-|u|^2$, Dirac's factorisation of the Laplacian; non-example: a module map with $u\cdot(u\cdot v) = +|u|^2v$ is a module over the other Clifford algebra.
- `Thm - Low-Dimensional Clifford Algebras and the Quaternionic Spinor Modules` — type: theorem; source items: A-E4.1.1, A-D4.1.4, A-I4.3.1 (existence and uniqueness of the irreducible complex Clifford module, proved here for $n = 3, 4$), A-R4.1.2; prereqs: [Def - Clifford Algebra and Clifford Multiplication, Def - Quaternions, Def - The Pauli Matrices]; statement: (i) $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^1)\cong\mathbb C$ and $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^2)\cong\mathbb H$ as real algebras. (ii) $\mathrm{Cl}^0(\mathbb R^3)\cong\mathbb H$, $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^3)\cong\mathbb H\oplus\mathbb H$, and $\mathbb C\otimes\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^3)\cong M_2(\mathbb C)\oplus M_2(\mathbb C)$; $\slashed S := \mathbb H$ with $\operatorname{Im}\mathbb H\otimes\slashed S\to\slashed S$, $h\otimes v\mapsto hv$, is a $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^3)$-module; right multiplication by $\bar i = -i$ commutes with the action and makes $\slashed S$ a complex 2-dimensional Hermitian $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^3)$-module (with $\langle v,w\rangle := $ the $\mathbb C$-valued part of $\bar v w$, made explicit), irreducible, and every irreducible complex $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^3)$-module is isomorphic to $\slashed S$ or to its conjugate $\slashed S'$ (obtained by composing with $u\mapsto-u$), the two being distinguished by the action of the volume element $e_1e_2e_3$ ($= \pm1$). (iii) $\mathrm{Cl}^0(\mathbb R^4)\cong\mathbb H\oplus\mathbb H$, $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^4)\cong M_2(\mathbb H)$, $\mathbb C\otimes\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^4)\cong M_4(\mathbb C)$; $\slashed S := \slashed S^+\oplus\slashed S^-$ with $\slashed S^\pm = \mathbb H$ and $h\cdot(v_1,v_2) = (hv_2,-\bar hv_1)$ is an irreducible complex 4-dimensional Hermitian $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^4)$-module, unique up to isomorphism; Clifford multiplication by vectors maps $\slashed S^\pm\to\slashed S^\mp$; the volume element $e_1e_2e_3e_4$ acts as $\mp1$ on $\slashed S^\pm$ (sign fixed by the writer's orientation convention and recorded); proof: E4.1.1 as Haydys ($x^2 = -1$; $(e_1+e_2)^2 = -2$ forces anticommutation; $\mathbb H$ via $i = e_1, j = e_2, k = e_1e_2$); (ii),(iii) by explicit isomorphisms (the standard ones: $\mathrm{Cl}^0(\mathbb R^3)\ni e_1e_2, e_2e_3, e_3e_1\mapsto$ quaternion units; $\mathbb H\otimes_\mathbb R\mathbb C\cong M_2(\mathbb C)$ via the Pauli matrices — link `Def - The Pauli Matrices`, `Ex - Pauli Matrices Generate Cl(R^3)`); uniqueness of irreducible modules without Wedderburn: Lemma 1 (every module over $M_k(\mathbb C)$ is a direct sum of copies of $\mathbb C^k$ — proof by matrix units $E_{ij}$: $V = \bigoplus_iE_{ii}V$ and $E_{i1}:E_{11}V\to E_{ii}V$ isomorphisms), Lemma 2 (modules over $A_1\oplus A_2$ split as $V = e_1V\oplus e_2V$ with $e_i$ the central idempotents); Hermitian structure: the module inner product with $\langle u\cdot v,u\cdot w\rangle = |u|^2\langle v,w\rangle$ (Haydys's condition (2) of a Dirac bundle) verified; spec: this page replaces the flagged `Thm - Classification of Clifford Algebras over R` for the series' needs; mechanism: **in dimensions three and four the Clifford algebra is built from quaternions, and quaternion multiplication is the spinor representation**; Bridges: `Def - SU(2) Action on Spinors`, `Def - Weyl Spinor` (Spinors).
- `Def - Spin Group and the Double Cover of SO(n)` — type: definition (anchor; compound: $Spin(n)$ inside $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^n)$, the twisted adjoint map $\xi$, the Lie algebra $\mathfrak{spin}(n)$); source items: A-D4.1.1 (→ inside, Motivation: Haydys's characterisation as the simply connected double cover, recorded as bold context with the chapter XII forward reference), A-R4.1.1, A-I4.1.2 (the definition adopted), A-I4.1.1 (→ context, not invoked; see `[NEEDED FROM XII]`); prereqs: [Def - Clifford Algebra and Clifford Multiplication, Def - Pin and Spin Groups, Def - Lie Group, Thm - The Closed Subgroup Theorem, Def - The Lie Algebra of a Lie Group]; spec: $Spin(n) := \{v_1\cdots v_{2k} : v_i\in S^{n-1}\subset\mathbb R^n, k\ge0\}\subset\mathrm{Cl}^0(\mathbb R^n)^\times$; $\xi:Spin(n)\to GL(\mathbb R^n)$, $\xi(a)u := a\,u\,a^{-1}$; the *twisted adjoint* $\tilde\xi(a)u := \alpha(a)ua^{-1}$ ($\alpha$ the grading automorphism) satisfies $\tilde\xi(v)u = -vuv^{-1} = u - 2\langle u,v\rangle v$ = reflection in $v^\perp$ for a unit vector $v$, and $\tilde\xi = \xi$ on even elements; both are stated and their agreement on $Spin(n)$ is proved; $\mathfrak{spin}(n) := \operatorname{span}\{e_ie_j : i<j\}\subset\mathrm{Cl}^0(\mathbb R^n)$ with the commutator bracket; the map $\mathfrak{so}(n)\to\mathfrak{spin}(n)$, $A\mapsto\tfrac14\sum_{i,j}\langle Ae_i,e_j\rangle e_ie_j$ (its properties proved on the next page); Convention callout: the vault's `Def - Pin and Spin Groups` defines $Pin(n)$ and $Spin(n)$ the same way (check the sign of its Clifford relation and record the dictionary); examples: $n = 2$: $Spin(2) = \{\cos t + \sin t\,e_1e_2\}\cong U(1)$ with $\xi$ the double cover $z\mapsto z^2$ (computed); $n = 3$ via quaternions (next page); degenerate case: $Spin(1) = \{\pm1\}$ with $\xi$ trivial (stated, so that $n\ge2$ is seen to be needed for connectedness).
- `Thm - Spin(n) inside the Clifford Algebra is a Double Cover of SO(n)` — type: theorem; source items: A-R4.1.1, A-I4.1.2 (the "it can be shown"), A-R4.3.3, A-I4.3.4 ($\{\pm1\}$ is central in $Spin(n)$); prereqs: [Def - Spin Group and the Double Cover of SO(n), Def - Clifford Algebra and Clifford Multiplication, Thm - The Closed Subgroup Theorem, Thm - Lie Group Homomorphism Induces Lie Algebra Homomorphism, Def - Covering Space]; statement: For $n\ge2$: (i) $Spin(n)$ is a compact connected Lie subgroup of $\mathrm{Cl}^0(\mathbb R^n)^\times$; (ii) $\xi:Spin(n)\to SO(n)$ is a surjective Lie group homomorphism with kernel $\{\pm1\}$, and $\{\pm1\}$ is central; (iii) $\xi$ is a two-sheeted covering map and a local diffeomorphism, so $d\xi:\mathfrak{spin}(n)\to\mathfrak{so}(n)$ is a Lie algebra isomorphism, with inverse $A\mapsto\tfrac14\sum_{i,j}\langle Ae_i,e_j\rangle e_ie_j$; (iv) the curve $t\mapsto\cos t + \sin t\,e_1e_2$, $t\in[0,\pi]$, joins $1$ to $-1$ in $Spin(n)$, so $\xi$ is a non-trivial double cover ($Spin(n)$ is not $SO(n)\times\{\pm1\}$); proof: Lawson–Michelsohn, *Spin Geometry*, Thm I.2.9, Prop I.2.10 (kernel) and the Cartan–Dieudonné theorem (Thm I.2.7: every element of $O(n)$ is a product of at most $n$ reflections — prove by induction on $n$ as a lemma on the page; then $SO(n)$ = even products), Lemma 3 (the kernel of $\tilde\xi$ on $\mathrm{Cl}^\times$ is $\mathbb R^\times$: LM Prop I.2.4, via the grading argument), Lemma 4 (norm $N(a) = a\bar a$ multiplicative on $Pin$, $=1$ on unit-vector products, so $\ker\xi\cap Spin = \{\pm1\}$), Lemma 5 (compactness and the Lie structure: the writer shows $Spin(n)$ is the image of the compact set $(S^{n-1})^{2n}$ under a continuous map, using Cartan–Dieudonné to bound the number of factors by $n$, hence compact; closed subgroup theorem gives the Lie structure), Lemma 6 (local diffeomorphism: $d\xi$ injective on $\mathfrak{spin}(n)$ by computing $d\xi(e_ie_j) = 2E_{ij}$-type skew matrices, and dimensions agree, so $d\xi$ is an isomorphism; a homomorphism with discrete kernel and bijective differential between compact Lie groups is a covering — prove via the inverse function theorem and homogeneity); spec: replaces the flagged `Thm - Spin(n) is the Double Cover of SO(n)`; mechanism: **conjugation by a unit vector is minus a reflection, so even products of unit vectors act by rotations, and the only scalars among them are $\pm1$**.
- `Thm - Spin Groups in Dimensions Three and Four via Quaternions` — type: theorem; source items: A-T4.1.1, A-T4.1.2; prereqs: [Thm - Spin(n) inside the Clifford Algebra is a Double Cover of SO(n), Thm - Low-Dimensional Clifford Algebras and the Quaternionic Spinor Modules, Def - Quaternions, Thm - SU(2) is the Double Cover of SO(3)]; statement: (i) $\alpha:Sp(1)\to SO(3)$, $\alpha(q)h = qh\bar q$ on $\operatorname{Im}\mathbb H\cong\mathbb R^3$, is a surjective Lie group homomorphism with kernel $\{\pm1\}$, and the isomorphism $\mathrm{Cl}^0(\mathbb R^3)\cong\mathbb H$ of the low-dimensional page restricts to an isomorphism $Spin(3)\cong Sp(1)$ under which $\xi$ becomes $\alpha$; the Lie algebra map $d\alpha:\operatorname{Im}\mathbb H\to\mathfrak{so}(3)$ is $x\mapsto(h\mapsto xh - hx = 2x\times h)$, an isomorphism. (ii) $\beta:Sp_+(1)\times Sp_-(1)\to SO(4)$, $\beta(q_+,q_-)h = q_+h\bar q_-$ on $\mathbb H\cong\mathbb R^4$, is a surjective Lie group homomorphism with kernel $\{\pm(1,1)\}$; $\mathrm{Cl}^0(\mathbb R^4)\cong\mathbb H\oplus\mathbb H$ restricts to $Spin(4)\cong Sp(1)\times Sp(1)$ with $\xi = \beta$; the composite $Sp_+(1)\times Sp_-(1)\to SO(4)\to SO(3)\times SO(3)$ induced by $\mathfrak{so}(4)\cong\Lambda^2_+\oplus\Lambda^2_-\cong\mathfrak{so}(3)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(3)$ is $(q_+,q_-)\mapsto(\alpha(q_+),\alpha(q_-))$ (the "explicit computation" Haydys omits, written out: the action of $SO(4)$ on $\Lambda^2_\pm\mathbb R^4$ and the identification $\Lambda^2_\pm\cong\operatorname{Im}\mathbb H$ via $h\mapsto$ the 2-form $\langle h\cdot,\cdot\rangle$, resp. $\langle\cdot h,\cdot\rangle$); proof: Haydys's are short with the computations omitted; the page does all of them: $|qh\bar q| = |h|$ and $\overline{qh\bar q} = -qh\bar q$ for $h$ imaginary; kernel computation ($qh = hq$ for all imaginary $h$ ⇒ $q$ real ⇒ $q = \pm1$); surjectivity from the local-diffeomorphism-plus-connectedness argument of the previous page or directly (every rotation of $\mathbb R^3$ is $\alpha(\cos\tfrac\theta2 + \sin\tfrac\theta2\,u)$ — computed; for $SO(4)$: connectedness of $SO(4)$ and openness of the image); the identification of $\xi$ with $\alpha$, $\beta$ via the explicit Clifford isomorphisms; spec: link `Thm - SU(2) is the Double Cover of SO(3)` (vault, unflagged) as the same statement in $2\times2$ matrices; mechanism: quaternion conjugation preserves the imaginary part and the norm.
- `Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms` — type: theorem (the linear algebra Haydys uses in §4.3–§7.1; four parts); source items: A-T4.1.3, A-T4.1.4, A-I4.1.3, A-T7.1.1, A-I7.1.1, A-I4.4.2 (anti-self-dual forms act trivially on $\slashed S^+$), A-R7.1.1 (→ inside, Examples: $\mu(\psi)$ as an imaginary self-dual 2-form; the quadratic map itself is defined in XI); prereqs: [Thm - Spin Groups in Dimensions Three and Four via Quaternions, Thm - Low-Dimensional Clifford Algebras and the Quaternionic Spinor Modules, Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions, Thm - Complex Representations of U(1) and SU(2)]; statement: (i) $\operatorname{Im}\mathbb H\otimes\mathbb C\cong\operatorname{End}_0(\slashed S)$ as $Sp(1)$-representations ($Sp(1)$ acting on the left via $\alpha$), the real subspace $\operatorname{Im}\mathbb H$ corresponding to the traceless Hermitian endomorphisms $-i\cdot$(Clifford multiplication) — equation (111). (ii) $\mathbb H\otimes\mathbb C\cong\operatorname{Hom}_\mathbb C(\slashed S^+,\slashed S^-)$ as $Spin(4)$-representations, the real subspace $\mathbb H = \mathbb R^4$ mapping to Clifford multiplication $v\mapsto(\psi\mapsto v\cdot\psi)$ (equation (185)); similarly $\mathbb R^4\to\operatorname{Hom}(\slashed S^-,\slashed S^+)$; for $\psi_0\ne0$ the map $\mathbb R^4\to\slashed S^-$, $v\mapsto v\cdot\psi_0$, is an $\mathbb R$-linear isomorphism. (iii) The Clifford action of 2-forms, $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^4\to\operatorname{End}(\slashed S^\pm)$, $e_i\wedge e_j\mapsto e_i\cdot e_j\cdot$, has kernel $\Lambda^2_\mp\mathbb R^4$ on $\slashed S^\pm$ and skew-Hermitian image, inducing $Spin(4)$-equivariant isomorphisms $\Lambda^2_+\mathbb R^4\cong\mathfrak{su}(\slashed S^+)$ and $\Lambda^2_+\mathbb R^4\otimes\mathbb C\cong\operatorname{End}_0(\slashed S^+)$ (equation (187)); consequently $\Lambda^2_+\otimes\mathbb C$ and $\Lambda^2_-\otimes\mathbb C$ act on $\slashed S^+$, resp. $\slashed S^-$, through $\mathfrak{sl}(\slashed S^\pm)$, and anti-self-dual 2-forms act as zero on $\slashed S^+$. (iv) The same statements hold for $Spin^c(4)$ acting through $\rho_\pm$ (see §8.3), with (ii) $Spin^c(4)$-equivariant when $U(1)$ acts trivially on $\mathbb H\otimes\mathbb C$; proof: Haydys omits all of these ("elementary exercise in representation theory"); prove by explicit bases: (i) the map $x\mapsto(v\mapsto xv)$ from $\operatorname{Im}\mathbb H$ to $\operatorname{End}_\mathbb C(\slashed S)$ (complex-linear because right multiplication by $i$ commutes with left multiplication) lands in traceless skew-Hermitian endomorphisms, is injective, and complexifies to an isomorphism by dimension count ($3\cdot2 = 6 = \dim_\mathbb R\mathfrak{sl}_2(\mathbb C)$); equivariance from $\alpha$'s definition; (ii) $v\mapsto(\psi\mapsto v\cdot\psi)$ is injective ($v\cdot v\cdot\psi = -|v|^2\psi$), real 4-dimensional into the real 8-dimensional $\operatorname{Hom}_\mathbb C(\mathbb H,\mathbb H)$, and its complex span is everything (dimension count plus injectivity of the complexification, shown by exhibiting that $i$ times the image intersects the image trivially); (iii) compute $e_ie_j$ on $\slashed S^\pm$ in the quaternionic model: on $\slashed S^+ = \mathbb H$ (first summand), $e_ie_j\cdot(v,0) = (-e_i\bar e_j v, 0)$ by two applications of (112); the writer computes the action of $\omega = \sum\omega_{ij}e_i\wedge e_j$ and shows it equals left multiplication by an imaginary quaternion depending only on $\omega_+$ (resp. right multiplication for $\slashed S^-$, depending on $\omega_-$), using the bases of $\Lambda^2_\pm$ from `Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions`; skew-Hermitian since left multiplication by imaginary quaternions is; then dimension count $3 = \dim\mathfrak{su}(2)$; spec: this page is the linear-algebra backbone of chapter XI (`Def - Seiberg-Witten Equations`, `Thm - The Origin is a Regular Value of the Perturbed Seiberg-Witten Map`, chiral Weitzenböck); mechanism: **$\mathfrak{so}(4) = \Lambda^2 = \Lambda^2_+\oplus\Lambda^2_-$ and each factor is the Lie algebra of one $Sp(1)$, acting on its own spinor half**; Bridges: `Def - Weyl Spinors (Left and Right Handed)` (SR XI) is the Lorentzian analogue.
- `Ex - The Clifford Algebras of R^1 and R^2` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-E4.1.1 (drill); prereqs: [Def - Clifford Algebra and Clifford Multiplication]; spec: verify $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^1)\cong\mathbb C$ and $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^2)\cong\mathbb H$ by writing down the isomorphisms and checking the relations; then show $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^2)$ with the opposite sign convention is $M_2(\mathbb R)$ (the point of the Convention callout).
- `Ex - The Exterior Algebra is a Faithful Clifford Module` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-E4.1.2 (drill); prereqs: [Def - Clifford Algebra and Clifford Multiplication, Def - Interior Product (Contraction with a Vector Field)]; spec: prove $(\iota_u - u^\flat\wedge)^2 = -|u|^2$ on $\Lambda U^*$; prove the induced map $\mathrm{Cl}(U)\to\operatorname{End}(\Lambda U^*)$ is injective (apply to $1\in\Lambda^0$ and use the symbol map $a\mapsto a\cdot1$ to show $\{e_I\}$ is linearly independent); deduce $\dim\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^n) = 2^n$.
- `Ex - The Kernel of the Double Cover of SO(4)` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (the "readily checked" kernel computation of A-T4.1.2, drilled); prereqs: [Thm - Spin Groups in Dimensions Three and Four via Quaternions]; spec: show directly that $q_+h\bar q_- = h$ for all $h\in\mathbb H$ forces $q_+ = q_- = \pm1$ (take $h = 1$, then $h$ imaginary); then show $\beta$ is surjective by computing $d\beta$ and using connectedness of $SO(4)$.
- `Ex - so(4) Splits as so(3) plus so(3) via Self-Dual Forms` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (Haydys's standing identification $\mathfrak{so}(4)\cong\Lambda^2(\mathbb R^4)^*\cong\mathfrak{so}(3)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(3)$, p. 35); prereqs: [Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions, Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms]; spec: identify $\mathfrak{so}(n)\cong\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$ via $A\mapsto\tfrac12\sum\langle Ae_i,e_j\rangle e_i\wedge e_j$ (an isomorphism of $SO(n)$-representations); show $\Lambda^2_\pm\mathbb R^4$ are Lie subalgebras (each isomorphic to $\mathfrak{so}(3)\cong\operatorname{Im}\mathbb H$) commuting with each other; conclude $\mathfrak{so}(4)\cong\mathfrak{so}(3)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(3)$ and identify the two factors with $d\beta$ of the two $Sp(1)$ factors.
- `Exercise Index - §8.1 Clifford Algebras and Spin Groups` — lists: [Ex - The Clifford Algebras of R^1 and R^2, Ex - The Exterior Algebra is a Faithful Clifford Module, Ex - The Kernel of the Double Cover of SO(4), Ex - so(4) Splits as so(3) plus so(3) via Self-Dual Forms]

### §8.2 Dirac Bundles and Dirac Operators
- `Def - Clifford Bundle and Bundle of Clifford Modules` — type: definition (compound: $\mathrm{Cl}(M)$ with its Levi-Civita connection; bundles of Clifford modules); source items: A-D4.2.1, A-D4.2.2 (first half); prereqs: [Def - Clifford Algebra and Clifford Multiplication, Def - Frame Bundle of a Vector Bundle, Def - Associated Bundle, Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles, Def - Levi-Civita Connection]; spec: $M$ oriented Riemannian, $\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}\to M$ the oriented orthonormal frame bundle (`[NEEDED FROM I–VI: Fr_SO with the Levi-Civita connection as a principal connection]`); the tautological $SO(n)$-action on $\mathbb R^n$ extends to algebra automorphisms of $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^n)$ (universal property — proved); $\mathrm{Cl}(M) := \operatorname{Fr}_{SO}\times_{SO(n)}\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^n)$ with fibre $\mathrm{Cl}(T_mM)\cong\mathrm{Cl}(T_m^*M)$, a bundle of algebras; the induced connection $\nabla^{LC}$ on $\mathrm{Cl}(M)$ is a derivation of the fibrewise product (proved: the structure constants are $SO(n)$-invariant); a *bundle of Clifford modules* is $E\to M$ with a bundle map $TM\otimes E\to E$, $(v,e)\mapsto v\cdot e$, $v\cdot(v\cdot e) = -|v|^2e$, equivalently an algebra-bundle homomorphism $\mathrm{Cl}(M)\to\operatorname{End}(E)$; examples: $\Lambda T^*M$ with $v\cdot\phi = v^\flat\wedge\phi - \iota_v\phi$ (sign fixed to match $v\cdot v = -|v|^2$; E4.1.2 globalised); the spinor bundle of §8.3; non-example: $TM$ itself with $v\cdot w := $ anything bilinear cannot satisfy the relation for $n\ge2$ unless … (the writer gives a concrete failing candidate).
- `Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator` — type: definition (anchor; compound: Dirac bundle (Haydys's three conditions), Dirac operator, the spin Dirac operator as the special case with $E = \slashed S$ forward-referenced to §8.3); source items: A-D4.2.2 (second half: the Dirac bundle conditions), A-D4.2.3; prereqs: [Def - Clifford Bundle and Bundle of Clifford Modules, Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle, Def - Metric-Compatible Connection, Def - Induced Connection on Tensor Bundles]; spec: a *Dirac bundle* is a bundle of Clifford modules $E$ with a Euclidean (or Hermitian) metric and a connection $\nabla$ such that (1) $\nabla$ is metric, (2) $\langle v\cdot e_1,v\cdot e_2\rangle = |v|^2\langle e_1,e_2\rangle$ (equivalently Clifford multiplication by a unit vector is orthogonal, equivalently $v\cdot$ is skew-adjoint — proved), (3) $\nabla(\phi\cdot s) = (\nabla^{LC}\phi)\cdot s + \phi\cdot\nabla s$ for $\phi\in\Gamma(\mathrm{Cl}(M))$, $s\in\Gamma(E)$; the *Dirac operator* $D := \mathrm{Cl}\circ\nabla:\Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(T^*M\otimes E)\to\Gamma(E)$, i.e. $Ds = \sum_ie_i\cdot\nabla_{e_i}s$ in a local orthonormal frame (independence of the frame proved: the contraction $T^*M\otimes E\to E$ is tensorial); the *spinor bundle* $\slashed S := P\times_{Spin(n),\rho}\slashed S$ over a spin manifold and the *spin Dirac operator* $\slashed D$ are named here and constructed in §8.3 (`Def - Spin Structure and Spin-c Structure`, `Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection`), so that this anchor page is the front door; first-order, formally self-adjoint (next page), elliptic (chapter IX, `Thm - Dirac Operators are Elliptic`); Convention callout: Haydys $D = \sum e_i\cdot\nabla_{e_i}$; Lawson–Michelsohn the same; some physics texts use $i\gamma^\mu\partial_\mu$ (Hermitian convention); the vault's `Def - Spin Connection and the Dirac Operator` defines $\slashed D$ via the spin connection in a frame — recorded as the same operator; examples: $\mathbb R^n$ with $E$ trivial and constant Clifford matrices ($D = \sum\gamma_i\partial_i$), verified conditions (1)–(3); non-example: $\Lambda T^*M$ with a non-metric connection fails (1).
- `Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint` — type: theorem; source items: A-X4.2.1 (the exercise's content is this page's proof); prereqs: [Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Def - Riemannian Volume Form, Thm - Cartan's Magic Formula]; statement: For a Dirac bundle $E$ over a Riemannian manifold $M$ and $s_1,s_2\in\Gamma(E)$ with $s_1$ or $s_2$ compactly supported (in particular for all $s_1,s_2$ when $M$ is closed): $\int_M\langle Ds_1,s_2\rangle\,\mathrm{vol} = \int_M\langle s_1,Ds_2\rangle\,\mathrm{vol}$; more precisely the writer proves the pointwise identity $\langle Ds_1,s_2\rangle - \langle s_1,Ds_2\rangle = \operatorname{div}(V)$ for the vector field $V$ defined by $\langle V,X\rangle := -\langle X\cdot s_1,s_2\rangle$ (sign verified on the page), then integrates; proof: local orthonormal frame, skew-adjointness of Clifford multiplication (condition (2)), metric compatibility (1), compatibility (3) with $\nabla^{LC}e_i$, the divergence formula $\operatorname{div}V = \sum_i\langle\nabla_{e_i}V,e_i\rangle$ and $\int\operatorname{div}V\,\mathrm{vol} = 0$ for compactly supported $V$ (Lemma 1 via $\operatorname{div}V\,\mathrm{vol} = d(\iota_V\mathrm{vol})$ and Stokes; same lemma as in VII); spec: mechanism: **Clifford multiplication is skew-adjoint and the connection is metric, so the only non-symmetric term is a divergence**; targets: $\langle\slashed D^+\dot\psi,\phi\rangle = \langle\dot\psi,\slashed D^-\phi\rangle$ in XI; Weitzenböck integration; ellipticity plus self-adjointness gives $\operatorname{coker}D\cong\ker D$ in IX.
- `Thm - The Hodge-de Rham Operator is a Dirac Operator` — type: theorem; source items: A-E4.2.1, A-I4.2.1 ([LM89, Thm 5.12]), A-I5.3.3 (the formula $d^* = (-1)^{n(j-1)+1}\star d\star$, established here via the vault's page), A-D5.3.6 (partial: the operator $d^*$ on forms — its definition is the vault's `Def - The Codifferential`); prereqs: [Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator, Def - Clifford Bundle and Bundle of Clifford Modules, Def - Exterior Derivative on a Manifold, Def - The Codifferential, Thm - Codifferential is the Adjoint of d, Def - Levi-Civita Connection, Def - Torsion Tensor, Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint]; statement: On an oriented Riemannian $n$-manifold, $E := \Lambda T^*M = \bigoplus_k\Lambda^kT^*M$ with Clifford multiplication $v\cdot\phi = v^\flat\wedge\phi - \iota_v\phi$, the induced metric, and the Levi-Civita connection is a Dirac bundle, and its Dirac operator is $D = d + d^*$ where $d^* = (-1)^{n(k-1)+1}\star d\star$ on $k$-forms is the codifferential; in particular $d = \sum_ie^i\wedge\nabla_{e_i}$ and $d^* = -\sum_i\iota_{e_i}\nabla_{e_i}$ in any local orthonormal frame; proof: Lawson–Michelsohn Thm II.5.12 (their Lemma 5.13 gives the two frame formulas): Lemma 1 (Dirac-bundle conditions: (1) LC is metric on forms, (2) $v^\flat\wedge$ and $\iota_v$ are mutually adjoint so $v\cdot$ is skew-adjoint, (3) $\nabla^{LC}$ commutes with $\wedge$ and $\iota$ — the derivation property of the induced connection on tensors, `Def - Induced Connection on Tensor Bundles`); Lemma 2 ($d = \sum e^i\wedge\nabla_{e_i}$: both sides are derivations agreeing on functions and on exact 1-forms; the exact-1-form case uses torsion-freeness $\nabla_XY - \nabla_YX = [X,Y]$); Lemma 3 ($\delta := -\sum\iota_{e_i}\nabla_{e_i}$ is a formal adjoint of $d$ — direct integration by parts with the previous page's divergence lemma; then $\delta = d^*$ because formal adjoints are unique (Lemma 4: two operators with $\langle Ls,t\rangle = \langle s,L^*t\rangle$ for all compactly supported $s$ agree — density/fundamental lemma) and the vault's `Thm - Codifferential is the Adjoint of d` proves $(-1)^{n(k-1)+1}\star d\star$ is a formal adjoint of $d$ (the writer checks the vault page's sign convention $(-1)^{n(k+1)+1}$ agrees — same parity); spec: sources: any Clifford module built from forms; targets: $d^++d^*$ on 4-manifolds (Atiyah complex, XI) is a Dirac operator restricted to a subbundle; the Hodge Laplacian $D^2 = dd^*+d^*d$ — Bridge to `Def - Hodge Laplacian`.
- `Ex - Clifford Multiplication is Skew-Adjoint on a Dirac Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill of condition (2)); prereqs: [Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator]; spec: prove $\langle v\cdot e_1,e_2\rangle = -\langle e_1,v\cdot e_2\rangle$ from $\langle v\cdot e_1,v\cdot e_2\rangle = |v|^2\langle e_1,e_2\rangle$ by polarisation in $v$ and the Clifford relation; conversely show skew-adjointness plus the Clifford relation implies (2).
- `Ex - Self-Adjointness of the Dirac Operator with Boundary Term` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X4.2.1 (the drill version on a manifold with boundary); prereqs: [Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint, Def - Manifold with Boundary and Induced Orientation]; spec: on a compact Riemannian manifold with boundary and outward normal $\nu$ prove $\int_M\langle Ds_1,s_2\rangle - \int_M\langle s_1,Ds_2\rangle = -\int_{\partial M}\langle\nu\cdot s_1,s_2\rangle$ (sign fixed by the writer); check on $[0,1]$ with $E$ trivial and $D = e\,d/dx$, $e^2 = -1$.
- `Ex - The Twisted Bundle of a Dirac Bundle is a Dirac Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (verification behind A-D4.3.4); prereqs: [Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator, Def - Operations on Vector Bundles and Pull-Back Bundles]; spec: for a Dirac bundle $E$ and a Hermitian bundle $F$ with unitary connection $\nabla^F$, show $E\otimes F$ with $v\cdot(e\otimes f) := (v\cdot e)\otimes f$, the product metric, and $\nabla^{E\otimes F} = \nabla^E\otimes1+1\otimes\nabla^F$ satisfies (1)–(3); compute $D_{E\otimes F}$ in a frame.
- `Ex - d plus d-star on Euclidean Space Squares to the Laplacian on Coefficients` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill toward §8.4); prereqs: [Thm - The Hodge-de Rham Operator is a Dirac Operator]; spec: on $\mathbb R^n$ with the flat metric show $(d+d^*)^2 = dd^*+d^*d = -\sum_i\partial_i^2$ acting coefficientwise on $\Omega^k(\mathbb R^n)$; use the frame formulas and $[\iota_{e_i},e^j\wedge] = \delta_{ij}$.
- `Exercise Index - §8.2 Dirac Bundles and Dirac Operators` — lists: [Ex - Clifford Multiplication is Skew-Adjoint on a Dirac Bundle, Ex - Self-Adjointness of the Dirac Operator with Boundary Term, Ex - The Twisted Bundle of a Dirac Bundle is a Dirac Bundle, Ex - d plus d-star on Euclidean Space Squares to the Laplacian on Coefficients]

### §8.3 Spin and Spin-c Structures
- `Def - Spin Structure and Spin-c Structure` — type: definition (anchor; compound: spinnable manifold, spin structure, spin manifold; $Spin^c(n)$ with $\rho_0$, $\rho_{\det}$ and the exact sequence (120); spin$^c$ structure; determinant line bundle $L_{\det}$ and $P_{\det}$); source items: A-D4.3.1, A-D4.3.5, A-D4.3.6, A-D4.3.7, A-R4.3.1 (→ inside, Relate: Stiefel–Whitney criterion as bold context, A-I4.3.2), A-R4.3.6, A-T4.3.1 and A-I4.3.3 (→ inside, as a `[PROPOSED IMPORT]` callout — see below), A-R4.3.4 (→ inside, Motivation: "neither $\slashed S$ nor $L_0$ need exist globally, but the product does"); prereqs: [Def - Spin Group and the Double Cover of SO(n), Thm - Spin(n) inside the Clifford Algebra is a Double Cover of SO(n), Def - Principal G-Bundle, Def - Reduction and Extension of the Structure Group, Def - Associated Bundle, Def - Spin Structure on a Manifold, Def - Equivariant Map]; spec: $M$ oriented Riemannian $n$-manifold, $\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}$; $Spin(n)$ acts on $\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}$ through $\xi$; $M$ is *spinnable* if there is a principal $Spin(n)$-bundle $P$ with a $Spin(n)$-equivariant map $\tau:P\to\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}$ covering $\mathrm{id}_M$ that is a fibrewise double covering; $P$ (with $\tau$) is a *spin structure*, $M$ with a chosen one is a *spin manifold*; $Spin^c(n) := Spin(n)\times U(1)/\{\pm1\}$ ($\{\pm1\}$ diagonal; $\{\pm1\}$ central in $Spin(n)$ by the double-cover page), containing $Spin(n)$ and the central $U(1)$; $\rho_0([g,z]) := \xi(g)$, $\rho_{\det}([g,z]) := z^2$; the exact sequences $1\to U(1)\to Spin^c(n)\to SO(n)\to1$, $1\to Spin(n)\to Spin^c(n)\to U(1)\to1$, and (120) $1\to\{\pm1\}\to Spin^c(n)\xrightarrow{(\rho_0,\rho_{\det})}SO(n)\times U(1)\to1$ (exactness proved clause by clause); a *spin$^c$ structure* is a principal $Spin^c(n)$-bundle $P\to M$ with a $Spin^c(n)$-equivariant map $P\to\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}$ inducing $P/U(1)\cong\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}$ (`[NEEDED FROM I–VI: quotient P/H]`); $L_{\det} := P\times_{\rho_{\det}}\mathbb C$, $P_{\det} := P/Spin(n)$, so $\tau:P\to P/\{\pm1\} = \operatorname{Fr}_{SO}\times_MP_{\det}$ is a double cover; isomorphism of spin$^c$ structures = bundle isomorphism covering $\mathrm{id}_{\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}}$; the set $\mathcal S(M)$ of isomorphism classes; **`[PROPOSED IMPORT]` callout:** "Every closed oriented 4-manifold admits a spin$^c$ structure (Hirzebruch–Hopf 1958); every closed oriented 3-manifold is spin (Stiefel 1935). Source: Morgan, *The Seiberg–Witten Equations*, Lemma 3.1.2 and Gompf–Stipsicz Prop. 2.4.16 with Wu's formula (Milnor–Stasheff §11). Architecture: $w_2(M)$ has an integral lift because it is characteristic for the mod-2 intersection form (Wu's formula $v_2 = w_2$ for oriented $M$) and characteristic elements of unimodular forms lift (chapter XIII, `Def - Characteristic Element of a Unimodular Form`, `Thm - Van der Blij Lemma`); the obstruction to a spin$^c$ structure is the image of $w_2$ under the Bockstein $H^2(M;\mathbb Z/2)\to H^3(M;\mathbb Z)$. Why imported: Wu's formula requires Steenrod squares and the Thom isomorphism, neither in the vault; **nothing in chapters VIII–XI depends on this statement — every theorem takes a spin$^c$ structure as given** (Haydys's standing assumption). Orchestrator: approve as an import, or delete the callout and keep the fact as bold plain text."; examples: $\mathbb R^n$ (trivial spin structure), $S^n$ (the writer reads the vault's `Ex - Spin Structure on the Sphere S^n` and links it only if its solution is complete; otherwise the spin structure $Spin(n+1)\to Spin(n+1)/Spin(n) = S^n$ is recorded as bold context), a spin manifold's induced spin$^c$ structure (E4.3.3, made an exercise); $\mathbb{CP}^2$ is spin$^c$ but not spin (bold context, since $w_2\ne0$ needs SW classes); Convention callout: the vault's `Def - Spin Structure on a Manifold` is the same notion; some authors (Lawson–Michelsohn) define $Spin^c(n) = Spin(n)\times_{\mathbb Z/2}U(1)$ — identical.
- `Thm - Spin-c(3) is U(2) and Spin-c(4) is the Fibre Product of Two Copies of U(2)` — type: theorem; source items: A-T4.3.3, A-E4.3.2; prereqs: [Def - Spin Structure and Spin-c Structure, Thm - Spin Groups in Dimensions Three and Four via Quaternions]; statement: (a) $Spin^c(3) = SU(2)\times U(1)/\{\pm1\}\cong U(2)$ via $[A,z]\mapsto zA$; under this, $\rho_{\det}(A) = \det A$ and $\rho_0:U(2)\to SO(3) = PU(2)$ is the natural projection composed with $\alpha$; (120) reads $1\to\{\pm1\}\to U(2)\to SO(3)\times U(1)\to1$. (b) $Spin^c(4) = (SU(2)\times SU(2))\times U(1)/\{\pm1\}\cong\{(A_+,A_-)\in U(2)\times U(2) : \det A_+ = \det A_-\}$ via $[(A_+,A_-),z]\mapsto(zA_+,zA_-)$, with $\rho_{\det}(A_+,A_-) = \det A_\pm$ and $\rho_\pm(A_+,A_-) := A_\pm$ homomorphisms $Spin^c(4)\to U(2)$; the spinor representations $\slashed S^\pm$ of $Spin^c(4)$ are $\mathbb C^2$ via $\rho_\pm$; proof: Haydys omits; write $Sp(1) = SU(2)$ (via `Def - SU(2) Action on Spinors` / the quaternionic model with right complex structure), check the maps are well-defined homomorphisms, injective (kernel computation), surjective (polar-type decomposition $B = zA$ with $A\in SU(2)$, $z^2 = \det B$); spec: needed for `Def - Seiberg-Witten Equations`; mechanism: **$U(2)$ is $SU(2)$ times a circle modulo the shared $-1$**.
- `Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection` — type: definition (anchor; compound: Levi-Civita connection on a spin structure; spinor bundle and spin Dirac operator; twisted spinor bundle $\slashed S\otimes E$ and $\slashed D_A$; spin$^c$ connection $\tau^*(\omega+A)$, spin$^c$ spinor bundle and spin$^c$ Dirac operator $\slashed D_A$; chirality in dimension 4); source items: A-D4.3.2, A-D4.3.3, A-D4.3.4, A-D4.3.8, A-R4.3.2, A-R4.3.5 (→ inside, Axiom Motivation: why the factor $\tfrac12$); prereqs: [Def - Spin Structure and Spin-c Structure, Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator, Thm - Low-Dimensional Clifford Algebras and the Quaternionic Spinor Modules, Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms, Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle, Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles, Def - Associated Bundle]; spec: (a) for a spin structure $\tau:P\to\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}$ and the Levi-Civita connection form $\omega\in\Omega^1(\operatorname{Fr}_{SO};\mathfrak{so}(n))$, $\tau^*\omega\in\Omega^1(P;\mathfrak{spin}(n))$ (via $d\xi^{-1}:\mathfrak{so}(n)\to\mathfrak{spin}(n)$) is a connection on $P$ (both axioms verified using $\tau$ equivariant and $\xi$ a local diffeomorphism); (b) fix a complex Hermitian $\mathrm{Cl}(\mathbb R^n)$-module $\slashed S$ with the restricted $Spin(n)$-action $\rho$ (for $n = 3,4$ the quaternionic modules; general $n$: any irreducible one, existence/uniqueness recorded as bold context with reference LM I.5); $\slashed S := P\times_{Spin(n),\rho}\slashed S$ is a bundle of Clifford modules (the Clifford multiplication descends because $\rho(g)(v\cdot s) = \xi(g)v\cdot\rho(g)s$ — proved from $\rho(g) = g\cdot$ in the Clifford algebra and $\xi(g)v = gvg^{-1}$), and with the induced connection it is a Dirac bundle (conditions (1)–(3) verified: (3) because the connection on $\mathrm{Cl}(M)$ and on $\slashed S$ are both induced from $\tau^*\omega$ and the module structure is $Spin(n)$-equivariant); $\slashed D$ its Dirac operator; in dimension 4, $\slashed S = \slashed S^+\oplus\slashed S^-$, Clifford multiplication by 1-forms is odd, $\slashed D = \begin{pmatrix}0&\slashed D^-\\\slashed D^+&0\end{pmatrix}$ (R4.3.2); (c) twisting: for a principal $G$-bundle $Q$ with connection $A$ and a unitary representation $G\to U(k)$, $E = Q\times_G\mathbb C^k$, $\slashed S\otimes E$ is a Dirac bundle (exercise of §8.2) with Dirac operator $\slashed D_A$; (d) spin$^c$: for a spin$^c$ structure $P$ and a connection $A$ on $P_{\det}$ (equivalently a unitary connection on $L_{\det}$), $\tau^*(\omega+A)\in\Omega^1(P;\mathfrak{spin}^c(n))$ with $\mathfrak{spin}^c(n)\cong\mathfrak{so}(n)\oplus\mathfrak u(1)$ is a connection on $P$; $\slashed S$ becomes a $Spin^c(n)$-module by $[g,z]\cdot s := z\rho(g)s$ (well-defined because $\rho(-1) = -1$); the spin$^c$ spinor bundle $\slashed S := P\times_{Spin^c(n)}\slashed S$ is a Dirac bundle and $\slashed D_A$ is the spin$^c$ Dirac operator; (e) R4.3.5: heuristically $\slashed S = \slashed S_0\otimes L_0$ with $L_{\det} = L_0^2$, so a connection $A_0$ on $L_0$ induces $A$ on $L_{\det}$ and $A_0+a_0$ corresponds to $A+2a_0$ — the origin of the $\tfrac12$ in `Thm - Variation of the Twisted and Spin-c Dirac Operators with the Connection` and of the $2g^{-1}dg$ in chapter XI; Convention callout: the vault's `Def - Spin Connection and the Dirac Operator` in frames; examples: $\mathbb R^3$, $\mathbb R^4$ with the trivial spin structure — the spinor bundle is the product bundle and $\slashed D$ has the explicit quaternionic form (proved in §8.4); E4.3.3 (spin manifold with a $U(1)$-bundle $P_0$: $P := P_{Spin}\times_MP_0/\{\pm1\}$ is a spin$^c$ structure with spinor bundle $\slashed S_0\otimes L_0$ and $\slashed D_A$ the twisted Dirac operator — made an exercise below; the verification is also recorded in Examples).
- `Thm - Variation of the Twisted and Spin-c Dirac Operators with the Connection` — type: theorem (two parts; the exercises X4.3.1, X4.3.2 are its content); source items: A-X4.3.1, A-X4.3.2 ((127)); prereqs: [Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection, Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space, Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles]; statement: (i) For a twisted Dirac operator $\slashed D_A$ on $\slashed S\otimes E$ and $a\in\Omega^1(M;\operatorname{ad}Q)$: $\slashed D_{A+a}\psi = \slashed D_A\psi + a\cdot\psi$, where $a\cdot\psi$ is the twisted Clifford multiplication $T^*M\otimes\operatorname{ad}Q\to\operatorname{End}(\slashed S)\otimes\operatorname{End}(E)$. (ii) For the spin$^c$ Dirac operator and $a\in\Omega^1(M;i\mathbb R)$: $\slashed D_{A+a}\psi = \slashed D_A\psi + \tfrac12a\cdot\psi$; proof: (i) the covariant derivative on $\slashed S\otimes E$ changes by $1\otimes a$ (associated-bundle anchor), and $\mathrm{Cl}\circ(1\otimes a) = a\cdot$; (ii) the connection $\tau^*(\omega+A+a)$ on $P$ differs from $\tau^*(\omega+A)$ by $\tau^*a$, and under $\mathfrak{spin}^c(n)\cong\mathfrak{so}(n)\oplus\mathfrak u(1)$ the $\mathfrak u(1)$-summand acts on $\slashed S$ through $d\rho_{\det}$-compatible scaling: $[g,z]\cdot s = z\rho(g)s$ and $\rho_{\det}[g,z] = z^2$, so the infinitesimal generator $i\theta\in\mathfrak u(1)$ of $P_{\det}$ acts on $\slashed S$ as $\tfrac12i\theta$ — this is the whole content: the $U(1)$ of $P_{\det}$ is the square of the central $U(1)$ of $Spin^c$; spec: load-bearing for XI (`Thm - Differential of the Seiberg-Witten Map`, `Thm - Equivariance of the Seiberg-Witten Map`); mechanism: **the connection enters the Dirac operator linearly through the Clifford contraction, and the determinant line sees the central circle with weight two**.
- `Thm - Classification of Spin-c Structures` — type: theorem (anchor); source items: A-D4.3.9, A-T4.3.2, A-I4.3.5, A-E4.3.3 (→ the spin-manifold case as Corollary 1); prereqs: [Def - Spin Structure and Spin-c Structure, Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class, Def - Transition Functions and the Cocycle Condition, Thm - Principal Bundles are Classified by Cocycles]; statement: Let $M$ be an oriented Riemannian manifold admitting a spin$^c$ structure. (i) For a spin$^c$ structure $P$ and a principal $U(1)$-bundle $Q$, $P\cdot Q := (P\times_MQ)/U(1)$ where $U(1)$ acts freely by $(p,q)\cdot w := (p\cdot[1,w]^{-1}, q\cdot w)$, with the residual $Spin^c(n)$-action on the first factor (well-definedness and freeness proved on the page) is a spin$^c$ structure with spinor bundle $\slashed S_P\otimes L_Q$ and $L_{\det}(P\cdot Q) = L_{\det}(P)\otimes L_Q^2$; this defines an action of the group of isomorphism classes of $U(1)$-bundles, i.e. of $H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$ (by the chapter III anchor), on $\mathcal S(M)$. (ii) The action is free and transitive: $\mathcal S(M)$ is an $H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$-torsor; in particular $\mathcal S(M)\cong H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$ non-canonically, and canonically once a base point is chosen. (iii) If $M$ is spin with spin structure $P_{Spin}$, then $P_{Spin}\times_M(M\times U(1))/\{\pm1\}$ is a spin$^c$ structure with $L_{\det}$ trivial (the origin), and every spin$^c$ structure is $P_{Spin}\times_MQ/\{\pm1\}$ for a $U(1)$-bundle $Q$ (E4.3.3), with $c_1(L_{\det}) = 2c_1(Q)$; proof: Haydys: "can be shown"; follow Gompf–Stipsicz, *4-Manifolds and Kirby Calculus*, Prop. 2.4.16 / Morgan, *The Seiberg–Witten Equations*, §3.1: Lemma 1 (the homomorphism $\phi:Spin^c(n)\times_{SO(n)}Spin^c(n)\to U(1)$, $\phi([g,z],[\epsilon g,z']) := \epsilon z^{-1}z'$, is well defined with kernel the diagonal); Lemma 2 (transitivity: for spin$^c$ structures $P_1,P_2$ over the same $\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}$, $Q := (P_1\times_{\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}}P_2)\times_\phi U(1)$ is a $U(1)$-bundle and $P_1\cdot Q\cong P_2$ — constructed explicitly); Lemma 3 (freeness: $P\cdot Q\cong P$ implies $Q$ has a section, hence is trivial, using `Thm - Sections of a Principal Bundle and Triviality`); (iii) direct; the identification with $H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$ via the chapter III anchor; spec: mechanism: **two lifts of the same $SO(n)$-bundle to $Spin^c(n)$ differ by a $U(1)$-bundle because $Spin^c(n)\times_{SO(n)}Spin^c(n)$ is $Spin^c(n)\times U(1)$**; targets: the Seiberg–Witten invariant as a function on $H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$; finiteness (XI).
- `Ex - Spin-c(3) is U(2)` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-E4.3.2 (a) (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Spin-c(3) is U(2) and Spin-c(4) is the Fibre Product of Two Copies of U(2)]; spec: verify the isomorphism $[A,z]\mapsto zA$ in detail and compute $\rho_0,\rho_{\det}$ on $\operatorname{diag}(e^{i\theta},e^{i\theta})$ and on $\operatorname{diag}(e^{i\theta},e^{-i\theta})$.
- `Ex - Spin-c(4) inside U(2) x U(2)` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-E4.3.2 (b) (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Spin-c(3) is U(2) and Spin-c(4) is the Fibre Product of Two Copies of U(2)]; spec: verify the description $\{(A_+,A_-) : \det A_+ = \det A_-\}$; show $\rho_\pm$ are surjective with kernel $\cong SU(2)$; describe the spinor representations $\slashed S^\pm = \mathbb C^2$ and $\Lambda^2\slashed S^\pm = \rho_{\det}$.
- `Ex - Spin-c Structures on a Spin Manifold` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-E4.3.3 (drill; the theorem is Corollary 1 of the classification page); prereqs: [Thm - Classification of Spin-c Structures, Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection]; spec: verify that $P := P_{Spin}\times_MP_0/\{\pm1\}$ is a principal $Spin^c(n)$-bundle with $P/U(1)\cong\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}$; identify its spinor bundle with $\slashed S_0\otimes L_0$ and $L_{\det}$ with $L_0^2$; show the spin$^c$ Dirac operator for the connection induced by $A_0$ on $L_0$ is the twisted Dirac operator $\slashed D_{A_0}$.
- `Ex - Twisted Spinors in Dimension Three are Complexified Forms` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: A-E4.3.1, A-I4.3.6; prereqs: [Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms, Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection, Thm - The Hodge-de Rham Operator is a Dirac Operator]; spec: for $M^3$ spin and $E = \slashed S$ with the Levi-Civita connection, show $\slashed S\otimes_\mathbb C\slashed S = \operatorname{Sym}^2\slashed S\oplus\Lambda^2\slashed S\cong T^*_\mathbb CM\oplus\mathbb C$ as bundles with connection (route: $\slashed S\cong\slashed S^*$ via the $Spin(3)$-invariant symplectic form, so $\slashed S\otimes\slashed S\cong\operatorname{End}(\slashed S) = \operatorname{End}_0\oplus\mathbb C$, and (111)); show the twisted Clifford multiplication corresponds under this isomorphism to the form-Clifford multiplication of `Thm - The Hodge-de Rham Operator is a Dirac Operator` composed with $\star$ (odd forms ↔ even forms), and conclude that the twisted Dirac operator on $\Gamma(\slashed S\otimes\slashed S)$ is the complexification of $d+d^*:\Omega^{\mathrm{odd}}\to\Omega^{\mathrm{even}}$ up to the isomorphism (all connections are induced from the Levi-Civita connection on $\operatorname{Fr}_{SO}$ through $Spin(3)$-equivariant maps, so they correspond); the writer must fix all signs explicitly.
- `Exercise Index - §8.3 Spin and Spin-c Structures` — lists: [Ex - Spin-c(3) is U(2), Ex - Spin-c(4) inside U(2) x U(2), Ex - Spin-c Structures on a Spin Manifold, Ex - Twisted Spinors in Dimension Three are Complexified Forms]

### §8.4 The Weitzenböck Formula
- `Thm - The Dirac Operator on Flat Space Squares to the Laplacian` — type: theorem; source items: A-T4.4.1 ((128)), A-E4.4.1, A-R4.4.1; prereqs: [Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection, Thm - Low-Dimensional Clifford Algebras and the Quaternionic Spinor Modules]; statement: (i) On $\mathbb R^4 = \mathbb H$ with the trivial spin structure, $\slashed S^\pm = \mathbb R^4\times\mathbb H$ and for $u:\mathbb R^4\to\mathbb H$: $\slashed D^+u = \partial_{x_0}u + i\partial_{x_1}u + j\partial_{x_2}u + k\partial_{x_3}u$, $\slashed D^-u = -\partial_{x_0}u + i\partial_{x_1}u + j\partial_{x_2}u + k\partial_{x_3}u$ (signs from the module (112) with $e_0 = 1$; the writer derives them), and $\slashed D^-\slashed D^+ = \slashed D^+\slashed D^- = \Delta := -\sum_{i=0}^3\partial_{x_i}^2$ (coefficientwise), so $\slashed D^2 = \Delta$. (ii) On $\mathbb R^3$ with $\slashed S = \mathbb R^3\times\mathbb H$: $\slashed D u = i\partial_{x_1}u + j\partial_{x_2}u + k\partial_{x_3}u$ and $\slashed D^2 = \Delta$. (iii) On $\mathbb R^2$: $\slashed D = \begin{pmatrix}0&2\partial_z\\-2\partial_{\bar z}&0\end{pmatrix}$ on $C^\infty(\mathbb R^2;\mathbb C^2)$ and $\slashed D^2 = \Delta$ (dimension 2 treated directly since $Spin(2) = U(1)$; the analogy $\slashed D^\pm\leftrightarrow\bar\partial,\partial$); proof: Haydys: "straightforward computation" — write it out: the trivial spin structure has $\tau^*\omega = 0$, so $\nabla = d$ on the product bundle; expand $\slashed D^-\slashed D^+$ and use $i^2 = j^2 = k^2 = -1$ and anticommutation to kill the mixed terms; spec: mechanism: **the Dirac operator is a square root of the Laplacian because the Clifford relations are exactly the relations needed for the cross terms to cancel**; Bridges: `Thm - Dirac Equation Squares to Klein-Gordon` (Spinors) is the Lorentzian version.
- `Thm - Existence of Synchronous Orthonormal Frames` — type: theorem; source items: A-I4.4.1; prereqs: [Def - Metric-Compatible Connection, Def - Connection Matrix and Local Form of a Connection, Def - Local Frame]; statement: Let $E$ be a Euclidean vector bundle with a metric connection $\nabla$ over $M$ and $m\in M$. There is a local orthonormal frame $(e_i)$ near $m$ with $(\nabla e_i)(m) = 0$ for all $i$. In particular on a Riemannian manifold there is an orthonormal frame of $TM$ near $m$ with $\nabla^{LC}e_i = 0$ at $m$, hence $[e_i,e_j](m) = 0$; proof: take any orthonormal frame $\tilde e$ near $m$ with connection matrix $\tilde A = \sum_k\tilde A_kdx^k$ in coordinates $x$ centred at $m$ ($\tilde A_k(x)$ skew-symmetric by metric compatibility); set $g(x) := \exp(-\sum_kx^k\tilde A_k(m))\in SO(r)$ and $e := \tilde e\,g$; then $A(e) = g^{-1}\tilde Ag + g^{-1}dg$ (anchor `Thm - Gauge Action on Connection Matrices` / the change-of-frame formula) equals $\tilde A(m) - \tilde A(m) = 0$ at $m$; torsion-freeness gives $[e_i,e_j] = \nabla_{e_i}e_j - \nabla_{e_j}e_i = 0$ at $m$; spec: technical lemma reused by Weitzenböck, Lichnerowicz, the connection Laplacian formula and chapter XI's Bochner identity; alternative (recorded, not used): parallel transport along radial geodesics in normal coordinates (`Def - Normal Coordinates and Geodesic Coordinates`).
- `Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian` — type: theorem (three parts; X4.4.1(a)–(c) are its content); source items: A-D4.4.1 (→ inside, Notation/Statement: $\nabla^*\nabla := \nabla^*\circ\nabla$ with $\nabla^* = -\star d^\nabla\star:\Omega^1(E)\to\Gamma(E)$), A-X4.4.1; prereqs: [Def - Exterior Covariant Derivative on a Vector Bundle, Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature, Thm - Existence of Synchronous Orthonormal Frames, Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint, Thm - Codifferential is the Adjoint of d]; statement: For a Euclidean bundle $E$ with metric connection $\nabla$ over an oriented Riemannian manifold, define $\nabla^* := -\star d^\nabla\star:\Omega^1(M;E)\to\Gamma(E)$ and $\nabla^*\nabla := \nabla^*\circ\nabla$. (a) In a local orthonormal frame $(e_i)$ of $TM$: $\nabla^*\nabla s = -\sum_i(\nabla_{e_i}\nabla_{e_i}s - \nabla_{\nabla^{LC}_{e_i}e_i}s)$; in a synchronous frame at $m$, $(\nabla^*\nabla s)(m) = -\sum_i\nabla_{e_i}\nabla_{e_i}s(m)$. (b) $\nabla^*$ is a formal adjoint of $\nabla$: $\int\langle\nabla s,\alpha\rangle = \int\langle s,\nabla^*\alpha\rangle$ for compactly supported $s$; hence $\nabla^*\nabla$ is formally self-adjoint and non-negative. (c) For $M$ closed, $\langle\nabla^*\nabla s,s\rangle_{L^2} = \|\nabla s\|_{L^2}^2$; proof: (b) first: $\star d^\nabla\star$ on $E$-valued 1-forms is computed as $-\sum\iota_{e_i}\nabla_{e_i}$ in a frame (Lemma 1, the $E$-valued version of Lemma 3 of `Thm - The Hodge-de Rham Operator is a Dirac Operator`, proved by the same divergence argument), giving (a) and (b) together; (c) from (b); the signs: $\Delta = \nabla^*\nabla\ge0$ (Haydys's non-negative convention); spec: load-bearing in XI (Bochner identity, $C^0$ bound); mechanism: **the connection Laplacian is minus the trace of the second covariant derivative, and integration by parts makes it the square of $\nabla$**.
- `Def - Curvature Endomorphism of a Dirac Bundle` — type: definition; source items: A-D4.4.2; prereqs: [Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator, Def - Curvature of a Vector-Bundle Connection]; spec: for a Dirac bundle $E$ with curvature $R\in\Omega^2(\operatorname{End}E)$, $\mathcal R(s) := \tfrac12\sum_{i,j}e_i\cdot e_j\cdot R_{e_i,e_j}(s)$ in a local orthonormal frame — the image of $R$ under the Clifford-multiplication map $\Lambda^2T^*M\otimes\operatorname{End}(E)\to\operatorname{End}(E)$; frame-independence proved (it is the composition of tensorial maps); $\mathcal R$ is self-adjoint (the writer proves $\langle\mathcal Rs,t\rangle = \langle s,\mathcal Rt\rangle$ using condition (3) and antisymmetry in $i,j$); examples: flat bundle ($\mathcal R = 0$); $\Lambda T^*M$ (computed on 1-forms in the exercise below); non-example: $\sum_{i,j}e_ie_jR_{e_i,e_j}$ without the $\tfrac12$ is twice $\mathcal R$ — a normalisation, not a different object; Convention callout: Lawson–Michelsohn write $\mathcal R = \tfrac12\sum e_ie_jR_{e_i,e_j}$ — same.
- `Thm - Weitzenbock Formula for the Dirac Operator` — type: theorem (anchor); source items: A-T4.4.2 (Theorem 132), A-R4.4.2; prereqs: [Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator, Def - Curvature Endomorphism of a Dirac Bundle, Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian, Thm - Existence of Synchronous Orthonormal Frames, Def - Curvature of a Vector-Bundle Connection]; statement: For a Dirac bundle $E$ with Dirac operator $D$: $D^2 = \nabla^*\nabla + \mathcal R$; proof: Haydys's proof with all four gaps filled: fix $m$ and a synchronous orthonormal frame at $m$ (previous theorem); at $m$: $D^2s = \sum_ie_i\cdot\nabla_{e_i}(\sum_je_j\cdot\nabla_{e_j}s) = \sum_{i,j}e_i\cdot e_j\cdot\nabla_{e_i}\nabla_{e_j}s$ (condition (3) and $\nabla^{LC}_{e_i}e_j(m) = 0$) $= \sum_ie_i\cdot e_i\cdot\nabla_{e_i}\nabla_{e_i}s + \tfrac12\sum_{i\ne j}e_i\cdot e_j\cdot(\nabla_{e_i}\nabla_{e_j}-\nabla_{e_j}\nabla_{e_i})s$ (anticommutation; symmetrising the $i\ne j$ sum) $= -\sum_i\nabla_{e_i}\nabla_{e_i}s + \tfrac12\sum_{i\ne j}e_ie_jR_{e_i,e_j}s$ ($e_i^2 = -1$; $[\nabla_{e_i},\nabla_{e_j}] = R_{e_i,e_j} + \nabla_{[e_i,e_j]}$ and $[e_i,e_j](m) = 0$) $= \nabla^*\nabla s + \mathcal Rs$ at $m$ (part (a) of the connection-Laplacian page in a synchronous frame; the $i = j$ terms of $\mathcal R$ vanish); both sides are frame-independent so the identity holds everywhere; spec: mechanism: **the Dirac operator squared is the connection Laplacian plus a zero-order curvature term because the Clifford relations turn the symmetric part of $\nabla^2$ into a trace and the antisymmetric part into curvature**; Bridges: Bochner's method (`Thm - Bochner's Theorem`, Hodge I, sketched — the exercise below gives a complete proof of the 1-form case); Cross-Field: Kato's inequality, Bochner vanishing theorems.
- `Thm - The Lie Algebra of the Spin Group and the Curvature of the Spinor Connection` — type: theorem; source items: (the computation Haydys's Corollary 133 requires; no separate source item — feeds A-T4.4.3); prereqs: [Thm - Spin(n) inside the Clifford Algebra is a Double Cover of SO(n), Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection, Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles, Def - Riemann Curvature Tensor]; statement: (i) The inverse of $d\xi:\mathfrak{spin}(n)\to\mathfrak{so}(n)$ is $A\mapsto\tfrac14\sum_{i,j}\langle Ae_i,e_j\rangle e_ie_j = \tfrac12\sum_{i<j}\langle Ae_i,e_j\rangle e_ie_j$, and $d\rho$ of this element acts on $\slashed S$ by Clifford multiplication. (ii) For a spin structure with the Levi-Civita connection, the curvature of $\slashed S$ is $R^{\slashed S}_{X,Y}s = \tfrac14\sum_{i,j}\langle R_{X,Y}e_i,e_j\rangle e_i\cdot e_j\cdot s$, $R$ the Riemann tensor. (iii) For a spin$^c$ structure with connection $A$ on $P_{\det}$ with curvature $F_A\in\Omega^2(M;i\mathbb R)$: $R^{\slashed S}_{X,Y}s = \tfrac14\sum_{i,j}\langle R_{X,Y}e_i,e_j\rangle e_ie_j\cdot s + \tfrac12F_A(X,Y)s$; proof: (i) by computing $d\xi(e_ie_j)$ on basis vectors ($d\xi(e_ie_j)e_k = e_ie_je_k - e_ke_ie_j = 2(\delta_{jk}e_i - \delta_{ik}e_j)$, i.e. $2$ times the elementary skew matrix); (ii) the curvature of an associated bundle is $d\rho\circ\Omega$ (anchor / `[NEEDED FROM I–VI]`), $\Omega$ of $\tau^*\omega$ is $\tau^*$ of the Levi-Civita curvature form, whose value on $X,Y$ is the skew endomorphism $R_{X,Y}$ (RG I `Thm - Cartan's Second Structural Equation` gives the dictionary between the curvature form and the Riemann tensor — verify sign conventions); (iii) the $\mathfrak u(1)$ component with weight $\tfrac12$ as in the variation theorem; spec: Lawson–Michelsohn Thm II.4.15; mechanism: the spinor connection is the Levi-Civita connection read through the Lie algebra isomorphism $\mathfrak{so}(n)\cong\mathfrak{spin}(n)\subset\mathrm{Cl}$.
- `Thm - Lichnerowicz Formula for the Spin-c Dirac Operator` — type: theorem; source items: A-T4.4.3 (Corollary 133), A-X4.4.2, A-I4.4.3; prereqs: [Thm - Weitzenbock Formula for the Dirac Operator, Thm - The Lie Algebra of the Spin Group and the Curvature of the Spinor Connection, Thm - Symmetries of the Riemann Tensor, Thm - First and Second Bianchi Identities, Def - Ricci Tensor, Def - Scalar Curvature]; statement: For the spin$^c$ Dirac operator with connection $A$ on $L_{\det}$: $\slashed D_A^2\psi = \nabla_A^*\nabla_A\psi + \tfrac14s_g\psi + \tfrac12F_A\cdot\psi$, where $s_g$ is the scalar curvature and $F_A\cdot$ is Clifford multiplication by the 2-form $F_A$ ($F_A\cdot\psi = \tfrac12\sum_{i,j}F_A(e_i,e_j)e_ie_j\cdot\psi$ — normalisation stated); for a spin manifold and the pure spinor bundle, $\slashed D^2 = \nabla^*\nabla + \tfrac14s_g$; proof: Haydys leaves it as an exercise; follow Lawson–Michelsohn Thm II.8.8: $\mathcal R = \tfrac12\sum_{i,j}e_ie_jR^{\slashed S}_{e_i,e_j} = \tfrac18\sum_{i,j,k,l}R_{ijkl}e_ie_je_ke_l + \tfrac14\sum_{i,j}F_A(e_i,e_j)e_ie_j$; Lemma 1: $\sum_{j,k,l}R_{ijkl}e_je_ke_l = 2\sum_l\operatorname{Ric}_{il}e_l$ up to the sign fixed by the curvature convention — the writer carries out the standard reduction: split the sum over $(j,k,l)$ into the totally antisymmetric part (which vanishes by the first Bianchi identity, `Thm - First and Second Bianchi Identities`) and the remainder (which contracts to the Ricci tensor via $e_je_k + e_ke_j = -2\delta_{jk}$ and the symmetries $R_{ijkl} = -R_{jikl} = R_{klij}$, `Thm - Symmetries of the Riemann Tensor`), obtaining $\tfrac18\sum R_{ijkl}e_ie_je_ke_l = \tfrac14s_g$ (with the sign convention for $R$ fixed so that $s_g>0$ on spheres — the writer states the convention of RG III and checks on $S^2$); the $F_A$ term is $\tfrac12F_A\cdot$ by the normalisation; spec: replaces the flagged `Thm - Lichnerowicz Formula`; mechanism: **the Riemann tensor contracted against three Clifford generators collapses to Ricci by the Bianchi identity, and Ricci contracted against the fourth collapses to scalar curvature**.
- `Thm - Clifford Action of 2-Forms and the Chiral Weitzenbock Formula in Dimension Four` — type: theorem; source items: A-T4.4.5, A-R4.4.3, A-I4.4.2 (the representation-theoretic input is `Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms` (iii)); prereqs: [Thm - Lichnerowicz Formula for the Spin-c Dirac Operator, Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms, Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint, Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Forms]; statement: On a 4-manifold with spin$^c$ structure: (i) $\slashed D^-_A = (\slashed D^+_A)^*$ (formal adjoint), so $\slashed D^-\slashed D^+ = (\slashed D^+)^*\slashed D^+$; (ii) for a 2-form $\omega = \omega^++\omega^-$, $\omega\cdot\psi = \omega^+\cdot\psi$ for $\psi\in\Gamma(\slashed S^+)$; (iii) for $\psi\in\Gamma(\slashed S^+)$: $\slashed D_A^-\slashed D_A^+\psi = \nabla_A^*\nabla_A\psi + \tfrac14s_g\psi + \tfrac12F_A^+\cdot\psi$; proof: (i) from self-adjointness of $\slashed D$ and the block form; (ii) from the linear algebra page (kernel of $\Lambda^2\to\operatorname{End}\slashed S^+$ is $\Lambda^2_-$); (iii) restrict Lichnerowicz to $\slashed S^+$; spec: the exact form used in the $C^0$ bound of XI; mechanism: half the curvature is invisible to half the spinors.
- `Thm - Positive Scalar Curvature Kills Harmonic Spinors` — type: theorem; source items: A-T4.4.4; prereqs: [Thm - Lichnerowicz Formula for the Spin-c Dirac Operator, Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian, Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint]; statement: Let $M$ be a closed spin manifold with a metric of positive scalar curvature $s_g>0$. Then $\ker\slashed D = 0$: every harmonic spinor vanishes. More generally, on a closed spin$^c$ 4-manifold with $\slashed D_A^+\psi = 0$: $\|\nabla_A\psi\|^2 + \tfrac14\int s_g|\psi|^2 + \tfrac12\int\langle F_A^+\cdot\psi,\psi\rangle = 0$; proof: integrate $\langle\slashed D^2\psi,\psi\rangle = \|\slashed D\psi\|^2$ (self-adjointness) against Lichnerowicz: $0 = \|\nabla\psi\|^2 + \tfrac14\int s_g|\psi|^2$, both terms non-negative, the second strictly positive unless $\psi = 0$; spec: link the vault's `Ex - Lichnerowicz Vanishing for Harmonic Spinors on Positive Scalar Curvature` as the exercise version (its Recall transcludes the flagged Lichnerowicz page — the writer of that exercise is not our concern, but this page cites only the series' Lichnerowicz); mechanism: **a non-negative operator plus a positive multiple of the identity has trivial kernel**; targets: Witten's vanishing theorem (XI).
- `Ex - Dirac Operators on R^2 and R^3 Square to the Laplacian` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-E4.4.1 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - The Dirac Operator on Flat Space Squares to the Laplacian]; spec: verify by direct computation the two low-dimensional cases and identify the $\mathbb R^2$ case with $\bar\partial,\partial$: $4\partial_z\partial_{\bar z} = \partial_x^2+\partial_y^2$.
- `Ex - Local Formula for the Connection Laplacian` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X4.4.1 (a) (drill; the proof is on the Thm page); prereqs: [Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian]; spec: derive $\nabla^*\nabla s = -\sum_i(\nabla_{e_i}\nabla_{e_i}s - \nabla_{\nabla_{e_i}e_i}s)$ from $\nabla^* = -\star d^\nabla\star$ by computing $\star d^\nabla\star$ on $\alpha = \sum e^i\otimes\alpha_i$ in a frame; then check on $\mathbb R^n$ with the trivial bundle.
- `Ex - Integrated Weitzenbock Identity` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (A-X4.4.1 (c) combined with Weitzenböck); prereqs: [Thm - Weitzenbock Formula for the Dirac Operator, Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian]; spec: prove $\|Ds\|^2_{L^2} = \|\nabla s\|^2_{L^2} + \int_M\langle\mathcal Rs,s\rangle$ on a closed manifold and deduce: if $\mathcal R\ge c>0$ pointwise then $\ker D = 0$ and $\|Ds\|^2\ge c\|s\|^2$.
- `Ex - Bochner's Formula for 1-Forms from the Weitzenbock Formula` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: (additional; Lawson–Michelsohn Thm II.8.2 / the vault's sketched `Thm - Bochner's Theorem`); prereqs: [Thm - Weitzenbock Formula for the Dirac Operator, Thm - The Hodge-de Rham Operator is a Dirac Operator, Def - Curvature Endomorphism of a Dirac Bundle, Def - Ricci Tensor]; spec: compute $\mathcal R$ for the Dirac bundle $\Lambda T^*M$ restricted to 1-forms and show $(dd^*+d^*d)\alpha = \nabla^*\nabla\alpha + \operatorname{Ric}(\alpha)$; deduce that a closed Riemannian manifold with $\operatorname{Ric}>0$ has no non-zero harmonic 1-forms (the statement of the vault's Bochner page, now with a complete proof); link `Ex - First Betti Number Bounds via Bochner's Theorem` (Hodge I) as context.
- `Exercise Index - §8.4 The Weitzenböck Formula` — lists: [Ex - Dirac Operators on R^2 and R^3 Square to the Laplacian, Ex - Local Formula for the Connection Laplacian, Ex - Integrated Weitzenbock Identity, Ex - Bochner's Formula for 1-Forms from the Weitzenbock Formula]

Chapter VIII page count: §8.1: 6 Def/Thm + 4 Ex + 1 index = 11; §8.2: 4 Def/Thm + 4 Ex + 1 index = 9; §8.3: 5 Def/Thm + 4 Ex + 1 index = 10; §8.4: 9 Def/Thm + 4 Ex + 1 index = 14; topic page 1. **Total VIII: 45 pages** (24 Def/Thm, 16 Ex, 4 indices, 1 topic).

## Gauge Theory IX — Sobolev Spaces, Elliptic Operators, and Elliptic Complexes            (folder: Gauge Theory IX/)
Sources: A §5.1 (pp. 43–46), §5.2 (pp. 46–49), §5.3 (pp. 49–52); also A-I7.1.2, A-I7.1.6, A-I7.1.7, A-I7.1.10, A-I7.1.12, A-I7.1.13 (the analytic facts §7.1 imports, proved here), A-I6.4.1, A-I6.4.2 (the stabilisation lemma, proved here in §9.5). No Bär items.
Existing vault pages to link: Linear Algebra VI: `Def - Inner Product Space`, `Thm - Cauchy-Schwarz Inequality`, `Def - Orthogonal Complement`, `Thm - Orthogonal Decomposition`, `Def - Orthonormal Basis`, `Thm - Gram-Schmidt Procedure`, `Thm - Riesz Representation Theorem (Finite-Dimensional)`; the open mapping theorem for Banach spaces is used), `Def - Dense Subset`, `Def - Nowhere Dense and Meager`; Measure Theory II: `Def - Lp Spaces` (context only — see group decision 3; its flagged theorems are never cited); **their proofs black-box the Fredholm theory — the series' `Thm - Hodge Theorem for Elliptic Complexes` and `Thm - The de Rham Complex is Elliptic and Harmonic Forms Represent de Rham Cohomology` carry the proofs and are the pages cited by XI**);
