Non-anchor needs: `[NEEDED FROM I–VI: the induced connection on Hom(E,F), E ⊗ F, E^* and on T^*M ⊗ E (Haydys §2.1.2 operations), so that ∇^i u makes sense for sections]`.
Design (group decision 2 restated for the writers): all Sobolev spaces are $L^2$-based; the integer-order scale $H_k(M;E) = W^{k,2}(M;E)$, $k\in\mathbb Z$, is built on the torus by Fourier series and transported to compact manifolds by charts and partitions of unity (Lawson–Michelsohn, *Spin Geometry*, III.§5, with Wells, *Differential Analysis on Complex Manifolds*, Ch. IV §1–4 as the second reference); every estimate is proved for smooth sections and extended by density. Haydys's general-$p$ statements are recorded verbatim in Convention callouts as unproved context.
Sections: §9.1 Sobolev Spaces and Fourier Series on the Torus | §9.2 Sobolev Embedding, Rellich Compactness, and Multiplication | §9.3 Differential Operators, Symbols, and Ellipticity | §9.4 Elliptic Regularity and the Elliptic Estimate | §9.5 Fredholm Operators and the Fredholm Property of Elliptic Operators | §9.6 Elliptic Complexes and the Hodge Theorem

### §9.1 Sobolev Spaces and Fourier Series on the Torus
- `Def - Sobolev Space of Sections` — type: definition (anchor; compound: $L^p$ as a completion; $W^{k,p}$ on $\mathbb R^n$; $W^{k,p}(M;E)$ via connections; $C^r$ norms); source items: A-D5.1.1, A-D5.1.2, A-R5.1.2, A-R5.1.3, A-R5.1.1 (→ inside, Axiom Motivation: Dirichlet's principle — the minimising-sequence argument fails in $C^2$, which is why completions are needed; the computation $\frac{d}{dt}|_0E(u+tw) = \int w\Delta u$ with $\Delta = -\sum\partial_i^2$ written out); prereqs: [Def - Cauchy Sequence and Complete Metric Space, Def - Inner Product Space, Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle, Def - Riemannian Metric, Def - Section of a Vector Bundle, Def - Lp Spaces]; spec: $L^p(\mathbb R^n)$ := completion of $C_0^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ in $\|u\|_{L^p} = (\int|u|^p\,dx)^{1/p}$, $p\ge1$ (Riemann integrals of compactly supported smooth functions suffice); $L^2$ a Hilbert space with $\langle u,v\rangle = \int uv$; Sobolev norm $\|u\|_{W^{k,p}} := (\sum_{i\le k}\|\nabla^iu\|_{L^p}^p)^{1/p}$ where $\nabla^iu$ is the $i$-th iterated derivative (on $\mathbb R^n$: the tensor of all $i$-th partials; on a Riemannian $M$ with bundle $E$: $\nabla^iu\in\Gamma((T^*M)^{\otimes i}\otimes E)$ using $\nabla^E$ and $\nabla^M$ on $T^*M$ — `[NEEDED FROM I–VI: induced connections]`); $W^{k,p}(M;E)$ := completion of $\Gamma(E)$ (compactly supported sections when $M$ is non-compact); $W^{0,p} = L^p$; the chain $L^p\supset W^{1,p}\supset\cdots$; $H_k := W^{k,2}$, a Hilbert space with the obvious inner product; $C^r(M;E)$ with $\|u\|_{C^r} := \sum_{i\le r}\sup|\nabla^iu|$; notation remark (Haydys's Remark 135 on the inconsistency of $L^s_r$ notations); dependence on choices (R5.1.3) stated here and proved on the next page; **Convention callout (group decision 2):** the series proves everything for $p = 2$; Haydys's $W^{k,p}$ for $p>1$ is recorded; the vault's `Def - Lp Spaces` (Lebesgue) is context: the identification of the completion with the Lebesgue space is stated with the reference (Riesz–Fischer plus density of $C_0^\infty$, e.g. Folland *Real Analysis* Thm 6.6 and Prop 8.17) and marked "not used in this series"; examples: on $S^1$, $u\in W^{1,2}$ iff $\sum(1+n^2)|\hat u(n)|^2<\infty$ (proved in §9.1 below — forward link); non-example: the indicator of an interval is in $L^2(S^1)$ but not in $W^{1,2}(S^1)$ (its Fourier coefficients decay like $1/n$; verified after the Fourier page); calibration: compute $\|e^{in\theta}\|_{W^{k,2}}$.
- `Thm - Sobolev Norms are Independent of the Metric and Connections up to Equivalence` — type: theorem; source items: A-I5.1.5, A-R5.1.3; prereqs: [Def - Sobolev Space of Sections, Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space, Def - Compact Space]; statement: Let $M$ be compact, $E\to M$ a vector bundle. For any two choices $(g,\nabla^E,\nabla^M)$ and $(g',\nabla'^E,\nabla'^M)$ of Riemannian metric and connections, and any two fibre metrics on $E$, the norms $\|\cdot\|_{W^{k,2}}$ and $\|\cdot\|'_{W^{k,2}}$ on $\Gamma(E)$ are equivalent for every $k\ge0$; hence $W^{k,2}(M;E)$ is well defined as a topological vector space (and as a Hilbertable space); proof: induction on $k$: $\nabla' = \nabla + a$ with $a$ a smooth bundle-valued 1-form (affine-space anchor) on each of $E$ and $(T^*M)^{\otimes i}\otimes E$; then $\nabla'^{i}u - \nabla^iu$ is a linear combination of $\nabla^ju$, $j<i$, with smooth (hence bounded, by compactness) coefficients; the metrics are comparable by compactness ($c\,g\le g'\le C\,g$ pointwise, and likewise on $E$), and the volume forms differ by a bounded positive smooth factor; combine into $\|u\|'_{k}\le C\|u\|_k$ and symmetrically; spec: the same proof gives the $W^{k,p}$ statement for all $p$ — record; mechanism: **on a compact manifold every smooth object is bounded, and the difference of two connections is a smooth zero-order object**.
- `Def - Fourier Series on the Torus and Sobolev Spaces of Integer Order` — type: definition (compound: the torus $T^n$, Fourier coefficients, $H_k(T^n)$ for $k\in\mathbb Z$, vector-valued version, the $L^2$ pairing); source items: (the series' construction; feeds A-T5.1.1, A-T5.2.1, A-T5.2.5); prereqs: [Def - Sobolev Space of Sections, Def - Inner Product Space, Def - Cauchy Sequence and Complete Metric Space]; spec: $T^n := \mathbb R^n/2\pi\mathbb Z^n$ with the flat metric; for $u\in C^\infty(T^n;\mathbb C^r)$ the Fourier coefficients $\hat u(\xi) := (2\pi)^{-n}\int_{T^n}u(x)e^{-i\langle\xi,x\rangle}dx$, $\xi\in\mathbb Z^n$; for $k\in\mathbb Z$ the norm $\|u\|_k^2 := \sum_{\xi\in\mathbb Z^n}(1+|\xi|^2)^k|\hat u(\xi)|^2$ and $H_k(T^n;\mathbb C^r)$ := completion of $C^\infty$ in $\|\cdot\|_k$, identified with the weighted sequence space $\{(c_\xi) : \sum(1+|\xi|^2)^k|c_\xi|^2<\infty\}$ (the identification proved: the completion of the rapidly decreasing sequences in the weighted $\ell^2$ norm is the weighted $\ell^2$ space, by truncation); $H_0 = L^2(T^n)$ (Parseval, next page); the $L^2$ pairing $\langle u,v\rangle := (2\pi)^n\sum_\xi\hat u(\xi)\overline{\hat v(\xi)}$ extends to $H_k\times H_{-k}\to\mathbb C$ with $|\langle u,v\rangle|\le(2\pi)^n\|u\|_k\|v\|_{-k}$ (Cauchy–Schwarz on the weighted sequences); elements of $H_k$ for $k<0$ are called distributions of order $-k$ in the Motivation (no distribution theory is used); real-valued subspaces; examples: $e^{i\langle\xi,x\rangle}$ has $\|\cdot\|_k = (1+|\xi|^2)^{k/2}$; the Dirac comb $\sum_\xi e^{i\langle\xi,x\rangle}$ lies in $H_k$ iff $2k<-n$ (with the lattice-sum lemma); non-example: a sequence $(c_\xi)$ with $|c_\xi| = 1$ is in no $H_k$ with $k\ge-n/2$.
- `Thm - Fourier Series of Smooth Functions on the Torus` — type: theorem (four parts); source items: (the analytic foundation; no numbered Haydys item — Haydys's Remark 138 (R5.1.4) is the one-dimensional instance and is recorded in the Motivation); prereqs: [Def - Fourier Series on the Torus and Sobolev Spaces of Integer Order, Thm - Cauchy-Schwarz Inequality]; statement: For $u\in C^\infty(T^n;\mathbb C)$: (i) $\widehat{\partial_ju}(\xi) = i\xi_j\hat u(\xi)$, hence $|\hat u(\xi)|\le C_N(1+|\xi|)^{-N}$ for every $N$ (rapid decrease); (ii) the Fourier series $\sum_\xi\hat u(\xi)e^{i\langle\xi,x\rangle}$ converges absolutely and uniformly together with all its derivatives, and its sum is $u$; (iii) (Parseval) $\int_{T^n}u\bar v\,dx = (2\pi)^n\sum_\xi\hat u(\xi)\overline{\hat v(\xi)}$, in particular $\|u\|_{L^2}^2 = (2\pi)^n\|u\|_0^2$; (iv) the map $u\mapsto(\hat u(\xi))_\xi$ is a linear bijection from $C^\infty(T^n)$ onto the rapidly decreasing sequences, with inverse the Fourier series; proof: (i) integration by parts on the torus (no boundary terms — periodicity); (ii) Weierstrass M-test for uniform convergence of the series and each derived series (from (i)); the sum $v$ is continuous with the same Fourier coefficients as $u$ (termwise integration of a uniformly convergent series — justified); Lemma 1 (uniqueness): a continuous function on $T^n$ with all Fourier coefficients zero is zero — proved with the Fejér kernel $K_N(x) := \prod_{j=1}^n F_N(x_j)$, $F_N(t) := \frac1{N+1}\big(\frac{\sin((N+1)t/2)}{\sin(t/2)}\big)^2$: $K_N\ge0$, $(2\pi)^{-n}\int K_N = 1$, $K_N\to0$ uniformly off any neighbourhood of $0$, so $K_N*w\to w$ uniformly for continuous $w$ (approximate identity, Lemma 2, written out), while $K_N*w$ is a trigonometric polynomial whose coefficients are multiples of $\hat w$; hence $w = 0$; (iii) from (ii) by termwise integration against $\bar v$; (iv) from (i)–(iii) and the M-test in the other direction; spec: reference Stein–Shakarchi *Fourier Analysis* Ch. 2–3 (one variable) and Folland *Real Analysis* §8.3; mechanism: **differentiation is multiplication by $i\xi$ on the Fourier side, so smoothness is decay of coefficients, and the Fejér kernel shows nothing is lost**; targets: every page of this chapter.
- `Thm - Sobolev Norms on the Torus via Fourier Coefficients` — type: theorem (five parts); source items: (foundation; feeds A-T5.1.1, A-T5.2.1); prereqs: [Thm - Fourier Series of Smooth Functions on the Torus, Def - Fourier Series on the Torus and Sobolev Spaces of Integer Order, Def - Sobolev Space of Sections]; statement: (i) For integer $k\ge0$ and $u\in C^\infty(T^n)$: $c_k\|u\|_k\le\|u\|_{W^{k,2}}\le C_k\|u\|_k$ (with the flat metric and trivial connection), hence $W^{k,2}(T^n) = H_k(T^n)$ with equivalent norms. (ii) $H_k\subset H_m$ with $\|u\|_m\le\|u\|_k$ for $k\ge m$; $\bigcap_kH_k = C^\infty(T^n)$ (as sets; with the topology of all the $\|\cdot\|_k$). (iii) (Peetre's inequality) $(1+|\xi|^2)^s\le2^{|s|}(1+|\xi-\eta|^2)^{|s|}(1+|\eta|^2)^s$ for all $\xi,\eta\in\mathbb R^n$, $s\in\mathbb R$. (iv) (Duality) the pairing induces an isometric anti-linear isomorphism $H_{-k}\to(H_k)^*$; and $\|u\|_k = \sup\{|\langle u,\phi\rangle| : \phi\in C^\infty, \|\phi\|_{-k}\le1\}$ (up to the factor $(2\pi)^n$). (v) (Interpolation) For integers $r<t<s$ and every $\epsilon>0$ there is $C_\epsilon$ with $\|u\|_t\le\epsilon\|u\|_s + C_\epsilon\|u\|_r$; proof: (i) $\|\partial^\alpha u\|_{L^2}^2 = (2\pi)^n\sum\xi^{2\alpha}|\hat u|^2$ and $\sum_{|\alpha|\le k}\xi^{2\alpha}\asymp(1+|\xi|^2)^k$ (elementary inequalities with explicit constants); (ii) from the weights and part (iv) of the previous page; (iii) $1+|\xi|^2\le2(1+|\xi-\eta|^2)(1+|\eta|^2)$ by the triangle inequality, then raise to the power; (iv) Riesz representation for weighted $\ell^2$ (prove directly: a bounded functional on weighted $\ell^2$ is given by a sequence, via evaluation on basis vectors and Cauchy–Schwarz); (v) Young: $(1+|\xi|^2)^t\le\epsilon(1+|\xi|^2)^s + C_\epsilon(1+|\xi|^2)^r$ pointwise (prove: $x^{t-r}\le\epsilon x^{s-r} + C_\epsilon$ for $x\ge1$); spec: mechanism: the Sobolev scale on the torus is a family of weighted $\ell^2$ spaces indexed by the weight exponent.
- `Thm - Sobolev Spaces of Sections on a Compact Manifold via Charts` — type: theorem; source items: (foundation; feeds A-T5.1.1, A-T5.2.1, A-T5.2.5); prereqs: [Thm - Sobolev Norms on the Torus via Fourier Coefficients, Thm - Sobolev Norms are Independent of the Metric and Connections up to Equivalence, Def - Partition of Unity on a Manifold, Thm - Existence of Smooth Partitions of Unity, Def - Local Trivialization, Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem]; statement: Let $M$ be a compact $n$-manifold and $E\to M$ a vector bundle of rank $r$. Fix a finite atlas of charts $\kappa_i:U_i\to\kappa_i(U_i)\subset(-\pi,\pi)^n\subset T^n$ over which $E$ is trivialised, and a subordinate partition of unity $(\phi_i)$. For $k\in\mathbb Z$ and $u\in\Gamma(E)$ set $\|u\|_{k}^2 := \sum_i\|(\phi_iu)\circ\kappa_i^{-1}\|_{H_k(T^n;\mathbb C^r)}^2$ (extended by zero). Then: (i) for $k\ge0$ this norm is equivalent to the connection norm $\|\cdot\|_{W^{k,2}}$ of `Def - Sobolev Space of Sections`; (ii) for every $k\in\mathbb Z$ the norms obtained from two such choices of data are equivalent; $H_k(M;E)$ := completion, a Hilbertable space, $H_k = W^{k,2}$ for $k\ge0$; (iii) $\Gamma(E)$ is dense in $H_k(M;E)$; $H_k\subset H_m$ continuously for $k\ge m$; (iv) the $L^2$ pairing $\Gamma(E)\times\Gamma(E)\to\mathbb R$ extends to a perfect pairing $H_k(M;E)\times H_{-k}(M;E)\to\mathbb R$ identifying $H_{-k}$ with the dual of $H_k$; (v) multiplication by a smooth function, and more generally a smooth bundle map $E\to F$, is bounded $H_k(M;E)\to H_k(M;F)$ for every $k\in\mathbb Z$; (vi) (interpolation) as on the torus; proof: Lawson–Michelsohn III.§5 (the paragraph after Def 5.1) and Wells IV §1; Lemma 1 (for $k\ge0$, multiplication by a smooth compactly supported function is bounded on $H_k(T^n)$ — Leibniz and (i) of the torus page; for $k<0$ by duality (iv)); Lemma 2 (for a diffeomorphism $\psi$ between open subsets of $T^n$ and $u$ supported in the domain, $\|u\circ\psi\|_k\le C\|u\|_k$: $k\ge0$ by the chain rule and induction; $k<0$ by duality with the transposed map $v\mapsto|\det D\psi^{-1}|\,v\circ\psi^{-1}$); Lemma 3 (change of partition of unity/atlas: combine Lemmas 1–2); (i) by comparing $\nabla^i(\phi_iu)$ with coordinate derivatives (the connection differs from $d$ by a smooth zero-order term; the previous equivalence theorem); (iv) from the torus duality and the partition of unity (the writer must show the two pairings agree and that $\sum_i\phi_i^2$-type weights do not matter — LM's argument); spec: this page is the bridge from the torus to manifolds; every later "on a compact manifold" statement reduces to the torus through it; mechanism: **a compact manifold is finitely many pieces of a torus glued by smooth maps, and Sobolev norms only see smooth maps as bounded operators**.
- `Ex - Fourier Coefficients of Smooth Functions Decay Rapidly` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill of part (i) of the Fourier page); prereqs: [Thm - Fourier Series of Smooth Functions on the Torus]; spec: prove $|\hat u(\xi)|\le C_N(1+|\xi|)^{-N}$ by repeated integration by parts; compute the coefficients of $u(\theta) = \theta(2\pi-\theta)$ on $S^1$ (only $C^0$ across $0$, coefficients $\sim n^{-2}$) and of $|\sin\theta|$ and compare decay rates with regularity.
- `Ex - An Unbounded Function in W-1-2 of the Two-Torus` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (additional; Evans §5.6, the borderline case $kp = n$); prereqs: [Def - Sobolev Space of Sections, Thm - Sobolev Norms on the Torus via Fourier Coefficients]; spec: show $u(x) := \chi(|x|)\log\log(1/|x|)$ (with $\chi$ a cutoff supported in the unit disc, extended periodically to $T^2$) lies in $W^{1,2}(T^2)$ but is unbounded; conclude that $W^{k,2}\subset C^0$ fails when $2k = n$, so the strict inequality in the Sobolev embedding theorem cannot be dropped (route: polar coordinates; $|\nabla u|^2\sim(r\log(1/r))^{-2}$ is integrable near $0$).
- `Ex - Duality of Positive and Negative Order Sobolev Spaces on the Torus` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of part (iv)); prereqs: [Thm - Sobolev Norms on the Torus via Fourier Coefficients]; spec: prove that every bounded linear functional on $H_k(T^n)$ is $u\mapsto\langle u,v\rangle$ for a unique $v\in H_{-k}$ with $\|v\|_{-k}$ equal to the operator norm (up to $(2\pi)^n$); identify the Dirac comb with evaluation at $0$ on $H_k$ for $2k>n$ (after the embedding theorem).
- `Ex - Equivalence of Sobolev Norms for Two Connections on a Line Bundle` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of the equivalence theorem in rank one); prereqs: [Thm - Sobolev Norms are Independent of the Metric and Connections up to Equivalence]; spec: on the trivial line bundle over $S^1$ with $\nabla = d + ia(\theta)d\theta$, prove $\|u\|_{W^{2,2},\nabla}\le C\|u\|_{W^{2,2},d}$ with an explicit $C$ in terms of $\sup|a|$ and $\sup|a'|$, and conversely.
- `Exercise Index - §9.1 Sobolev Spaces and Fourier Series on the Torus` — lists: [Ex - Fourier Coefficients of Smooth Functions Decay Rapidly, Ex - An Unbounded Function in W-1-2 of the Two-Torus, Ex - Duality of Positive and Negative Order Sobolev Spaces on the Torus, Ex - Equivalence of Sobolev Norms for Two Connections on a Line Bundle]

### §9.2 Sobolev Embedding, Rellich Compactness, and Multiplication
- `Thm - Convergence of the Lattice Sum` — type: theorem (technical lemma reused four times); source items: (foundation); prereqs: [Def - Fourier Series on the Torus and Sobolev Spaces of Integer Order]; statement: For $t\in\mathbb R$, $\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb Z^n}(1+|\xi|^2)^{-t}<\infty$ if and only if $2t>n$; moreover $\sum_{|\xi|\ge R}(1+|\xi|^2)^{-t}\le C_{n,t}R^{n-2t}$ for $R\ge1$ when $2t>n$; proof: compare with the integral $\int_{\mathbb R^n}(1+|x|^2)^{-t}dx$ via the unit cubes centred at lattice points (monotonicity of the weight along rays, or the elementary bound $(1+|\xi|^2)\asymp(1+|x|^2)$ for $x$ in the cube at $\xi$), and evaluate the integral in polar coordinates; the shell count $\#\{\xi : R\le|\xi|<R+1\}\le C R^{n-1}$ gives the tail estimate; spec: mechanism: dimension counting on the lattice.
- `Thm - Sobolev Embedding Theorem` — type: theorem (anchor); source items: A-T5.1.1 (i), (iii), A-I5.1.1, A-R5.1.4 (→ inside, Motivation / Why Is It True: Haydys's $S^1$ argument with the mean value theorem and Cauchy–Schwarz written out, as the one-dimensional model), A-I7.1.2 (the instances $W^{5,2}\subset C^2$ and $W^{5,2}\subset C^0$ in dimension four, recorded as Corollary 1); prereqs: [Thm - Sobolev Spaces of Sections on a Compact Manifold via Charts, Thm - Fourier Series of Smooth Functions on the Torus, Thm - Convergence of the Lattice Sum]; statement: Let $M$ be a compact $n$-manifold, $E\to M$ a vector bundle. (i) For integers $k\ge m$, $H_k(M;E)\subset H_m(M;E)$ with $\|u\|_m\le C\|u\|_k$. (ii) For integers $k, r\ge0$ with $k - \frac n2>r$ there is a continuous injection $H_k(M;E)\hookrightarrow C^r(M;E)$: every element of $H_k$ is (represented by) a $C^r$ section and $\|u\|_{C^r}\le C\|u\|_{H_k}$. (iii) $\bigcap_{k\ge0}H_k(M;E) = C^\infty(M;E)$: if $u\in H_k$ for all $k$ then $u$ is smooth. **Convention callout:** Haydys's Theorem 136 (i),(iii) for $W^{k,p}$, $p>1$: $W^{k,p}\subset W^{m,q}$ continuously when $k-\frac np\ge m-\frac nq$, $k\ge m$, and $W^{k,p}\subset C^r$ when $k-\frac np>r$ — recorded verbatim as unproved context with references (Evans 5.6.1–5.6.3 and 5.6.5; Adams–Fournier Thm 4.12); proof: (i) is the torus statement plus charts; (ii) on the torus: for $u\in C^\infty$ and $|\alpha|\le r$, $\sup|\partial^\alpha u|\le\sum_\xi|\xi|^{|\alpha|}|\hat u(\xi)|\le\big(\sum_\xi(1+|\xi|^2)^{r-k}\big)^{1/2}\big(\sum_\xi(1+|\xi|^2)^k|\hat u(\xi)|^2\big)^{1/2} = C\|u\|_k$ (Cauchy–Schwarz; the lattice sum converges since $2(k-r)>n$); the estimate on smooth sections and completeness of $C^r$ (Lemma 1: $C^r(T^n)$ with the $C^r$ norm is complete — uniform limits of derivatives) give the embedding of the completion; injectivity: the $C^r$ representative determines the $H_k$ element because the map $C^r\to L^2\to H_0$ is injective on continuous functions (Parseval/Fejér); patch to $M$ via the charts page; (iii) from (ii) for every $r$; spec: mechanism: **$k$ derivatives in $L^2$ control $r$ derivatives uniformly exactly when the leftover weight $(1+|\xi|^2)^{r-k}$ is summable, which is dimension counting**; targets: $C^0$ bound in XI, smoothness of kernels of elliptic operators.
- `Def - Compact Operator` — type: definition; source items: (the notion used in A-T5.1.1 (ii) and A-D5.2.6); prereqs: [Def - Compact Space, Def - Totally Bounded Metric Space, Thm - Compactness in Metric Spaces (Three Equivalents)]; spec: a bounded linear map $K:X\to Y$ between Banach spaces is compact if the image of the unit ball is relatively compact, equivalently (in a complete space) totally bounded, equivalently every bounded sequence $(x_j)$ has a subsequence with $(Kx_j)$ convergent; examples: finite-rank operators; the inclusion $H_k(T^n)\subset H_m(T^n)$ for $k>m$ (proved on the Rellich page); diagonal operators on $\ell^2$ with $\lambda_j\to0$ (verified); non-example: the identity on an infinite-dimensional space (Riesz's lemma, proved on `Thm - Riesz-Schauder Theory for Compact Perturbations of the Identity`).
- `Thm - Basic Properties of Compact Operators` — type: theorem; source items: (foundation for A-T5.1.1 (ii), A-T5.2.5, A-T7.1.20); prereqs: [Def - Compact Operator, Def - Cauchy Sequence and Complete Metric Space]; statement: (i) Compact operators form a closed subspace of the bounded operators $X\to Y$ (operator norm); (ii) if $K$ is compact and $A,B$ bounded then $BKA$ is compact (ideal property); (iii) the sum of two compact operators is compact; (iv) in a Hilbert space, an operator is compact if and only if it is an operator-norm limit of finite-rank operators; (v) the Hilbert adjoint of a compact operator is compact; proof: (i) $\epsilon/3$-argument with total boundedness; (ii)–(iii) direct; (iv) "$\Leftarrow$" from (i); "$\Rightarrow$": for compact $K$ and $\epsilon>0$ pick a finite $\epsilon$-net $y_1,\dots,y_N$ of $K(B_1)$ and let $P$ be the orthogonal projection onto their span; then $\|K - PK\|\le\epsilon$ (`Thm - Best Approximation by Orthogonal Projection`, Linear Algebra VI, valid in any inner product space for finite-dimensional subspaces); (v) from (iv) and $\|(PK)^*-K^*\| = \|PK-K\|$; spec: reference Brezis *Functional Analysis* §6.1; mechanism: compactness is stable under everything that does not enlarge the image of the ball.
- `Thm - Rellich Compactness Theorem` — type: theorem (anchor); source items: A-T5.1.1 (ii) ((137)), A-I5.1.2, A-I5.1.4 (Arzelà–Ascoli is *not* used — the Fourier route replaces it; recorded in the Motivation as the route of Haydys's Remark 138); prereqs: [Thm - Sobolev Spaces of Sections on a Compact Manifold via Charts, Def - Compact Operator, Thm - Basic Properties of Compact Operators, Thm - Convergence of the Lattice Sum]; statement: Let $M$ be a compact manifold, $E\to M$ a vector bundle, $k>m$ integers. The inclusion $H_k(M;E)\hookrightarrow H_m(M;E)$ is a compact operator: every sequence bounded in $H_k$ has a subsequence converging in $H_m$. **Convention callout:** Haydys's (ii) for $W^{k,p}\subset W^{m,q}$ with $k-\frac np>m-\frac nq$, $k>m$ (Rellich–Kondrachov) recorded as context (Evans 5.7.1); proof: on the torus, the inclusion is the norm limit of the finite-rank truncations $T_R u := \sum_{|\xi|\le R}\hat u(\xi)e^{i\langle\xi,x\rangle}$: $\|u - T_Ru\|_m^2 = \sum_{|\xi|>R}(1+|\xi|^2)^m|\hat u|^2\le(1+R^2)^{m-k}\|u\|_k^2$, so $\|\iota - T_R\|_{H_k\to H_m}\le(1+R^2)^{(m-k)/2}\to0$; compact by (i) of the basic-properties page; on $M$: the inclusion is $\sum_i$ (extension by zero) $\circ$ (torus inclusion) $\circ$ (localisation $u\mapsto(\phi_iu)\circ\kappa_i^{-1}$), each factor bounded, so compact by the ideal property and (iii); spec: mechanism: **a bounded set in $H_k$ has uniformly small Fourier tails in $H_m$, so it is totally bounded in $H_m$**; targets: finite-dimensionality of kernels of elliptic operators; compactness of the Seiberg–Witten moduli space.
- `Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem` — type: theorem (three parts); source items: A-T5.1.1 (iv)(a),(b), A-I5.1.3, A-I7.1.7 (the instance "$W^{k,2}(M^4)$ is an algebra for $k\ge3$" as Corollary 1; source typo: Haydys Theorem 136 (iv)(b) omits the side conditions $k\le\min(k_1,k_2)$ and strictness of the inequality; the corrected statement is the one below); prereqs: [Thm - Sobolev Norms on the Torus via Fourier Coefficients, Thm - Convergence of the Lattice Sum, Thm - Sobolev Spaces of Sections on a Compact Manifold via Charts, Thm - Sobolev Embedding Theorem]; statement: Let $M$ be a compact $n$-manifold. (a) If $2k>n$ then pointwise multiplication extends to a bounded bilinear map $H_k(M)\times H_k(M)\to H_k(M)$, $\|uv\|_k\le C\|u\|_k\|v\|_k$: $H_k(M)$ is a Banach algebra (after renorming). (b) For integers $k_1,k_2\ge k\ge0$ with $k_1+k_2-k>\frac n2$, multiplication extends to a bounded bilinear map $H_{k_1}(M)\times H_{k_2}(M)\to H_k(M)$; more generally for a smooth bilinear bundle map $E_1\otimes E_2\to E_3$, $H_{k_1}(E_1)\times H_{k_2}(E_2)\to H_k(E_3)$. (c) For $2k>n$ and $u\in H_k(M)$, $v\in H_m(M)$ with $|m|\le k$: $\|uv\|_m\le C\|u\|_k\|v\|_m$ (multiplication by an $H_k$ function is bounded on $H_m$ for $|m|\le k$). **Convention callout:** Haydys's general-$p$ (iv) recorded as context; proof: on the torus, $\widehat{uv}(\xi) = \sum_\eta\hat u(\xi-\eta)\hat v(\eta)$ (for smooth $u,v$, from the Fourier page); by Peetre, $(1+|\xi|^2)^{k/2}\le2^{k/2}\big((1+|\xi-\eta|^2)^{k/2}+(1+|\eta|^2)^{k/2}\big)$ for $k\ge0$ (Lemma 1, the standard splitting: either $|\xi-\eta|\ge|\xi|/2$ or $|\eta|\ge|\xi|/2$); split the convolution accordingly and estimate each piece by Cauchy–Schwarz against a summable weight; the writer follows the standard proof of $H^{s_1}\cdot H^{s_2}\subset H^s$ for $s\le\min(s_1,s_2)$, $s_1+s_2-s>n/2$ (e.g. Taylor *PDE III* §13.3 Prop 3.7 / Palais *Foundations of Global Non-Linear Analysis* §9, in the periodic setting): write $(1+|\xi|^2)^{k/2}|\widehat{uv}(\xi)|\le\sum_\eta[(1+|\xi-\eta|^2)^{k/2}+(1+|\eta|^2)^{k/2}]|\hat u(\xi-\eta)||\hat v(\eta)|$ up to $2^{k/2}$, then in the first sum insert $1 = (1+|\eta|^2)^{k_2/2}(1+|\eta|^2)^{-k_2/2}$ and use Cauchy–Schwarz in $\eta$ with $\sum_\eta(1+|\eta|^2)^{-k_2}\cdot(\dots)$ — since $k\le k_1$, $(1+|\xi-\eta|^2)^{k/2}|\hat u(\xi-\eta)|\le(1+|\xi-\eta|^2)^{k_1/2}|\hat u(\xi-\eta)|$; the resulting $\ell^2$-in-$\xi$ norm is bounded by Young's inequality for sequences ($\ell^2*\ell^1\subset\ell^2$, proved as Lemma 2) using that $(1+|\eta|^2)^{-k_2/2}(1+|\eta|^2)^{k_2/2}|\hat v(\eta)|\in\ell^1$ requires $\sum(1+|\eta|^2)^{-k_2}<\infty$, i.e. $2k_2>n$ — **this is the case (a) and the case $k_2>n/2$ of (b)**; the general case of (b) with $k_1+k_2-k>n/2$ but neither $k_i>n/2$ needs the refined splitting, which the writer proves in full following the reference: on the region $|\eta|\le|\xi-\eta|$ bound $(1+|\xi|^2)^{k/2}\le C(1+|\xi-\eta|^2)^{k/2}$ and write $(1+|\xi-\eta|^2)^{k/2} = (1+|\xi-\eta|^2)^{k_1/2}(1+|\xi-\eta|^2)^{(k-k_1)/2}$; since $k-k_1\le0$ and $|\eta|\le|\xi-\eta|$, $(1+|\xi-\eta|^2)^{(k-k_1)/2}\le(1+|\eta|^2)^{(k-k_1)/2}$, so the weight on $\eta$ becomes $(1+|\eta|^2)^{(k-k_1-k_2)/2}\cdot(1+|\eta|^2)^{k_2/2}|\hat v(\eta)|$, and $\sum(1+|\eta|^2)^{k-k_1-k_2}<\infty$ iff $2(k_1+k_2-k)>n$ — that is the full proof); (c): the case $m\ge0$ by the same argument with the roles arranged as in (b) ($k_1 = k$, $k_2 = m$, target $m$: needs $k+m-m>n/2$ ✓); $m<0$ by duality from the case $-m\ge0$; on $M$ via charts and $\phi_i$'s; spec: mechanism: **convolution of Fourier coefficients spreads regularity: the product has the lesser regularity provided the excess $k_1+k_2-k$ pays for the dimension**; targets: $\mathcal G^{k,2}$ is a group (XI), smoothness of the Seiberg–Witten map, bootstrapping in the compactness proof.
- `Thm - Composition with Analytic Functions on the Sobolev Algebra` — type: theorem; source items: (needed by A-D7.1.8 — the Banach Lie group structure of $\mathcal G^{k,p}$ — and by the slice theorem; no numbered item); prereqs: [Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem, Thm - Sobolev Embedding Theorem]; statement: Let $M$ be compact, $2k>n$, and $f:\mathbb C\to\mathbb C$ entire (in particular $f = \exp$). Then $u\mapsto f\circ u$ is a well-defined smooth map $H_k(M;\mathbb C)\to H_k(M;\mathbb C)$ (smooth in the Fréchet sense between Banach spaces), with derivative $v\mapsto(f'\circ u)v$; in particular $\xi\mapsto e^{i\xi}$ is smooth $H_k(M;\mathbb R)\to H_k(M;\mathbb C)$ with image in $\{g : |g| = 1\}$; proof: the power series $\sum a_ju^j$ converges absolutely in the Banach algebra $H_k$ ($\|u^j\|_k\le C^{j-1}\|u\|_k^j$); a convergent power series in a Banach algebra is smooth with the termwise derivative (Lemma 1: differentiability of power series in Banach algebras — Dieudonné *Foundations* (9.3)); spec: also record: for $2k>n$ and $g\in H_k(M;S^1)$, $g^{-1} = \bar g\in H_k$ and $\mathcal G^{k} := H_k(M;S^1)$ is a group under pointwise multiplication (from (a)); mechanism: the Sobolev algebra is a Banach algebra, and analytic functional calculus works in any Banach algebra.
- `Ex - Sobolev Embedding on the Circle by Hand` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-R5.1.4 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Sobolev Embedding Theorem]; spec: reproduce Haydys's Remark 138: for $u\in C^\infty(S^1)$ prove $\|u\|_{C^0}\le\sqrt{2\pi}\|u\|_{W^{1,2}} + \tfrac1{2\pi}|\int u|$-type bound via the mean value theorem and Cauchy–Schwarz, then the Hölder-$\tfrac12$ estimate $|u(\theta_1)-u(\theta_2)|\le\sqrt{2\pi}\|u\|_{W^{1,2}}\operatorname{dist}(\theta_1,\theta_2)^{1/2}$; compare with the Fourier proof.
- `Ex - The Lattice Sum Converges iff 2t Exceeds n` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill of `Thm - Convergence of the Lattice Sum`); prereqs: [Thm - Convergence of the Lattice Sum]; spec: prove both directions by comparison with integrals; compute the exact value for $n = 1$, $t = 1$ ($\sum(1+m^2)^{-1} = \pi\coth\pi$ — bold context only) or verify numerically.
- `Ex - Rellich Fails on Non-Compact Domains` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (additional; Evans §5.7 remarks); prereqs: [Thm - Rellich Compactness Theorem]; spec: on $\mathbb R^n$ show the translates $u_j := \phi(\cdot - je_1)$ of a bump function are bounded in $W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n)$ but have no $L^2$-convergent subsequence ($\|u_j-u_l\|_{L^2} = \sqrt2\|\phi\|_{L^2}$ for $j\ne l$); explain which step of the torus proof uses compactness.
- `Ex - W-3-2 is an Algebra in Dimension Four` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-I7.1.7 (drill of Corollary 1 of the multiplication theorem — the Seiberg–Witten case); prereqs: [Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem]; spec: verify the numerology $2\cdot3>4$ and write out the proof of $\|uv\|_3\le C\|u\|_3\|v\|_3$ on $T^4$ with explicit constants; show $H_2(T^4)$ is not an algebra by exhibiting $u\in H_2(T^4)$ with $u^2\notin H_2$ (route: $u$ with $\hat u(\xi) = (1+|\xi|^2)^{-2}\log(2+|\xi|)^{-1}$-type coefficients; or cite the failure of $H_2(T^4)\subset C^0$ from `Ex - An Unbounded Function in W-1-2 of the Two-Torus` scaled up — ⭐⭐⭐ if done fully).
- `Ex - Composition with the Exponential on the Sobolev Algebra` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: (drill of the composition theorem); prereqs: [Thm - Composition with Analytic Functions on the Sobolev Algebra]; spec: prove directly that $\xi\mapsto e^{i\xi}$ is $C^1$ from $H_3(M^4;\mathbb R)$ to $H_3(M^4;\mathbb C)$ with derivative $v\mapsto ie^{i\xi}v$, estimating the remainder $e^{i(\xi+v)} - e^{i\xi} - ie^{i\xi}v = e^{i\xi}(e^{iv}-1-iv)$ in $H_3$ by the algebra bound; deduce that $\{g\in H_3(M;\mathbb C) : |g| = 1\}$ is a Banach Lie group modelled on $H_3(M;\mathbb R)$ (charts $\xi\mapsto g_0e^{i\xi}$ near $g_0$, using the embedding $H_3\subset C^0$ to see that small $\xi$ give nearby $g$ and the logarithm on a neighbourhood of $1$ in $S^1$ composed with $g_0^{-1}g$ gives the inverse chart — smoothness of the inverse chart via composition with the analytic branch of $\log$ near $1$).
- `Exercise Index - §9.2 Sobolev Embedding, Rellich Compactness, and Multiplication` — lists: [Ex - Sobolev Embedding on the Circle by Hand, Ex - The Lattice Sum Converges iff 2t Exceeds n, Ex - Rellich Fails on Non-Compact Domains, Ex - W-3-2 is an Algebra in Dimension Four, Ex - Composition with the Exponential on the Sobolev Algebra]

### §9.3 Differential Operators, Symbols, and Ellipticity
- `Def - Elliptic Differential Operator and Principal Symbol` — type: definition (anchor; compound: linear differential operator of order $\ell$ locally and on bundles; principal symbol; ellipticity); source items: A-D5.2.1, A-D5.2.2, A-D5.2.3, A-D5.2.4; prereqs: [Def - Vector Bundle, Def - Local Trivialization, Def - Section of a Vector Bundle, Def - Cotangent Space and Cotangent Bundle]; spec: on $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^n$: $Lf = \sum_{|\alpha|\le\ell}A_\alpha(x)\partial^\alpha f$, $A_\alpha\in C^\infty(\Omega;\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb R^r,\mathbb R^s))$ (Haydys (140); the $\ell = 2$, $n = 2$ example written out); principal symbol $\sigma_L(x,\xi) := \sum_{|\alpha|=\ell}A_\alpha(x)\xi^\alpha$, homogeneous of degree $\ell$ in $\xi\in(\mathbb R^n)^*$ — **no factor of $i$** (Convention callout: many texts use $\sigma_L(\xi) = \sum A_\alpha(i\xi)^\alpha$, so that the Laplacian's symbol is $+|\xi|^2$; Haydys's convention gives $\sigma_\Delta(\xi) = -|\xi|^2$ for the non-negative Laplacian; the two differ by $i^\ell$, which does not affect ellipticity); on bundles: $L:\Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(F)$ is a differential operator of order $\ell$ if it has the local form (140) in every chart and trivialisation; the symbol as a map $\sigma_L:\pi^*E\to\pi^*F$ over $T^*M$ (well-definedness is the next Thm page); $L$ is *elliptic* if $\sigma_L(x,\xi)$ is invertible for all $\xi\ne0$ (so necessarily $r = s$); order is well defined (the minimal $\ell$); examples: $d$ on forms (symbol $\xi\wedge$, not elliptic for $n\ge2$ as $\Lambda^k\to\Lambda^{k+1}$ but injective — leads to elliptic complexes), $\Delta$, Dirac (next Thm page); non-examples: $\partial_t - \Delta$ (heat, symbol $-|\xi|^2$ on the space part only — degenerate), $\partial_t^2-\Delta_x$ (wave, symbol vanishes on the light cone), $\bar\partial:C^\infty(\mathbb C)\to C^\infty(\mathbb C)$ (symbol $\tfrac12(\xi_1+i\xi_2)$, elliptic as a complex operator $\mathbb C\to\mathbb C$ — instance).
- `Thm - The Principal Symbol is Well-Defined and Multiplicative` — type: theorem; source items: A-R5.2.1; prereqs: [Def - Elliptic Differential Operator and Principal Symbol, Thm - Tensoriality Lemma for C-Infinity-Linear Maps]; statement: (i) For a differential operator $L$ of order $\ell$, $x\in M$, $\xi\in T_x^*M$ and $v\in E_x$: $\sigma_L(x,\xi)v = \frac1{\ell!}L(f^\ell s)(x)$ for any $f\in C^\infty(M)$ with $f(x) = 0$, $df_x = \xi$ and any $s\in\Gamma(E)$ with $s(x) = v$; hence the symbol is independent of charts and trivialisations and defines a smooth bundle map $\sigma_L:\pi^*E\to\pi^*F$ over $T^*M$, fibrewise a homogeneous polynomial of degree $\ell$ in $\xi$. (ii) $\sigma_{L_2\circ L_1} = \sigma_{L_2}\circ\sigma_{L_1}$ and $\sigma_{L^*}(\xi) = (-1)^\ell\sigma_L(\xi)^*$ (the latter after `Def - Formal Adjoint of a Differential Operator`). (iii) Ellipticity is invariant under changes of charts, trivialisations, and under composition and adjoints; proof: (i) Leibniz: $\partial^\alpha(f^\ell s)$ at $x$ is non-zero only when all $\ell$ derivatives fall on the $f$-factors, giving $\ell!\,\xi^\alpha v$ for $|\alpha| = \ell$ and $0$ for $|\alpha|<\ell$; (ii) from (i) applied to $f^{\ell_1+\ell_2}$ (Leibniz count) and integration by parts for the adjoint; spec: mechanism: the top-order part of a differential operator is detected by conjugating with $e^{tf}$ or by the $\ell$-th power trick.
- `Thm - Laplacians and Dirac Operators are Elliptic` — type: theorem (three parts); source items: A-E5.2.1, A-E5.2.2, A-E5.2.3 (source typo: Haydys Example 144 writes the inverse of Clifford multiplication by $\xi$ as $|\xi|^{-2}\xi$; with $u\cdot u = -|u|^2$ it is $-|\xi|^{-2}\xi$), A-T7.1.5 (partial: ellipticity of $\slashed D^\pm$ and of $d^++d^*$ as a Dirac-type operator is recorded here; the Atiyah complex itself is in XI); prereqs: [Def - Elliptic Differential Operator and Principal Symbol, Thm - The Principal Symbol is Well-Defined and Multiplicative, Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator, Def - Hodge Laplacian, Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature, Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian]; statement: (i) The Laplacian $\Delta = -\sum\partial_i^2$ on $\mathbb R^n$ has $\sigma_\Delta(\xi) = -|\xi|^2$ and is elliptic. (ii) The Laplace–Beltrami operator $\Delta f = -\star d\star df$ on an oriented Riemannian manifold has coordinate expression $\Delta f = -|g|^{-1/2}\sum_{i,j}\partial_i(|g|^{1/2}g^{ij}\partial_jf)$ and symbol $\sigma_\Delta(\xi) = -|\xi|_g^2$; more generally every connection Laplacian $\nabla^*\nabla$ and the Hodge Laplacian $dd^*+d^*d$ have symbol $-|\xi|^2\,\mathrm{id}$ and are elliptic. (iii) The Dirac operator of any Dirac bundle has $\sigma_D(\xi) = \xi^\sharp\cdot$ (Clifford multiplication), invertible with inverse $-|\xi|^{-2}\xi^\sharp\cdot$, hence elliptic; in even dimensions the chiral operators $D^\pm:\Gamma(E^\pm)\to\Gamma(E^\mp)$ are elliptic; $d + d^*$ on $\Lambda T^*M$ is elliptic, and in dimension four $d^++d^*:\Omega^1\to\Omega^2_+\oplus\Omega^0$ is elliptic (symbol $\xi\mapsto(\xi\wedge\cdot)^+ - \iota_\xi$, injective on the 4-dimensional $\Lambda^1$ into the 4-dimensional $\Lambda^2_+\oplus\Lambda^0$, hence invertible — computed); proof: (i) direct; (ii) compute $\star d\star df$ in coordinates using $\star dx^j = \sum_i|g|^{1/2}g^{ij}\iota_{\partial_i}(dx^1\wedge\dots\wedge dx^n)$ (from `Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature` (1)), then read off the top-order term; the connection-Laplacian symbol from its local formula; (iii) $D = \sum e_i\cdot\nabla_{e_i}$, the symbol is $\sum\xi(e_i)e_i\cdot = \xi^\sharp\cdot$ by (i) of the symbol page; $d^++d^*$: explicit symbol computation with the bases of $\Lambda^2_+$; spec: mechanism: **ellipticity of the Laplacian is the positive-definiteness of the metric; ellipticity of the Dirac operator is the Clifford relation**.
- `Def - Formal Adjoint of a Differential Operator` — type: definition; source items: A-D5.2.5; prereqs: [Def - Elliptic Differential Operator and Principal Symbol, Def - The L2 Inner Product on Differential Forms, Def - Riemannian Volume Form]; spec: given fibre metrics on $E,F$ and a Riemannian metric on $M$, $L^*:\Gamma(F)\to\Gamma(E)$ is a formal adjoint of $L$ if $\int_M\langle Ls,t\rangle\,\mathrm{vol} = \int_M\langle s,L^*t\rangle\,\mathrm{vol}$ for all $s\in\Gamma_c(E)$, $t\in\Gamma_c(F)$; uniqueness (Lemma, proved: the fundamental lemma for sections — if $\int\langle s,w\rangle = 0$ for all compactly supported smooth $s$ then $w = 0$); examples: $d^*$ (`Thm - Codifferential is the Adjoint of d`), $\nabla^*$, $D^* = D$ for Dirac operators (`Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint`); $(\partial_j)^* = -\partial_j$ on $\mathbb R^n$; non-example: the Hilbert-space adjoint of the unbounded operator $L$ on $L^2$ is a different (larger-domain) object — remark only.
- `Thm - Existence and Ellipticity of the Formal Adjoint` — type: theorem; source items: A-I5.2.2, A-R5.2.3; prereqs: [Def - Formal Adjoint of a Differential Operator, Thm - The Principal Symbol is Well-Defined and Multiplicative, Thm - Existence of Smooth Partitions of Unity, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; statement: Every differential operator $L:\Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(F)$ of order $\ell$ has a unique formal adjoint $L^*$; $L^*$ is a differential operator of order $\ell$ with $\sigma_{L^*}(x,\xi) = (-1)^\ell\sigma_L(x,\xi)^*$ (fibrewise adjoint); $L$ is elliptic if and only if $L^*$ is; $(L^*)^* = L$ and $(L_2L_1)^* = L_1^*L_2^*$; proof: in a chart with orthonormal trivialisations, $\int\langle\sum A_\alpha\partial^\alpha s,t\rangle\rho\,dx = \int\langle s,\sum(-1)^{|\alpha|}\rho^{-1}\partial^\alpha(\rho A_\alpha^*t)\rangle\rho\,dx$ ($\rho = |g|^{1/2}$) by repeated integration by parts (compact supports); glue with a partition of unity ($L^* = \sum_iL_i^*\circ\phi_i$-type formula, uniqueness makes the pieces agree on overlaps); the symbol statement from the top-order term; ellipticity: $\sigma_L(\xi)$ invertible iff its adjoint is; spec: mechanism: **integration by parts moves each derivative across at the cost of a sign, and the top-order coefficients are just transposed**.
- `Thm - Differential Operators are Bounded between Sobolev Spaces` — type: theorem; source items: A-R5.2.2 ((145)), A-I7.1.6 (partial); prereqs: [Def - Elliptic Differential Operator and Principal Symbol, Thm - Sobolev Spaces of Sections on a Compact Manifold via Charts, Def - Formal Adjoint of a Differential Operator]; statement: Let $M$ be compact and $L:\Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(F)$ a differential operator of order $\ell$. For every $k\in\mathbb Z$, $L$ extends uniquely to a bounded linear map $L:H_{k+\ell}(M;E)\to H_k(M;F)$, and the extensions are compatible with the inclusions; for $u\in H_{k+\ell}$ and $\phi\in\Gamma(F)$: $\langle Lu,\phi\rangle = \langle u,L^*\phi\rangle$ (the extension is the weak/distributional action); in particular for $u\in H_m$ (any $m$) the element $Lu\in H_{m-\ell}$ is characterised by $\langle Lu,\phi\rangle = \langle u,L^*\phi\rangle$ for all smooth $\phi$; proof: on the torus, $\partial^\alpha:H_{k+|\alpha|}\to H_k$ has norm $\le1$ (Fourier), multiplication by smooth coefficients is bounded on every $H_k$ (Lemma 1 of the charts page); patch; the pairing identity for smooth $u$ is the definition of $L^*$ and extends by density and continuity of the pairing (duality (iv) of the charts page); spec: mechanism: differentiation costs exactly one order on the Fourier side.
- `Ex - Symbol of the Laplace-Beltrami Operator in Coordinates` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-E5.2.2 (drill of part (ii)); prereqs: [Thm - Laplacians and Dirac Operators are Elliptic]; spec: derive $\Delta f = -|g|^{-1/2}\partial_i(|g|^{1/2}g^{ij}\partial_jf)$ from $\Delta = -\star d\star d$ on functions; verify on the round $S^2$ in spherical coordinates and on the hyperbolic plane; read off the symbol.
- `Ex - Symbols of the Dirac and Chiral Dirac Operators` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-E5.2.3 (drill of part (iii); includes Haydys's sign slip B10 as the "illegal but tempting" step); prereqs: [Thm - Laplacians and Dirac Operators are Elliptic]; spec: compute $\sigma_D(\xi)$ and its inverse; on $\mathbb R^4$ with the quaternionic model compute $\sigma_{\slashed D^+}(\xi) = \xi_0 + i\xi_1 + j\xi_2 + k\xi_3$ and its inverse $\bar\xi/|\xi|^2$ as quaternions.
- `Ex - The Formal Adjoint of a First-Order Operator in Coordinates` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of the adjoint theorem); prereqs: [Thm - Existence and Ellipticity of the Formal Adjoint]; spec: for $L = \sum_jA_j(x)\partial_j + B(x)$ on $\mathbb R^n$ with the Euclidean metric compute $L^* = -\sum_j\partial_j(A_j^*\cdot) + B^*$ and its symbol; specialise to $\bar\partial$ on $\mathbb C$ and to the Dirac operator $\sum\gamma_j\partial_j$ with $\gamma_j$ skew-Hermitian.
- `Ex - Ellipticity Forces Equal Ranks and Even Dimension of Chiral Splittings` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill of the definition); prereqs: [Def - Elliptic Differential Operator and Principal Symbol, Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms]; spec: show an elliptic operator $\Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(F)$ forces $\operatorname{rank}E = \operatorname{rank}F$; show that $d:\Omega^0\to\Omega^1$ on a manifold of dimension $\ge2$ is not elliptic but has injective symbol, and that $d^*:\Omega^1\to\Omega^0$ has surjective symbol; deduce that $d+d^*:\Omega^0\oplus\Omega^2\to\Omega^1$ on a surface is elliptic.
