DG IX: `Def - Orientation of a Vector Space`, `Def - Orientation of a Smooth Manifold`, `Def - Manifold with Boundary and Induced Orientation`, `Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds`, `Thm - A Manifold is Orientable iff it Admits a Nowhere-Vanishing Top Form`. DG X: `Def - Smooth Homotopy of Maps`, `Def - de Rham Cohomology`. Topology: `Thm - Baire Category Theorem`, `Def - Nowhere Dense and Meager`, `Def - Dense Subset`, `Def - Paracompact Space`, `Def - Partition of Unity`, `Thm - Paracompact Has Partitions of Unity` (**flagged** — the partitions of unity used here are smooth ones on Banach manifolds modelled on separable Hilbert spaces, proved as a lemma where needed, or the continuous ones on metrisable spaces, proved as Lemma 1 of `Thm - Homotopy Invariance of the Determinant Line Bundle`), `Thm - Compactness in Metric Spaces (Three Equivalents)`, `Def - Quotient Topology and Identification Map`, `Thm - Universal Property of the Quotient`, `Def - Homotopy`. Linear Algebra IX: `Def - Alternating Multilinear Form`, `Def - Determinant`, `Thm - Determinant is Multiplicative`, `Def - Tensor Product of Vector Spaces`; Linear Algebra VI: `Def - Orthogonal Complement`, `Thm - Orthogonal Decomposition`. RG IV: `Def - Brouwer Degree of a Map` (context; `Thm - Brouwer Degree is a Homotopy Invariant` is flagged — the series' degree theory proves the Brouwer degree's invariance as the finite-dimensional case). AT II: `Def - Path-Connected Space`. Chapter IX pages used throughout: `Def - Fredholm Operator and Index`, `Thm - Closed Range is Automatic for Finite-Dimensional Cokernel`, `Thm - Stabilisation of Fredholm Operators and Continuity of the Kernel`, `Thm - Stability of the Fredholm Property under Small and Compact Perturbations`, `Def - Compact Operator`. Chapter VI anchor: `Def - Chern Classes`, `Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature`, `Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem` (for the characteristic numbers of §10.4). Chapter XII request: `[NEEDED FROM XII: integral cohomology classes pair integrally with the fundamental class of a closed oriented manifold (de Rham–singular comparison for c_1 of a U(1)-bundle), so that the characteristic numbers of §10.4 and the Seiberg–Witten invariant for d > 0 are integers; the series states integrality as bold context until XII supplies it]`.
Design: (1) Banach manifolds are Hausdorff, second countable, modelled on separable Banach spaces; from §10.4 on, Hilbert manifolds. (2) The mod-2 degree and the integer degree share one anchor theorem page, fed by four helper theorem pages (regular values open and dense; local constancy of the count; homogeneity; cobordism along a generic path). (3) Haydys's "second category" is renamed *residual* (countable intersection of open dense sets) with a Convention callout; Baire's theorem is the vault's.
Sections: §10.1 Banach Manifolds, Fredholm Maps, and the Kuranishi Model | §10.2 Sard's Theorem and the Sard–Smale Theorem | §10.3 The Mod-2 Degree and Parametric Transversality | §10.4 Determinant Line Bundles, Orientations, and the Integer Degree | §10.5 The Equivariant Setup, Slices, and Moduli Spaces

### §10.1 Banach Manifolds, Fredholm Maps, and the Kuranishi Model
- `Def - Banach Manifold and Smooth Maps between Banach Spaces` — type: definition (compound: Fréchet derivative and $C^k$/smooth maps between open subsets of Banach spaces; Banach and Hilbert manifolds, tangent spaces, submanifolds; Banach Lie groups and smooth actions); source items: (the standing framework of A §6; feeds A-D6.1.1, A-D6.6.2, A-D7.1.8); prereqs: [Def - Cauchy Sequence and Complete Metric Space, Def - Smooth Manifold, Def - Smooth Map between Manifolds, Def - Lie Group, Def - Smooth Action of a Lie Group]; spec: $F:U\subset X\to Y$ is differentiable at $x$ if there is a bounded linear $d_xF$ with $F(x+h) = F(x) + d_xF\,h + o(\|h\|)$; $C^1$ if $x\mapsto d_xF\in\operatorname{Hom}(X,Y)$ is continuous; $C^k$, $C^\infty$ inductively; chain rule (proved); a *Banach manifold* is a Hausdorff second countable space with an atlas of charts into open subsets of a fixed separable Banach space with smooth transition maps; tangent space $T_xX$ (via curves or charts — Banach version of `Def - The Tangent Space`); *embedded submanifold* = locally a closed complemented subspace in a chart (complemented is automatic in Hilbert spaces and for finite-dimensional or finite-codimensional subspaces); *Banach Lie group* = group and Banach manifold with smooth structure maps; smooth action; examples: Banach spaces, open subsets, $H_k(M;E)$, the affine space $A_0 + H_k(T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)$ (XI), $\mathcal G^{k} = H_k(M;S^1)$ for $2k>n$ (via `Thm - Composition with Analytic Functions on the Sobolev Algebra` and its exercise); non-example: $C^\infty(M)$ with the $C^\infty$ topology is a Fréchet, not Banach, space (Haydys footnote 5); Convention callout: Haydys assumes paracompactness in the Sard–Smale theorem; second countable metrisable spaces are paracompact (the vault's `Thm - Locally Compact σ-Compact Hausdorff is Paracompact` does not apply; the writer proves "second countable + metrisable ⇒ Lindelöf ⇒ (for metric spaces) paracompact" only where needed, via countable subcovers — Sard–Smale uses only the Lindelöf property, which is proved on that page).
- `Thm - Inverse Function Theorem on Banach Spaces` — type: theorem (three parts: inverse function theorem, implicit function theorem, regular value theorem for maps with complemented kernel); source items: A-I6.2.1; prereqs: [Def - Banach Manifold and Smooth Maps between Banach Spaces, Thm - The Contraction Mapping Principle, Thm - Banach–Steinhaus and Open Mapping (Application of Baire)]; statement: (i) Let $F:U\subset X\to Y$ be $C^k$ ($k\ge1$) with $d_{x_0}F$ a bounded linear isomorphism. Then there are open $x_0\in U'\subset U$, $F(x_0)\in V'\subset Y$ with $F:U'\to V'$ a $C^k$ diffeomorphism. (ii) (Implicit function theorem) If $F:X_1\times X_2\to Y$ is $C^k$ with $\partial_2F(a,b)$ an isomorphism and $F(a,b) = 0$, then near $(a,b)$ the zero set is the graph of a $C^k$ map $g$ with $g(a) = b$. (iii) If $F:X\to Y$ is $C^k$ between Banach manifolds, $y$ a regular value, and $\ker d_xF$ is complemented for every $x\in F^{-1}(y)$ (automatic when $d_xF$ is Fredholm), then $F^{-1}(y)$ is a $C^k$ embedded submanifold with $T_xF^{-1}(y) = \ker d_xF$; proof: (i) Lang, *Real and Functional Analysis*, XIV §1 / Dieudonné (10.2.5): reduce to $X = Y$, $d_{x_0}F = \mathrm{id}$; the writer writes the standard contraction argument: $G_y(x) := x - L^{-1}(F(x)-y)$ with $L = d_{x_0}F$ is a contraction on a small ball for $y$ near $F(x_0)$, giving a continuous inverse; differentiability of the inverse via the Neumann series (`Thm - Stabilisation of Fredholm Operators and Continuity of the Kernel`, Lemma 1) and $C^k$ by induction; (ii) apply (i) to $(x_1,x_2)\mapsto(x_1,F(x_1,x_2))$; (iii) local submersion form: with $X = \ker d_xF\oplus X_1$, the map $(k,x_1)\mapsto(k,F(x))$ is a local diffeomorphism by (i); spec: the vault's `Thm - The Inverse Function Theorem` (finite-dimensional) is the model; mechanism: **a map whose linearisation is invertible is invertible near the point, because the nonlinear error is a contraction relative to the linear part**.
- `Def - Fredholm Map and Its Index` — type: definition (anchor); source items: A-D6.1.1 (Definition 161), A-R6.1.1; prereqs: [Def - Banach Manifold and Smooth Maps between Banach Spaces, Def - Fredholm Operator and Index, Thm - Stability of the Fredholm Property under Small and Compact Perturbations]; spec: $F\in C^\infty(X;Y)$ is a *Fredholm map* if $d_xF:T_xX\to T_{F(x)}Y$ is a Fredholm operator for every $x$; $\operatorname{index}d_xF$ is locally constant in $x$ (stability theorem, IX) hence constant on each component; for connected $X$, $\operatorname{index}F$; Convention callout: "smooth" throughout, though $C^{q}$ with $q>\max(\operatorname{index},0)$ suffices for Sard–Smale (Smale's hypothesis — recorded); examples: any smooth map between finite-dimensional manifolds (index $\dim X-\dim Y$); $u\mapsto Lu + N(u)$ for $L$ elliptic and $N$ a lower-order nonlinearity (exercise); the Seiberg–Witten map (XI); non-example: the inclusion $H_1\to H_0$.
- `Def - Regular Value and Transversality for Fredholm Maps` — type: definition (anchor; compound: regular value, critical point/value, transversality to a finite-dimensional submanifold); source items: A-D6.1.2, A-D6.1.4, A-R6.1.2; prereqs: [Def - Fredholm Map and Its Index, Def - Regular and Critical Points, Def - Embedded Submanifold]; spec: $y\in Y$ is a *regular value* of $F$ if $d_xF$ is surjective for every $x\in F^{-1}(y)$ (vacuously if $F^{-1}(y) = \varnothing$); $\operatorname{Crit}(F)$ := points where $d_xF$ is not surjective, critical values $F(\operatorname{Crit}(F))$; for $Z\subset Y$ a finite-dimensional embedded submanifold, $F\pitchfork Z$ if $\operatorname{Im}d_xF + T_zZ = T_zY$ for all $z\in Z$, $x\in F^{-1}(z)$; $Z = \{z\}$ recovers regular values; examples: finite-dimensional examples ($x\mapsto x^2$ on $\mathbb R$: $0$ critical value; $F\pitchfork Z$ for a curve and a line); non-example: a point in the image where $d_xF$ has cokernel $\ne0$.
- `Thm - Kuranishi Model for a Fredholm Map` — type: theorem (anchor); source items: A-T6.1.1 (Theorem 162), A-I6.1.1; prereqs: [Def - Fredholm Map and Its Index, Thm - Inverse Function Theorem on Banach Spaces, Def - Fredholm Operator and Index, Thm - Closed Range is Automatic for Finite-Dimensional Cokernel]; statement: Let $X,Y$ be Banach spaces, $F:X\to Y$ a smooth Fredholm map, $p\in F^{-1}(0)$, $X_0 := \ker d_pF$, $Y_0 := \operatorname{Im}d_pF$, and choose closed complements $X = X_0\oplus X_1$, $Y = Y_0\oplus Y_1$ ($\dim X_0<\infty$, $\dim Y_1<\infty$). Then there are: a diffeomorphism $\phi$ from a neighbourhood of $0\in X$ onto a neighbourhood of $p$ with $\phi(0) = p$; the linear isomorphism $T := d_pF|_{X_1}:X_1\to Y_0$; and a smooth map $f:X\to Y_1$ (defined near $0$), such that $F(\phi(x_0,x_1)) = Tx_1 + f(x_0,x_1)$ for $(x_0,x_1)$ near $0$, with $f(0) = 0$ and $d_0f = 0$. In particular, with $f_0(x_0) := f(x_0,0)$, $\phi$ restricts to a homeomorphism from a neighbourhood of $0$ in $f_0^{-1}(0)\subset X_0$ onto a neighbourhood of $p$ in $F^{-1}(0)$; proof: Haydys omits; Lyapunov–Schmidt: $\Phi(x) := (x_0, \pi_{Y_0}(F(p+x)))\in X_0\oplus Y_0$ has $d_0\Phi = (x_0,x_1)\mapsto(x_0,Tx_1)$, an isomorphism (closed range of $d_pF$ and the open mapping theorem make $T$ an isomorphism onto $Y_0$); the inverse function theorem gives a local inverse $\Psi$; set $\phi(x_0,x_1) := p + \Psi(x_0,Tx_1)$ and $f := \pi_{Y_1}\circ F\circ\phi$; then $\pi_{Y_0}F\phi(x_0,x_1) = Tx_1$ by construction; the "in particular": $F\phi(x_0,x_1) = 0$ iff $Tx_1 = 0$ and $f(x_0,x_1) = 0$ iff $x_1 = 0$ and $f_0(x_0) = 0$; $d_0f = 0$ since $d_pF(X_0) = 0$ and $d_pF(X_1) = Y_0$; spec: Donaldson–Kronheimer §4.2.5; mechanism: **solve the equation in the directions where the linearisation is invertible by the inverse function theorem; what remains is a finite-dimensional equation on the kernel with values in the cokernel**; targets: local structure of moduli spaces near non-regular points (XI, XIII).
- `Thm - Regular Value and Transversality Theorems for Fredholm Maps` — type: theorem (three parts); source items: A-T6.1.2 (Corollary 163), A-T6.1.4 (Theorem 165), A-I6.1.5; prereqs: [Thm - Kuranishi Model for a Fredholm Map, Thm - Inverse Function Theorem on Banach Spaces, Def - Regular Value and Transversality for Fredholm Maps, Def - Smooth Manifold with Boundary]; statement: Let $F:X\to Y$ be a smooth Fredholm map between Banach manifolds. (i) If $y$ is a regular value then $F^{-1}(y)$ is a smooth embedded submanifold of $X$ of dimension $\operatorname{index}F$ (on each component), with $T_xF^{-1}(y) = \ker d_xF$. (ii) If $Z\subset Y$ is a finite-dimensional embedded submanifold and $F\pitchfork Z$, then $F^{-1}(Z)$ is a smooth submanifold of dimension $\operatorname{index}F + \dim Z$. (iii) (Boundary version) If $X$ is a Banach manifold with boundary (modelled on half-spaces $X'\times[0,\infty)$) and $y$ is a regular value of both $F$ and $F|_{\partial X}$, then $F^{-1}(y)$ is a manifold with boundary $\partial F^{-1}(y) = F^{-1}(y)\cap\partial X$; in particular for a smooth homotopy $H:X\times[0,1]\to Y$ with $y$ regular for $H$, $H_0$, $H_1$, $H^{-1}(y)$ is a manifold with boundary $H_0^{-1}(y)\times\{0\}\sqcup H_1^{-1}(y)\times\{1\}$; proof: (i) Haydys's argument: $Y_1 = 0$, so $f = 0$ and $F^{-1}(0)$ is locally $\phi(X_0\times0)$; (ii) reduce to (i) by composing with a local submersion $Y\supset U\to\mathbb R^{\operatorname{codim}}$ cutting out $Z$ (Lemma: a finite-dimensional embedded submanifold is locally the zero set of a submersion to a finite-dimensional space — from the submanifold chart) and using that $F\pitchfork Z$ makes $0$ a regular value of the composite; the dimension count is the direct argument of Guillemin–Pollack: $F^{-1}(Z)$ near $x$ is $(g\circ F)^{-1}(0)$ where $g$ is the local defining submersion of $Z$; $\ker d_x(g\circ F) = d_xF^{-1}(T_zZ)$ has dimension $\dim\ker d_xF + \dim(T_zZ\cap\operatorname{Im}d_xF)$, and transversality gives $\dim(T_zZ\cap\operatorname{Im}d_xF) = \dim Z - \operatorname{codim}\operatorname{Im}d_xF$; so $\dim = \operatorname{index}F + \dim Z$; (iii) the Kuranishi model relative to the boundary (Guillemin–Pollack §2.1 argument transplanted); spec: Haydys: "just as in the finite-dimensional case" ([GP10]); mechanism: the Kuranishi model with zero cokernel is a chart.
- `Ex - Fredholm Maps have Locally Constant Index` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill of the definition); prereqs: [Def - Fredholm Map and Its Index]; spec: from the openness of $\operatorname{Fred}$ and local constancy of the index deduce that $x\mapsto\operatorname{index}d_xF$ is constant on components; exhibit a smooth map between Banach manifolds with two components of different index.
- `Ex - Kuranishi Models in the Plane` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of the Kuranishi model); prereqs: [Thm - Kuranishi Model for a Fredholm Map]; spec: for $F:\mathbb R^2\to\mathbb R$, $F(x,y) = y - x^2$ at $p = 0$ and $F(x,y) = xy$ at $p = 0$, identify $X_0,X_1,Y_0,Y_1$, construct $\phi$ explicitly and compute $f_0$; observe that $f_0 = 0$ in the first case (regular value) and $f_0(x_0) = $ a quadratic form in the second (the zero set is a cone).
- `Ex - Semilinear Elliptic Maps are Fredholm` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: (additional; the prototype of the Seiberg–Witten map); prereqs: [Def - Fredholm Map and Its Index, Thm - Elliptic Operators on Closed Manifolds are Fredholm, Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem, Thm - Rellich Compactness Theorem]; spec: on a closed $n$-manifold with $2k>n$ show $F:H_{k+2}\to H_k$, $F(u) := \Delta u + u^3$, is a smooth Fredholm map of index $0$: smoothness via the Sobolev algebra; $d_uF = \Delta + 3u^2$ is $\Delta$ plus a compact operator ($H_{k+2}\to H_{k+1}\to H_k$ by Rellich and multiplication), so Fredholm of index $\operatorname{index}\Delta = 0$.
- `Ex - Transversality in Finite Dimensions` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill of the definition); prereqs: [Def - Regular Value and Transversality for Fredholm Maps, Thm - Regular Value and Transversality Theorems for Fredholm Maps]; spec: decide transversality of the parabola $y = x^2$ to the lines $y = c$ for each $c$, and of the map $F(x,y,z) = (x^2+y^2, z)$ to the circle $\{(1,t)\}$; compute the dimensions of the preimages via the formula $\operatorname{index}F + \dim Z$.
- `Exercise Index - §10.1 Banach Manifolds, Fredholm Maps, and the Kuranishi Model` — lists: [Ex - Fredholm Maps have Locally Constant Index, Ex - Kuranishi Models in the Plane, Ex - Semilinear Elliptic Maps are Fredholm, Ex - Transversality in Finite Dimensions]

### §10.2 Sard's Theorem and the Sard–Smale Theorem
- `Thm - Sard's Theorem for Smooth Maps` — type: theorem (replaces the flagged `Thm - Sard's Theorem`); source items: A-I6.1.2; prereqs: [Def - Set of Measure Zero on a Manifold, Def - Regular and Critical Points, Def - Smooth Manifold, Thm - Heine–Borel Theorem]; statement: Let $f:U\to\mathbb R^n$ be smooth, $U\subset\mathbb R^m$ open. Then the set of critical values $f(\operatorname{Crit}(f))$ has Lebesgue measure zero in $\mathbb R^n$. Consequently, for a smooth map $f:M\to N$ between manifolds ($M$ second countable), the critical values have measure zero in $N$ and the regular values are dense; proof: Milnor, *Topology from the Differentiable Viewpoint*, §3 (following Pontryagin), every step written out: **Step 0** (measure zero in $\mathbb R^n$ is defined via countable covers by cubes; countable unions of null sets are null; smooth maps between open subsets of $\mathbb R^n$ carry null sets to null sets — Lemma 1, via the mean value inequality on compact subsets and Lindelöf); **Step 1** (Fubini-type lemma, used in Milnor's proof that $f(C\setminus C_1)$ is null: a compact set $A\subset\mathbb R^n$ meeting every hyperplane $\{x_1 = c\}$ in a set of $(n-1)$-measure zero has measure zero — Lemma 2, proved for compact sets by uniform covering; for the induction on $m$); **Step 2** (induction on $m$; $m = 0$ trivial); **Step 3** ($f(C\setminus C_1)$ null, $C_1$ = points where all first partials vanish: near $x\in C\setminus C_1$ some $\partial_if_j\ne0$; following Milnor, take $h(x) = (f_1(x),x_2,\dots,x_m)$ a local diffeomorphism, then $g = f\circ h^{-1}$ maps hyperplanes $\{t\}\times\mathbb R^{m-1}$ into $\{t\}\times\mathbb R^{n-1}$ and the critical points of $g$ in a hyperplane are critical points of the restriction $g^t$, so by induction each slice of the critical-value set is null, and Lemma 2 applies); **Step 4** ($f(C_k\setminus C_{k+1})$ null: similar with a $(k+1)$-st derivative as the new coordinate); **Step 5** ($f(C_k)$ null for $k\ge m/n - 1$: Taylor's theorem with remainder gives $|f(x+h)-f(x)|\le c|h|^{k+1}$ for $x\in C_k$, so a cube of side $\delta$ containing points of $C_k$ maps into a cube of side $\le c'\delta^{k+1}$; subdividing a unit cube into $r^m$ subcubes gives total image volume $\le r^m(c'/r)^{(k+1)n}\to0$ when $(k+1)n>m$); the manifold statement via countable charts (Lindelöf); density of regular values from "null ⇒ empty interior"; spec: reference also Lee, *Introduction to Smooth Manifolds*, 2nd ed., Thm 6.10 and Sternberg *Lectures on Differential Geometry* II.3; mechanism: **at a critical point the map crushes at least one direction; iterated Taylor expansion shows the image of the deeply-critical set is thin, and induction on dimension handles the rest**; targets: Sard–Smale, transversality, degree theory, $\pi_k(S^m)$ for $k<m$ (XII).
- `Def - Residual Set and Generic Property` — type: definition; source items: A-D6.1.3, A-I6.1.4; prereqs: [Def - Dense Subset, Def - Nowhere Dense and Meager, Thm - Baire Category Theorem, Def - Banach Manifold and Smooth Maps between Banach Spaces]; spec: a subset of a topological space is *residual* (Haydys: "of second category") if it contains a countable intersection of open dense subsets; a property holds *generically* if it holds on a residual set; by Baire's theorem (vault, unflagged) a residual subset of a complete metric space, hence of a Banach manifold (locally complete-metrisable; the writer shows a Banach manifold is a Baire space via `Thm - Baire Category Theorem` applied in charts together with second countability — Lemma), is dense; Convention callout: Baire's own "second category" means "not meagre"; Haydys's usage is "comeagre/residual" — the series says residual; examples: irrationals in $\mathbb R$; the complement of a countable union of proper closed submanifolds; non-example: a dense set need not be residual (rationals).
- `Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem` — type: theorem (anchor); source items: A-T6.1.3 (Theorem 164), A-I6.1.3, A-I6.3.3, A-I7.1.14 (partial); prereqs: [Thm - Sard's Theorem for Smooth Maps, Thm - Kuranishi Model for a Fredholm Map, Def - Residual Set and Generic Property, Def - Fredholm Map and Its Index, Thm - Regular Value and Transversality Theorems for Fredholm Maps, Thm - Proper Maps are Closed and Fredholm Maps are Locally Proper]; statement: Let $F:X\to Y$ be a smooth Fredholm map between (second countable) Banach manifolds. Then the set of regular values of $F$ is residual in $Y$, in particular dense; proof: Smale, *An infinite dimensional version of Sard's theorem*, Amer. J. Math. 87 (1965), Thm 1.3; Abraham–Robbin, *Transversal Mappings and Flows*, §16: **Step 1** (local properness): each $p\in X$ has an open neighbourhood $U_p$, a Kuranishi chart around it, such that $F|_{\overline{U_p}}$ is proper (in the chart $F\phi(x_0,x_1) = Tx_1 + f(x_0,x_1)$, the preimage of a compact set $K$ within a closed bounded neighbourhood is $\{(x_0,x_1) : Tx_1\in\pi_{Y_0}K, f(x_0,x_1)\in\pi_{Y_1}K\}$, whose $x_1$-component lies in the compact $T^{-1}\pi_{Y_0}K$ and whose $x_0$-component lies in a closed bounded subset of the finite-dimensional $X_0$; a closed subset of a product of two compact sets is compact — written out with the shrinking of $U_p$ to make the model hold on $\overline{U_p}$); **Step 2** (critical values of $F|_{\overline{U_p}}$ are closed: $\operatorname{Crit}(F)$ is closed in $X$ because surjectivity of Fredholm operators is an open condition (`Thm - Stabilisation of Fredholm Operators and Continuity of the Kernel` (ii)); a proper continuous map to a metrisable space is closed (next Thm page); so $F(\operatorname{Crit}(F)\cap\overline{U_p})$ is closed); **Step 3** (critical values of $F|_{U_p}$ have empty interior: in the chart, $(y_0,y_1)$ is a critical value of $F\phi$ iff $y_1$ is a critical value of the finite-dimensional map $x_0\mapsto f(x_0,T^{-1}y_0)$ — proved by the surjectivity computation: $d(F\phi)(v_0,v_1) = Tv_1 + d_{x_0}f\,v_0 + d_{x_1}f\,v_1$ is onto $Y_0\oplus Y_1$ iff $d_{x_0}f$ is onto $Y_1$ — so by Sard's theorem each slice $\{y_0\}\times Y_1$ meets the critical-value set in a null set, hence the critical-value set contains no open set (an open set would contain a product of open sets, whose $Y_1$-slice has positive measure)); **Step 4** (assemble: $R_p := Y\setminus F(\operatorname{Crit}(F)\cap\overline{U_p})$ is open and dense; $X$ is second countable hence Lindelöf, so countably many $U_{p_i}$ cover $X$; the regular values of $F$ are exactly $\bigcap_iR_{p_i}$, a residual set; dense by Baire); spec: Haydys cites [BT03] for Sard and omits the proof; mechanism: **a Fredholm map is, in Kuranishi charts, a finite-dimensional map in the kernel–cokernel directions plus an isomorphism; Sard's theorem applies slice by slice, and local properness makes the critical values closed so that "null in every slice" becomes "nowhere dense"**; What Makes This Hard: null sets have no meaning in a Banach space — one needs closedness (properness) to convert measure-zero slices into a topological statement.
- `Ex - Sard's Theorem for Polynomial Maps` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Sard's Theorem for Smooth Maps]; spec: find the critical values of $f(x,y) = (x^2 - y^2, 2xy)$ and of $f(x,y) = x^3 - 3xy^2$; verify they are null; show that for $f:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R^2$ the whole image is null (Step 5 with $k = 0$: every point is critical).
- `Ex - The Deeply Critical Set and Taylor Expansion` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of Step 5); prereqs: [Thm - Sard's Theorem for Smooth Maps]; spec: prove that for a smooth $f:\mathbb R^m\to\mathbb R^n$ and $C_k$ the set where all partials of order $\le k$ vanish, $f(C_k\cap\bar B)$ has measure zero whenever $(k+1)n>m$, with explicit constants from Taylor's theorem; deduce that a smooth $f:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R^2$ cannot be surjective, but a continuous one can (Peano curve — bold context).
- `Ex - Residual Sets and Baire's Theorem in Banach Spaces` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of the definition and its Baire property); prereqs: [Def - Residual Set and Generic Property, Thm - Baire Category Theorem]; spec: prove that a countable intersection of residual sets is residual and that a residual subset of a Banach space is dense and uncountable; show that the set of $y\in Y$ for which a given countable family of Fredholm maps $F_i$ all have $y$ regular is residual.
- `Ex - Regular Values of a Fredholm Map of Negative Index` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (consequence of Sard–Smale); prereqs: [Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem, Thm - Regular Value and Transversality Theorems for Fredholm Maps]; spec: show that if $\operatorname{index}F<0$ then a residual set of $y$ is *not* in the image of $F$ (regular values have empty preimage); apply to $F:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R^2$ and to an elliptic operator of negative index plus a smooth nonlinearity.
- `Exercise Index - §10.2 Sard's Theorem and the Sard–Smale Theorem` — lists: [Ex - Sard's Theorem for Polynomial Maps, Ex - The Deeply Critical Set and Taylor Expansion, Ex - Residual Sets and Baire's Theorem in Banach Spaces, Ex - Regular Values of a Fredholm Map of Negative Index]

### §10.3 The Mod-2 Degree and Parametric Transversality
- `Thm - Proper Maps are Closed and Fredholm Maps are Locally Proper` — type: theorem (two parts); source items: A-D6.2.1 (→ inside, Notation: proper map), A-I6.2.4, (the local-properness half of A-T6.1.3's Step 1, placed here because §10.2's `Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem` lists it as a prerequisite); prereqs: [Def - Banach Manifold and Smooth Maps between Banach Spaces, Thm - Kuranishi Model for a Fredholm Map, Thm - Compactness in Metric Spaces (Three Equivalents), Def - Fredholm Map and Its Index]; statement: (i) A continuous map $F:X\to Y$ between topological spaces with $Y$ locally compact and Hausdorff and $X$ Hausdorff, which is *proper* (preimages of compact sets are compact), is closed (maps closed sets to closed sets); the same holds when $Y$ is any metrisable space. (ii) A smooth Fredholm map $F:X\to Y$ between Banach manifolds is *locally proper*: every $p\in X$ has an open neighbourhood $U_p$ with compact closure in a chart such that $F|_{\overline{U_p}}$ is proper; proof: (i) let $C\subset X$ be closed and $y\in\overline{F(C)}$; take a compact neighbourhood $K$ of $y$ (or, in the metrisable case, a sequence $y_n = F(c_n)\to y$ and $K = \{y_n\}\cup\{y\}$, compact); $F^{-1}(K)$ is compact, $C\cap F^{-1}(K)$ is compact, its image is compact hence closed and contains points of $F(C)$ arbitrarily close to $y$, so $y\in F(C)$ (the writer gives the sequential argument in the metrisable case and the neighbourhood argument in the locally compact case); (ii) in a Kuranishi chart $F\phi(x_0,x_1) = Tx_1 + f(x_0,x_1)$ ($T:X_1\to Y_0$ an isomorphism, $\dim X_0<\infty$, $\dim Y_1<\infty$); shrink to a closed ball $\overline{U_p}$ on which the model holds; the preimage of a compact $K$ is closed in $\overline{U_p}$ and its $x_1$-component lies in the compact $T^{-1}\pi_{Y_0}(K)$, its $x_0$-component in a closed bounded subset of the finite-dimensional $X_0$, hence compact; spec: **used by `Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem` (§10.2), which lists this page as a prerequisite; logically it precedes that use, and the orchestrator should sequence this page ahead of Sard–Smale**; mechanism: **properness turns the topology of the target into control over the source, and a Fredholm map is finite-dimensional in the kernel–cokernel directions after the Kuranishi split**.
- `Def - Mod-2 Degree of a Proper Fredholm Map` — type: definition (anchor); source items: A-D6.2.2, A-R6.2.1 (→ inside, Motivation: the finite-dimensional degree counts preimages of a regular value); prereqs: [Def - Fredholm Map and Its Index, Thm - Proper Maps are Closed and Fredholm Maps are Locally Proper, Def - Regular Value and Transversality for Fredholm Maps, Thm - Regular Value and Transversality Theorems for Fredholm Maps]; spec: let $F:X\to Y$ be a proper Fredholm map of index $0$ between (second countable) Banach manifolds with $Y$ connected; for a regular value $y$, $F^{-1}(y)$ is a compact $0$-manifold (dimension $= \operatorname{index}F = 0$ by the regular-value theorem, compact by properness), hence a finite set; $\deg_2 F := \#F^{-1}(y)\bmod 2 \in\mathbb Z/2$; the **well-definedness (independence of the regular value) and homotopy invariance are proved on `Thm - Well-Definedness and Homotopy Invariance of the Degree` (§10.4)**, which treats the mod-2 and the integer degree together; existence of a regular value is `Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem`; examples: a finite-dimensional proper map $S^n\to S^n$ (Brouwer degree mod 2); $z\mapsto z^k$ on $S^1$ ($\deg_2 = k\bmod 2$); a linear Fredholm isomorphism ($\deg_2 = 1$); non-example: a non-proper map (the count is not locally constant — e.g. $\arctan:\mathbb R\to(-\tfrac\pi2,\tfrac\pi2)$ has empty fibres over nearby regular values); Convention callout: Haydys writes the mod-2 degree for maps between paracompact Banach manifolds; the series works with second countable (hence, being metrisable, paracompact) manifolds and records the reduction.
- `Thm - Regular Values of a Proper Fredholm Map are Open and Dense` — type: theorem (helper 1 of the degree anchor); source items: A-T6.2.1 (Step 1); prereqs: [Def - Mod-2 Degree of a Proper Fredholm Map, Thm - Proper Maps are Closed and Fredholm Maps are Locally Proper, Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem, Thm - Stabilisation of Fredholm Operators and Continuity of the Kernel]; statement: For a proper Fredholm map $F:X\to Y$ between second countable Banach manifolds, the set of regular values is open and dense in $Y$; proof: density is `Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem`; openness: $\operatorname{Crit}(F) = \{x : d_xF\text{ not surjective}\}$ is closed in $X$ (surjectivity of a Fredholm operator is an open condition, `Thm - Stabilisation of Fredholm Operators and Continuity of the Kernel` (ii)), and $F$ proper is closed (previous page), so $F(\operatorname{Crit}(F))$ is closed, and its complement — the regular values — is open (a regular value with empty preimage is interior to the regular-value set because $F$ is closed and $F(\operatorname{Crit}F)$ is closed); spec: helper 1 of `Thm - Well-Definedness and Homotopy Invariance of the Degree`; mechanism: **critical points are closed by openness of surjectivity, and a proper map carries closed sets to closed sets, so critical values are closed**.
- `Thm - The Degree Count is Locally Constant on Regular Values` — type: theorem (helper 2); source items: A-T6.2.1 (Step 2); prereqs: [Def - Mod-2 Degree of a Proper Fredholm Map, Thm - Inverse Function Theorem on Banach Spaces, Thm - Proper Maps are Closed and Fredholm Maps are Locally Proper]; statement: Let $F:X\to Y$ be a proper Fredholm map of index $0$ and $y$ a regular value with $F^{-1}(y) = \{x_1,\dots,x_k\}$. Then there are disjoint open $V_j\ni x_j$ and an open $U\ni y$ with $F^{-1}(U)\subset\bigsqcup_jV_j$, $F:V_j\to U$ a diffeomorphism; hence every $y'\in U$ is regular with $\#F^{-1}(y') = k$; the count $y\mapsto\#F^{-1}(y)$ is locally constant on the open set of regular values; proof: at each $x_j$, $d_{x_j}F$ is a Fredholm isomorphism (index $0$, surjective ⇒ injective), so the inverse function theorem (Banach version) gives a local diffeomorphism $F:V_j\to U_j$; **the properness step Bär leaves implicit is written out**: shrink $U\subset\bigcap_jF(V_j)$ so that $F^{-1}(U)\subset\bigsqcup V_j$ — if not, there are $y_n\to y$ and $x_n\in F^{-1}(y_n)\setminus\bigsqcup V_j$; $\{y_n\}\cup\{y\}$ is compact, so by properness $\{x_n\}$ has a convergent subsequence $x_n\to x_\infty$ with $F(x_\infty) = y$, so $x_\infty = x_j$ for some $j$, contradicting $x_n\notin V_j$ eventually; spec: helper 2; mechanism: **near a regular value a proper index-zero Fredholm map is a finite covering, so the number of sheets is locally constant**.
- `Thm - Transitivity of the Group of Diffeomorphisms Isotopic to the Identity` — type: theorem (helper 3, homogeneity); source items: A-T6.2.1 (Steps 4–5), A-R6.2.1, A-I6.2.3; prereqs: [Def - Banach Manifold and Smooth Maps between Banach Spaces, Def - Path-Connected Space, Thm - Fundamental Theorem on Flows, Def - Bump Function and Smooth Cutoff]; statement: (i) (Isotopy lemma) For a Banach space $B$ and $x$ in the open unit ball there is a diffeomorphism $\phi$ of $B$ with $\phi(0) = x$, $\phi = \mathrm{id}$ outside the ball of radius $2$, and a smooth isotopy $\phi_t$ ($\phi_0 = \mathrm{id}$, $\phi_1 = \phi$) fixing that exterior. (ii) For a connected Banach manifold $Y$ and $y_1,y_2\in Y$ there is a diffeomorphism $\phi\in\operatorname{Diff}_0(Y)$ (isotopic to the identity, compactly supported) with $\phi(y_1) = y_2$; proof: (i) Bär's explicit construction reduced to finite dimensions: write $B = V\oplus V'$ with $V$ finite-dimensional containing $x$; the finite-dimensional isotopy $\psi_t:V\to V$ moving $0$ to $x$ inside the unit ball (Milnor, *Topology from the Differentiable Viewpoint*, p. 22 — proved here as Lemma 1 via the flow of a compactly supported vector field $Z$ with $Z(0) = x$, `Thm - Fundamental Theorem on Flows`, cut off by a bump function, `Def - Bump Function and Smooth Cutoff`); then $\phi_t(v,v') := \psi_{t\chi(|v'|)}(v) + v'$ with $\chi(0) = 1$, $\chi = 0$ for $r\ge1$; the writer verifies $\|\phi_t(v,v')\|\le 2$ on the unit ball and $\phi_t = \mathrm{id}$ where $\|v\|+\|v'\|\ge2$; (ii) the relation "$\exists\phi\in\operatorname{Diff}_0(Y)$ with $\phi(y_1) = y_2$" is open (by (i) in a chart) and its classes partition $Y$; each class is open, so by connectedness there is one class; spec: helper 3; **the vault has no `Def - Brouwer Degree of a Map` isotopy machinery in a form usable here; the isotopy lemma is proved from scratch**; mechanism: **any point of the unit ball can be reached from the centre by a compactly supported flow, and a connected manifold is homogeneous under such flows**.
- `Def - Parametric Family of Fredholm Maps` — type: definition (compound: the hypotheses (A)–(D); the parametric-count definition of the degree; path spaces as Banach manifolds); source items: A-D6.3.1, A-D6.3.2, A-R6.3.1, A-R6.3.3, A-I6.3.2; prereqs: [Def - Fredholm Map and Its Index, Def - Regular Value and Transversality for Fredholm Maps, Def - Mod-2 Degree of a Proper Fredholm Map, Def - Banach Manifold and Smooth Maps between Banach Spaces]; spec: a connected Banach manifold $W$ (parameters) and a smooth Fredholm $\mathcal F:X\times W\to Y$ with (A) each $\mathcal F_w := \mathcal F(\cdot,w)$ Fredholm; (B) $\mathcal F_{w_0} = F$ for some $w_0$; (C) $y$ a regular value of $\mathcal F$; (D) each $\mathcal F_w$ proper; the *generic-parameter degree* $\deg_2 F := \#\mathcal F_w^{-1}(y)\bmod 2$ for any $w$ in the residual set $W_0$ of good parameters (well-definedness on the next-but-one page); the path space $\Gamma(w_1,w_2) := \{\gamma\in C^k([1,2];W) : \gamma(1) = w_1,\gamma(2) = w_2\}$ is a Banach manifold (the writer proves the model: $C^k([1,2];W)$ is a Banach manifold with charts from those of $W$, `Def - Banach Manifold and Smooth Maps between Banach Spaces`; the fixed-endpoint condition cuts out a closed affine subspace); Axiom Motivation (R6.3.1): in equivariant problems the value $y$ must be a fixed point of a group action and cannot be moved, so one perturbs the map through $w$ instead of the value — this is exactly the Seiberg–Witten setup (XI), where $\eta$ is the parameter; examples: $\mathcal F(u,w) = \Delta u + u^3 - w$ ($w\in Y$ the value, recovering ordinary Sard–Smale); non-example: a family failing (D) (non-proper $\mathcal F_w$) has no well-defined count.
- `Thm - Parametric Transversality` — type: theorem (anchor; two parts); source items: A-T6.3.1 (Lemma 169), A-I6.3.1, A-I6.3.3; prereqs: [Def - Parametric Family of Fredholm Maps, Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem, Thm - Regular Value and Transversality Theorems for Fredholm Maps, Thm - Stabilisation of Fredholm Operators and Continuity of the Kernel]; statement: Under hypotheses (A)–(C), let $\mathcal Z := \mathcal F^{-1}(y)$ (a Banach submanifold by (C) and the regular-value theorem) and $\pi:\mathcal Z\to W$ the restriction of the projection. Then (i) $\pi$ is a Fredholm map with $\operatorname{index}\pi = \operatorname{index}\mathcal F_w =: \operatorname{index}F$; and $w\in W$ is a regular value of $\pi$ if and only if $y$ is a regular value of $\mathcal F_w$; (ii) the set $W_0 := \{w : y\text{ is a regular value of }\mathcal F_w\}$ is residual in $W$, hence dense; proof: (i) at $z = (x,w)\in\mathcal Z$, $T_z\mathcal Z = \ker d_z\mathcal F$; the writer runs the snake-lemma/linear-algebra argument on the surjection $d_z\mathcal F = (d_x\mathcal F_w,\partial_w\mathcal F):T_xX\oplus T_wW\to T_yY$: $\ker d\pi_z = \ker d_x\mathcal F_w\times\{0\}$ (finite-dimensional) and the map $\operatorname{coker}d_x\mathcal F_w\to\operatorname{coker}d\pi_z$ induced by the inclusion is an isomorphism (Lemma 1, a diagram chase using surjectivity of $d_z\mathcal F$), so $\pi$ is Fredholm with $\dim\ker d\pi - \dim\operatorname{coker}d\pi = \dim\ker d_x\mathcal F_w - \dim\operatorname{coker}d_x\mathcal F_w = \operatorname{index}F$; the equivalence "$w$ regular for $\pi$ ⟺ $y$ regular for $\mathcal F_w$" from $\operatorname{coker}d\pi_z\cong\operatorname{coker}d_x\mathcal F_w$; (ii) apply `Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem` to $\pi$; spec: **Haydys omits the proof (Lemma 169); the diagram chase is Lemma 1 and is the crux**; mechanism: **the universal zero set fibres over the parameters by a Fredholm map whose cokernel at a point is the cokernel of the individual map, so Sard–Smale on the projection is genericity of regular parameters**; targets: generic smoothness of the Seiberg–Witten moduli space (XI).
- `Thm - The Generic Parametric Count Defines the Mod-2 Degree` — type: theorem; source items: A-T6.3.2, A-R6.3.2; prereqs: [Def - Parametric Family of Fredholm Maps, Thm - Parametric Transversality, Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem, Thm - Regular Value and Transversality Theorems for Fredholm Maps, Thm - Well-Definedness and Homotopy Invariance of the Degree]; statement: Under (A)–(D) with $\operatorname{index}F = 0$: for $w_1,w_2\in W_0$, $\#\mathcal F_{w_1}^{-1}(y) = \#\mathcal F_{w_2}^{-1}(y)\bmod 2$; the common value equals $\deg_2 F$ of `Def - Mod-2 Degree of a Proper Fredholm Map`; proof: two routes, both given: (route 1) $W$ connected gives a path from $w_0$ to $w$, so $\mathcal F_w\simeq\mathcal F_{w_0} = F$ through proper index-0 Fredholm maps, and `Thm - Well-Definedness and Homotopy Invariance of the Degree` gives $\deg_2\mathcal F_w = \deg_2 F$; (route 2, self-contained) $y$ is a regular value of $\mathbb F:X\times\Gamma(w_1,w_2)\times[1,2]\to Y$, $\mathbb F(x,\gamma,t) := \mathcal F(x,\gamma(t))$ ("clear", written out on the page); apply `Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem` to the projection $\mathbb F^{-1}(y)\to\Gamma(w_1,w_2)$ to get, for generic $\gamma$, that $M_\gamma := \{(x,t) : \mathcal F(x,\gamma(t)) = y\}$ is a compact (by (D), the writer supplies compactness) $1$-manifold with boundary $\mathcal F_{w_1}^{-1}(y)\sqcup\mathcal F_{w_2}^{-1}(y)$, so the boundary has an even number of points (`Thm - Compact One-Manifolds have an Even Number of Boundary Points`, proved on the cobordism page in §10.4), giving equal parities; spec: **Haydys leaves compactness of $M_\gamma$ and the boundary statement asserted; both are supplied (compactness from (D) via a diagonal/limit argument, the boundary from the regular-value-with-boundary theorem)**; mechanism: **two generic parameters are joined by a generic path whose universal zero set is a compact cobordism between the two fibres**.
- `Ex - The Mod-2 Degree of a Complex Polynomial Map` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: (drill); prereqs: [Def - Mod-2 Degree of a Proper Fredholm Map]; spec: for $p:\mathbb C\to\mathbb C$ a polynomial of degree $k$, viewed as a proper map $\mathbb R^2\to\mathbb R^2$, compute $\deg_2 p = k\bmod 2$ from a regular value with $k$ preimages (fundamental theorem of algebra); verify $\deg_2$ is unchanged under $p\mapsto p + c$ (homotopy through proper maps) but that $\deg_2(z\mapsto z + \bar z^2/(1+|z|))$-type non-proper perturbations break the count.
- `Ex - Properness is Necessary for the Degree` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of the properness hypothesis); prereqs: [Def - Mod-2 Degree of a Proper Fredholm Map, Thm - The Degree Count is Locally Constant on Regular Values]; spec: exhibit a non-proper Fredholm map of index $0$ (e.g. an embedding $\mathbb R\hookrightarrow\mathbb R^2$ composed to a chart, or $\arctan:\mathbb R\to(-\tfrac\pi2,\tfrac\pi2)$) whose preimage count jumps between $0$ and $1$ across regular values; identify exactly which step of `Thm - The Degree Count is Locally Constant on Regular Values` fails.
- `Ex - The Isotopy Lemma in the Plane` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-I6.2.3 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Transitivity of the Group of Diffeomorphisms Isotopic to the Identity]; spec: for $x$ in the open unit disc of $\mathbb R^2$ write down explicitly a compactly supported vector field whose time-one flow sends $0$ to $x$ and is the identity outside the disc of radius $2$; verify the isotopy fixes that exterior; conclude that $\operatorname{Diff}_0(\mathbb R^2)$ acts transitively on $\mathbb R^2$.
- `Ex - Parametric Transversality for a Family of Elliptic Equations` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: (drill toward XI); prereqs: [Thm - Parametric Transversality, Ex - Semilinear Elliptic Maps are Fredholm]; spec: for $\mathcal F:H_{k+2}(M)\times C^\infty(M)\to H_k(M)$, $\mathcal F(u,w) = \Delta u + u^3 - w$ on a closed surface, verify (A)–(C), show $0$ is a regular value of $\mathcal F$ (the $w$-derivative is $-\mathrm{id}$, surjective), and conclude that for a residual set of $w$ the equation $\Delta u + u^3 = w$ has $0$ as a regular value; identify $W_0$ with the regular values of the projection.
- `Exercise Index - §10.3 The Mod-2 Degree and Parametric Transversality` — lists: [Ex - The Mod-2 Degree of a Complex Polynomial Map, Ex - Properness is Necessary for the Degree, Ex - The Isotopy Lemma in the Plane, Ex - Parametric Transversality for a Family of Elliptic Equations]

### §10.4 Determinant Line Bundles, Orientations, and the Integer Degree
- `Thm - Determinant Lines and Short Exact Sequences` — type: theorem (two parts); source items: A-X6.4.1 (Exercise 171); prereqs: [Def - Alternating Multilinear Form, Def - Tensor Product of Vector Spaces, Thm - Fundamental Theorem of Linear Maps, Def - Quotient Space]; statement: For finite-dimensional real vector spaces write $\det V := \Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}V$ (top exterior power, a line). (i) A short exact sequence $0\to U_0\to U_1\to U_2\to0$ induces a canonical isomorphism $\det U_1\cong\det U_0\otimes\det U_2$. (ii) Any exact sequence $0\to U_0\to U_1\to\cdots\to U_k\to0$ induces a canonical isomorphism $\bigotimes_{j\text{ even}}\det U_j\cong\bigotimes_{j\text{ odd}}\det U_j$; proof: (i) choose a splitting $U_1\cong U_0\oplus U_2$ and use $\det(A\oplus B)\cong\det A\otimes\det B$ (from a basis, with the sign of the shuffle absorbed into the canonical identification — the writer checks independence of the splitting: two splittings differ by a map $U_2\to U_0$, which acts trivially on $\det$ because it is unipotent, $\det = 1$); (ii) split the long exact sequence into short exact sequences $0\to Z_j\to U_j\to Z_{j+1}\to0$ with $Z_j := \operatorname{im}(U_{j-1}\to U_j) = \ker(U_j\to U_{j+1})$ and multiply the isomorphisms of (i), the interior $\det Z_j$ cancelling in pairs; spec: mechanism: **the top exterior power is multiplicative on exact sequences because a splitting adds dimensions and a unipotent change of splitting has determinant one**; targets: the determinant line of a Fredholm operator (next page); the orientation of the Seiberg–Witten moduli space (XI).
- `Def - Determinant Line Bundle of a Family of Fredholm Operators` — type: definition (anchor; compound: the determinant line of one operator; continuous families; the line bundle; homotopy of families); source items: A-D6.4.1, A-D6.4.2, A-D6.4.3 (Definition 172), A-R6.4.1, A-R6.4.2; prereqs: [Def - Fredholm Operator and Index, Thm - Determinant Lines and Short Exact Sequences, Thm - Stabilisation of Fredholm Operators and Continuity of the Kernel]; spec: for a Fredholm operator $T:X\to Y$ of Hilbert spaces, $\det T := \Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}\ker T\otimes\Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}(\operatorname{coker}T)^*$, a real line; a *continuous family* is a norm-continuous $P\to\operatorname{Fred}(X;Y)$, $p\mapsto T_p$; the *determinant line bundle* $\det T\to P$ has fibres $\det T_p$; two families are *homotopic* if they extend to a continuous family over $P\times[0,1]$; Axiom Motivation (R6.4.1): the integer degree needs orientations, and in infinite dimensions the finite-dimensional orientation line $\Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}$ is replaced by the determinant line, which does not jump even though $\dim\ker$ and $\dim\operatorname{coker}$ do (R6.4.2, via the stabilisation sequence (170)); local triviality is the next page; examples: a family of isomorphisms ($\det T_p = \mathbb R$ canonically, trivial); the family $t\mapsto t\cdot\mathrm{id}$ on $\mathbb R$ ($\det$ jumps in $\ker$ at $t = 0$ but the line bundle is trivial — computed); non-example: the naive bundle $\Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}\ker T_p$ alone is not locally trivial (its rank jumps).
- `Thm - Local Triviality of the Determinant Line Bundle` — type: theorem; source items: A-T6.4.1, A-I6.4.1, A-I6.4.2; prereqs: [Def - Determinant Line Bundle of a Family of Fredholm Operators, Thm - Stabilisation of Fredholm Operators and Continuity of the Kernel, Thm - Determinant Lines and Short Exact Sequences]; statement: For a continuous family $\{T_p\}_{p\in P}$ of Fredholm operators, $\det T\to P$ is a locally trivial real line bundle; proof: near $p_0$ choose a finite-dimensional $V\subset Y$ with $\operatorname{Im}T_{p_0} + V = Y$; the stabilised operators $T_{p,V}:X\oplus V\to Y$, $(x,v)\mapsto T_px + v$, are surjective at $p_0$, hence on a neighbourhood $W$ (`Thm - Stabilisation of Fredholm Operators and Continuity of the Kernel` (ii)), with $\ker T_{p,V}$ of constant dimension forming a continuous (indeed locally trivial) finite-rank vector bundle over $W$ (`Thm - Stabilisation ...` (iii)); the exact sequence $0\to\ker T_p\to\ker T_{p,V}\to V\to\operatorname{coker}T_p\to0$ (170) and `Thm - Determinant Lines and Short Exact Sequences` give a canonical isomorphism $\det T_p\cong\Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}\ker T_{p,V}\otimes\Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}V^*$, natural in $p$, so $\det T|_W\cong\det(\ker T_{p,V})\otimes(\det V)^*$ is a line bundle trivialised by a local trivialisation of $\ker T_{p,V}$; **the two facts Haydys cites ([MS12] local triviality of $\ker T_{p,V}$; openness of surjectivity) are `Thm - Stabilisation ...` (ii),(iii); the compatibility of the trivialisations for two choices $V,V'$ (Haydys does not discuss) is checked via $V + V'$ and the naturality of (170)**; spec: mechanism: **stabilising by a finite-dimensional space makes the kernel a genuine vector bundle, and the determinant line is that bundle's top power twisted by a constant line**.
- `Thm - Homotopy Invariance of the Determinant Line Bundle` — type: theorem (two parts, with the explicit-isomorphism construction); source items: A-T6.4.2 (Proposition 173), A-I6.4.3, A-T7.1.24 (Lemma 219, placed here), A-T7.1.25 (Remark 220, placed here); prereqs: [Def - Determinant Line Bundle of a Family of Fredholm Operators, Thm - Local Triviality of the Determinant Line Bundle, Thm - Paracompact Has Partitions of Unity]; statement: (i) If $\{T_{p,0}\}$ and $\{T_{p,1}\}$ are homotopic families of Fredholm operators over a paracompact $P$, then $\det T_0\cong\det T_1$; precisely, $\det T_1$ is trivial if and only if $\det T_0$ is, and a trivialisation of one and a homotopy induce a trivialisation of the other, unique up to an everywhere-positive function. (ii) An explicit isomorphism $\det T_0\cong\det T_1$ is obtained by choosing a connection on the line bundle $\det T\to P\times[0,1]$ and parallel-transporting along $t\mapsto(p,t)$; proof: (i) a line bundle $L\to P\times[0,1]$ restricts isomorphically to $P\times\{0\}$ and $P\times\{1\}$ because $P\times[0,1]\simeq P$ (homotopy invariance of line bundles: **the writer proves that a line bundle over $Z\times[0,1]$ with $Z$ paracompact is isomorphic to the pullback of its restriction to $Z\times\{0\}$**, Lemma 1, via `Thm - Paracompact Has Partitions of Unity` — but note that page is flagged in `unproved-pages.txt`, so the writer proves the metrisable case in full on this page: cover by trivialising sets, build a global nowhere-zero section over $Z\times\{t\}$ and extend it in $t$ by a partition-of-unity patching, giving a trivialisation of $L$ pulled back from the slice); (ii) parallel transport of the (one-dimensional) fibres along the line bundle with any connection is an isomorphism independent of the connection up to a positive factor (the holonomy of a line bundle over the contractible $[0,1]$ is trivial); spec: **A-T7.1.24 and A-T7.1.25 are Haydys's §7.1.8 statements, placed here per the chapter X header; they are the special case used to orient the Seiberg–Witten moduli space (XI)**; since `Thm - Paracompact Has Partitions of Unity` is flagged, the metrisable-space partition of unity used here is proved as Lemma 1 (second countable + metrisable ⇒ paracompact, via Lindelöf and shrinking); mechanism: **a line bundle over a cylinder is the pullback of its end, so its two ends are isomorphic**; targets: orientability of the Seiberg–Witten moduli space is exactly this applied to the family $D_{(\psi,A)}$ (XI).
- `Def - Integer Degree of an Oriented Proper Fredholm Map` — type: definition (anchor; compound: orientation of a Fredholm map via a trivialisation of the determinant bundle of $d F$; the sign of a regular point; the integer degree); source items: A-D6.5.1, A-D6.5.2 (equation (175)), A-R6.5.1, A-I6.5.2 (→ inside, Notation: the boundary orientation convention); prereqs: [Def - Determinant Line Bundle of a Family of Fredholm Operators, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of the Determinant Line Bundle, Def - Mod-2 Degree of a Proper Fredholm Map, Def - Orientation of a Vector Space, Thm - Regular Value and Transversality Theorems for Fredholm Maps]; spec: an *orientation* of a Fredholm map $F:X\to Y$ of index $d$ is a trivialisation of the determinant line bundle $\det dF\to X$ of the family $x\mapsto d_xF$ (i.e. a nowhere-zero continuous section, equivalently a continuous choice of orientation of $\det d_xF$); at a regular point $x$ ($\operatorname{coker}d_xF = 0$) $\det d_xF = \Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}\ker d_xF = \Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}T_x\mathcal M$ where $\mathcal M = F^{-1}(y)$, so an orientation of $F$ orients the $d$-manifold $\mathcal M$; for $d = 0$, $\det d_xF = \mathbb R$ canonically and the sign $\varepsilon(x)\in\{\pm1\}$ compares the trivialisation with the canonical orientation; the *integer degree* of an oriented proper Fredholm map of index $0$ is $\deg F := \sum_{x\in F^{-1}(y)}\varepsilon(x)\in\mathbb Z$ (equation (175); **source typo: Haydys p. 58 (175) prints $\sum_i\varepsilon_k$ for $\sum_i\varepsilon_i$**), reducing mod $2$ to $\deg_2 F$; well-definedness on `Thm - Well-Definedness and Homotopy Invariance of the Degree`; boundary orientation convention for the cobordism (A-I6.5.2) recorded here and used on the cobordism page; examples: a finite-dimensional oriented proper map (Brouwer degree, the vault's `Def - Brouwer Degree of a Map` as the finite-dimensional model); an orientation-reversing diffeomorphism ($\deg = -1$); non-example: a Fredholm map whose determinant bundle is non-trivial admits no orientation and no integer degree (only $\deg_2$).
- `Thm - Compact One-Manifolds have an Even Number of Boundary Points` — type: theorem; source items: A-I6.2.2; prereqs: [Def - Smooth Manifold with Boundary, Thm - Regular Value Theorem on Manifolds]; statement: A compact smooth $1$-manifold with boundary is diffeomorphic to a finite disjoint union of circles and closed intervals; in particular its boundary consists of an even number of points; moreover if it is oriented, the boundary points carry alternating induced signs summing to $0$; proof: the classification of compact $1$-manifolds (Milnor, *Topology from the Differentiable Viewpoint*, Appendix; the writer gives the arc-length/maximal-integral-curve argument: a unit-speed parametrisation extends maximally to an interval or a circle, using compactness to close it up), then boundary points come in the two ends of each interval; the signed statement from the induced boundary orientation (`Def - Manifold with Boundary and Induced Orientation`); spec: mechanism: **a compact $1$-manifold is a finite union of arcs and loops, and each arc contributes two boundary points of opposite induced orientation**; targets: the cobordism argument for both degrees.
- `Thm - Cobordism of the Preimage along a Generic Homotopy` — type: theorem (helper 4; two parts, mod-2 and oriented); source items: A-T6.2.1 (Step 3), A-T6.5.1 (Theorem 176), A-T6.5.2, A-I6.5.1, A-I6.5.2; prereqs: [Def - Integer Degree of an Oriented Proper Fredholm Map, Thm - Regular Value and Transversality Theorems for Fredholm Maps, Thm - Compact One-Manifolds have an Even Number of Boundary Points, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of the Determinant Line Bundle, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; statement: Let $H:X\times[0,1]\to Y$ be a smooth proper homotopy of proper Fredholm maps of index $0$ with $y$ a regular value of $H$, $H_0 := H(\cdot,0)$, $H_1 := H(\cdot,1)$, both having $y$ as a regular value. Then $H^{-1}(y)$ is a compact $1$-manifold with boundary $H_0^{-1}(y)\sqcup H_1^{-1}(y)$; consequently (i) $\#H_0^{-1}(y) = \#H_1^{-1}(y)\bmod 2$; and (ii) if $H$ is oriented (a trivialisation of $\det dH$ over $X\times[0,1]$, restricting to the given ones on the ends), then $H^{-1}(y)$ is an oriented $1$-manifold and $\partial H^{-1}(y) = H_1^{-1}(y)\sqcup\overline{H_0^{-1}(y)}$ with signs, so $\deg H_0 = \deg H_1$; more generally for a parametric family of index $d$ the oriented preimages $\mathcal M_{w_0},\mathcal M_{w_1}$ over generic parameters are oriented-cobordant, and for a closed $d$-form $\omega$ on $X$ with integral periods $\int_{\mathcal M_w}\omega$ is independent of $w$ (Stokes); proof: the boundary version of the regular-value theorem (`Thm - Regular Value and Transversality Theorems for Fredholm Maps` (iii)) makes $H^{-1}(y)$ a manifold with boundary; compactness from properness of $H$ (the writer supplies it: $H^{-1}(y)\subset H^{-1}(\{y\})$ with $\{y\}$ compact); (i) `Thm - Compact One-Manifolds ...`; (ii) the orientation of $H^{-1}(y)$ comes from the trivialisation of $\det dH$ via $\det d_{(x,t)}H = \Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}\ker d_{(x,t)}H = \Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}T_{(x,t)}H^{-1}(y)$ at regular points, and the induced boundary orientation gives the signs (the boundary convention (174)/(A-I6.5.2) written out); the characteristic-number statement by Stokes on the cobordism ($\int_{\mathcal M_{w_1}}\omega - \int_{\mathcal M_{w_0}}\omega = \int_{\partial}\omega = \int_{\text{cobordism}}d\omega = 0$); spec: **Haydys's Step 3 uses the classification of compact $1$-manifolds and the boundary version of Corollary 163; both are supplied; the orientation of the cobordism from the determinant-bundle trivialisation (A-R6.5.1) is written out, filling the gap the content map flags**; mechanism: **a generic homotopy has a compact-cobordism preimage, and signed boundary counts of an oriented cobordism cancel**; targets: well-definedness of both degrees; cobordism invariance of the Seiberg–Witten invariant (XI).
- `Thm - Well-Definedness and Homotopy Invariance of the Degree` — type: theorem (anchor; the shared degree theorem, four parts); source items: A-T6.2.1 (Theorem 166), A-T6.5.1 (Theorem 176); prereqs: [Def - Mod-2 Degree of a Proper Fredholm Map, Def - Integer Degree of an Oriented Proper Fredholm Map, Thm - Regular Values of a Proper Fredholm Map are Open and Dense, Thm - The Degree Count is Locally Constant on Regular Values, Thm - Transitivity of the Group of Diffeomorphisms Isotopic to the Identity, Thm - Cobordism of the Preimage along a Generic Homotopy]; statement: Let $F:X\to Y$ be a proper Fredholm map of index $0$ between second countable Banach manifolds, $Y$ connected. (i) $\deg_2 F$ is independent of the regular value $y$. (ii) If $F_0,F_1$ are homotopic through proper Fredholm maps of index $0$ then $\deg_2 F_0 = \deg_2 F_1$. (iii) If $F$ is oriented, $\deg F\in\mathbb Z$ is independent of the regular value, and (iv) is invariant under homotopies through oriented proper Fredholm maps of index $0$ (compatible orientations); proof: (i) for two regular values $y_1,y_2$ take $\phi\in\operatorname{Diff}_0(Y)$ with $\phi(y_1) = y_2$ (`Thm - Transitivity ...`); $y_2$ is a common regular value of $F$ and $\phi\circ F$, which are homotopic (through $\phi_t\circ F$), so `Thm - Cobordism ...` (i) gives $\#F^{-1}(y_2) = \#(\phi F)^{-1}(y_2) = \#F^{-1}(y_1)\bmod2$ (using $\#(\phi F)^{-1}(y_2) = \#F^{-1}(\phi^{-1}y_2) = \#F^{-1}(y_1)$); (ii) choose $y$ regular for $F_0$, $F_1$ and the homotopy (`Thm - Regular Values ...` + Sard–Smale for the homotopy), apply `Thm - Cobordism ...` (i); (iii),(iv) the same with signs from `Thm - Cobordism ...` (ii); spec: **this is the single anchor the design assigns to both degrees, fed by the four helper pages; Haydys's six-step proof of Theorem 166 is distributed across the helpers, and the sign refinement for the integer degree (Theorem 176) uses the determinant-bundle orientation of the cobordism page**; mechanism: **regular values are connected by ambient isotopies and by generic cobordisms, along which the signed and unsigned counts are invariant**.
- `Thm - Nonzero Degree Implies Surjectivity` — type: theorem; source items: A-T6.2.2 (Corollary 168); prereqs: [Thm - Well-Definedness and Homotopy Invariance of the Degree, Thm - Regular Values of a Proper Fredholm Map are Open and Dense, Thm - Proper Maps are Closed and Fredholm Maps are Locally Proper]; statement: Let $F:X\to Y$ be a proper Fredholm map of index $0$, $Y$ connected. If $\deg_2 F\ne0$ (or $\deg F\ne0$) then $F$ is surjective; proof: every $y\in Y$ is a limit of regular values (`Thm - Regular Values ...` density); a regular value $y'$ has $\#F^{-1}(y') \ge |\deg F| > 0$ (or odd $\ge1$), so $F^{-1}(y')\ne\varnothing$; picking $y'_n\to y$ regular with $x_n\in F^{-1}(y'_n)$, properness (closedness of $F$, `Thm - Proper Maps ...`) gives $y\in F(X)$; spec: **Haydys omits the proof; the closedness-of-proper-maps step is supplied**; mechanism: **a nonzero degree forces every regular value to have a preimage, and a proper map has closed image, so the image is everything**; targets: existence of solutions to the Seiberg–Witten and Yang–Mills equations by degree arguments (bold context; XI uses the signed count directly).
- `Def - Cobordism Class and Characteristic Numbers of a Parametric Moduli Space` — type: definition (compound: the oriented cobordism class of $\mathcal M_w$; pairing with characteristic classes of a bundle over $X$; the resulting integers); source items: A-D6.5.3, A-T6.5.2, A-R6.5.2, A-I6.5.3; prereqs: [Thm - Cobordism of the Preimage along a Generic Homotopy, Def - Chern Classes, Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds, Def - de Rham Cohomology]; spec: for an oriented parametric family with each $\mathcal M_w = F_w^{-1}(y)$ a compact oriented $d$-manifold, the oriented cobordism class $[\mathcal M_w]$ is independent of the good parameter $w$ (`Thm - Cobordism ...`); given a bundle $Q\to X$ with characteristic classes $\alpha_1,\dots,\alpha_r$ and $\alpha := \alpha_1\smile\cdots\smile\alpha_r$ of total de Rham degree $d$ represented by a closed form $\omega$, the *characteristic number* $\langle\alpha,[\mathcal M_w]\rangle := \int_{\mathcal M_w}\omega$ is independent of $w$ (Stokes on the cobordism) and, when $\omega$ has integral periods, is an integer (A-I6.5.3, recorded as bold context until chapter XII supplies integrality — `[NEEDED FROM XII: integral periods of a Chern class over a closed oriented manifold are integers]`); Axiom Motivation (R6.5.2): cobordism classes are unwieldy, so one extracts numbers by integrating characteristic forms — this is exactly how the Seiberg–Witten invariant is built in chapter XI from $\mu = c_1$ of the framed moduli space; examples: $d = 0$ (the integer degree $\sum\varepsilon_i$); the Seiberg–Witten invariant $\langle\mu^{d/2},[\mathcal M_\eta]\rangle$ (XI); non-example: a non-orientable $\mathcal M_w$ has no integer characteristic numbers over $\mathbb Z$ (only mod 2).
- `Ex - The Determinant Line of a Family with Jumping Kernel` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (drill of the determinant bundle); prereqs: [Def - Determinant Line Bundle of a Family of Fredholm Operators, Thm - Local Triviality of the Determinant Line Bundle]; spec: for the family $T_t = \begin{pmatrix} t & 0 \\ 0 & 1\end{pmatrix}$ on $\mathbb R^2$, $t\in\mathbb R$, compute $\ker T_t$, $\operatorname{coker}T_t$ and $\det T_t$ at $t\ne0$ and $t = 0$; exhibit an explicit nowhere-zero section of $\det T\to\mathbb R$ (via the stabilisation with $V = \operatorname{span}(e_1)$) proving triviality despite the rank jump.
- `Ex - The Exterior-Power Isomorphism for a Three-Term Sequence` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-X6.4.1 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Determinant Lines and Short Exact Sequences]; spec: for $0\to U_0\xrightarrow{a} U_1\xrightarrow{b} U_2\to0$ construct the isomorphism $\det U_1\cong\det U_0\otimes\det U_2$ on bases and verify it is independent of the chosen splitting; apply to the deformation complex $0\to\operatorname{Lie}(G)\to T_xX\to T_yY\to0$ to get $\det D_x\cong\det(\operatorname{Lie}(G))^*\otimes\det T_xX\otimes\det(T_yY)^*$.
- `Ex - Orientation of a Zero-Dimensional Moduli Space and the Signed Count` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-D6.5.2 (drill); prereqs: [Def - Integer Degree of an Oriented Proper Fredholm Map]; spec: for a proper Fredholm map of index $0$ with a trivialisation of $\det dF$, show that at each regular preimage point $\det d_xF = \mathbb R$ and the sign $\varepsilon(x)$ is the comparison of the trivialisation with the canonical orientation; compute $\deg$ for $x\mapsto x^3 - x$ on $\mathbb R$ (three preimages of $0$, signs $+,-,+$, $\deg = 1$).
- `Ex - Cobordism Invariance of a Characteristic Number` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: A-T6.5.2 (drill); prereqs: [Def - Cobordism Class and Characteristic Numbers of a Parametric Moduli Space, Thm - Cobordism of the Preimage along a Generic Homotopy, Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds]; spec: given a family $F_w$ with $\mathcal M_w$ a compact oriented surface and a closed integral $2$-form $\omega$ on $X$, prove $\int_{\mathcal M_{w_0}}\omega = \int_{\mathcal M_{w_1}}\omega$ by Stokes on the oriented cobordism $\mathcal M_\gamma$; identify the obstruction to the statement when $\mathcal M_\gamma$ fails to be compact (properness in the parameter direction) and when $\omega$ is not closed.
- `Exercise Index - §10.4 Determinant Line Bundles, Orientations, and the Integer Degree` — lists: [Ex - The Determinant Line of a Family with Jumping Kernel, Ex - The Exterior-Power Isomorphism for a Three-Term Sequence, Ex - Orientation of a Zero-Dimensional Moduli Space and the Signed Count, Ex - Cobordism Invariance of a Characteristic Number]

### §10.5 The Equivariant Setup, Slices, and Moduli Spaces
- `Def - Infinitesimal Action and Local Slice` — type: definition (compound: the infinitesimal action $R_x$; local slice of a group action); source items: A-D6.6.1 (equation (177)), A-D6.6.2 (Definition 178), A-I6.6.1 (→ inside, Notation: slices are a hypothesis, not proved in general); prereqs: [Def - Banach Manifold and Smooth Maps between Banach Spaces, Def - Smooth Action of a Lie Group, Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action, Def - Embedded Submanifold]; spec: from §10.5 on, $X$ and $G$ are **Hilbert** manifolds (design note (1)); for a smooth action of a Banach Lie group $G$ on $X$, the *infinitesimal action* at $x$ is $R_x:\operatorname{Lie}(G)\to T_xX$, $R_x\xi := \frac{d}{dt}\big|_0(\exp(t\xi)\cdot x)$, with image the tangent space to the orbit $G\cdot x$ (the Banach-manifold analogue of `Def - Fundamental Vector Field of a Group Action`); a *local slice* at $x$ is a Hilbert submanifold $S\ni x$ such that $GS := \{g\cdot s\}$ is open in $X$ and $G\times S\to GS$, $(g,s)\mapsto g\cdot s$, is a diffeomorphism; when the action is free, a slice gives a chart for the orbit space; the *orthogonal slice* used in practice is $S = x + \ker R_x^*$ (the $L^2$-orthogonal complement of the orbit direction), whose tangent space $\ker R_x^*$ is transverse to $\operatorname{Im}R_x$; examples: E6.6.1 (the rotation action of $U(1)$ on $S^2$ minus the poles, global slice $\{(x,0,z) : x>0\}$, quotient an interval — made an exercise); the Coulomb slice $\ker R_{(\psi,A)}^*$ of the Seiberg–Witten gauge action (XI); non-example: at a point with non-trivial stabiliser (a reducible Seiberg–Witten configuration) the action is not free and no slice of this form exists — the reason XI restricts to irreducibles.
- `Def - The Equivariant Deformation Complex and its Folded Operator` — type: definition (compound: the deformation complex (181); its cohomology; the folded operator $D_x$ (182)); source items: A-D6.6.3 (equation (181)), A-D6.6.4 (equation (182)), A-R6.6.1, A-R6.6.2; prereqs: [Def - Infinitesimal Action and Local Slice, Def - Fredholm Map and Its Index, Def - Elliptic Complex and Its Laplacians, Thm - A Short Complex is Elliptic iff its Folded Operator is Elliptic]; spec: for a $G$-equivariant $F_w:X\to Y$ and a fixed point $y$ ($g\cdot y = y$) and $x\in F_w^{-1}(y)$, the *deformation complex* is $0\to\operatorname{Lie}(G)\xrightarrow{R_x} T_xX\xrightarrow{d_xF_w} T_yY\to0$ (a complex because $d_xF_w\circ R_x = 0$ by equivariance and $y$ fixed); its cohomology: $H^0 = \operatorname{Lie}(\operatorname{Stab}_x)$ (trivial when the action is free), $H^1 = \ker d_xF_w/\operatorname{Im}R_x$ = Zariski tangent space of the moduli space $\mathcal M_w = F_w^{-1}(y)/G$ at $[x]$, $H^2 = \operatorname{coker}d_xF_w$ (trivial iff $y$ is a regular value of $F_w$); the *folded operator* $D_x := (R_x^*,d_xF_w):T_xX\to\operatorname{Lie}(G)\oplus T_yY$ (using the Hilbert structure for $R_x^*$), with $\ker D_x\cong H^1$ (R6.6.2: cf. the folding theorem `Thm - A Short Complex is Elliptic iff its Folded Operator is Elliptic`, IX); the complex is *elliptic*/$D_x$ *Fredholm* in the applications; examples: the Seiberg–Witten deformation complex (191) folded to $D_{(\psi,A)}$ (XI); non-example: at a reducible point $H^0\ne0$ and the moduli space is singular.
- `Thm - A Free Action with Slices has a Manifold Quotient` — type: theorem; source items: A-T6.6.1 (Proposition 179); prereqs: [Def - Infinitesimal Action and Local Slice, Def - Quotient Topology and Identification Map, Thm - Universal Property of the Quotient]; statement: Let a Banach Lie group $G$ act freely on a Hilbert manifold $X$ and admit a local slice at every point. Then the quotient $X/G$ is a Hilbert manifold and $\pi:X\to X/G$ is a smooth submersion admitting local sections; proof: **Haydys says "clear"; the writer supplies it in full**: a slice $S$ at $x$ maps homeomorphically to an open neighbourhood of $[x]$ in $X/G$ (freeness makes $G\times S\to GS$ a diffeomorphism, so $GS/G\cong S$); the transition maps between two slice charts $S_1,S_2$ are smooth (composition of the diffeomorphism $G\times S_i\to GS_i$ and the projection); **Hausdorffness of $X/G$ (which Haydys omits) is proved from the slice property: two orbits $G\cdot x\ne G\cdot x'$ are separated because $GS$ is open and $G\times S\to GS$ is a diffeomorphism, so a small slice neighbourhood of one misses the other orbit** (the writer gives the argument; in the applications $X/G$ is metrisable, which also gives Hausdorffness); second countability from that of $X$; spec: mechanism: **a slice is a chart for the orbit space, and freeness plus the slice diffeomorphism makes the charts compatible**; targets: the configuration space modulo gauge $\mathcal B = \mathcal C_{\mathrm{irr}}/\mathcal G$ is a Hilbert manifold (XI).
- `Thm - The Equivariant Moduli Space is a Manifold of Index Dimension` — type: theorem; source items: A-T6.6.2 (Theorem 183), A-R6.6.1; prereqs: [Def - The Equivariant Deformation Complex and its Folded Operator, Thm - A Free Action with Slices has a Manifold Quotient, Thm - Regular Value and Transversality Theorems for Fredholm Maps, Thm - Fredholm Alternative for Elliptic Operators]; statement: Let $y$ be a fixed point of the $G$-action on $Y$; assume (i) $G$ acts freely on $X$; (ii) $y$ is a regular value of $F_w$; (iii) there is a local slice at each $x\in F_w^{-1}(y)$; (iv) $D_x = (R_x^*,d_xF_w)$ is Fredholm of index $d$. Then $\mathcal M_w = F_w^{-1}(y)/G$ is a smooth manifold of dimension $d$; proof: local statement at $x$; take the orthogonal slice $S = x + \ker R_x^*$ (so $T_xS = (\operatorname{Im}R_x)^\perp = \ker R_x^*$, transverse to the orbit); **the identification $\ker d_x(F_w|_S) = \ker D_x$ that Haydys's proof needs (content-map gap) is written out**: $\ker d_x(F_w|_S) = \ker d_xF_w\cap T_xS = \ker d_xF_w\cap\ker R_x^* = \ker(R_x^*,d_xF_w) = \ker D_x$; $y$ is a regular value of $F_w|_S$ because $\operatorname{Im}d_x(F_w|_S) = \operatorname{Im}d_xF_w$ (since $d_xF_w$ annihilates $\operatorname{Im}R_x$, adding the orbit directions does not enlarge the image) $= T_yY$ by (ii); so $F_w^{-1}(y)\cap S$ is a manifold of dimension $\dim\ker D_x$ (regular-value theorem), and $\operatorname{coker}D_x = 0$ (freeness ⇒ $R_x$ injective with closed range ⇒ $R_x^*$ surjective onto $\operatorname{Lie}(G)$; combined with (ii)) gives $\dim\ker D_x = \operatorname{index}D_x = d$; the slice identifies $F_w^{-1}(y)\cap S$ with a neighbourhood of $[x]$ in $\mathcal M_w$; spec: **the three gaps the content map flags (the orthogonal slice, surjectivity of $R_x^*$ on the complement, regularity of $y$ for $F_w|_S$) are all filled**; mechanism: **cutting the equation down to a slice removes the gauge directions, and the folded operator's kernel is the tangent space of the moduli space**; targets: `Thm - Generic Regularity and Orientability of the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space` (XI).
- `Thm - Orientation and Bordism Invariance of the Equivariant Moduli Space` — type: theorem; source items: A-T6.6.3 (Theorem 184), A-I6.6.2; prereqs: [Thm - The Equivariant Moduli Space is a Manifold of Index Dimension, Def - Determinant Line Bundle of a Family of Fredholm Operators, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of the Determinant Line Bundle, Thm - Cobordism of the Preimage along a Generic Homotopy]; statement: Under the hypotheses of `Thm - The Equivariant Moduli Space is a Manifold of Index Dimension`, suppose in addition that the determinant line bundle $\det D$ of the family $x\mapsto D_x$ over $F_w^{-1}(y)$ is trivialised and the trivialisation is $G$-invariant. Then $\mathcal M_w$ is oriented. If moreover $\mathcal M_w$ is compact for every $w$ in a connected parameter family, its oriented bordism class is independent of $w$; proof: **Haydys omits it ("by tracing through §6.5")**; the writer writes it: the $G$-invariant trivialisation of $\det D$ descends to a trivialisation of $\det(\text{the folded operator})$ over $\mathcal M_w$, and at a point $\det D_x = \Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}\ker D_x = \Lambda^{\mathrm{top}}T_{[x]}\mathcal M_w$ (since $\operatorname{coker}D_x = 0$), so the trivialisation orients $\mathcal M_w$ (`Def - Integer Degree of an Oriented Proper Fredholm Map`, orientation via the determinant line); bordism invariance is `Thm - Cobordism of the Preimage along a Generic Homotopy` applied to the family of folded operators over the parametric universal moduli space, with the $G$-invariant trivialisation giving the cobordism's orientation (`Thm - Homotopy Invariance of the Determinant Line Bundle`); spec: mechanism: **a $G$-invariant orientation of the determinant line descends to the quotient and orients the moduli space, and the determinant bundle's homotopy invariance orients the cobordism**; targets: orientability and bordism invariance of the Seiberg–Witten moduli space and invariant (XI).
- `Ex - A Global Slice for the Rotation Action on the Sphere` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-E6.6.1 (Example 180); prereqs: [Def - Infinitesimal Action and Local Slice, Thm - A Free Action with Slices has a Manifold Quotient]; spec: for $U(1)$ acting on $S^2$ by rotations about the $z$-axis, show the action is free off the poles, that $S := \{(x,0,z) : x^2 + z^2 = 1, x>0\}$ is a global slice on $S^2$ minus the poles, and that the quotient is diffeomorphic to the open interval $(-1,1)$; compute the infinitesimal action $R_p$ and $\ker R_p^*$.
- `Ex - The Deformation Complex Computes the Tangent Space of the Quotient` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-R6.6.1 (drill); prereqs: [Def - The Equivariant Deformation Complex and its Folded Operator, Thm - The Equivariant Moduli Space is a Manifold of Index Dimension]; spec: for a free action with slices and $y$ a regular value, prove $H^0 = 0$, $H^1 = T_{[x]}\mathcal M_w$, $H^2 = 0$ directly from the definitions; verify $\ker D_x = H^1$ and $\operatorname{index}D_x = \dim\mathcal M_w$; interpret $H^2\ne0$ as an obstruction.
- `Ex - Folding the Deformation Complex into a Single Elliptic Operator` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-R6.6.2 (drill; cf. Exercise 159); prereqs: [Def - The Equivariant Deformation Complex and its Folded Operator, Thm - A Short Complex is Elliptic iff its Folded Operator is Elliptic]; spec: show that a three-term elliptic deformation complex $0\to E_0\xrightarrow{R} E_1\xrightarrow{L} E_2\to0$ of first-order operators is elliptic if and only if $D := (R^*,L):E_1\to E_0\oplus E_2$ is elliptic, and that then $\ker D\cong H^1$; apply to the Atiyah complex and to the Seiberg–Witten deformation complex (forward reference to XI).
- `Ex - The Equivariant Moduli Space of a Linear Model` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: (additional; a finite-dimensional model of §10.6/XI); prereqs: [Thm - The Equivariant Moduli Space is a Manifold of Index Dimension]; spec: let $G = U(1)$ act on $X = \mathbb C^n\setminus\{0\}$ by scalar multiplication and $F:X\to\mathbb R$, $F(z) = |z|^2 - 1$, with $y = 0$ a fixed point of the trivial action on $\mathbb R$; verify freeness, the slice (a sphere), regularity, and that $\mathcal M = F^{-1}(0)/U(1) = \mathbb{CP}^{n-1}$ is a manifold of the predicted dimension $2n - 2$; compute $D_z$ and $\operatorname{index}D_z$.
- `Exercise Index - §10.5 The Equivariant Setup, Slices, and Moduli Spaces` — lists: [Ex - A Global Slice for the Rotation Action on the Sphere, Ex - The Deformation Complex Computes the Tangent Space of the Quotient, Ex - Folding the Deformation Complex into a Single Elliptic Operator, Ex - The Equivariant Moduli Space of a Linear Model]

Chapter X page count: §10.1: 6 Def/Thm + 4 Ex + 1 index = 11; §10.2: 3 Def/Thm + 4 Ex + 1 index = 8; §10.3: 8 Def/Thm + 4 Ex + 1 index = 13; §10.4: 10 Def/Thm + 4 Ex + 1 index = 15; §10.5: 5 Def/Thm + 4 Ex + 1 index = 10; topic page 1. **Total X: 58 pages** (32 Def/Thm, 20 Ex, 5 indices, 1 topic).

## Gauge Theory XI — Seiberg–Witten Theory            (folder: Gauge Theory XI/)
Sources: A §7.1 (pp. 61–70), §7.2 (pp. 71–72). No Bär items. (A §7.1's linear-algebra items T7.1.1, I7.1.1, R7.1.1 and I4.4.2 live on chapter VIII's `Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms`, and the two determinant-bundle items T7.1.24, T7.1.25 live on chapter X's `Thm - Homotopy Invariance of the Determinant Line Bundle`; they are recorded there and only referenced here.)
Existing vault pages to link: Hodge Theory I: `Def - The Hodge Star Operator`, `Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star`, `Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Forms`, `Def - The Codifferential`, `Thm - Codifferential is the Adjoint of d`, `Def - The L2 Inner Product on Differential Forms`, `Def - Hodge Laplacian`, `Def - Harmonic Form` (all Riemannian; the star agrees with the series' $\star_B$ in dimension four on 2-forms). Linear Algebra VI: `Def - Inner Product Space`, `Thm - Cauchy-Schwarz Inequality`, `Thm - Orthogonal Decomposition`, `Def - Orthogonal Complement`. DG IX: `Def - Riemannian Volume Form`, `Thm - Stokes' Theorem on Manifolds`, `Def - Orientation of a Smooth Manifold`. DG XII: `Def - Riemannian Manifold`, `Def - Scalar Curvature` (via RG III). RG III: `Def - Scalar Curvature`, `Def - Ricci Tensor`, `Thm - Symmetries of the Riemann Tensor`. Chapter VII pages: `Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature`, `Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature`, `Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions`, `Thm - Top de Rham Cohomology of a Closed Connected Oriented Manifold`, `Def - The Yang-Mills Field Strength`, `Ex - Energy Identity for U(1) Connections on a Four-Manifold`, `Thm - Energy Identity and the Topological Bound for Yang-Mills`. Chapter VIII pages: `Def - Spin Structure and Spin-c Structure`, `Thm - Spin-c(3) is U(2) and Spin-c(4) is the Fibre Product of Two Copies of U(2)`, `Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection`, `Thm - Variation of the Twisted and Spin-c Dirac Operators with the Connection`, `Thm - Classification of Spin-c Structures`, `Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms`, `Thm - Clifford Action of 2-Forms and the Chiral Weitzenbock Formula in Dimension Four`, `Thm - Lichnerowicz Formula for the Spin-c Dirac Operator`, `Thm - Positive Scalar Curvature Kills Harmonic Spinors`, `Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint`, `Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian`. Chapter IX pages: `Def - Sobolev Space of Sections`, `Thm - Sobolev Embedding Theorem`, `Thm - Rellich Compactness Theorem`, `Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem`, `Thm - Composition with Analytic Functions on the Sobolev Algebra`, `Thm - Elliptic Regularity and the Elliptic Estimate`, `Thm - Elliptic Operators on Closed Manifolds are Fredholm`, `Thm - Fredholm Alternative for Elliptic Operators`, `Def - Elliptic Complex and Its Laplacians`, `Thm - A Short Complex is Elliptic iff its Folded Operator is Elliptic`, `Thm - Hodge Theorem for Elliptic Complexes`, `Thm - The de Rham Complex is Elliptic and Harmonic Forms Represent de Rham Cohomology`, `Thm - Stability of the Fredholm Property under Small and Compact Perturbations`, `Def - Fredholm Operator and Index`, `Thm - Laplacians and Dirac Operators are Elliptic`. Chapter X pages: `Def - Fredholm Map and Its Index`, `Def - Regular Value and Transversality for Fredholm Maps`, `Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem`, `Thm - Parametric Transversality`, `Def - Determinant Line Bundle of a Family of Fredholm Operators`, `Thm - Homotopy Invariance of the Determinant Line Bundle`, `Def - Integer Degree of an Oriented Proper Fredholm Map`, `Def - Cobordism Class and Characteristic Numbers of a Parametric Moduli Space`, `Def - Infinitesimal Action and Local Slice`, `Def - The Equivariant Deformation Complex and its Folded Operator`, `Thm - A Free Action with Slices has a Manifold Quotient`, `Thm - The Equivariant Moduli Space is a Manifold of Index Dimension`, `Thm - Orientation and Bordism Invariance of the Equivariant Moduli Space`, `Def - Banach Manifold and Smooth Maps between Banach Spaces`.
