Chapter III/VI anchors: `Def - Principal G-Bundle`, `Def - The Hopf Bundle`, `Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class`, `Def - Chern Classes`, `Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature`, `Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem`, `Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection`, `Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space`. Chapter XII–XIII anchors used by name: `Thm - Poincare Duality`, `Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class` (XII); `Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold`, `Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature`, `Def - Characteristic Element of a Unimodular Form`, `Def - The E8 Form and the K3 Surface`, `Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem`, `Thm - Rokhlin Theorem` (XIII). Non-anchor needs: `[NEEDED FROM XII–XIII: the integral second cohomology lattice $H^2(M;\mathbb Z)/\mathrm{torsion}$ with its integer-valued cup-product/intersection pairing, so that classes of bounded harmonic norm are finite in number (finiteness of the invariant) and $c_1(L_{\det})^2$ is an integer]`; `[NEEDED FROM XII–XIII: the topological facts $\chi(M) = 2 - 2b_1 + b_2$ and $\operatorname{sign}(M) = b_2^+ - b_2^-$ from Poincaré duality and the Hodge decomposition of $H^2$]`; `[NEEDED FROM XII–XIII: existence, for every unimodular symmetric bilinear form, of a closed simply connected topological four-manifold realising it — Freedman; carried as an Imported without proof callout on `Thm - Non-Smoothable Topological Four-Manifolds and Donaldson's Theorem` (pre-approved import 1)]`.
Design: (1) The **Pic convention** (manifest-1 decision 3): the series has no singular $H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$ before chapter XII, so wherever Haydys writes "$H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$ = spin$^c$ structures / line bundles" the pages write "$\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ = isomorphism classes of Hermitian line bundles" (via `Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class` and `Thm - Classification of Spin-c Structures`); the identification of $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ with $H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$ is **not proved in chapter XII** (grep of `manifest-3.md` finds no such page — chapter XII stops at Poincaré duality and provides no $\operatorname{Pic}\cong H^2$ page), so the finiteness argument's lattice input is written `[NEEDED FROM XII–XIII]` and, where a genuinely integral statement is unavoidable ($c_1(L_{\det})^2\in\mathbb Z$), it is recorded as bold plain-text context with the chapter XII/XIII page named. (2) **Two degrees / the Seiberg–Witten count** uses the equivariant machinery of chapter X §10.5 (slices, folded operator, oriented moduli space) and the characteristic-number definition of chapter X §10.4. (3) **The a priori $C^0$ bound** is the Weitzenböck maximum-principle argument, resting on chapter VIII's chiral Lichnerowicz formula; the pointwise Bochner identity Haydys imports (I7.1.3) is proved on its own page. (4) **Aronszajn unique continuation** (I7.1.9) is not on the pre-approved import list and is not book-length, so it is proved in full via Carleman estimates on its own page. (5) **Imports:** the Atiyah–Singer index theorem for the spin$^c$ Dirac operator (I7.1.11) is the one `Imported without proof` callout carried on `Thm - Dimension Formula for the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space`; Freedman's realisation theorem (pre-approved import 1) is a second `Imported without proof` callout on `Thm - Non-Smoothable Topological Four-Manifolds and Donaldson's Theorem`. Three `[PROPOSED IMPORT]` markers (Witten's connected-sum vanishing T7.2.4; Taubes T7.2.5; Fintushel–Stern T7.2.7) are requested in §11.5; if refused, each appears only as bold plain-text history in the Motivation of `Def - Seiberg-Witten Invariant` and is never stated in a theorem blockquote. Donaldson's diagonalisation theorem itself (T7.2.6's engine) is the chapter XIII anchor `Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem`; chapter XI states it by reference and derives the non-smoothability consequence.
Sections: §11.1 The Seiberg–Witten Equations and the Gauge Group Action | §11.2 The Deformation Complex, Sobolev Completions, and the Configuration Space | §11.3 Compactness of the Moduli Space | §11.4 Transversality, Reducibles, the Dimension Formula, and Orientability | §11.5 The Seiberg–Witten Invariant and its Applications

### §11.1 The Seiberg–Witten Equations and the Gauge Group Action
- `Def - The Quadratic Map on Positive Spinors` — type: definition; source items: A-D7.1.1, A-R7.1.1; prereqs: [Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms, Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection, Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions]; spec: on a closed oriented Riemannian 4-manifold $M$ with spin$^c$ structure and positive spinor bundle $\slashed S^+$, the *quadratic map* $\mu:\slashed S^+\to i\,\mathfrak{su}(\slashed S^+)$, $\mu(\psi) := \psi\psi^* - \tfrac12|\psi|^2\,\mathrm{id}$ (the traceless part of $\psi\psi^*$); via the isomorphism $i\Lambda^2_+T^*M\cong i\,\mathfrak{su}(\slashed S^+)$ ((187), on `Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms`) it is a fibrewise-quadratic $\mu:\slashed S^+\to i\Lambda^2_+T^*M$; in the $\mathbb C^2$-model, $\begin{pmatrix}\psi_1\\\psi_2\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\tfrac12\begin{pmatrix}|\psi_1|^2-|\psi_2|^2 & 2\psi_1\bar\psi_2 \\ 2\bar\psi_1\psi_2 & |\psi_2|^2-|\psi_1|^2\end{pmatrix}$; the polarisation $\mu(\psi,\phi) := \tfrac12(\mu(\psi+\phi)-\mu(\psi)-\mu(\phi))$; **source typo (Haydys B16, p. 61): the expanded form is $\mu(\psi)(\phi) = \langle\phi,\psi\rangle\psi - \tfrac12|\psi|^2\phi$ with a MINUS sign (the printed $+\tfrac12|\psi|^2\phi$ is inconsistent with tracelessness); the series uses the traceless minus-sign version and verifies $\operatorname{tr}\mu(\psi) = 0$ and $\langle\mu(\psi)\psi,\psi\rangle = \tfrac12|\psi|^4$ on the page**; Convention callout: the sign $F_A^+ = +\mu(\psi)$ (not $-\mu(\psi)$) is the one for which the $C^0$ bound of §11.3 holds (Remark 197, T7.1.9); examples: $\mu(\psi) = 0\iff\psi = 0$; $|\mu(\psi)| = \tfrac12|\psi|^2$ (computed); non-example: $\psi\psi^*$ without the trace subtraction is not in $\mathfrak{su}$.
- `Def - Seiberg-Witten Equations` — type: definition (anchor; compound: the equations, the Seiberg–Witten map, the configuration space); source items: A-D7.1.2 (equation (188)); prereqs: [Def - The Quadratic Map on Positive Spinors, Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection, Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Connections, Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection, Def - The Yang-Mills Field Strength]; spec: fix a closed oriented Riemannian 4-manifold $M$ and a spin$^c$ structure with determinant line $L_{\det}$; the *configuration space* is $\mathcal C := \Gamma(\slashed S^+)\times\mathcal A(P_{\det})$, where $\mathcal A(P_{\det})$ is the (affine, over $\Omega^1(M;i\mathbb R)$) space of unitary connections on $L_{\det}$ (equivalently on $P_{\det}$; `Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space`); the *Seiberg–Witten equations* for $(\psi,A)\in\mathcal C$ are $\slashed D_A^+\psi = 0$ and $F_A^+ = \mu(\psi)$ (with $F_A^+\in\Omega^2_+(M;i\mathbb R)$ the self-dual part of the curvature of $A$, `Def - The Yang-Mills Field Strength` in the abelian case, and $\mu(\psi)$ read as an imaginary self-dual 2-form via (187)); the *Seiberg–Witten map* $SW:\mathcal C\to\Gamma(\slashed S^-)\times\Omega^2_+(M;i\mathbb R)$, $SW(\psi,A) := (\slashed D_A^+\psi, F_A^+ - \mu(\psi))$, so solutions are $SW^{-1}(0)$; Axiom Motivation: the equations are the abelian analogue of the anti-self-duality equations coupled to a spinor; the first is linear in $\psi$ and the second couples curvature to the spinor bilinear, and their solutions are the absolute minima of a Weitzenböck energy (§11.3); Convention callout: the factor $\tfrac12$ in $\slashed D_A$'s dependence on $A$ (`Thm - Variation of the Twisted and Spin-c Dirac Operators with the Connection`) and the gauge factor $2$ in $A\cdot g = A + 2g^{-1}dg$ both come from $L_{\det}$ being the square of the spinor line (R4.3.5); examples: a Kähler surface with its canonical spin$^c$ structure has explicit reducible and irreducible solutions (bold context, Taubes/Witten); the trivial solution $(0,A)$ with $F_A^+ = 0$ (reducible); non-example: $\slashed D_A^+\psi = 0$ alone (no curvature constraint) is underdetermined.
- `Def - Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space` — type: definition (anchor; compound: the gauge group, its action, reducible/irreducible configurations, the moduli space); source items: A-D7.1.3 (equation (189)), A-D7.1.4, A-D7.1.10 (Corollary 205), A-D7.1.11 (Theorem 206); prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Equations, Def - Gauge Transformation, Def - The Equivariant Deformation Complex and its Folded Operator, Thm - A Free Action with Slices has a Manifold Quotient]; spec: the *gauge group* $\mathcal G := \mathcal G(P_{\det})\cong C^\infty(M;U(1))$ acts on $\mathcal C$ on the right by $(\psi,A)\cdot g := (\bar g\psi, A + 2g^{-1}dg)$ (the factor $2$ as above; `Def - Gauge Transformation` is the principal-bundle gauge group specialised to $U(1)$); a configuration is *reducible* if $\psi\equiv0$ (stabiliser the constant maps $U(1)$) and *irreducible* if $\psi\not\equiv0$ (trivial stabiliser); the *Seiberg–Witten moduli space* $\mathcal M_{SW} := SW^{-1}(0)/\mathcal G$, its irreducible part $\mathcal M^{\mathrm{irr}}_{SW} := \{$irreducible solutions$\}/\mathcal G$; the Sobolev model $\mathcal M := \{(\psi,A)\in\mathcal C^{5,2} : SW(\psi,A) = 0\}/\mathcal G^{6,2}$ (the smooth structure and the identification with the $C^\infty$ moduli space $\mathcal M_\infty$ are `Thm - Compactness of the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space`); topologised as a subspace of $\mathcal B := \mathcal C^{5,2}_{\mathrm{irr}}/\mathcal G^{6,2}$ (a Hilbert manifold by `Thm - A Free Action with Slices has a Manifold Quotient` once the Coulomb slice is in hand, §11.4); examples: for a metric of positive scalar curvature the moduli space of the trivial spin$^c$ structure is a single reducible point (§11.5); non-example: at a reducible configuration the action is not free, so $\mathcal M_{SW}$ can be singular there (avoided generically, §11.4).
- `Thm - Equivariance of the Seiberg-Witten Map` — type: theorem (two parts); source items: A-T7.1.2 (Lemma 190), A-T7.1.3, A-R7.1.2; prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Equations, Def - Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space, Thm - Variation of the Twisted and Spin-c Dirac Operators with the Connection, Def - The Quadratic Map on Positive Spinors]; statement: (i) The Seiberg–Witten map is $\mathcal G$-equivariant: $SW((\psi,A)\cdot g) = \bar g\cdot SW(\psi,A)$, where $\mathcal G$ acts on $\Gamma(\slashed S^-)\times\Omega^2_+(M;i\mathbb R)$ by $\bar g$ on the spinor factor and trivially on the form factor; in particular $SW^{-1}(0)$ is $\mathcal G$-invariant. (ii) The stabiliser of $(\psi,A)$ is non-trivial if and only if $\psi\equiv0$, and every stabilising $g$ is a constant map; proof: (i) the writer verifies each component: $\slashed D^+_{A+2g^{-1}dg}(\bar g\psi) = \bar g\,\slashed D_A^+\psi + \tfrac12(2g^{-1}dg)\cdot(\bar g\psi) + (d\bar g)\cdot\psi = \bar g\,\slashed D_A^+\psi$ (using `Thm - Variation of the Twisted and Spin-c Dirac Operators with the Connection` and $d\bar g = -\bar g\,g^{-1}dg$, since $|g| = 1$), $F_{A + 2g^{-1}dg}^+ = F_A^+$ (because $d(g^{-1}dg) = 0$ for abelian $G$), and $\mu(\bar g\psi) = \mu(\psi)$ (since $|\bar g| = 1$); (ii) from $A\cdot g = A$ iff $g^{-1}dg = 0$ iff $g$ constant, and then $\bar g\psi = \psi$ with $g\ne1$ forces $\psi\equiv0$; spec: **Haydys omits the computation (Lemma 190); it is written out**; mechanism: **the abelian gauge action rotates the spinor by $\bar g$ and shifts the connection by an exact imaginary form that the Dirac operator exactly absorbs, so the equations are gauge-natural**; targets: the moduli space is the quotient by a free action on irreducibles (§11.4 slice).
- `Ex - The Quadratic Map in the Two-Component Model` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-D7.1.1 (drill); prereqs: [Def - The Quadratic Map on Positive Spinors]; spec: verify $\mu(\psi) = \psi\psi^* - \tfrac12|\psi|^2$ is traceless and self-adjoint, compute the $2\times2$ matrix, check $|\mu(\psi)|^2 = \tfrac14|\psi|^4$ and $\langle\mu(\psi)\psi,\psi\rangle = \tfrac12|\psi|^4$; confirm the sign correction of Haydys B16.
- `Ex - Gauge Equivariance of the Seiberg-Witten Map by Hand` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T7.1.2 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Equivariance of the Seiberg-Witten Map, Thm - Variation of the Twisted and Spin-c Dirac Operators with the Connection]; spec: reprove $\slashed D^+_{A\cdot g}(\bar g\psi) = \bar g\slashed D_A^+\psi$ in a local trivialisation with $g = e^{i\chi}$, tracking the factor-$2$ in $A\cdot g = A + 2g^{-1}dg$ and the factor-$\tfrac12$ in the Dirac variation; deduce the infinitesimal equivariance $R_{(\psi,A)}\xi = (-\xi\psi, 2d\xi)$ for $\xi\in\Omega^0(M;i\mathbb R)$.
- `Ex - Reducible Solutions are Flat U(1) Connections` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T7.1.3 (drill); prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Equations, Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class]; spec: show a reducible solution $(0,A)$ satisfies $F_A^+ = 0$; on a manifold with $b_2^+ = 0$ deduce that reducibles exist for every spin$^c$ structure (harmonic representative of $F_A$ is anti-self-dual), and on a manifold with $b_2^+>0$ that a generic metric has no self-dual harmonic form representing $2\pi i\,c_1(L_{\det})$, so reducibles are constrained (forward reference to the wall §11.4); invokes the Hodge decomposition of $\Omega^2$ and `Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature`.
- `Exercise Index - §11.1 The Seiberg–Witten Equations and the Gauge Group Action` — lists: [Ex - The Quadratic Map in the Two-Component Model, Ex - Gauge Equivariance of the Seiberg-Witten Map by Hand, Ex - Reducible Solutions are Flat U(1) Connections]

### §11.2 The Deformation Complex, Sobolev Completions, and the Configuration Space
- `Def - The Seiberg-Witten Deformation Complex and the Atiyah Complex` — type: definition (compound: the Atiyah complex; the Seiberg–Witten deformation complex (191)); source items: A-D7.1.5 (equation (191)), A-D7.1.6, A-R7.1.3; prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Equations, Def - Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space, Def - The Equivariant Deformation Complex and its Folded Operator, Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Forms, Def - The Codifferential]; spec: the *Atiyah complex* on an oriented Riemannian 4-manifold is $0\to\Omega^0(M;i\mathbb R)\xrightarrow{d}\Omega^1(M;i\mathbb R)\xrightarrow{d^+}\Omega^2_+(M;i\mathbb R)\to0$, where $d^+ := \tfrac12(1+\star)d$ is the self-dual part of $d$; the *Seiberg–Witten deformation complex* at a solution $(\psi,A)$ is $0\to\Omega^0(M;i\mathbb R)\xrightarrow{R_{(\psi,A)}}\Gamma(\slashed S^+)\oplus\Omega^1(M;i\mathbb R)\xrightarrow{d_{(\psi,A)}SW}\Gamma(\slashed S^-)\oplus\Omega^2_+(M;i\mathbb R)\to0$ with $R_{(\psi,A)}\xi = (-\xi\psi, 2d\xi)$ (the infinitesimal gauge action, R7.1.3) and $d_{(\psi,A)}SW$ the linearised map (next page); it is a complex at a solution ($d_{(\psi,A)}SW\circ R_{(\psi,A)} = 0$) by `Thm - Equivariance of the Seiberg-Witten Map`; its cohomology $H^0,H^1,H^2$ is the stabiliser algebra, the moduli tangent space, and the obstruction, in the sense of chapter X's `Def - The Equivariant Deformation Complex and its Folded Operator`; examples: at an irreducible solution $H^0 = 0$; the Atiyah complex is the "gauge + curvature" half with the spinor set to zero; non-example: at a reducible solution $H^0 = i\mathbb R\ne0$.
- `Thm - Differential of the Seiberg-Witten Map` — type: theorem; source items: A-T7.1.4 (Lemma 192); prereqs: [Def - The Seiberg-Witten Deformation Complex and the Atiyah Complex, Def - Seiberg-Witten Equations, Thm - Variation of the Twisted and Spin-c Dirac Operators with the Connection, Def - The Quadratic Map on Positive Spinors]; statement: The differential of $SW$ at $(\psi,A)$ is $d_{(\psi,A)}SW(\dot\psi,\dot a) = \big(\slashed D_A^+\dot\psi + \tfrac12\dot a\cdot\psi,\ d^+\dot a - 2\mu(\psi,\dot\psi)\big)$, where $\dot a\cdot\psi$ is Clifford multiplication (of the imaginary $1$-form $\dot a$), $d^+\dot a$ the self-dual part of $d\dot a$, and $\mu(\psi,\dot\psi)$ the polarisation of $\mu$; proof: differentiate each component along $A + t\dot a$, $\psi + t\dot\psi$: the Dirac part from `Thm - Variation of the Twisted and Spin-c Dirac Operators with the Connection` ($\slashed D_{A+t\dot a}^+(\psi+t\dot\psi) = \slashed D_A^+\psi + t(\slashed D_A^+\dot\psi + \tfrac12\dot a\cdot\psi) + O(t^2)$) and the curvature part from $F_{A+t\dot a}^+ = F_A^+ + t\,d^+\dot a$ and the quadratic expansion $\mu(\psi + t\dot\psi) = \mu(\psi) + 2t\mu(\psi,\dot\psi) + O(t^2)$; spec: **Haydys omits it; the polarisation and the factor $\tfrac12$ are written out**; mechanism: **the Seiberg–Witten map is affine-linear in the connection through Clifford multiplication and quadratic in the spinor, so its differential is Dirac plus a Clifford term plus $d^+$ minus the polarised quadratic**; targets: the deformation operator $D_{(\psi,A)}$ and transversality (§11.4).
- `Thm - The Atiyah Complex and the Seiberg-Witten Deformation Complex are Elliptic` — type: theorem (two parts); source items: A-T7.1.5 (Proposition 194), A-T7.1.6 (Proposition 193), A-X7.1.2; prereqs: [Def - The Seiberg-Witten Deformation Complex and the Atiyah Complex, Thm - A Short Complex is Elliptic iff its Folded Operator is Elliptic, Thm - Laplacians and Dirac Operators are Elliptic, Thm - Differential of the Seiberg-Witten Map, Def - Elliptic Complex and Its Laplacians]; statement: (i) The Atiyah complex $0\to\Omega^0\xrightarrow{d}\Omega^1\xrightarrow{d^+}\Omega^2_+\to0$ on an oriented Riemannian 4-manifold is elliptic; equivalently $(d^+, d^*):\Omega^1\to\Omega^2_+\oplus\Omega^0$ (its folded operator) is elliptic, and it is a Dirac-type operator. (ii) At any solution $(\psi,A)$, the Seiberg–Witten deformation complex (191) is elliptic; proof: (i) two routes, both recorded (Haydys's X7.1.2 asks for one): the symbol route computes $\sigma$ of $d$ and $d^+$ and checks exactness of $0\to\Lambda^0\xrightarrow{\xi\wedge}\Lambda^1\xrightarrow{\pi_+\circ\xi\wedge}\Lambda^2_+\to0$ for $\xi\ne0$ (the folding theorem `Thm - A Short Complex is Elliptic iff its Folded Operator is Elliptic` reduces it to ellipticity of $(d^+,d^*)$, whose symbol injectivity is the computation on `Thm - Laplacians and Dirac Operators are Elliptic`); the Dirac route identifies $d^+ + d^*:\Omega^1\to\Omega^2_+\oplus\Omega^0$ with a twisted Dirac operator (VIII), hence elliptic; (ii) modulo zero-order terms (immaterial for ellipticity, since ellipticity is a top-symbol condition — the writer proves this insensitivity as Lemma 1), (191) is the direct sum of the Atiyah complex and $0\to0\to\Gamma(\slashed S^+)\xrightarrow{\slashed D_A^+}\Gamma(\slashed S^-)\to0$, both elliptic ($\slashed D_A^+$ by `Thm - Laplacians and Dirac Operators are Elliptic`); spec: **fills Haydys's gap that "(191) is a complex" needs the equivariance lemma and that ellipticity ignores zero-order terms**; mechanism: **the Seiberg–Witten complex is the Atiyah complex plus the chiral Dirac operator up to lower order, and a direct sum of elliptic complexes is elliptic**; targets: Fredholmness of $D_{(\psi,A)}$ and the dimension formula (§11.4).
- `Def - Sobolev Configuration Space and Gauge Group` — type: definition (compound: Sobolev connections; the Sobolev gauge group as a Banach Lie group; the configuration space $\mathcal C^{5,2}$); source items: A-D7.1.7, A-D7.1.8, A-D7.1.9; prereqs: [Def - Sobolev Space of Sections, Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem, Thm - Composition with Analytic Functions on the Sobolev Algebra, Def - Banach Manifold and Smooth Maps between Banach Spaces, Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space]; spec: fix a smooth reference connection $A_0$ on $L_{\det}$; $\mathcal A^{k,2}(P_{\det}) := A_0 + W^{k,2}(T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)$, an affine Hilbert space independent of $A_0$; the *Sobolev gauge group* $\mathcal G^{k,2} := \{g:M\to S^1 : g\in W^{k,2}(M;\mathbb C)\}$, which for $2k>4$ (i.e. $k\ge3$, since $\dim M = 4$) is a group under pointwise multiplication (`Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem`, the algebra property) and a Banach Lie group modelled on $W^{k,2}(M;i\mathbb R)$ with $\exp\xi = e^\xi$ smooth (`Thm - Composition with Analytic Functions on the Sobolev Algebra`, and `Ex - Composition with the Exponential on the Sobolev Algebra`); the working configuration space $\mathcal C^{5,2} := W^{5,2}(\slashed S^+)\times\mathcal A^{5,2}(P_{\det})$ with gauge group $\mathcal G^{6,2}$ (one more derivative because $g^{-1}dg$ costs a derivative in $A\cdot g$); Convention callout: Haydys allows any $(k,p)$ with $kp>4$; the series fixes $(k,p) = (5,2)$ per group decision 2 (only $p = 2$ is proved in chapter IX); examples: $\mathcal G^{6,2}$ acts smoothly on $\mathcal C^{5,2}$ (next page); non-example: $\mathcal G^{2,2}$ is not a group in dimension four ($2\cdot2 = 4\not>4$; $W^{2,2}(M^4)\not\subset C^0$, `Ex - An Unbounded Function in W-1-2 of the Two-Torus` scaled up).
- `Thm - Smooth Extension of the Seiberg-Witten Map and the Gauge Action to Sobolev Spaces` — type: theorem; source items: A-T7.1.7 (Proposition 195); prereqs: [Def - Sobolev Configuration Space and Gauge Group, Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem, Thm - Composition with Analytic Functions on the Sobolev Algebra, Thm - Differential of the Seiberg-Witten Map, Thm - Variation of the Twisted and Spin-c Dirac Operators with the Connection]; statement: For $k\ge1$ and $2(k+1)>4$, the Seiberg–Witten map extends to a smooth map of Hilbert manifolds $SW:W^{k+1,2}(\slashed S^+)\times\mathcal A^{k+1,2}(P_{\det})\to W^{k,2}(\slashed S^-)\times W^{k,2}(\Lambda^2_+T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)$; the gauge action of $\mathcal G^{k+2,2}$ on $W^{k+1,2}(\slashed S^+)\times\mathcal A^{k+1,2}$ is smooth and $SW$ is equivariant; in particular $SW:\mathcal C^{5,2}\to W^{4,2}(\slashed S^-)\times W^{4,2}(\Lambda^2_+T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)$ is smooth and $\mathcal G^{6,2}$-equivariant; proof: with $A = A_0 + a$, $a\in W^{k+1,2}$: $\slashed D_A^+\psi = \slashed D_{A_0}^+\psi + \tfrac12 a\cdot\psi$ (Dirac variation), and $a\cdot\psi\in W^{k,2}$ by `Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem` (with $a\in W^{k+1,2}$, $\psi\in W^{k+1,2}$, product in $W^{k,2}$ since $2(k+1) - k>2$); $F_A^+ = F_{A_0}^+ + d^+a\in W^{k,2}$; $\mu(\psi)$ is quadratic, in $W^{k,2}$ (algebra property, $2(k+1)>4$); smoothness (not just well-definedness) of these polynomial and Clifford-bilinear maps between Sobolev spaces from `Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem` (bilinearity + boundedness ⇒ smooth) and `Thm - Composition with Analytic Functions on the Sobolev Algebra` for the gauge action ($g^{-1}dg$ needs $g\in W^{k+2,2}$, explaining the $+2$); spec: **Haydys asserts smoothness via Theorem 136(iv); the series proves it from the $p = 2$ multiplication theorem and the analytic-composition theorem of chapter IX**; mechanism: **the Seiberg–Witten map is a polynomial in $(\psi,a)$ with Clifford-bilinear coefficients, and Sobolev multiplication makes each monomial a bounded, hence smooth, multilinear map**.
- `Ex - The Sobolev Gauge Group is a Banach Lie Group in Dimension Four` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-D7.1.8 (drill); prereqs: [Def - Sobolev Configuration Space and Gauge Group, Thm - Composition with Analytic Functions on the Sobolev Algebra]; spec: prove that $\mathcal G^{6,2} = W^{6,2}(M^4;S^1)$ is a group under pointwise multiplication (algebra property $2\cdot6>4$) and a Banach Lie group with Lie algebra $W^{6,2}(M;i\mathbb R)$ and charts $\xi\mapsto g_0 e^\xi$; verify $g^{-1}dg\in W^{5,2}$ so the action lands in $\mathcal A^{5,2}$.
- `Ex - The Linearised Seiberg-Witten Operator in a Local Trivialisation` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T7.1.4 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Differential of the Seiberg-Witten Map]; spec: in a local trivialisation over an open set of $\mathbb R^4$ with the flat metric and quaternionic spinor model, write $d_{(\psi,A)}SW$ explicitly as a first-order operator; verify $d_{(\psi,A)}SW\circ R_{(\psi,A)} = 0$ at a solution by direct computation, confirming (191) is a complex.
- `Ex - Ellipticity of the Atiyah Complex by the Koszul Symbol` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-X7.1.2 (drill; Proposition 194); prereqs: [Thm - The Atiyah Complex and the Seiberg-Witten Deformation Complex are Elliptic, Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions]; spec: compute the principal symbols of $d$ and $d^+$ and verify exactness of the symbol sequence $0\to\Lambda^0\to\Lambda^1\to\Lambda^2_+\to0$ for $\xi\ne0$ (dimension count $1 - 4 + 3 = 0$ and injectivity/surjectivity via the self-dual basis); cross-check with the Dirac-operator route ($d^+ + d^*$ twisted Dirac).
- `Exercise Index - §11.2 The Deformation Complex, Sobolev Completions, and the Configuration Space` — lists: [Ex - The Sobolev Gauge Group is a Banach Lie Group in Dimension Four, Ex - The Linearised Seiberg-Witten Operator in a Local Trivialisation, Ex - Ellipticity of the Atiyah Complex by the Koszul Symbol]

### §11.3 Compactness of the Moduli Space
- `Thm - The Bochner Identity for the Spinor Norm` — type: theorem; source items: A-I7.1.3 (proved here, not imported); prereqs: [Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection, Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian, Def - Metric-Compatible Connection]; statement: For a section $\psi$ of a Hermitian bundle with a metric connection $\nabla_A$ over a Riemannian manifold, $\tfrac12\Delta|\psi|^2 = \langle\nabla_A^*\nabla_A\psi,\psi\rangle - |\nabla_A\psi|^2$ pointwise, where $\Delta = -\sum\partial_i^2$-type (non-negative) Laplacian on functions and $\nabla_A^*\nabla_A$ the connection Laplacian; proof: in a synchronous orthonormal frame at the point (`Thm - Existence of Synchronous Orthonormal Frames`, VIII), $\Delta|\psi|^2 = -\sum_i\partial_{e_i}\partial_{e_i}\langle\psi,\psi\rangle = -\sum_i\partial_{e_i}(2\operatorname{Re}\langle\nabla_{e_i}\psi,\psi\rangle) = -2\sum_i(\operatorname{Re}\langle\nabla_{e_i}\nabla_{e_i}\psi,\psi\rangle + |\nabla_{e_i}\psi|^2)$ (metric compatibility twice), and $-\sum_i\nabla_{e_i}\nabla_{e_i}\psi = \nabla_A^*\nabla_A\psi$ at the point (`Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian`, synchronous frame), giving the identity; spec: **Haydys imports this (I7.1.3); the series proves it, the key being the synchronous-frame formula for $\nabla^*\nabla$**; mechanism: **the Laplacian of the squared norm is the connection Laplacian tested against the section minus the norm of the first derivative — the Bochner/Kato identity**; targets: the maximum-principle $C^0$ bound.
- `Thm - The C0 Bound on the Spinor` — type: theorem; source items: A-T7.1.8 (Lemma 196), A-T7.1.9 (Remark 197), A-R7.1.5; prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Equations, Thm - The Bochner Identity for the Spinor Norm, Thm - Clifford Action of 2-Forms and the Chiral Weitzenbock Formula in Dimension Four, Def - The Quadratic Map on Positive Spinors, Thm - Sobolev Embedding Theorem, Def - Scalar Curvature]; statement: There is a constant depending only on the metric such that every solution $(\psi,A)\in\mathcal C^{5,2}$ of the Seiberg–Witten equations satisfies the pointwise bound $|\psi|^2\le\max\{0, -\min_M s_g\}$; in particular $\|\psi\|_{C^0}^2\le\tfrac12\max_M(-s_g)^+$ where $s_g$ is the scalar curvature (the constant made precise on the page); proof: $\psi\in W^{5,2}\subset C^2$ (`Thm - Sobolev Embedding Theorem`, $5 - 2>2$); at a maximum $x_0$ of $|\psi|^2$, $\Delta|\psi|^2\ge0$ (non-negative Laplacian), so by `Thm - The Bochner Identity for the Spinor Norm` $\langle\nabla_A^*\nabla_A\psi,\psi\rangle\ge|\nabla_A\psi|^2\ge0$ at $x_0$; the chiral Weitzenböck (Lichnerowicz) formula `Thm - Clifford Action of 2-Forms and the Chiral Weitzenbock Formula in Dimension Four` with $\slashed D_A^+\psi = 0$ and $F_A^+ = \mu(\psi)$ gives $0 = \langle\nabla_A^*\nabla_A\psi,\psi\rangle + \tfrac14 s_g|\psi|^2 + \tfrac12\langle F_A^+\cdot\psi,\psi\rangle$ and $\langle F_A^+\cdot\psi,\psi\rangle = \langle\mu(\psi)\cdot\psi,\psi\rangle = \tfrac12|\psi|^4$ (from `Def - The Quadratic Map on Positive Spinors`); combining, at $x_0$: $\tfrac14 s_g|\psi|^2 + \tfrac14|\psi|^4\le -|\nabla_A\psi|^2\le0$, so $|\psi(x_0)|^2\le -s_g(x_0)\le\max_M(-s_g)$ (dividing by $|\psi(x_0)|^2$ if non-zero); **source typo (Haydys B17, p. 65): the printed constants show a factor-2 inconsistency between $\tfrac14|\psi|^4$ in the Weitzenböck identity and $\tfrac12|\psi|^4$ in the following inequality; the page uses $\langle\tfrac12 F_A^+\cdot\psi,\psi\rangle = \tfrac12\cdot\tfrac12|\psi|^4 = \tfrac14|\psi|^4$ consistently and states the resulting bound $|\psi|^2\le -s_g$**; the sign remark (T7.1.9, R7.1.6): for the equation $F_A^+ = -\mu(\psi)$ the $|\psi|^4$ term flips sign and the argument fails — recorded; spec: mechanism: **at the maximum of $|\psi|^2$ the connection Laplacian is non-negative, and the Lichnerowicz formula turns the equation into a pointwise inequality bounding $|\psi|^4$ by the scalar curvature**; targets: the vanishing theorem (§11.5), compactness.
- `Thm - Curvature Bounds for Seiberg-Witten Solutions` — type: theorem (two parts); source items: A-T7.1.10 (Corollary 198), A-T7.1.11 (Corollary 199, equation (200)), A-T7.1.12 (Remark 201), A-R7.1.7; prereqs: [Thm - The C0 Bound on the Spinor, Def - The Quadratic Map on Positive Spinors, Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature, Ex - Energy Identity for U(1) Connections on a Four-Manifold, Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions]; statement: There are constants depending only on the metric such that for every solution $(\psi,A)$: (i) $\|\psi\|_{L^p}\le\kappa_p$ for every $p$ (from the $C^0$ bound and finite volume); (ii) $\|F_A^+\|_{L^2}\le C$ (since $F_A^+ = \mu(\psi)$ is quadratic in $\psi$, $\|F_A^+\|_{L^2}^2 = \tfrac14\|\,|\psi|^2\|_{L^2}^2\le C\|\psi\|_{L^4}^4$); and (iii) $\|F_A^-\|_{L^2}^2\le C - 4\pi^2 c_1(L_{\det})^2$, hence $\|F_A\|_{L^2}^2 = \|F_A^+\|_{L^2}^2 + \|F_A^-\|_{L^2}^2$ is bounded by a constant depending only on the metric and the spin$^c$ structure; **source typo (Haydys B18, p. 65, (200)): the printed second inequality bounds $\|F_A^-\|_{L^2}$ but the proof bounds the square $\|F_A^-\|_{L^2}^2$; the series states the squared bound**; proof: (i) trivial; (ii) from the $C^0$/$L^4$ bound; (iii) the energy identity for the abelian curvature: $c_1(L_{\det})^2 = \tfrac{1}{4\pi^2}(\|F_A^+\|_{L^2}^2 - \|F_A^-\|_{L^2}^2)$ with the signs fixed on `Ex - Energy Identity for U(1) Connections on a Four-Manifold` (VII), so $\|F_A^-\|_{L^2}^2 = \|F_A^+\|_{L^2}^2 - 4\pi^2 c_1(L_{\det})^2\le C - 4\pi^2 c_1(L_{\det})^2$; **the sign convention Haydys leaves ambiguous (B17, the $\int F^\pm\wedge F^\pm = \pm\|F^\pm\|^2$ question) is settled by `Ex - Energy Identity for U(1) Connections on a Four-Manifold`, which chapter XI cites**; (iii)/Remark 201 (T7.1.12): the right-hand side is a constant independent of the solution, used in the finiteness theorem (§11.5); spec: mechanism: **the curvature's self-dual part is controlled by the spinor's $C^0$ bound, and its anti-self-dual part by the topological energy identity**; targets: uniform Sobolev bounds and compactness.
- `Thm - Coulomb Gauge Fixing with Elliptic Estimates` — type: theorem; source items: A-T7.1.13 (Lemma 202), A-I7.1.5; prereqs: [Def - Sobolev Configuration Space and Gauge Group, Thm - The de Rham Complex is Elliptic and Harmonic Forms Represent de Rham Cohomology, Thm - Hodge Theorem for Elliptic Complexes, Thm - Elliptic Regularity and the Elliptic Estimate, Thm - Composition with Analytic Functions on the Sobolev Algebra]; statement: Let $A_0$ be a smooth reference connection on $L_{\det}$. For every $k\ge1$ there are constants $C_1,C_2$ (depending on the metric, $A_0$, $k$) such that: for every $W^{k,2}$-connection $A$ there is a gauge transformation $g\in\mathcal G^{k+1,2}$ with $A\cdot g = A_0 + \alpha$, $\alpha\in W^{k,2}(T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)$, satisfying the Coulomb condition $d^*\alpha = 0$ and the estimate $\|\alpha\|_{W^{k,2}}\le C_1\|F_A^+\|_{W^{k-1,2}} + C_2$; the harmonic component $\alpha_h$ of $\alpha$ (in $\mathcal H^1$) can be taken bounded in $L^2$ independently of $k$; proof: **Haydys cites Morgan, *The Seiberg–Witten Equations*, Lemma 5.3.1; the series proves it in full** (it is not on the pre-approved import list): write $A = A_0 + b$, $b\in W^{k,2}(T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)$; solve for $g = e^{i\chi}$ with $d^*(b - d\chi\cdot 2/?)$... precisely, since $A\cdot g = A + 2g^{-1}dg = A_0 + b + 2i\,d\chi$ (for $g = e^{i\chi}$), require $d^*(b + 2i\,d\chi) = 0$, i.e. $2i\,d^*d\chi = -d^*b$; this Laplace equation $\Delta\chi = -\tfrac{1}{2i}d^*b$ on functions is solvable modulo constants iff the right side is $L^2$-orthogonal to the harmonic functions (the constants) — automatic since $d^*b\perp\ker\Delta$ (`Thm - Fredholm Alternative for Elliptic Operators`, IX), giving $\chi\in W^{k+1,2}$ with the elliptic estimate $\|\chi\|_{W^{k+1,2}}\le C\|d^*b\|_{W^{k-1,2}}$; set $\alpha = b + 2i\,d\chi$, so $d^*\alpha = 0$; the estimate $\|\alpha\|_{W^{k,2}}\le C_1\|F_A^+\|_{W^{k-1,2}} + C_2$ comes from the Hodge decomposition $\alpha = \alpha_h + d^*\beta$ (no $d$-part since $d^*\alpha = 0$ forces the exact part to vanish — $\alpha$ is coclosed, so $\alpha\in\mathcal H^1\oplus d^*\Omega^2$, `Thm - The de Rham Complex is Elliptic and Harmonic Forms Represent de Rham Cohomology`), the identity $d^+\alpha = F_A^+ - F_{A_0}^+$, and the elliptic estimate for the folded operator $(d^+,d^*)$ (`Thm - Elliptic Regularity and the Elliptic Estimate`, applied to the elliptic $(d^+,d^*)$: $\|\alpha - \alpha_h\|_{W^{k,2}}\le C\|(d^+\alpha, d^*\alpha)\|_{W^{k-1,2}} = C\|F_A^+ - F_{A_0}^+\|_{W^{k-1,2}}$), with $\alpha_h$ chosen in the fixed finite-dimensional $\mathcal H^1$ (its $L^2$ bound absorbed into $C_2$; the residual gauge freedom of harmonic gauge transformations $H^1(M;\mathbb Z)$ used to reduce $\alpha_h$ to a bounded fundamental domain); spec: mechanism: **a connection can be put in Coulomb gauge by solving a Laplace equation for the gauge parameter, and the elliptic estimate for the Atiyah operator then controls the coclosed remainder by the self-dual curvature**; targets: the bootstrap giving uniform $W^{k,2}$ bounds.
- `Thm - Uniform Sobolev Bounds after Gauge Fixing` — type: theorem; source items: A-T7.1.14 (Proposition 203), A-X7.1.1; prereqs: [Thm - Coulomb Gauge Fixing with Elliptic Estimates, Thm - The C0 Bound on the Spinor, Thm - Curvature Bounds for Seiberg-Witten Solutions, Thm - Elliptic Regularity and the Elliptic Estimate, Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem]; statement: For every $k\ge0$ there is $C_k>0$ such that every solution $(\psi,A)$ of the Seiberg–Witten equations is gauge equivalent, via some $g\in\mathcal G^{k+1,2}$, to a configuration $(\psi', A_0 + \alpha)$ (with $\psi' = \bar g\psi$, $d^*\alpha = 0$) satisfying $\|(\psi',\alpha)\|_{W^{k,2}}\le C_k$; proof: induction on $k$: $k = 0$ — $\|\psi\|_{L^2}$ bounded (`Thm - The C0 Bound on the Spinor`) and $\|F_A^+\|_{L^2}$ bounded (`Thm - Curvature Bounds`), so `Thm - Coulomb Gauge Fixing` bounds $\|\alpha\|_{L^2}$; $k = 1$ — from $0 = \slashed D_A^+\psi = \slashed D_{A_0}^+\psi + \tfrac12\alpha\cdot\psi$, the term $\alpha\cdot\psi$ is $L^2$-bounded ($\psi\in C^0$, $\alpha\in L^2$), so the elliptic estimate for $\slashed D_{A_0}^+$ bounds $\psi$ in $W^{1,2}$; then $F_A^+ = \mu(\psi)\in L^2$ bounds $\alpha$ in $W^{1,2}$; $k = 2$ — the pointwise estimate $|\nabla_{A_0}\mu(\psi)|\le C|\nabla_A\psi||\psi|$ ($\mu$ quadratic; **the content-map gap mixing $\nabla^{LC}$ and $\nabla_A$ is resolved by writing $\nabla_{A}\mu(\psi)$ and controlling the difference $\alpha\cdot\psi$**) gives $F_A^+\in W^{1,2}$, hence $\alpha\in W^{2,2}$, and Sobolev multiplication gives $\alpha\cdot\psi\in W^{1,2}$, so $\psi\in W^{2,2}$; the induction step $k\ge3\Rightarrow k+1$ uses that $W^{k,2}(M^4)$ is an algebra for $k\ge3$ (`Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem`), so $\mu(\psi)\in W^{k,2}$, `Thm - Coulomb Gauge Fixing` bounds $\alpha\in W^{k+1,2}$, and the elliptic estimate for $\slashed D_{A_0}^+$ bounds $\psi\in W^{k+1,2}$; **the $k = 3$ step (Haydys's Exercise X7.1.1) is written out; the compatibility of the gauge transformations across levels is handled by using one Coulomb gauge (unique modulo constants and $H^1(M;\mathbb Z)$)**; spec: mechanism: **the elliptic estimate lets each derivative of the curvature be traded for a derivative of the spinor and vice versa, and the Sobolev algebra closes the bootstrap once past the critical exponent**; targets: compactness.
- `Thm - Compactness of the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space` — type: theorem (anchor; three parts); source items: A-T7.1.15 (Corollary 205), A-T7.1.16 (Theorem 206), A-T7.1.21 (Remark 216), A-X7.1.3, A-R7.1.4; prereqs: [Thm - Uniform Sobolev Bounds after Gauge Fixing, Thm - Rellich Compactness Theorem, Thm - Elliptic Regularity and the Elliptic Estimate, Def - Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space, Thm - The de Rham Complex is Elliptic and Harmonic Forms Represent de Rham Cohomology]; statement: (i) The Seiberg–Witten moduli space $\mathcal M = SW^{-1}(0)\cap\mathcal C^{5,2}/\mathcal G^{6,2}$ is compact. (ii) Every solution in $\mathcal C^{5,2}$ is gauge equivalent to a smooth solution, and $\mathcal M$ is homeomorphic to the $C^\infty$ moduli space $\mathcal M_\infty = \{$smooth solutions$\}/C^\infty(M;U(1))$, which is compact in the $C^\infty$ topology. (iii) The same holds for the perturbed equations $F_A^+ = \mu(\psi) + \eta$ with $\eta\in W^{4,2}(\Lambda^2_+T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)$, with the $C^0$ bound $|\psi|^2\le\max\{0, -\min s_g\} + C\|\eta\|_{C^0}$-type; proof: (i) a sequence of solutions is gauge equivalent (via $\mathcal G^{6,2}$) to $(\psi_n, A_0 + \alpha_n)$ with $(\psi_n,\alpha_n)$ bounded in $W^{6,2}$ (`Thm - Uniform Sobolev Bounds`, $k = 6$); by `Thm - Rellich Compactness Theorem` a subsequence converges in $\mathcal C^{5,2}$ (compact embedding $W^{6,2}\hookrightarrow W^{5,2}$), and the limit solves the equations by continuity of $SW$; (ii) regularity: $F_A = F_{A_0} + d\alpha\in W^{k-1,2}$ for all $k$ (uniform bounds), so $d\alpha = (d^+ + d^*)\alpha$ (since $d^*\alpha = 0$) is smooth, hence $\alpha$ is smooth by elliptic regularity for the elliptic $(d^+ + d^*)$ (`Thm - Elliptic Regularity and the Elliptic Estimate`), so $A$ is smooth, and $\psi\in\ker\slashed D_A^+$ (smooth elliptic operator) is smooth; the $C^\infty$ compactness by a diagonal argument over $k$ (bounded in $W^{k,2}$ for every $k$ after gauge fixing, convergent subsequences in each $W^{k-1,2}$, diagonalise); **the level-compatibility of the gauge transformations (content-map gap) is handled by the uniqueness of Coulomb gauge modulo the finite $H^1(M;\mathbb Z)$**; (iii) the perturbed $C^0$ bound and the same bootstrap (Haydys's Exercise X7.1.3, "cosmetic changes"), written out; spec: mechanism: **uniform bounds in every Sobolev norm after gauge fixing plus Rellich give sequential compactness, and elliptic regularity upgrades $W^{5,2}$ limits to smooth ones**; targets: the moduli space is a compact manifold (§11.4), the invariant is a count/integral over a compact manifold (§11.5).
- `Ex - The Bochner Identity on Euclidean Space` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐; source items: A-I7.1.3 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - The Bochner Identity for the Spinor Norm]; spec: for the trivial bundle over $\mathbb R^4$ with $\nabla_A = d + iA$, verify $\tfrac12\Delta|\psi|^2 = \langle\nabla_A^*\nabla_A\psi,\psi\rangle - |\nabla_A\psi|^2$ by direct computation; deduce the sub/super-solution behaviour of $|\psi|^2$ at extrema.
- `Ex - The C0 Bound and Positive Scalar Curvature` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T7.1.8 (drill toward §11.5); prereqs: [Thm - The C0 Bound on the Spinor]; spec: show that if $s_g>0$ everywhere then every solution has $\psi\equiv0$ (so is reducible); if $s_g\ge0$ with $s_g>0$ somewhere, show $\psi\equiv0$ using the strong maximum principle applied to the Bochner identity (the writer supplies the strong-max-principle step); this is the analytic heart of Witten's vanishing theorem.
- `Ex - The Energy Identity Fixes the Sign in the Curvature Bound` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: A-T7.1.11 (drill; Haydys B17/B18); prereqs: [Thm - Curvature Bounds for Seiberg-Witten Solutions, Ex - Energy Identity for U(1) Connections on a Four-Manifold]; spec: reprove $\|F_A^-\|_{L^2}^2 = \|F_A^+\|_{L^2}^2 - 4\pi^2 c_1(L_{\det})^2$ with all signs tracked for the imaginary-valued curvature (writing $F_A = iG$, $G$ real), reconciling Haydys's B17/B18 sign ambiguity via `Ex - Energy Identity for U(1) Connections on a Four-Manifold`; deduce the constant on the right of (200) depends only on the metric and $c_1(L_{\det})^2$.
- `Exercise Index - §11.3 Compactness of the Moduli Space` — lists: [Ex - The Bochner Identity on Euclidean Space, Ex - The C0 Bound and Positive Scalar Curvature, Ex - The Energy Identity Fixes the Sign in the Curvature Bound]

### §11.4 Transversality, Reducibles, the Dimension Formula, and Orientability
- `Def - Coulomb Slice and the Irreducible Configuration Space` — type: definition (compound: the irreducible configuration space; the adjoint $R^*$; the Coulomb slice); source items: A-D7.1.12, A-T7.1.17 (Proposition 207), A-R7.1.8, A-I7.1.8; prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space, Def - Infinitesimal Action and Local Slice, Thm - A Free Action with Slices has a Manifold Quotient, Def - Sobolev Configuration Space and Gauge Group]; spec: $\mathcal C^{5,2}_{\mathrm{irr}} := \{(\psi,A)\in\mathcal C^{5,2} : \psi\not\equiv0\}$, on which $\mathcal G^{6,2}$ acts freely (`Thm - Equivariance of the Seiberg-Witten Map` (ii)); the infinitesimal action $R_{(\psi,A)}\xi = (-\xi\psi, 2d\xi)$ has formal $L^2$-adjoint $R^*_{(\psi,A)}(\dot\psi,\dot a) = 2d^*\dot a + i\operatorname{Re}\langle\psi, i\dot\psi\rangle$ (target $\Omega^0(M;i\mathbb R)$; the writer derives it by integration by parts, using the Hermitian inner product on spinors); the *Coulomb slice* at an irreducible $(\psi,A)$ is $(\psi,A) + \ker R^*_{(\psi,A)}$ — a slice for the $\mathcal G^{6,2}$-action (T7.1.17, Proposition 207); **Haydys omits the slice proof; the series proves it as the local-slice property of `Def - Infinitesimal Action and Local Slice`**: $R^*_{(\psi,A)}$ is surjective with $R_{(\psi,A)}$ injective (freeness at irreducibles) and the folded operator $(R^*_{(\psi,A)}, \cdot)$ elliptic, so $\ker R^*_{(\psi,A)}$ is a closed complement of the orbit direction $\operatorname{Im}R_{(\psi,A)}$ and the exponential/gauge map $G\times S\to X$ is a local diffeomorphism (inverse function theorem on the Hilbert manifold); Axiom Motivation (R7.1.8): the slice tangent must be transverse to the orbit, and the $L^2$-orthogonal complement $\ker R^*$ is the natural choice; examples: $\mathcal B^{\mathrm{irr}} := \mathcal C^{5,2}_{\mathrm{irr}}/\mathcal G^{6,2}$ is a Hilbert manifold (`Thm - A Free Action with Slices has a Manifold Quotient`); non-example: no such slice at a reducible ($R_{(\psi,A)}$ has kernel = constants).
- `Def - The Perturbed Seiberg-Witten Equations and the Deformation Operator` — type: definition (compound: the perturbation; the perturbed moduli space; the operators $D_{(\psi,A)}$ and $D_0$); source items: A-D7.1.13 (Proposition 208), A-D7.1.14 (Corollary 212), A-D7.1.15 (equations (214), (215)), A-R7.1.9, A-R7.1.10; prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Equations, Def - Coulomb Slice and the Irreducible Configuration Space, Thm - Differential of the Seiberg-Witten Map, Thm - The Atiyah Complex and the Seiberg-Witten Deformation Complex are Elliptic]; spec: the *perturbed Seiberg–Witten map* $SW(\psi,A,\eta) := (\slashed D_A^+\psi, F_A^+ - \mu(\psi) - \eta)$ for $\eta\in W^{4,2}(\Lambda^2_+T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)$, with $SW_\eta := SW(\cdot,\cdot,\eta)$; the *perturbed equations* $\slashed D_A^+\psi = 0$, $F_A^+ = \mu(\psi) + \eta$; the perturbed irreducible moduli space $\mathcal M^{\mathrm{irr}}_\eta := \{(\psi,A) : SW_\eta(\psi,A) = 0, \psi\not\equiv0\}/\mathcal G^{6,2}$; the *deformation operator* $D_{(\psi,A)} := (d_{(\psi,A)}SW_\eta, R^*_{(\psi,A)}):T_{(\psi,A)}\mathcal C^{5,2}\to W^{4,2}(\slashed S^-\oplus\Lambda^2_+T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R\oplus i\mathbb R)$ — the folded operator of chapter X §10.5 for this problem (**source typo Haydys B20, p. 68: the printed target of (211) omits the $\Omega^0(i\mathbb R)$ summand present in (214); the series includes it**); $D_{(\psi,A)} = D_0 + B$ with $D_0 = \slashed D_A^+\oplus(d^+ + d^*)$ block-diagonal and $B$ of order zero (equation (215)); Axiom Motivation (R7.1.9, R7.1.10): $\eta$ enters only the curvature equation, making $\partial_\eta SW = -\mathrm{id}$ surjective onto the second factor, so the whole transversality problem reduces to surjectivity of the first (Dirac) component — this is the parametric-transversality setup of chapter X with $W$ the space of perturbations; examples: $\eta = 0$ recovers the unperturbed equations; non-example: a perturbation of the Dirac equation instead of the curvature equation would not decouple the surjectivity.
- `Thm - Aronszajn Unique Continuation for Dirac-Type Operators` — type: theorem; source items: A-I7.1.9 (proved here, not imported); prereqs: [Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection, Thm - Clifford Action of 2-Forms and the Chiral Weitzenbock Formula in Dimension Four, Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian]; statement: Let $P$ be a second-order elliptic operator on a Hermitian bundle over a connected Riemannian manifold with $P = \nabla^*\nabla + (\text{order } \le 1)$ (in particular $P = \slashed D_A^-\slashed D_A^+ = \nabla_A^*\nabla_A + (\text{zero order})$ by Weitzenböck), and let $\phi$ solve $P\phi = 0$ (or the differential inequality $|P\phi|\le C(|\phi| + |\nabla\phi|)$). If $\phi$ vanishes on a non-empty open set, then $\phi\equiv0$; equivalently, if $\slashed D_A^-\phi = 0$ and $\phi$ vanishes on an open set then $\phi\equiv0$; proof: **Aronszajn's unique continuation is not on the pre-approved import list and is not book-length, so it is proved in full via Carleman estimates** (following Aronszajn, *A unique continuation theorem for solutions of elliptic PDE*, J. Math. Pures Appl. 36 (1957); or Kazdan, *Unique continuation in geometry*, Comm. Pure Appl. Math. 41 (1988); Booß-Bavnbek–Wojciechowski, *Elliptic Boundary Problems for Dirac Operators*, Ch. 8): Lemma 1 (Carleman estimate) — in geodesic normal coordinates near a point, for a weight $\varphi(x) = |x|^{-\tau}$-type (or $\varphi = e^{\gamma|x|}$) there is $C$ and $\gamma_0$ such that $\gamma\int e^{2\gamma\varphi}|u|^2 + \int e^{2\gamma\varphi}|\nabla u|^2\le C\int e^{2\gamma\varphi}|\Delta u|^2$ for $u\in C_c^\infty$ supported in a small ball, all $\gamma\ge\gamma_0$; Lemma 2 (reduction of the first-order Dirac equation to a Laplace-type inequality via $\slashed D_A^-\slashed D_A^+ = \nabla_A^*\nabla_A + \mathcal R$, `Thm - Clifford Action of 2-Forms and the Chiral Weitzenbock Formula in Dimension Four`); Lemma 3 (the Carleman estimate forces $\phi$ to vanish on a slightly larger ball, then a connectedness/continuity argument propagates the vanishing set to all of $M$); the writer carries out the standard convexity-of-the-weight and integration-by-parts computation for Lemma 1 in full; spec: this is the longest analytic page of chapter XI after the compactness pages; **Haydys imports it ([Aro57]); the series proves it because it is used essentially in the transversality theorem and is within chapter length**; mechanism: **an exponential weight makes the elliptic operator invertible on functions concentrated where the weight is large, so a solution vanishing on an open set cannot grow into the region without violating the weighted estimate**; targets: surjectivity of the linearised Dirac component in transversality.
- `Thm - The Origin is a Regular Value of the Perturbed Seiberg-Witten Map` — type: theorem; source items: A-T7.1.18 (Proposition 208), A-T7.1.19; prereqs: [Def - The Perturbed Seiberg-Witten Equations and the Deformation Operator, Thm - Differential of the Seiberg-Witten Map, Thm - Aronszajn Unique Continuation for Dirac-Type Operators, Thm - Elliptic Operators on Closed Manifolds are Fredholm, Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms, Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint]; statement: (i) $0$ is a regular value of the perturbed map $SW:\mathcal C^{5,2}_{\mathrm{irr}}\times W^{4,2}(\Lambda^2_+T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)\to W^{4,2}(\slashed S^-)\times W^{4,2}(\Lambda^2_+T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)$; (ii) at any solution the deformation operator $D_{(\psi,A)}$ is elliptic (`Thm - A Short Complex is Elliptic iff its Folded Operator is Elliptic`) and Fredholm (`Thm - Elliptic Operators on Closed Manifolds are Fredholm`); proof: (i) since $\partial_\eta SW = -\mathrm{id}$ is surjective onto the second factor, it suffices that $T := \mathrm{pr}_1\,d_{(\psi,A)}SW_\eta:(\dot\psi,\dot a)\mapsto\slashed D_A^+\dot\psi + \tfrac12\dot a\cdot\psi$ is surjective onto $W^{4,2}(\slashed S^-)$ at every solution; suppose $\phi\in(\operatorname{Im}T)^\perp$ (the range is closed since $T$ is $\slashed D_A^+$ plus a compact term, `Thm - Elliptic Operators on Closed Manifolds are Fredholm` — **the closed-range/Hahn–Banach step Haydys leaves implicit is supplied**); taking $\dot a = 0$: $\langle\slashed D_A^+\dot\psi,\phi\rangle = \langle\dot\psi,\slashed D_A^-\phi\rangle = 0$ for all $\dot\psi$, so $\slashed D_A^-\phi = 0$ (`Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint`); taking $\dot\psi = 0$: $\langle\dot a\cdot\psi,\phi\rangle_{L^2} = 0$ for all imaginary $1$-forms $\dot a$; since $\psi\not\equiv0$ and $\psi\in C^0$, $\psi\ne0$ on an open set $U$, and pointwise the real map $\mathbb R^4\to\slashed S^-$, $v\mapsto v\cdot\psi$, is a real-linear isomorphism (`Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms` (ii); the $i$-factor from $\dot a\in i\mathbb R$ preserves the image — **the source glosses this; the writer notes $i(\mathbb R^4\cdot\psi) = \slashed S^-$**), so $\phi\equiv0$ on $U$; by `Thm - Aronszajn Unique Continuation for Dirac-Type Operators` applied to $\slashed D_A^-\phi = 0$, $\phi\equiv0$ everywhere, contradiction; (ii) from the deformation-operator structure $D_0 + B$; spec: **the two gaps (closed range; the $\mathbb Ri$-valued surjectivity) are filled, and Aronszajn is now a proved page**; mechanism: **the perturbation makes the curvature equation trivially transverse, so transversality is surjectivity of the Dirac component, which fails only if a non-zero co-spinor is orthogonal to all $v\cdot\psi$ and Dirac-harmonic — impossible by pointwise surjectivity of Clifford multiplication and unique continuation**; targets: generic smoothness of the moduli space.
- `Thm - Dimension Formula for the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space` — type: theorem (anchor; carries the one Atiyah–Singer import); source items: A-T7.1.20 (Corollary 212), A-I7.1.11, A-I7.1.12, A-I7.1.13, A-I7.1.14, A-R7.1.11; prereqs: [Thm - The Origin is a Regular Value of the Perturbed Seiberg-Witten Map, Thm - Parametric Transversality, Thm - The Equivariant Moduli Space is a Manifold of Index Dimension, Thm - Stability of the Fredholm Property under Small and Compact Perturbations, Thm - Hodge Theorem for Elliptic Complexes, Thm - The de Rham Complex is Elliptic and Harmonic Forms Represent de Rham Cohomology, Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold]; statement: There is a residual set $\mathcal H\subset W^{4,2}(\Lambda^2_+T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)$ such that for $\eta\in\mathcal H$, $\mathcal M^{\mathrm{irr}}_\eta$ is a smooth manifold of dimension $d = \tfrac14\big(c_1(L_{\det})^2 - 2\chi(M) - 3\operatorname{sign}(M)\big)$, where $\chi(M)$ is the Euler characteristic, $\operatorname{sign}(M) = b_2^+ - b_2^-$ the signature, and $c_1(L_{\det})^2 = \langle c_1(L_{\det})\smile c_1(L_{\det}), [M]\rangle$ the self-intersection; proof: genericity of $\mathcal H$ from `Thm - The Origin is a Regular Value ...` and `Thm - Parametric Transversality` (the parameter is $\eta$; Sard–Smale on the projection $SW^{-1}(0)\to\{\eta\}$) plus `Thm - The Equivariant Moduli Space is a Manifold of Index Dimension` (the free $\mathcal G^{6,2}$-action on irreducibles, the Coulomb slice, and $\operatorname{coker}D_{(\psi,A)} = 0$ at a regular irreducible point make $\mathcal M^{\mathrm{irr}}_\eta$ a manifold of dimension $\operatorname{index}D_{(\psi,A)}$); the index: $D_{(\psi,A)} = D_0 + B$ with $B$ order zero, so $\operatorname{index}D_{(\psi,A)} = \operatorname{index}D_0$ by homotopy-invariance of the Fredholm index (`Thm - Stability of the Fredholm Property under Small and Compact Perturbations`, R7.1.11); $D_0 = \slashed D_A^+\oplus(d^+ + d^*)$ decouples, $\operatorname{index}_{\mathbb R}D_0 = 2\operatorname{index}_{\mathbb C}\slashed D_A^+ + \operatorname{index}(d^+ + d^*)$; $\operatorname{index}(d^+ + d^*) = b_1 - b_0 - b_2^+$ (kernel $H^1$, cokernel $H^0\oplus H^2_+$ by `Thm - Hodge Theorem for Elliptic Complexes` / `Thm - The de Rham Complex is Elliptic ...`); **source typo (Haydys B21, p. 69): the printed intermediate $\operatorname{index}(d^+ + d^*) = \tfrac12(\chi + \operatorname{sign})$ has the wrong sign; the correct value is $b_1 - b_0 - b_2^+ = -\tfrac12(\chi + \operatorname{sign})$ (using $\chi = 2b_0 - 2b_1 + b_2^+ + b_2^-$, $\operatorname{sign} = b_2^+ - b_2^-$), and the final formula (213) is nevertheless correct — the writer records the correction**; and $\operatorname{index}_{\mathbb C}\slashed D_A^+ = \tfrac18(c_1(L_{\det})^2 - \operatorname{sign}(M))$ by the Atiyah–Singer index theorem for the spin$^c$ Dirac operator (imported); combining, $d = 2\cdot\tfrac18(c_1^2 - \operatorname{sign}) - (b_0 - b_1 + b_2^+) = \tfrac14(c_1^2 - 2\chi - 3\operatorname{sign})$; **`Imported without proof` callout (the single planned import in VII–XI, per group decision 6):** the Atiyah–Singer index theorem $\operatorname{index}_{\mathbb C}\slashed D_A^+ = \tfrac18(c_1(L_{\det})^2 - \operatorname{sign}(M))$, source Lawson–Michelsohn *Spin Geometry* Thm III.13.10, IV.1.1, with the architecture paragraph and the note that everything else is proved; **the topological identities $\chi, \operatorname{sign}$ in terms of Betti numbers are `[NEEDED FROM XII–XIII: Poincaré duality and the Hodge signature]`**; $c_1(L_{\det})^2$ as an integer is recorded as bold context with `Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold` (XIII) named (the de Rham self-intersection $\int_M(\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F_A)^2$ is computed here; its integrality is the XII/XIII input); spec: mechanism: **the dimension of the moduli space is the index of the deformation operator, which is the index of the decoupled Dirac plus Atiyah operator, computed by Atiyah–Singer and Hodge theory**; What Makes This Hard: the index must be computed on the deformation operator, not the equations, via the homotopy $D_0 + tB$.
- `Thm - Generic Absence of Reducible Solutions` — type: theorem (two parts); source items: A-D7.1.16 (Proposition 217), A-T7.1.22 (Proposition 217), A-T7.1.23 (Corollary 218); prereqs: [Def - The Perturbed Seiberg-Witten Equations and the Deformation Operator, Thm - The de Rham Complex is Elliptic and Harmonic Forms Represent de Rham Cohomology, Thm - Hodge Theorem for Elliptic Complexes, Def - Residual Set and Generic Property]; statement: Assume $b_2^+(M)\ge1$. (i) Reducible solutions $(0,A)$ of the perturbed equations satisfy $F_A^+ = \eta$; writing $A = A_0 + a$, $F_A^+ = F_{A_0}^+ + d^+a\in Q := F_{A_0}^+ + \operatorname{Im}d^+$, an affine subspace of $W^{4,2}(\Lambda^2_+T^*M\otimes i\mathbb R)$ of codimension $b_2^+$ (since $\Omega^2_+/\operatorname{Im}d^+\cong\mathcal H^2_+$, the harmonic self-dual forms, of dimension $b_2^+$, by the Hodge theorem for the Atiyah complex); hence if $\eta\notin Q$ there are no reducible solutions. (ii) For a generic (residual) $\eta$ — indeed for $\eta$ outside the proper closed affine subspace $Q$ and inside the transversality set $\mathcal H$ — the perturbed moduli space $\mathcal M_\eta$ contains no reducibles, so $\mathcal M_\eta = \mathcal M^{\mathrm{irr}}_\eta$; proof: (i) $F_A^+ = \mu(0) + \eta = \eta$; the codimension and closedness of $\operatorname{Im}d^+$ from the Hodge decomposition (`Thm - The de Rham Complex is Elliptic ...`, `Thm - Hodge Theorem for Elliptic Complexes`) — **Haydys leaves these asserted; supplied**; (ii) the complement of $Q$ is open and dense (proper affine subspace of positive codimension), intersect with the residual $\mathcal H$; spec: mechanism: **reducibles force the perturbation to lie on a codimension-$b_2^+$ wall, which a generic perturbation avoids when $b_2^+\ge1$**; targets: the invariant needs $b_2^+\ge2$ for wall-independence (§11.5).
- `Thm - Generic Regularity and Orientability of the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space` — type: theorem (anchor; two parts); source items: A-T7.1.26, A-T7.1.27 (Remark 221); prereqs: [Thm - Dimension Formula for the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space, Thm - Generic Absence of Reducible Solutions, Def - Determinant Line Bundle of a Family of Fredholm Operators, Thm - Homotopy Invariance of the Determinant Line Bundle, Thm - Orientation and Bordism Invariance of the Equivariant Moduli Space, Thm - Hodge Theorem for Elliptic Complexes]; statement: For $b_2^+\ge1$ and a generic $\eta$, $\mathcal M_\eta = \mathcal M^{\mathrm{irr}}_\eta$ is a smooth compact manifold of dimension $d$ (from the dimension-formula and reducible-avoidance and compactness pages), and it is orientable; a choice of orientation of the real lines $\det H^0(M;\mathbb R)$, $\det H^1(M;i\mathbb R)$ and $\det H^2_+(M;i\mathbb R)$ (equivalently of $H^0(M;\mathbb R)$, $H^1(M;\mathbb R)$ and a maximal positive subspace of $H^2(M;\mathbb R)$) orients it; proof: over the configuration space $\mathcal B^{\mathrm{irr}}$ the family $(\psi,A)\mapsto D_{(\psi,A)}$ of Fredholm operators has a determinant line bundle $\det D$ (`Def - Determinant Line Bundle of a Family of Fredholm Operators`); by homotopy through $D_0 + tB$, $\det D\cong\det D_0 = \det\slashed D_A^+\otimes\det(d^+ + d^*)$ (`Thm - Homotopy Invariance of the Determinant Line Bundle`); $\slashed D_A^+$ is complex-linear, so $\ker\slashed D_A^+$ and $\operatorname{coker}\slashed D_A^+\cong\ker\slashed D_A^-$ are complex vector spaces, hence canonically oriented, trivialising $\det\slashed D_A^+$; $\ker(d^+ + d^*) = H^1(M;i\mathbb R)$ and $\operatorname{coker}(d^+ + d^*) = H^0(M;i\mathbb R)\oplus H^2_+(M;i\mathbb R)$ are metric-independent up to the maximal-positive-subspace choice (all such being homotopic), so orientations of these cohomology groups trivialise $\det(d^+ + d^*)$; the resulting trivialisation of $\det D$ is $\mathcal G$-invariant (**the content-map gap that the complex orientation varies continuously and is gauge-invariant is filled by noting $\slashed D_A^+$ depends continuously on $(\psi,A)$ and the complex structure is gauge-natural**), so it orients $\mathcal M_\eta$ by `Thm - Orientation and Bordism Invariance of the Equivariant Moduli Space`; the non-canonicity (T7.1.27, Remark 221): the two conventions for orienting a complex space differ by $(-1)^{k(k-1)/2}$, and the cohomology orientations are a topological choice — recorded; spec: mechanism: **the deformation operator is homotopic to a decoupled operator whose Dirac half is complex (hence oriented) and whose Atiyah half has constant cohomology kernel and cokernel, so the determinant line is trivial and orients the moduli space**; targets: the signed count / fundamental class defining the invariant (§11.5).
- `Ex - The Adjoint of the Infinitesimal Gauge Action` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-D7.1.12 (drill); prereqs: [Def - Coulomb Slice and the Irreducible Configuration Space]; spec: derive $R^*_{(\psi,A)}(\dot\psi,\dot a) = 2d^*\dot a + i\operatorname{Re}\langle\psi, i\dot\psi\rangle$ from $R_{(\psi,A)}\xi = (-\xi\psi, 2d\xi)$ by integration by parts against the $L^2$ inner products on spinors and imaginary $1$-forms; verify $R^*$ is surjective at an irreducible $(\psi,A)$ (so the slice is transverse to the orbit).
- `Ex - The Wall of Reducibles has Codimension b_2 plus` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T7.1.22 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Generic Absence of Reducible Solutions, Thm - Hodge Theorem for Elliptic Complexes]; spec: show $\operatorname{Im}d^+\subset\Omega^2_+$ is closed of codimension $b_2^+ = \dim\mathcal H^2_+$; conclude that the reducible wall $Q$ is a closed affine subspace of codimension $b_2^+$ and that a generic $\eta$ (resp. a generic path of $\eta$'s when $b_2^+\ge2$) avoids it (resp. avoids it throughout).
- `Ex - Orientation of the Moduli Space from the Complex Dirac Index` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: A-T7.1.26 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Generic Regularity and Orientability of the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space, Def - Determinant Line Bundle of a Family of Fredholm Operators]; spec: show that the determinant line of a complex-linear Fredholm operator is canonically oriented (both $\ker$ and $\operatorname{coker}$ are complex), so $\det\slashed D_A^+$ is trivialised; combine with a chosen orientation of $H^0\oplus H^1\oplus H^2_+$ to orient $\mathcal M_\eta$; compute how the orientation changes under the two conventions for orienting a complex space (the $(-1)^{k(k-1)/2}$ ambiguity).
- `Ex - The Dimension Formula on the Four-Sphere and the Projective Plane` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: (consistency check of A-T7.1.20); prereqs: [Thm - Dimension Formula for the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space]; spec: for $M = S^4$ ($\chi = 2$, $\operatorname{sign} = 0$, only spin$^c$ structure has $c_1 = 0$) compute $d = -1$ (empty moduli space, no invariant); for $M = \mathbb{CP}^2$ ($\chi = 3$, $\operatorname{sign} = 1$) compute $d$ for the spin$^c$ structures with $c_1(L_{\det}) = (2k+1)H$ and identify which give $d\ge0$; check parity against the odd-dimensional-set-to-zero convention (§11.5).
- `Exercise Index - §11.4 Transversality, Reducibles, the Dimension Formula, and Orientability` — lists: [Ex - The Adjoint of the Infinitesimal Gauge Action, Ex - The Wall of Reducibles has Codimension b_2 plus, Ex - Orientation of the Moduli Space from the Complex Dirac Index, Ex - The Dimension Formula on the Four-Sphere and the Projective Plane]

### §11.5 The Seiberg–Witten Invariant and its Applications
- `Thm - The Perturbed Moduli Space is a Compact Oriented Manifold` — type: theorem; source items: A-T7.2.1 (Theorem 222), A-R7.2.1; prereqs: [Thm - Generic Regularity and Orientability of the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space, Thm - Compactness of the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space, Thm - Generic Absence of Reducible Solutions, Thm - Orientation and Bordism Invariance of the Equivariant Moduli Space]; statement: Assume $b_2^+(M)\ge2$. For any spin$^c$ structure $\sigma$ and any generic $\eta$, the perturbed moduli space $\mathcal M_\eta$ is a smooth compact oriented manifold of dimension $d$ (equation (213)); and for two generic perturbations $\eta_0,\eta_1$, $\mathcal M_{\eta_0}$ and $\mathcal M_{\eta_1}$ are oriented-bordant; proof: smoothness and dimension from `Thm - Dimension Formula for the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space`; no reducibles (so $\mathcal M_\eta = \mathcal M^{\mathrm{irr}}_\eta$) from `Thm - Generic Absence of Reducible Solutions` ($b_2^+\ge1$); compactness from `Thm - Compactness of the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space` (irreducible + compact ⇒ compact manifold); orientation from `Thm - Generic Regularity and Orientability ...`; bordism: a generic path $\eta_t$ from $\eta_0$ to $\eta_1$ gives a parametric moduli space $\bigcup_t\mathcal M_{\eta_t}$ that is a compact oriented bordism (`Thm - Orientation and Bordism Invariance of the Equivariant Moduli Space`), and **$b_2^+\ge2$ ensures the codimension-$b_2^+\ge2$ reducible wall is missed by the generic $1$-parameter family $\{\eta_t\}$** (R7.2.1; the writer supplies "a generic curve avoids a codimension-$\ge2$ affine subspace"), so the bordism contains no reducibles and is smooth; spec: **Haydys assembles this from the preceding sections without proof (Theorem 222); the assembly is written out, the only new point being the $b_2^+\ge2$ transversality of paths**; mechanism: **for $b_2^+\ge2$ the space of good perturbations is connected through paths avoiding reducibles, so the moduli space is well-defined up to oriented bordism**; targets: the Seiberg–Witten invariant.
- `Def - Seiberg-Witten Invariant` — type: definition (anchor; compound: the based gauge group; the framed moduli space and the class $\mu$; the invariant); source items: A-D7.2.1, A-D7.2.2 (Definition 224), A-R7.2.2, A-R7.2.3, A-R7.2.4; prereqs: [Thm - The Perturbed Moduli Space is a Compact Oriented Manifold, Def - Cobordism Class and Characteristic Numbers of a Parametric Moduli Space, Thm - Classification of Spin-c Structures, Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class, Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class]; spec: assume $b_2^+\ge2$; the *based gauge group* $\mathcal G_0 := \{g\in\mathcal G^{6,2} : g(m_0) = 1\}$ for a base point $m_0$, with $\{1\}\to\mathcal G_0\to\mathcal G^{6,2}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ev}_{m_0}}U(1)\to\{1\}$; the *framed moduli space* $\hat{\mathcal M}_\eta := \{$solutions$\}/\mathcal G_0$, a principal $U(1) = \mathcal G/\mathcal G_0$-bundle over $\mathcal M_\eta$, with first Chern class $\mu := c_1(\hat{\mathcal M}_\eta\to\mathcal M_\eta)\in H^2(\mathcal M_\eta;\mathbb Z)$ — the restriction of the universal $U(1)$-bundle $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{irr}}/\mathcal G_0\to\mathcal C^{\mathrm{irr}}/\mathcal G$ (**source typo Haydys B22, p. 71: $\mu$ denotes both this class and the quadratic map of §11.1; the series writes $\mu$ for the quadratic map and $c_1(\hat{\mathcal M}_\eta)$ or $\hat\mu$ for this class**); the *Seiberg–Witten invariant* is the map $\mathrm{sw}:\mathcal S(M)\to\mathbb Z$ on the set $\mathcal S(M)\cong\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ of spin$^c$ structures (via `Thm - Classification of Spin-c Structures`; **Pic convention, group decision 3: written $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$, not $H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$**), $\mathrm{sw}(\sigma) := \langle\hat\mu^{d/2}, [\mathcal M_\eta]\rangle$ if $d = d(\sigma)$ is even (the pairing of the $d/2$-th power of the framing class with the fundamental class of the compact oriented $d$-manifold $\mathcal M_\eta$, via `Def - Cobordism Class and Characteristic Numbers of a Parametric Moduli Space` and `Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class`), and $\mathrm{sw}(\sigma) := 0$ if $d$ is odd (R7.2.4); for $d = 0$ this is the signed count of points; Axiom Motivation (R7.2.3): this realises the abstract scheme of the introduction (a moduli space cut out by a section, its cobordism class paired with a characteristic class), the framing class $\hat\mu$ being the natural degree-two class on $\mathcal B^{\mathrm{irr}}$; **the three unproved sample results (Taubes non-vanishing, Fintushel–Stern, Witten connected-sum vanishing) are recorded here as bold plain-text history if their `[PROPOSED IMPORT]` markers below are refused**; examples: $d = 0$ (signed count); non-example: $d$ odd (invariant is zero by convention).
- `Thm - Seiberg-Witten Invariants are Diffeomorphism Invariants` — type: theorem (anchor; three parts, with the wall-crossing caveat); source items: A-T7.2.1 (the invariance half), A-R7.2.1, A-R7.2.2; prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Invariant, Thm - The Perturbed Moduli Space is a Compact Oriented Manifold, Thm - Orientation and Bordism Invariance of the Equivariant Moduli Space, Def - Cobordism Class and Characteristic Numbers of a Parametric Moduli Space]; statement: Assume $b_2^+(M)\ge2$. (i) $\mathrm{sw}(\sigma)$ is independent of the generic perturbation $\eta$ and of the Riemannian metric $g$ (through the space of pairs $(g,\eta)$, which is connected and whose good locus avoids reducibles for $b_2^+\ge2$). (ii) $\mathrm{sw}$ depends only on the diffeomorphism type of $(M,\text{orientation})$ and the spin$^c$ structure: an orientation-preserving diffeomorphism $\phi:M\to M'$ pulls back spin$^c$ structures and $\mathrm{sw}_{M'}(\sigma) = \pm\mathrm{sw}_M(\phi^*\sigma)$ (the sign from the orientation of $H^0\oplus H^1\oplus H^2_+$). (iii) (**Wall-crossing / $b_2^+$ caveat**) for $b_2^+ = 1$ the space of good pairs $(g,\eta)$ is disconnected by the reducible wall (codimension $1$), and $\mathrm{sw}$ jumps by a computable wall-crossing term across the wall — so $\mathrm{sw}$ is only a *chamber-wise* invariant; for $b_2^+ = 0$ it is not defined; proof: (i),(ii) $\mathcal M_{\eta_0}$ and $\mathcal M_{\eta_1}$ (and for two metrics $g_0,g_1$ the corresponding moduli spaces) are oriented-bordant through a family avoiding reducibles ($b_2^+\ge2$, path-transversality; `Thm - The Perturbed Moduli Space ...`, `Thm - Orientation and Bordism Invariance ...`), and the characteristic number $\langle\hat\mu^{d/2}, [\mathcal M]\rangle$ is a bordism invariant (`Def - Cobordism Class and Characteristic Numbers ...`, Stokes; the framing bundle extends over the bordism); (iii) the wall-crossing statement is recorded with the codimension-$b_2^+$ computation of `Thm - Generic Absence of Reducible Solutions` (for $b_2^+ = 1$ the wall is a hypersurface; the jump term is the Euler number of an obstruction bundle over the reducible — stated, with the reference Morgan, *The Seiberg–Witten Equations*, Ch. 6, as **bold context, not proved**, since the full wall-crossing formula is beyond the sources); spec: **the metric/perturbation independence is exactly the bordism invariance of chapter X applied to the parametric moduli space; the $b_2^+ = 1$ caveat is the reason the invariant is stated for $b_2^+\ge2$**; mechanism: **two generic choices are joined by a generic bordism, along which the signed characteristic number is unchanged, provided the reducible wall (codimension $b_2^+$) is missed — which needs $b_2^+\ge2$**; targets: the invariant is a diffeomorphism invariant of smooth four-manifolds, distinguishing smooth structures (below).
- `Thm - Finiteness of the Seiberg-Witten Invariant` — type: theorem; source items: A-T7.2.2 (Theorem 225), A-I7.2.2; prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Invariant, Thm - Curvature Bounds for Seiberg-Witten Solutions, Thm - Dimension Formula for the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space, Thm - Top de Rham Cohomology of a Closed Connected Oriented Manifold, Thm - Classification of Spin-c Structures, Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold]; statement: The Seiberg–Witten invariant $\mathrm{sw}:\mathcal S(M)\to\mathbb Z$ is non-zero for at most finitely many spin$^c$ structures; proof: if $\mathrm{sw}(\sigma)\ne0$ then $\mathcal M_\eta\ne\varnothing$ and $d = d(\sigma)\ge0$, so $c_1(L_{\det})^2\ge2\chi(M) + 3\operatorname{sign}(M)$ (dimension formula, a lower bound); the curvature bound `Thm - Curvature Bounds for Seiberg-Witten Solutions` gives $\|F_A\|_{L^2}^2\le C$ with $C$ depending only on the metric and topology (footnote 6: the perturbed estimate), so the harmonic representative $h$ of $\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F_A$ has $\|h\|_{L^2}\le\|\tfrac{i}{2\pi}F_A\|_{L^2}\le C'$ (harmonic representatives minimise the $L^2$-norm in their de Rham class, `Thm - Top de Rham Cohomology of a Closed Connected Oriented Manifold` and the Hodge decomposition), so the self-intersection $c_1(L_{\det})^2 = \int_M h\wedge h$ is bounded above; hence $c_1(L_{\det})$ ranges over a finite subset of the lattice of integral harmonic classes of bounded norm; **the lattice input — that integral classes of bounded harmonic norm are finite in number, and that $\mathcal S(M)\to H^2(M;\mathbb Z)$, $\sigma\mapsto c_1(L_{\det})$, has finite fibres — is `[NEEDED FROM XII–XIII: the integral lattice $H^2(M;\mathbb Z)/\text{torsion}$ with its intersection pairing]`; in the series before chapter XII this is recorded as bold plain-text context with `Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold` (XIII) named, and the norm-boundedness/finiteness argument is carried on the de Rham lattice $H^2_{dR}(M;\mathbb Z)$ of classes with integral periods** (which chapter XI can define via `Thm - Top de Rham Cohomology ...` and integrality of $c_1$ over surfaces); **source typo (Haydys B22 / the printed identity $|c_1\cup\operatorname{PD}(c_1)| = \tfrac1{4\pi^2}\|F_A\|_{L^2}^2$): the correct statement uses the harmonic representative, $\tfrac1{4\pi^2}\|h\|_{L^2}^2$; the writer states it for $h$**; spec: mechanism: **a non-zero invariant forces a solution whose curvature is bounded in $L^2$, so its first Chern class is a lattice point of bounded length, and there are only finitely many of those**; Bridges: the finiteness underlies the notion of a "basic class."
- `Thm - Vanishing of the Seiberg-Witten Invariant on Positive Scalar Curvature Metrics` — type: theorem; source items: A-T7.2.3 (Theorem 226, Witten); prereqs: [Thm - Seiberg-Witten Invariants are Diffeomorphism Invariants, Thm - The C0 Bound on the Spinor, Thm - Positive Scalar Curvature Kills Harmonic Spinors, Thm - Generic Absence of Reducible Solutions]; statement: If $M$ with $b_2^+\ge2$ admits a Riemannian metric of positive scalar curvature ($s_g>0$), then $\mathrm{sw}_M\equiv0$; proof: for the metric $g$ with $s_g>0$ and a small generic perturbation $\eta$, the $C^0$ bound `Thm - The C0 Bound on the Spinor` gives $|\psi|^2\le\max\{0, -\min s_g\} + C\|\eta\|_{C^0} = C\|\eta\|_{C^0}$ (since $\min s_g>0$); for $\eta$ small enough this forces $\psi\equiv0$ (the strong maximum principle applied to the Bochner identity, `Thm - Positive Scalar Curvature Kills Harmonic Spinors` in spirit — the writer runs the argument: $\int(|\nabla_A\psi|^2 + \tfrac14 s_g|\psi|^2)\le C\|\eta\|\|\psi\|^2$, so small $\eta$ and $s_g>0$ give $\psi = 0$), so all solutions are reducible; but $b_2^+\ge1$ and generic $\eta$ exclude reducibles (`Thm - Generic Absence of Reducible Solutions`), so $\mathcal M_\eta = \varnothing$ and $\mathrm{sw}(\sigma) = 0$ for every $\sigma$; the metric-independence of $\mathrm{sw}$ (`Thm - Seiberg-Witten Invariants are Diffeomorphism Invariants`, $b_2^+\ge2$) makes the vanishing hold for all metrics; spec: **this is "the vanishing theorem for positive scalar curvature" the task requires, proved in full (Haydys states it as "follows easily from Weitzenböck")**; mechanism: **positive scalar curvature forces the spinor to vanish through the Weitzenböck maximum principle, leaving only reducibles, which a generic perturbation removes**; targets: obstruction to positive scalar curvature; non-existence of PSC metrics on manifolds with $\mathrm{sw}\ne0$ (e.g. symplectic manifolds via Taubes).
- `Thm - Non-Smoothable Topological Four-Manifolds and Donaldson's Theorem` — type: theorem; source items: A-T7.2.6 (Theorem 229, Donaldson); prereqs: [Thm - Seiberg-Witten Invariants are Diffeomorphism Invariants, Def - The E8 Form and the K3 Surface, Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature, Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem, Thm - Rokhlin Theorem, Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold]; statement: There exist closed simply connected topological four-manifolds admitting no smooth structure; concretely, a closed simply connected topological four-manifold with definite intersection form $E_8\oplus E_8$ (or any definite non-diagonalisable unimodular form) exists (Freedman) but carries no smooth structure (Donaldson); proof: Freedman's realisation theorem (imported) produces, for every unimodular symmetric bilinear form $Q$, a closed simply connected topological four-manifold $X_Q$ with intersection form $Q$; if $X_Q$ admitted a smooth structure with $Q$ definite, then by **Donaldson's diagonalisation theorem `Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem` (chapter XIII anchor — proved there in the form the sources give it; the Seiberg–Witten route to the same conclusion runs through the reducible solutions on a definite manifold and the dimension count, sketched here and cross-referenced)** $Q$ would be diagonalisable, i.e. $Q\cong\pm\langle1\rangle^{\oplus n}$; since $E_8\oplus E_8$ (`Def - The E8 Form and the K3 Surface`) is definite, even, and non-diagonalisable (`Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature`), no smooth structure exists; the parallel Rokhlin obstruction (`Thm - Rokhlin Theorem`: a smooth closed spin four-manifold has signature divisible by $16$) is noted as the alternative for the $E_8$ form of signature $8$; **`Imported without proof` callout (second and last import in VII–XI, pre-approved import 1): Freedman's classification of simply connected closed topological four-manifolds** — every unimodular form is realised by such a manifold, unique up to homeomorphism in the even case — source Freedman, *The topology of four-dimensional manifolds*, J. Diff. Geom. 17 (1982); Freedman–Quinn, *Topology of 4-Manifolds*, with the architecture paragraph (the disc-embedding theorem and Casson handles) and the note that the Seiberg–Witten and Donaldson inputs used here are proved; spec: **this is Donaldson's diagonalisation theorem together with its consequence (non-smoothability), carried in chapter XI as the source lists it (T7.2.6); the diagonalisation statement is owned by chapter XIII, referenced by name**; mechanism: **Freedman realises every form topologically; Donaldson forbids the definite non-diagonal ones smoothly; the gap between them is the exotic phenomenon of dimension four**; Bridges: the $\tfrac{11}{8}$-conjecture and Furuta's bound (bold context; XIII).
- `Thm - Non-Vanishing for Symplectic Four-Manifolds` — type: theorem; source items: A-T7.2.5 (Theorem 228, Taubes); prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Invariant, Thm - Vanishing of the Seiberg-Witten Invariant on Positive Scalar Curvature Metrics]; statement: If $M$ is a closed symplectic four-manifold with $b_2^+(M)\ge2$, then $\mathrm{sw}_M(\sigma_{\mathrm{can}}) = \pm1$ for the canonical spin$^c$ structure, so $\mathrm{sw}_M\not\equiv0$; in particular a symplectic four-manifold with $b_2^+\ge2$ admits no metric of positive scalar curvature; proof: **`[PROPOSED IMPORT: Taubes's theorem $SW = Gr$ / the non-vanishing $\mathrm{sw}(\sigma_{\mathrm{can}}) = \pm1$ for symplectic four-manifolds]`** — the proof (Taubes, *The Seiberg–Witten and Gromov invariants*, and *SW ⟹ Gr*) perturbs the equations by a large multiple of the symplectic form, forces the spinor to concentrate, and identifies solutions with pseudo-holomorphic curves; it is far beyond the sources and not on the pre-approved list; **orchestrator: approve as an import (with the carrying page named here), or the result is stated only as bold plain-text history in `Def - Seiberg-Witten Invariant` and never in a theorem blockquote**; the consequence (no PSC metric) is then a corollary of `Thm - Vanishing of the Seiberg-Witten Invariant on Positive Scalar Curvature Metrics`, proved in full once the non-vanishing is granted; spec: mechanism (of the consequence, proved): **a non-zero invariant is incompatible with positive scalar curvature**.
- `Thm - Vanishing for Connected Sums and Infinitely Many Smooth Structures` — type: theorem (two parts, both proposed imports); source items: A-T7.2.4 (Theorem 227, Witten), A-T7.2.7 (Theorem 230, Fintushel–Stern), A-R7.2.5; prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Invariant, Thm - Seiberg-Witten Invariants are Diffeomorphism Invariants, Thm - Vanishing of the Seiberg-Witten Invariant on Positive Scalar Curvature Metrics]; statement: (i) If $M = M_1\# M_2$ with $b_2^+(M_i)\ge1$ then $\mathrm{sw}_M\equiv0$.
