(ii) There are infinitely many pairwise non-diffeomorphic but mutually homeomorphic closed four-manifolds (distinguished by $\mathrm{sw}$); proof: **both are `[PROPOSED IMPORT]` markers.** (i) `[PROPOSED IMPORT: Witten's connected-sum vanishing]` — the neck-stretching argument (stretch the connecting $S^3$; solutions concentrate; $S^3\times\mathbb R$ has positive scalar curvature so carries no finite-energy irreducible solution; $b_2^+\ge1$ on each side removes reducibles) is a cylindrical-end analysis beyond the sources and not pre-approved; if approved it is proved on this page using `Thm - Vanishing ...on Positive Scalar Curvature Metrics` for the neck; if refused, stated as bold history. (ii) `[PROPOSED IMPORT: Fintushel–Stern knot surgery / the log-transform construction]` — infinitely many smooth structures on a fixed homeomorphism type are produced by knot surgery on an elliptic surface, the Seiberg–Witten invariant computing the Alexander polynomial; far beyond the sources; if refused, stated as bold history in `Def - Seiberg-Witten Invariant`; spec: **per group decision 6 (updated): the two originally planned proposed imports (Taubes, Fintushel–Stern) plus Witten's connected-sum vanishing are the three `[PROPOSED IMPORT]` requests of §11.5**; mechanism (of what is proved): **the Seiberg–Witten invariant, being a diffeomorphism invariant, separates smooth structures whenever it can be computed to differ**; Bridges: exotic $\mathbb R^4$ (XIII §13.8).
- `Ex - The Seiberg-Witten Invariant of a Positive Scalar Curvature Manifold Vanishes` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-T7.2.3 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Vanishing of the Seiberg-Witten Invariant on Positive Scalar Curvature Metrics]; spec: for $M = S^4$, $\mathbb{CP}^2\#\overline{\mathbb{CP}^2}$-type manifolds with round/product PSC metrics (assume $b_2^+\ge2$ by taking connected sums with enough copies where needed, or argue chamber-wise), show every moduli space is empty and $\mathrm{sw}\equiv0$; contrast with the symplectic case where $\mathrm{sw}\ne0$ obstructs PSC.
- `Ex - The Framing Class and the Zero-Dimensional Invariant` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐; source items: A-D7.2.2 (drill); prereqs: [Def - Seiberg-Witten Invariant]; spec: for a spin$^c$ structure with $d(\sigma) = 0$, show $\mathrm{sw}(\sigma)$ is the signed count of points of $\mathcal M_\eta$ (the framing class plays no role); for $d(\sigma) = 2$, describe $\mathrm{sw}(\sigma) = \langle\hat\mu, [\mathcal M_\eta]\rangle$ as the degree of the framing $U(1)$-bundle over the compact oriented surface $\mathcal M_\eta$.
- `Ex - The Dimension Formula Constrains Basic Classes` — type: exercise; difficulty: ⭐⭐⭐; source items: A-T7.2.2 (drill); prereqs: [Thm - Finiteness of the Seiberg-Witten Invariant, Thm - Dimension Formula for the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space]; spec: combine the lower bound $c_1(L_{\det})^2\ge2\chi + 3\operatorname{sign}$ (from $d\ge0$) with the upper bound from the curvature estimate to show that the set of $c_1(L_{\det})$ with $\mathrm{sw}\ne0$ lies in a bounded region of the lattice; for a manifold with negative definite intersection form deduce a constraint on characteristic elements (forward reference to Donaldson's theorem, XIII), noting the lattice input is `[NEEDED FROM XII–XIII]`.
- `Exercise Index - §11.5 The Seiberg–Witten Invariant and its Applications` — lists: [Ex - The Seiberg-Witten Invariant of a Positive Scalar Curvature Manifold Vanishes, Ex - The Framing Class and the Zero-Dimensional Invariant, Ex - The Dimension Formula Constrains Basic Classes]

Chapter XI page count: §11.1: 4 Def/Thm + 3 Ex + 1 index = 8; §11.2: 5 Def/Thm + 3 Ex + 1 index = 9; §11.3: 6 Def/Thm + 3 Ex + 1 index = 10; §11.4: 7 Def/Thm + 4 Ex + 1 index = 12; §11.5: 8 Def/Thm + 3 Ex + 1 index = 12; topic page 1. **Total XI: 52 pages** (30 Def/Thm, 16 Ex, 5 indices, 1 topic).

## `Imported without proof` callouts in chapters VII–XI

Both are on the pre-approved list of `conventions.md`. Each is stated exactly, sourced, described architecturally, and every consequence drawn from it is proved with the import invoked as an explicit hypothesis; each chapter's topic page lists it under `# Imported Results`.

| Result | Page (chapter) | Pre-approved item |
|---|---|---|
| The Atiyah–Singer index theorem for the spin$^c$ Dirac operator, $\operatorname{index}_{\mathbb C}\slashed D_A^+=\tfrac18(c_1(L_{\det})^2-\operatorname{sign}(M))$ (Lawson–Michelsohn *Spin Geometry* Thm III.13.10, IV.1.1) | Thm - Dimension Formula for the Seiberg-Witten Moduli Space (XI) | conventions.md item 3 |
| Freedman's classification of simply connected closed topological four-manifolds (every unimodular form is realised; homeomorphism-uniqueness in the even case) (Freedman, J. Diff. Geom. 17 (1982); Freedman–Quinn) | Thm - Non-Smoothable Topological Four-Manifolds and Donaldson's Theorem (XI) | conventions.md item 1 |

No other page in VII–XI carries an `Imported without proof` callout. In particular the Coulomb-gauge lemma (Haydys I7.1.5 / Morgan 5.3.1) and Aronszajn's unique continuation (Haydys I7.1.9) are **proved in full** in chapter XI, not imported.

**`[PROPOSED IMPORT]` markers awaiting the orchestrator's decision (not yet imports; if refused each becomes bold plain-text history, never a theorem blockquote):**
- Every closed oriented 4-manifold admits a spin$^c$ structure; every closed oriented 3-manifold is spin (Haydys T4.3.1/I4.3.3) — on `Def - Spin Structure and Spin-c Structure` (VIII). Nothing in VIII–XI depends on it (every theorem takes a spin$^c$ structure as given).
- Witten's connected-sum vanishing $\mathrm{sw}_{M_1\#M_2}\equiv0$ for $b_2^+(M_i)\ge1$ (Haydys T7.2.4) — on `Thm - Vanishing for Connected Sums and Infinitely Many Smooth Structures` (XI).
- Taubes's non-vanishing $\mathrm{sw}(\sigma_{\mathrm{can}})=\pm1$ for symplectic four-manifolds (Haydys T7.2.5) — on `Thm - Non-Vanishing for Symplectic Four-Manifolds` (XI).
- Fintushel–Stern's infinitely many smooth structures via knot surgery (Haydys T7.2.7) — on `Thm - Vanishing for Connected Sums and Infinitely Many Smooth Structures` (XI).

Donaldson's diagonalisation theorem itself (the engine of Haydys T7.2.6's non-smoothability consequence) is **not imported**: it is the chapter XIII anchor `Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem`, proved there under conventions.md's pre-approved analytic-package import (item 2); chapter XI states it by reference and proves only the non-smoothability consequence, using Freedman.

## Cross-chapter dependency summary (chapters VII–XI)

- **VII** (Hodge star, electrodynamics, Yang–Mills) depends on chapters I–VI (`Def - Principal G-Bundle`, `Def - Connection on a Principal Bundle`, `Def - Curvature of a Principal Connection`, `Thm - Bianchi Identity for a Principal Connection`, `Thm - Structure Equation for the Curvature`, `Thm - Transformation of Local Connection and Curvature Forms`, `Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles`, `Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space`, `Def - Gauge Transformation`, `Thm - Gauge Transformations Act on Connections and Curvature`, `Def - Ad-Invariant Polynomial`, `Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem`, `Def - Pontryagin Classes`, `Def - Chern Classes`, `Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature`, `Def - The Hopf Bundle`) and on existing vault pages (Hodge Theory I, DG VIII–XII, RG I/III, GR I, SR XXI–XXIII, Linear Algebra IX/VI); it provides VIII–XI with `Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature`, `Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature`, `Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions`, `Def - The Yang-Mills Field Strength`, `Def - Self-Dual and Anti-Self-Dual Connections`, `Def - Instanton`, `Thm - Energy Identity and the Topological Bound for Yang-Mills`, `Ex - Energy Identity for U(1) Connections on a Four-Manifold`, `Thm - Top de Rham Cohomology of a Closed Connected Oriented Manifold`. Four B §3.3 items land on chapter I–VI anchors (recorded there, flagged for group 1).
- **VIII** (Clifford, spin, Dirac) depends on I (`Def - Representation of a Lie Group`, `Thm - Complex Representations of U(1) and SU(2)`), III (`Def - Reduction and Extension of the Structure Group`, `Def - Associated Bundle`, `Def - Frame Bundle of a Vector Bundle`, `Thm - Principal Bundles are Classified by Cocycles`, `Thm - Classification of Principal U(1)-Bundles by the First Chern Class`, `Def - The Hopf Bundle`), IV (`Thm - Principal Connections Induce Covariant Derivatives on Associated Bundles`), II (curvature, connections, affine space), VII (`Thm - Self-Dual Decomposition of 2-Forms in Four Dimensions`), and existing vault pages (Spinors, Hodge I, RG I–III, DG VIII/IX/XI); it re-proves four flagged Spinors pages under new names. It provides IX–XI with `Def - Spin-c Dirac Operator Twisted by a Connection`, `Thm - Variation of the Twisted and Spin-c Dirac Operators with the Connection`, `Def - Spin Structure and Spin-c Structure`, `Thm - Classification of Spin-c Structures`, `Thm - Spin-c(3) is U(2) and Spin-c(4) is the Fibre Product of Two Copies of U(2)`, `Thm - Spinor Representations of Spin(4) and the Self-Dual Forms`, `Thm - Clifford Action of 2-Forms and the Chiral Weitzenbock Formula in Dimension Four`, `Thm - Lichnerowicz Formula for the Spin-c Dirac Operator`, `Thm - Positive Scalar Curvature Kills Harmonic Spinors`, `Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint`, `Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian`, `Thm - Existence of Synchronous Orthonormal Frames`. It carries one forward request to XII (`[NEEDED FROM XII: π₁(SO(n))≅ℤ/2]`, never invoked) and one `[PROPOSED IMPORT]` (spin$^c$ existence).
- **IX** (Sobolev, elliptic, Fredholm, Hodge) depends on II (`Def - Connection on a Vector Bundle`, `Thm - The Space of Connections is an Affine Space`, `Thm - Tensoriality Lemma for C-Infinity-Linear Maps`), VII (`Def - Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature`, `Thm - Properties of the Hodge Star in Arbitrary Signature`), VIII (`Def - Spinor Bundle and Dirac Operator`, `Thm - The Hodge-de Rham Operator is a Dirac Operator`, `Thm - Dirac Operators are Formally Self-Adjoint`, `Thm - Local Formula and Self-Adjointness of the Connection Laplacian`), and existing vault pages (Linear Algebra, Topology I–IV including `Thm - Baire Category Theorem` and the open mapping theorem, DG II/VI/VIII–X/XII, Hodge I, Measure Theory II as context only). It re-proves the flagged `Thm - Sard's Theorem` only in chapter X; here it proves in full the Sobolev/elliptic/Fredholm/Hodge package that the vault's Hodge Theorem I sketches. It provides X and XI with `Def - Fredholm Operator and Index`, `Thm - Stability of the Fredholm Property under Small and Compact Perturbations`, `Thm - Stabilisation of Fredholm Operators and Continuity of the Kernel`, `Thm - Sobolev Embedding Theorem`, `Thm - Rellich Compactness Theorem`, `Thm - Sobolev Multiplication Theorem`, `Thm - Composition with Analytic Functions on the Sobolev Algebra`, `Thm - Elliptic Regularity and the Elliptic Estimate`, `Thm - Elliptic Operators on Closed Manifolds are Fredholm`, `Thm - Fredholm Alternative for Elliptic Operators`, `Thm - Hodge Theorem for Elliptic Complexes`, `Thm - A Short Complex is Elliptic iff its Folded Operator is Elliptic`, `Thm - The de Rham Complex is Elliptic and Harmonic Forms Represent de Rham Cohomology`, `Thm - Laplacians and Dirac Operators are Elliptic`. It absorbs two §6.4 items (I6.4.1, I6.4.2) and six §7.1 analytic items (I7.1.2, I7.1.6, I7.1.7, I7.1.10, I7.1.12, I7.1.13).
- **X** (Fredholm maps, transversality, degree, equivariance) depends on IX (the whole Fredholm/Sobolev package), VI (`Def - Chern Classes`, `Thm - First Chern Class of a Line Bundle from Curvature`, `Thm - Chern-Weil Theorem` for characteristic numbers), and existing vault pages (Multivariate Analysis II, DG I–V/IX/X, Topology including `Thm - Baire Category Theorem`, Linear Algebra IX/VI, RG IV as context); it re-proves the flagged `Thm - Sard's Theorem` as `Thm - Sard's Theorem for Smooth Maps` and the flagged partitions-of-unity/Brouwer-degree pages inline where needed; its `Thm - Proper Maps are Closed and Fredholm Maps are Locally Proper` is invoked by §10.2's Sard–Smale and should be sequenced before it. It provides XI with `Thm - Sard-Smale Theorem`, `Thm - Parametric Transversality`, `Def - Determinant Line Bundle of a Family of Fredholm Operators`, `Thm - Homotopy Invariance of the Determinant Line Bundle`, `Def - Integer Degree of an Oriented Proper Fredholm Map`, `Def - Cobordism Class and Characteristic Numbers of a Parametric Moduli Space`, `Def - Infinitesimal Action and Local Slice`, `Def - The Equivariant Deformation Complex and its Folded Operator`, `Thm - A Free Action with Slices has a Manifold Quotient`, `Thm - The Equivariant Moduli Space is a Manifold of Index Dimension`, `Thm - Orientation and Bordism Invariance of the Equivariant Moduli Space`. It absorbs two §7.1 items (T7.1.24, T7.1.25). One `[NEEDED FROM XII]` (integral periods of a Chern class).
- **XI** (Seiberg–Witten) depends on VII (Hodge star, self-dual decomposition, energy identity, top de Rham cohomology), VIII (spin$^c$ Dirac operators, Weitzenböck/Lichnerowicz, spinor representations, PSC vanishing), IX (Sobolev/elliptic/Fredholm/Hodge package), X (Sard–Smale, parametric transversality, determinant line bundles, the equivariant slice-and-moduli machinery), III/VI (line-bundle and Chern-class classification, in the $\operatorname{Pic}(M)$ form), and existing vault pages (Hodge I, Linear Algebra, RG III, DG IX/XII). It uses chapter XII–XIII anchors by name (`Thm - Poincare Duality`, `Def - R-Orientation and the Fundamental Class`; `Def - Intersection Form of a Four-Manifold`, `Def - Unimodular Symmetric Bilinear Form, Parity, and Signature`, `Def - Characteristic Element of a Unimodular Form`, `Def - The E8 Form and the K3 Surface`, `Thm - Donaldson Diagonalisation Theorem`, `Thm - Rokhlin Theorem`) and writes three `[NEEDED FROM XII–XIII]` markers (the integral second-cohomology lattice and pairing; the Betti-number expressions of $\chi$ and $\operatorname{sign}$; Freedman's realisation, carried as the pre-approved import). It carries the series' two `Imported without proof` callouts (Atiyah–Singer, Freedman) and three `[PROPOSED IMPORT]` markers (Witten connected-sum, Taubes, Fintushel–Stern). It provides chapter XIII with `Def - Seiberg-Witten Invariant`, `Thm - Seiberg-Witten Invariants are Diffeomorphism Invariants`, and `Thm - Vanishing of the Seiberg-Witten Invariant on Positive Scalar Curvature Metrics` (referenced by XIII's Donaldson and exotic-$\mathbb R^4$ pages).
